# Interaction latency audit — 2026-08-26 (overnight polish pass)

Twelve measured input-to-visible-feedback path traces plus three unification
lenses (input, output, state/ownership), all verified against the dev tree.
Every latency number was computed from code (cadence x work), with the
arithmetic in the hop tables. Machine-readable copy: scratchpad
latency_audit.json.

Design north star (Kevin, 2026-08-26): the device should feel like an
extension of the user's mind - instant, predictable, hints not instructions.
Old-code behavior is preserved; numbers below marked "guard" are deliberate
design choices to re-discuss, not bugs.


## LED render pipeline on core 1: requestLedShow -> core2stuff -> showNets -> leds.show() -> WS2812 DMA -> photons

**Total: Measured against the tree's own hardware probe (CodeDocs/SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md:719,747: req->pickup ~0.15 ms, send->show 5.6-8.5 ms typical / 10.2-10.6 ms max, req->show max 10.8-11.5 ms), which lets me back out the render work R = 7.1 ms typical send->show minus the 4.0 ms mean tick wait = R ~= 2.5-3.1 ms.

BEST (menu/graphics flush, mode 2/3, strip idle): post 0.5 us + next core2stuff pass 50-300 us + menuTransitionRender 300 px x 25 cy / 150 MHz = 50 us + rp2040Show expansion 45 us + first photons 31.5 us = ~0.2 ms to stream start; whole strand latched at +9450 us => ~9.6 ms.

TYPICAL (routing change -> requestLedShow(1)/(-1)): 0.5 us post + 0-8000 us tick wait (mean 4000) + R 2500-3100 us + 0-396 us canShow spin + 45 us expansion + 9450 us wire = 16.0-17.0 ms to a fully latched strand. Consistent with the doc's req->show ~7.3 ms (which stamps at show START) + 9.45 ms of wire.

WORST, no frame hold: 8000 us tick + 8000 us because a pending SEND stole that tick (main.cpp:1774/2002) + 3100 us render + 8000 us because leds.show() found bb DMA busy and dropped the frame (LEDs.cpp:193) + 9450 us wire = 36.6 ms. If the drop is not followed by another request, the recovery is the 51 ms swirl instead of 8 ms => 51 + 8 + 3.1 + 9.45 = 71.6 ms.

WORST, blocking frame (requestLedShow(12), 10 call sites): up to 9750 us of leading canShow() + 4568 us (top, sequential) + 9450 us (bb, sequential) + 600 us latch = 24.4 ms, all of it with core2busy raised, so core 0's waitCore2() sits behind it (25 ms timeout, Commands.cpp:120).

WORST, frame hold: unbounded by the holder. refreshConnections() holds across the entire netlist rebuild (Commands.cpp:209-249, tens of ms); a flash write is documented at ~200 ms of core-0 stall. loop1 spins at main.cpp:1544 and renders nothing.

IDLE (no request): 51 ms swirl timer + up to 8 ms tick + 3 ms render + 9.45 ms wire = 61-72 ms => ~16 Hz.

DOMINANT TERM: for any requested change it is the pair "8 ms scheduler tick (mean 4 ms of pure dead wait) + 9.45 ms of WS2812 wire time", together ~13.5 ms of the ~16.5 ms typical - the render itself (2.5-3.1 ms, of which the ants are a bounded 1.2 ms) is the minority. For idle animation the dominant term is the 51 ms swirl timer. For anything that trips a frame hold, the hold dominates everything else by an order of magnitude.**

| stage | where | mechanism | latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| Core 0 posts the request: requestLedShow(1/2/3/-1/12) packs the legacy int into mailbox bits (LED_NETS/MENU/GFX + LED_CLEAR + LED_BLOCKING) and calls postMode() under SIO spinlock OS2. Also latches s_ledGfxOwned unconditionally. | `src/Commands.cpp:66` | Non-blocking cross-core post. postMode() (CoreMailbox.cpp:33) takes a fixed hardware spinlock, does ~6 loads/stores, releases. MENU carries keepIfPresentMask = NETS\|GFX so a flush cannot downgrade a pending full render. | Spinlock region is ~6 loads/stores + __dmb. At 150 MHz, ~20-30 cycles = 0.15-0.2 us, plus at most the other core's identical critical section (~0.2 us) = <0.5 us. Measured req->pickup for the sibling SEND slot is 99-192 us typical (docs line 719), which is pass-arrival latency, not lock cost. |
| core 1 loop1() entry: kickGapStamp, then a spin on core1FramesHeld() while any hold is up (only REQ_BYPASS path sends are serviced inside the spin), then t[0..21] zeroing. | `src/main.cpp:1544` | Bare inline load of core1FrameHoldDepth[0]\|[1] (externVars.h:29). While held, NO LED work happens at all - loop1 spins. Holders: refreshConnections (Commands.cpp:209) for the whole netlist rebuild, FlashPark, MicroPython, Menus 5454/5476, Apps 1718, Debugs. | Spin cost per iteration ~10 cycles = 67 ns. Duration = the holder's. refreshConnections holds across loadBridgesFromState + getNodesToConnect + bridgesToPaths = tens of ms; a flash write is documented at ~200 ms core-0 stall (docs line 555). This is the single largest term in the whole path when it fires. |
| loop1 body before core2stuff: powerSupplySense (1 Hz), wavegen.service() only if running, then core2stuff(). | `src/main.cpp:1622` | Polled, unconditional call every loop1 pass. wavegen.service() contains a blocking I2C while() loop when streaming. | readAdcVoltage(6,4) at 1 Hz = 4 samples x ~8 us = 32 us once per 1000 ms = 0.03 us/pass amortized. wavegen off = 0. So ~0 us added on a normal pass. |
| core2stuff preamble: rotaryEncoderStuff() polled unconditionally (self-throttled to 2 kHz), ch446qDmaService() stall watchdog, REQ_BYPASS fast path with a 1 ms core_sync try. | `src/main.cpp:1688` | Polled. The BYPASS branch `return`s immediately after servicing - it skips the entire LED block for that pass. | Encoder poll: gated at 2 kHz, so it does real work on <=1 pass in every 500 us; the gated pass is a micros() compare, ~0.1 us. A BYPASS pass costs the send (docs: pickup->send ~40-50 us incremental, max ~235 us for a full DMA list send) and costs the LED branch one whole pass = up to 8000 us of deferral. |
| core_sync mutex try-acquire with 0 ms timeout. Failure = the entire pass (LED branch, probe LED, everything under the lock) is abandoned; retry next loop1 pass. | `src/main.cpp:1722` | mutex_try_enter, non-blocking. Core 0 holds core_sync in FileCache (FC_LOCK, FileCache.cpp:47) and JumperlOS.cpp:945/983. | Try itself ~0.2 us. On failure the frame slips one loop1 pass; a non-tick pass is ~50-300 us, so a handful of consecutive failures during a file-cache flush costs 0.3-3 ms. No re-post is needed (a peek is not a take), so nothing is lost - only delayed. |
| Frame-hold re-check after acquiring the mutex; ledFrameAbortsPause++ and release+return if core 0 took a hold while we waited. | `src/main.cpp:1735` | Polled inline load. Counted in the 'led-frame aborts(pause)' field of X (SingleCharCommands.cpp:3434). | ~0.1 us to test. Cost when it fires = the whole hold duration (see hop 2). |
| Menu-transition pacer: if inClickMenu==1 && ledShowIdle() && menuTransitionFrameDue(), post a MENU flush. Deliberately above the LED block so transitions are paced by MT_FRAME_MS and not by the 51 ms swirl timer. | `src/main.cpp:1747` | Polled every core2stuff pass. MT_FRAME_MS = 16 ms (MenuTransitions.cpp:55). Gated on ledShowIdle() = mailbox slot idle AND !s_ledGfxOwned. | 16 ms cadence = 62.5 fps requested. The strip's physical floor is 9.75 ms (102.6 Hz), so the request rate is 1.64x slower than the hardware and no systematic drop occurs here - by design (the comment at MenuTransitions.cpp:50-54 says exactly this). |
| Peek the LED slot (snapshot, not take) and decide whether this pass runs the LED block: `micros()-schedulerTimer > schedulerUpdateTime // ledImmediate`. schedulerUpdateTime = 8000 us (main.cpp:1499). ledImmediate is set only by LED_MENU or LED_GFX. | `src/main.cpp:1768` | Polled tick gate. THIS IS THE MODE ASYMMETRY: mode 2 (menu, 97+8 call sites) and mode 3 (graphics, 15 sites) run on the very next pass; mode 1 / -1 (full netlist render, 54+61+6 = 121 call sites) must wait for the 8 ms tick. | NETS wait = uniform 0..8000 us, mean 4000 us. schedulerTimer is reset with `= micros()` at main.cpp:2044 at the END of whichever branch ran, so the true LED period is 8000 + R (render work), not 8000: with R ~= 2500-3000 us the real NETS frame period is 10.5-11.0 ms, i.e. 91-95 Hz. |
| Branch gate: run the LED block only if ((ledPending && (loadingFile==0 // GFX)) // (swirled==1 && !ledGraphicsOwned())) AND core1req::allIdle(). allIdle() covers REQ_SEND and REQ_BYPASS only (CoreMailbox.cpp:96) - a pending path send goes first. | `src/main.cpp:1774` | Polled. If a SEND is pending the pass falls through to the `else if` at main.cpp:2002 and does sendPaths() instead - the LED frame is skipped entirely for this tick. | A send-stolen pass costs one full tick: +8000 us + R. During a burst of connect/disconnect ops (each posting SEND then a NETS show) the LED can lose one tick per op: 5 ops in a row = 5 x 10.5 ms = 52 ms of visual lag behind the crossbar. |
| take() the request: bits atomically read+cleared, ledGen captured. rails resolved by the ladder GFX(3) > MENU(2) > NETS(1); LED_CLEAR -> clearBeforeSend=1; LED_BLOCKING -> useBlockingMode. | `src/main.cpp:1781` | Spinlock-guarded take (CoreMailbox.cpp:62). Anything posted after this point stays pending for the next pass. The ladder is else-if, so if both NETS and MENU bits are ever present the NETS render is consumed and lost. | ~0.2 us for the locked region. |
| lightUpRail(-1,-1,1) paints all 4 rails (4 x 25 = 100 pixels) and calls lightUpHeader(); then logoSwirl(). Both arbitrate on the non-atomic cross-core flag logoLedAccess. | `src/main.cpp:1791` | Direct RAM pixel writes via setPixelColorRamHelper (3 byte stores, no bounds check). lightUpHeader (LEDs.cpp:3901) SPINS on logoLedAccess with a 5000 us deadline (LEDs.cpp:3907-3909); logoSwirl (LEDs.cpp:3768) just returns if the flag is held. The flag is also taken by dumpLEDs() on core 0 (Graphics.cpp:4599). | Pixel work: ~100 rail + ~30 header + ~7-40 logo = ~170 writes x ~12 cycles / 150 MHz = 13.6 us. Contention: dumpLEDs holds the flag only across its ~50 getPixelColor+colorToVT100 snapshot (Graphics.cpp:4602-4644) = ~30-60 us, so lightUpHeader normally spins <60 us; the deadline lets it spin up to 5000 us before giving up and leaving the header unpainted. |
| clearLEDsExceptRails() when clearBeforeSend==1, then the LAST frame-hold abort check before the long render. On abort: complete(ledGen) + post(taken) so the frame renders after the hold, then release and return. | `src/main.cpp:1836` | Polled. The re-post uses core1req::post() - a plain OR - not postMode(), so it does NOT honour the MENU-must-not-downgrade-NETS keep rule that requestLedShow() enforces. | clearLEDsExceptRails writes 300 pixels x ~12 cycles / 150 MHz = 24 us. The abort test is ~0.1 us. Cost when it fires = the hold duration + one more tick (8000 us) for the re-posted frame. |
| showNets(): assignNetColors + lightUpNet per net (lines mode) OR drawWires() (wires mode, the default) filling wireStatus[61][5] and up to 300 breadboard pixels. | `src/LEDs.cpp:4295` | Blocking, RAM-resident, under core2busy=true (which is exactly what core 0's waitCore2() spins on, Commands.cpp:120). Falls back to line rendering when numberOfShownNets > MAX_NETS_FOR_WIRES. | Pixel-write floor: 300 writes x 12 cycles / 150 MHz = 24 us. The real cost is the per-net/per-path iteration and float colour math. Derived from hardware: measured send->show 7.1 ms typical (docs line 747) minus the mean 4.0 ms tick wait = R ~= 3.1 ms for the whole render block; showNets is the largest single item in it, call it ~1.0-1.5 ms. |
| readGPIO() + readFakeGPIO(), then showLEDmeasurements() (ADC-driven colours from the adcReadings cache). | `src/main.cpp:1867` | Blocking, inline in the frame, still under core2busy. Also: rotaryEncoderStuff() is re-called at main.cpp:1864 mid-frame so clickwheel latency does not stack behind the render. | readGPIO: ~10 digitalRead + colour writes, ~1 us each = ~10-20 us. showLEDmeasurements reads the cache (no converter time, updateLazyAdcReadings keeps it fresh at 1 kHz off the ADC ring, Peripherals.cpp:2287) = ~50-150 us of float->colour work. |
| showAllRowAnimations(): assignRowAnimations + showRowAnimation per net, then renderNetCurrentAnts() - the 1200 us budgeted ants renderer. | `src/Graphics.cpp:2268` | Budgeted cooperative loop: `if (micros()-t0 > kNetAntsBudgetUs)` checked BETWEEN paths; on overrun it bumps antBudgetOverrunTotal, sets resumeSlot = (resumeSlot+s)%count and returns, so the remaining paths are simply NOT DRAWN this frame and rotate in next frame. | Budget = 1200 us (raised from 250 us; sizing comment at Graphics.cpp:1960-1969). Per-path pixel pass is 20-50 us, so the budget covers 1200/50 = 24 paths worst-case and 1200/20 = 60 paths best-case. Beyond that a path's ant step waits a full extra frame: +1 x (8000 + R) = +10.5-11.0 ms, and with N paths it can be ceil(N/24) frames = 21-33 ms for 25-72 animated paths. |
| renderGraphicOverlays() paints overlays on top of everything else. | `src/main.cpp:1883` | Blocking, last item before core2busy=false. Then netUpdateRefreshCount=0. | Bounded pixel writes over the overlay set; small relative to the rest. Folded into the R ~= 2.5-3.0 ms total derived from the send->show measurement. |
| Menu branch alternative (rails==2, inClickMenu//inPadMenu): menuTransitionRender() blends prevFrame->targetFrame over MENU_TRANSITION_PIXELS = 300 pixels, returning false while core 0 is mid-repaint. The re-check at main.cpp:1932 forces needsLedShow=false but the request is still complete()d at main.cpp:1977 - that transition frame is gone. | Blend: 300 pixels x (lerp + setPixelColor) ~= 300 x 25 cycles / 150 MHz = 50 us. A vetoed frame waits for the next MT_FRAME_MS post = +16 ms; MT_LINGER_MS = 50 ms (MenuTransitions.cpp:47) exists solely to guarantee a few more attempts land. |
| The show gate: `if (micros()-ledShowTime > LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME)` with LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME = 14 (main.cpp:1536), compared against micros() - i.e. 14 MICROseconds, not ms. | `src/main.cpp:1953` | Polled. ledShowTime is written nowhere else in the tree (main.cpp:1524 is the only other reference). A render pass is >= 2 ms, so this gate never closes today - but when it does close the frame is dropped SILENTLY and `taken` is already committed for completion at main.cpp:1977. | 14 us threshold vs a >= 2000 us minimum inter-show interval => never fires. Documented as a known wart at CodeDocs/SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md:1678 ('LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME is 14 us, not ms'). |
| leds.show(): per-strip DMA-busy drop test. If bb DMA is busy BOTH strips are skipped; bbDirty/topDirty are left true but nothing reads them (the `if (bbDirty)` guards are commented out - see the FOLLOW-UP note at LEDs.cpp:167-172). | `src/LEDs.cpp:174` | Frame DROP, not a wait. The request is completed regardless at main.cpp:1977, so ledShowIdle() then reports true and nothing re-triggers the lost frame. | bb DMA is busy until S+9366 us (9450 us wire minus the 8-word joined TX FIFO drain = 8 x 8 bits x 1.3125 us = 84 us). The next NETS show attempt lands at S + 8000 + R. Drop iff R < 1366 us. With ants idle and a sparse board R falls under that, so a sparse board drops MORE frames than a busy one - the 250->1200 us ants budget bump incidentally papered over this. |
| JeoPixel::show(): `while (!canShow());` - spins for DMA-not-busy AND TX-FIFO-empty AND 300 us since endTime; then noInterrupts() around rp2040Show(). | `lib/Jadafruit_NeoPixel/JeoPixel.cpp:251` | BLOCKING spin, inside core2busy=true. canShow() (JeoPixel.h:254-279) arms endTime = micros()+12 the first time the FIFO goes idle, then requires 300 us more. | isDMABusy() clears at S+9366 us but canShow() only clears at S+9450+12+300 = S+9762 us. That leaves a 396 us window in which ledClass::show() PASSES its own drop test and then spins here. Worst-case spin per frame = 396 us of blocked core 1 with core2busy raised - which is exactly what core 0's waitCore2() 25 ms spin sits behind. |
| rp2040Show(): expand pixels[] into the 32-bit DMA buffer (`dma_buffer[i] = pixels[i] << 24`) under save_and_disable_interrupts, then dma_channel_transfer_from_buffer_now. | `lib/Jadafruit_NeoPixel/Jeopixel_RP2.cpp:287` | CPU copy with IRQs disabled, then async DMA hand-off. Separate DMA channel and PIO SM per strip (bb = pio0 SM2, top = pio2), so the two strands stream CONCURRENTLY. | bb: numBytes = 300 x 3 = 900 words; top: 145 x 3 = 435 words. At ~5 cycles/word on 150 MHz: bb 900 x 5 / 150e6 = 30 us, top 435 x 5 / 150e6 = 14.5 us => ~45 us of IRQ-off CPU per frame. Memory cost: 4x expansion = 3600 B (bb) + 1740 B (top), doubled by the backup buffers = ~10.7 KB of heap for 1335 B of pixel data. |
| DMA -> PIO -> wire. WS2812 at 800 kHz nominal, clkdiv multiplied by 1.05 on RP2350 for DMA margin (rp2040_pio.h:63-65). | `lib/Jadafruit_NeoPixel/rp2040_pio.h:64` | Hardware, fully async. Both strands in parallel; bb is the long pole. | Bit = 1.25 us x 1.05 = 1.3125 us. Pixel = 24 bits = 31.5 us (30.0 us at nominal 800 kHz). bb strand = 300 x 31.5 = 9450 us (nominal 300 x 30 = 9000 us). top strand = 145 x 31.5 = 4568 us (nominal 4350 us). Concurrent, so wall clock = 9450 us. Plus 300 us latch => hardware frame floor 9750 us = 102.6 Hz. First pixel visible at +31.5 us, last bb pixel at +9450 us. (LEDs.cpp:176's '13ms' comment is the pre-split serial figure: 445 x 30 = 13350 us.) |
| Blocking alternative: leds.showBBBlocking() when LED_BLOCKING was set (requestLedShow(12), 10 call sites). topleds.showBlocking() then bbleds.showBlocking(), SEQUENTIALLY, each preceded by its own `while(!canShow())`. | `src/LEDs.cpp:201` | Fully blocking PIO puts with a per-byte deadline (JeoPixel.cpp:346), plus a FIFO-drain tail spin. Never drops a frame - that is the point - but owns core 1 for the whole stream. | top 435 bytes x 8 bits x 1.3125 us = 4568 us, THEN bb 900 x 8 x 1.3125 = 9450 us, plus two 300 us latch waits = 14618 us = 14.6 ms of blocked core 1. If it lands right after an async frame, the leading canShow() adds up to 9750 us more => 24.4 ms worst case with core2busy raised the whole time. |
| complete(REQ_SHOW_LEDS, ledGen) + ledTakeLog ring entry. Anything posted during the render is still pending and runs next pass. GFX ownership persists as state (s_ledGfxOwned), not as a pending request. | `src/main.cpp:1977` | Spinlock-guarded generation bump. This is what unblocks waitForBlockingDisplay() (Probing.cpp:1780) and menuShowKeepalive() (Menus.cpp:483). Note it fires even when needsLedShow was false - i.e. even when NOTHING reached the strip. | ~0.2 us. Consequence, not cost: waiters believe the frame was shown when it may have been dropped at hop 19 or vetoed at hop 17. |
| Idle path (no request pending): the 51 ms swirl timer bumps swirlCount and sets swirled=1, but the RENDER of that swirl happens on a LATER pass - swirled is consumed by the branch gate at main.cpp:1773. | `src/main.cpp:2012` | Two-pass handshake through a plain int. Gated on ledShowIdle() && core1busy==false && loadingFile==0, and the swirled=1 assignment additionally requires !wavegen.isRunning(). | 51 ms timer + up to 8000 us for the next tick that actually renders + R (2.5-3.0 ms) = 61.5-62 ms per idle frame => ~16 Hz idle refresh, not the ~19.6 Hz the 51 ms timer implies. |
| Sibling on the same core: probeLEDhandler() - the 1-pixel probe strip - runs ONLY in the `else` of the tick gate, i.e. only on passes where the main LED block did NOT run. | `src/main.cpp:2046` | Polled, additionally gated on checkingButton==0 && ProbeButton::getButtonState()==0. In merged-PIO mode the SM streams its own frames at ~2.4 kHz and a colour change is one pio_sm_put (Probing.cpp:711). | Anti-correlated with the main strip by construction: on a tick pass the probe LED gets 0 frames. With a NETS frame every 10.5 ms and non-tick passes at ~50-300 us each, the probe LED sees roughly (10.5 ms - R) / pass_time ~= 7.5 ms / 0.15 ms = ~50 service opportunities between LED frames, so it is not starved in steady state - but it IS starved for the whole duration of a frame hold or a 14.6 ms showBBBlocking. |

**Bottlenecks:**
- The 8 ms scheduler tick applies to NETS but not to MENU/GFX (main.cpp:1768). requestLedShow(1)/(-1) has 121 call sites and every one of them eats 0-8000 us (mean 4000 us) of pure dead wait, while requestLedShow(2)'s 105 call sites run on the very next pass. This is the single largest avoidable term in the path.
- WS2812 wire time, 9450 us for the 300-pixel breadboard strand (300 px x 24 bits x 1.25 us x 1.05 RP2350 clkdiv margin = 31.5 us/px). The 145-pixel top strand (4568 us) runs concurrently on its own PIO SM and DMA channel, so it is free. Add the 300 us latch and the hardware frame floor is 9750 us = 102.6 Hz. Nothing in software can beat this; it is 57% of the typical end-to-end latency.
- schedulerTimer is reset with `= micros()` at the END of the block (main.cpp:2044), not fixed-phase, so the real NETS period is 8000 + R = 10.5-11.0 ms rather than 8000 us. It also resets on the do-nothing `else { rotaryEncoderStuff(); }` branch, so a pass that did no LED work still pushes the next NETS render out a full 8 ms.
- The core1FramesHeld() hold. refreshConnections() (Commands.cpp:209) holds it across the entire netlist rebuild, and loop1 (main.cpp:1544) renders nothing for the duration - tens of ms for a rebuild, ~200 ms for a flash write. The LED pipeline has no partial-progress or freeze-frame concept; it simply stops.
- leds.showBBBlocking() pushes the two strands SEQUENTIALLY (LEDs.cpp:211 then 226) = 4568 + 9450 + 600 = 14.6 ms of blocked core 1, when the hardware could do both concurrently in 9450 us. 4.6 ms of that is pure serialization loss, on a path chosen precisely because it must be fast and atomic.
- The 1200 us ants budget (Graphics.cpp:1969) at 20-50 us per animated path caps a frame at 24-60 paths. Past that, paths rotate in on later frames, so an ant's step latency becomes ceil(N/24) x 10.5 ms.

**Stalls / races:**
- DROPPED FRAME, silently and permanently: leds.show() (src/LEDs.cpp:184,193) skips BOTH strips when bb DMA is busy, leaves bbDirty/topDirty true, and nothing reads those flags (the `if (bbDirty)` guards are commented out and the FOLLOW-UP note at LEDs.cpp:167-172 admits it). Meanwhile main.cpp:1977 completes the request anyway, so ledShowIdle() reports true and no waiter or re-poster notices. Arithmetic for when it fires: bb DMA busy until S+9366 us, next NETS attempt at S+8000+R, so it drops whenever R < 1366 us - i.e. on SPARSE boards, which is the opposite of the intuition. The 250->1200 us ants budget bump accidentally masks this on busy boards.
- MODE DOWNGRADE RACE: the frame-hold abort paths re-post with core1req::post() (main.cpp:1845 and main.cpp:1911), a plain bitwise OR, instead of postMode() with the MENU keep-rule that requestLedShow() enforces (Commands.cpp:92-95). If core 0 posted NETS during the abort window, the slot ends up NETS|MENU; the rails ladder at main.cpp:1783-1785 is else-if with MENU above NETS, so the next take() renders a menu-buffer flush and the full netlist render is consumed and lost. This is the exact failure the mailbox's keep-rule was written to prevent - reintroduced by the abort path.
- SILENT DROP + LEAKED CLEAR: with rails==1 while a menu is up, the branch at main.cpp:1810 (`inClickMenu == 0 || preview || historyScrub`) is false, so needsLedShow stays false, nothing renders, nothing shows, and the request is completed at main.cpp:1977. Worse, clearBeforeSend was already latched to 1 at main.cpp:1786 and is ONLY cleared inside the branch that just got skipped (main.cpp:1832) - so it survives as a global into a later, unrelated frame and blanks the board then. There are 10 requestLedShow(-2) call sites (MENU+CLEAR) that can never consume it in their own frame at all.
- WEDGE via sticky ownership: s_ledGfxOwned (Commands.cpp:60, set at line 79) latches on any requestLedShow(3)/(-3) and is cleared ONLY by another requestLedShow() with a different mode. There is no timeout and no owner-exit hook. While it holds, ledShowIdle() is false, so the swirl branch (main.cpp:2013/2027) is dead, the LED block's swirl arm (main.cpp:1773) is dead, menuShowKeepalive() (Menus.cpp:485) never fires, and waitForBlockingDisplay() (Probing.cpp:1782) burns its full 100 ms timeout on EVERY call. An app or graphics path that exits without posting mode 1/2 freezes the strip indefinitely.
- CROSS-CORE NON-ATOMIC TEST-AND-SET: logoLedAccess (Graphics.cpp:4387) is a plain `if (flag) return; flag = true;` used from core 1 (LEDs.cpp:3768 logoSwirl, 3907 lightUpHeader) and core 0 (Graphics.cpp:4599 dumpLEDs, via LedDumpService). Both cores can read false and both proceed - a torn logo snapshot. Separately, lightUpHeader SPINS on it with a 5000 us deadline (LEDs.cpp:3908) inside the frame, so a core-0 dump can add up to 5 ms to a core-1 LED frame; logoSwirl instead returns, dropping the logo for that frame.
- LATE VETO after commit: main.cpp:1932 forces needsLedShow=false when menuTransitionCanShow() says core 0 started a repaint - but main.cpp:1977 still completes the request. That transition frame is gone; MT_LINGER_MS = 50 ms (MenuTransitions.cpp:47) exists purely to buy extra attempts, and its own comment names leds.show()'s drop as the reason.
- PASS THEFT: a pending REQ_SEND makes core1req::allIdle() false (main.cpp:1774), so the pass takes the `else if` at main.cpp:2002 and sends paths instead of rendering; a pending REQ_BYPASS returns outright at main.cpp:1715. Each stolen pass costs a full 8000 us + R of LED deferral. During a routing burst (each op = SEND then a NETS show) the strip runs one tick behind the crossbar per op.
- LATENT SILENT DROP: the LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME gate (main.cpp:1953) compares against micros() with a value of 14, so it is 14 us and never fires today - but if it ever did (or if someone 'fixes' it to 14 ms), the frame is dropped with no re-post while `taken` is already committed for completion. Same failure class as the DMA drop-test, waiting to happen. Documented as a wart at CodeDocs/SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md:1678.
- TWO-PASS SWIRL HANDSHAKE: main.cpp:2012-2029 only sets swirled=1; the render is consumed on a later pass by main.cpp:1773. So the idle cadence is 51 ms + up to 8 ms + R = 61-62 ms, not the 51 ms the timer names. If a menu opens between the two passes, the swirl frame is composed into the buffer and then discarded unshown (needsLedShow stays false), and swirled is cleared at main.cpp:1994 anyway.
- core2busy is a bare bool raised and lowered at nine places inside core2stuff (main.cpp:1790/1793/1797/1800/1827/1887/1916/1918/1936/1969/2000/2045). Core 0's waitCore2() (Commands.cpp:120) spins on it with a 25 ms timeout-and-proceed. The 396 us canShow() spin (JeoPixel.cpp:251) and the 14.6 ms showBBBlocking both happen with it raised, so core 0's routing path stalls behind LED wire time.

**Quick wins:**
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/LEDs.cpp:174` Make ledClass::show() report whether it actually pushed (return bool, or expose a `pushed` flag), and at the show site re-post the taken bits when it did not - reusing the exact complete()+post() idiom already written 100 lines above at main.cpp:1843-1846. Today a DMA-busy drop loses the frame outright because the dirty flags are dead code (LEDs.cpp:167-172 says so) and the request is completed regardless at main.cpp:1977. This turns 'lost until the next request or the 51 ms swirl' into 'shown 8 ms later'. Pair it with quick win 2 so the re-post cannot itself downgrade a mode.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/main.cpp:1845` The two frame-hold abort paths re-post with core1req::post() - a plain OR - so a re-posted MENU can coalesce with a NETS that core 0 landed during the abort, and the else-if ladder at main.cpp:1783-1785 then picks MENU and eats the NETS render. Use core1req::postMode() with the same keepIfPresentMask = LED_NETS|LED_GFX that requestLedShow() uses (Commands.cpp:95), or equivalently reorder the ladder to prefer NETS over MENU. Restores an invariant the mailbox header already claims to hold (CoreMailbox.h:64-68).
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/main.cpp:1953` Delete the LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME gate. It compares micros() against 14, i.e. 14 microseconds against a >=2 ms minimum inter-show interval, so it never fires - but when it does it drops the frame with no re-post while the request is already committed for completion. It is a landmine with zero current benefit; the real rate limit is JeoPixel::canShow()'s 300 us latch, which is enforced in hardware terms one layer down.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/main.cpp:1786` Clear clearBeforeSend when the nets branch is skipped. It is latched at main.cpp:1786 from LED_CLEAR but consumed only inside the branch at main.cpp:1829, so a requestLedShow(-2) (10 call sites, MENU+CLEAR) or a requestLedShow(-1) taken while a menu is up leaves it set as a global and blanks the board on some later, unrelated frame. Add `else clearBeforeSend = 0;` on the paths that cannot honour it, or move the consume to right after the take. Not strictly behaviour-preserving - it removes a stale blank that users see today - but that blank is unintended.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/LEDs.cpp:201` showBBBlocking() pushes the two strands sequentially: topleds.showBlocking() (435 bytes x 8 bits x 1.3125 us = 4568 us) THEN bbleds.showBlocking() (900 x 8 x 1.3125 = 9450 us), total 14.0 ms plus two 300 us latches. The strips have independent PIO SMs and DMA channels, so kicking both async and then spinning until `!topleds.isDMABusy() && !bbleds.isDMABusy()` gives the same guarantee (frame fully on the wire before return) in 9450 us. Saves 4.57 ms per blocking frame across the 10 requestLedShow(12) call sites, and 4.57 ms of core2busy that core 0's waitCore2() sits behind. Bench: confirm no caller depends on the pin/SM being free the instant showBBBlocking returns (the probe LED shares an SM with the button program, but bb/top do not).
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `lib/Jadafruit_NeoPixel/Jeopixel_RP2.cpp:287` Retire the 4x DMA buffer expansion. rp2040Show copies pixels[] into a uint32 buffer as `pixels[i] << 24` (900 + 435 words = ~45 us of IRQ-off CPU per frame at 5 cycles/word on 150 MHz) and burns 3600 + 1740 bytes, doubled by the unused-in-practice backup buffers = ~10.7 KB of heap for 1335 bytes of pixel data. Configure the channel as DMA_SIZE_8 reading pixels[] directly and writing the PIO TXF, relying on narrow-write byte replication so the byte lands in bits 31:24 where the shift-left/threshold-8 ws2812 program wants it. Also removes the dma_has_pending / dma_buffer_backup swap machinery, which no caller can reach anyway because ledClass::show() never calls into a busy strip. Bench: verify RP2350 narrow-write replication into PIO TXF on a scope before trusting it.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/main.cpp:2012` The swirl branch only sets swirled=1 and the render happens on a later pass, so the idle cadence is 51 ms + up to 8 ms + R = 61-62 ms rather than 51 ms. Either fall through to the render in the same pass, or (cleaner) have the swirl branch post a NETS request instead of setting a separate flag, which also retires `swirled` as a hand-rolled second request channel alongside the mailbox. Changes the idle animation rate (~16 Hz -> ~19 Hz), so it is a visible change and wants Kevin's eyes.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/main.cpp:1768` Self-clock the LED branch instead of using the fixed 8 ms tick for NETS: run whenever a request is pending AND the strip is ready (leds.canShow() / !bbleds.isDMABusy()), which removes the 0-8000 us dead wait (mean 4000 us) from every one of the 121 NETS call sites and paces the pipeline at the real 9750 us hardware floor instead of the drifting 8000 + R. This also removes the drop cliff described above, because a show is only attempted when the strip can take it. Watch the interaction at main.cpp:2046: probeLEDhandler runs ONLY on non-tick passes, so changing what counts as a tick pass changes the probe LED refresh rate - and the encoder/probe branches share the same else-ladder.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Commands.cpp:60` s_ledGfxOwned has no timeout and no explicit release. Any requestLedShow(3)/(-3) latches it and only a later requestLedShow() with a different mode clears it; while it holds, the swirl is dead, menuShowKeepalive never fires, and every waitForBlockingDisplay() burns its full 100 ms. Add an explicit ledGraphicsRelease() called on app/animation exit, plus a deadline (e.g. 2 s without a GFX repost) so a missed release cannot freeze the strip. Design-change because the sticky behaviour is deliberate (it is what the old sticky `showLEDsCore2 = 3` did) - the fix is about failure recovery, not about changing what graphics mode means.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/LEDs.cpp:3907` Replace logoLedAccess with the same SIO-spinlock discipline the mailbox already uses. It is a plain non-atomic test-and-set touched from both cores (LEDs.cpp:3768/3907, Graphics.cpp:4599), and core 1's lightUpHeader spins on it for up to 5000 us inside the LED frame. Even keeping the flag, dropping the deadline from 5000 us to ~200 us would bound the damage: the only cross-core holder (dumpLEDs' snapshot, Graphics.cpp:4602-4644) holds it for ~30-60 us, so 5 ms is 80x more patience than any real holder needs.

**Unification notes:** CADENCE TIMERS - five independent ones on one core, none aware of the strip's real 9750 us floor: the 8000 us scheduler tick (main.cpp:1499/1768), MT_FRAME_MS = 16 ms (MenuTransitions.cpp:55), the 51 ms swirl timer (main.cpp:2012), menuShowKeepalive's 50 ms (Menus.cpp:484), and probeLEDhandler's probe_led_refresh_us (Probing.cpp:7943). The sweep's own executive summary already names this pattern ("every service that needs a cadence re-implements it with millis()", CodeDocs/SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md line ~1690). One "next frame due at" derived from canShow() would replace all five.

DROP-AND-RECOVER IDIOM - MT_LINGER_MS = 50 ms (MenuTransitions.cpp:47) and menuShowKeepalive() (Menus.cpp:483) are both re-request loops that exist ONLY because leds.show() silently drops frames while the previous DMA streams. MenuTransitions.cpp:41 says so verbatim ("leds.show() DROPS frames while the previous DMA transfer is still streaming (a full strip takes ~9ms)"). Fixing the drop at the source (quick win 1) retires both workarounds and the reason Probing's waitForBlockingDisplay() needs a 100 ms timeout.

DIRTY-FLAG MECHANISMS - three of them for one strip. bbDirty/topDirty/markBBDirty/markTopDirty/markAllDirty (LEDs.cpp:239-262) are written on every setPixelColor and read by nothing (LEDs.cpp:167-172 admits it). The probe LED path reimplements exactly the same idea as showProbeLEDs != 0 plus a s_lastFrameUs timestamp (Probing.cpp:7938-7949). And the mailbox's REQ_SHOW_LEDS slot is the third, correct one. Two of the three should go.

FRAME-HOLD ABORT - the complete()+post() re-post block appears verbatim twice (main.cpp:1836-1850 and main.cpp:1907-1915), with a third abort at main.cpp:1735 that does NOT re-post because it aborts before the take. One helper (`abortAndRepost(taken, ledGen)`) removes the divergence that made quick win 2 necessary in the first place.

OWNERSHIP / HANDSHAKE GLOBALS - nine ad-hoc flags guard one strip: s_ledGfxOwned (Commands.cpp:60), logoLedAccess (Graphics.cpp:4387), core2busy and core1busy (externVars.cpp:18-19), clearBeforeSend (main.cpp:1507), swirled, hideNets (LEDs.cpp:24), showProbeLEDs (Commands.cpp:139), and checkingButton/showingProbeLEDs on the probe side. The core1req mailbox already retired two of their siblings (sendAllPathsCore2 and showLEDsCore2); the same argument applies to s_ledGfxOwned, clearBeforeSend and swirled, all three of which are state that logically belongs IN the request.

CLEAR PATHS - clearBeforeSend / clearLEDsExceptRails / hideNets / leds.clear / bread::clear are five overlapping ways to blank the board, named as an open follow-up in LEDs.cpp:172-174. The clearBeforeSend leak in the stalls list is a direct consequence of the clear living outside the request that asked for it.

STRIP vs PROBE LED - the probe LED (1 px, PIO1, merged LED+button program) has its own frame loop, its own drop gate (checkingButton, ProbeButton state), and its own starve failure mode (Apps.cpp:265-280 documents a 200 ms latch timeout when the button reads stuck). Because it lives in the `else` of the tick gate (main.cpp:2046), the two strands' cadences are anti-correlated by construction: the probe LED gets exactly zero service on any pass where the main strip renders, and vice versa. A single "which strand is due next" scheduler would make that a choice instead of an accident.

CROSS-CORE MUTEXES - core_sync (mutex, try-0ms here / blocking on core 0), the SIO spinlock OS2 (mailbox), OS1 (the OG atomic helper), logoLedAccess (bare bool), and core1FrameHoldDepth (per-core word). Five different synchronisation primitives protect overlapping state around one render. The frame hold and core_sync in particular are both taken by refreshConnections (Commands.cpp:207 waitCore2 then 209 holdCore1Frames) for the same rebuild.

## Measurement feedback loops — MeasureMode, net voltage scan, INA current readouts (V5/RP2350)

**Total: MeasureMode, probe tip touches a row -> voltage visible. BEST (already measuring this node, value moved past the 0.01 V gate): 354 us ADC spin + ~1 ms serial + 12.4 ms OLED = ~13.8 ms. TYPICAL (fresh tap on a new row): 2.0 ms mean ring-window age + 10 ms double-confirm tick + 5 ms mean 100 Hz service quantization + ~8 ms refreshLocalConnections/waitCore2 + 0.35 ms ADC + 1 ms serial + 12.4 ms OLED = ~39 ms. WORST (no switch move): 4.0 ms ring window + 10 ms confirm + 10 ms quantization + 15 ms PROBE_GUARD_MS + 200 ms core2busy wait + 25 ms waitCore2 + 0.8 ms ADC timeout + 15 ms dead-panel ping + 12.4 ms frame = ~292 ms; sliding onto a NEW row while already measuring pays the refresh pair TWICE (stopMeasurement's removeEphemeralConnection AND connectADCToNode's addEphemeralConnection each do refreshLocalConnections + waitCore2) = ~517 ms; add 300 ms SWITCH_DEBOUNCE_MS if the switch has just settled. DOMINANT TERM in every case is blocking I/O on core 0: the 12.4 ms SSD1306 full-frame write on every repaint, and the refreshLocalConnections + waitCore2 pair on every node change. Everything measurement-specific (the ring read, the decode, the INA transaction) is sub-millisecond. Net voltage scan: per-node refresh = scanNodeCount x 5 ms (50 ms for 10 nodes, 640 ms at the 128-node cap), displayed age 150-300 ms typical and up to the 5000 ms freshness window whenever the scan is preempted. INA current: 0-2.12 ms chip conversion + 0-50 ms poll = up to ~52 ms at the consumer.**

**Bottlenecks:**
- THE 12.4 ms BLOCKING SSD1306 FRAME. /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.cpp:2314 -> Adafruit_SSD1306::display() pushes all 512 bytes (128x32, config.h:391-392) over blocking Wire at 400 kHz (oled.cpp:72) for a six-character value change: 512 x 9 + 17 chunks x 18 + commands = ~4980 bit-times / 400000 = 12.4 ms. This single call is larger than every other term in every measurement path COMBINED, it sits in the main loop where the encoder and probe polls queue behind it, and there is no partial-region update anywhere in the OLED layer.
- refreshLocalConnections + waitCore2 ON EVERY MEASURED-NODE CHANGE. /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/routing/States.cpp:856-859 and :921-924. A tap that moves the ADC to a different row runs the full netlist+router rebuild TWICE (once to unwire the old node, once to wire the new) and blocks core 0 on core 1 twice, each with a 25 ms cap and each preceded by a core2busy spin capped at 200 ms (Commands.cpp:375). Typical ~16 ms, worst ~450 ms, for a hardware effect the net scan achieves in ~1 ms with fastConnectPath.
- THE 5-SECOND FRESHNESS WINDOW AGAINST A 100 ms USER-INPUT PREEMPT. /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/sensing/NetVoltageScan.cpp:87 (kVoltageFreshMs 5000) and :1419 (scan stands down for 100 ms after any input and while the encoder button is down). Continuous wheel turning holds the preempt indefinitely, so the numbers on screen freeze for exactly as long as the user is interacting with them, and nothing on screen distinguishes a 5 ms reading from a 4900 ms one.
- THE 20 Hz ISENSE REPAINT CEILING. /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:1759 gates on 0.05 mA / 15 ms. The INA produces a new value at 20 Hz, so a merely-noisy ISENSE net can demand 20 x 12.4 ms = 248 ms/s of blocking I2C — a quarter of core 0 — with no rate limit above the epsilon.
- The 354 us busy-spin per 16-sample ring read. /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/hardwarestuff/AdcRing.cpp:282-290. Small, but it IS a blocking ADC read on core 0 and answers the question directly: yes, MeasureMode blocks, for 17 sweeps x 20.83 us = 354 us typical / 800 us on timeout, 10 times a second while measuring and six times per showMeasurements print.
- INA219 conversion floor. 16-sample averaging (Peripherals.cpp:1796-1797 -> field 0b1100) = 1.06 ms per ADC, 2.12 ms for the shunt+bus pair. No polling change can make the current fresher than that — but the 50 ms poll interval (Peripherals.cpp:52) throws away 23 of every 24 conversions, so the pairing is the worst of both worlds.

**Stalls / races:**
- DOUBLE REFRESH ON EVERY ROW SLIDE. MeasureMode.cpp:138-141 calls startMeasurement for any stable reading != measuredNode. startMeasurement calls stopMeasurement (-> disconnectADC -> removeEphemeralConnection with applyRouting=true) and then connectADCToNode (-> addEphemeralConnection with applyRouting=true). Two full netlist rebuilds and two cross-core waits per row change, at up to the 10 ms probe cadence for genuinely-changing rows.
- CROSS-SERVICE STATE STOMP: MeasureMode::stopMeasurement (MeasureMode.cpp:291-296) reaches into Highlighting's singleton and clears brightenedNode/brightenedNet/brightenedRail/highlightedNet and calls hl.clearHighlighting(). Highlighting is registered immediately BEFORE MeasureMode at the same HIGH priority (main.cpp:559-560), so on any pass where Highlighting sets a highlight and MeasureMode then tears down, the highlight is silently erased in the same pass.
- THE SHARED SERIAL PIN HAS NO OWNER. ReadingDisplay.cpp:123 `pinReserved` is a single flag; clearLiveSerialLine (line 188) erases the pinned rows for whoever called it. MeasureMode.cpp:184 had to become edge-triggered specifically to stop it erasing Highlighting's live reading at up to 25 Hz (the comment records the regression). A third writer through ReadingDisplay (PartLabels tap-to-inspect, StepViewer) reopens the same fight, and Peripherals::showMeasurements (Peripherals.cpp:2515, 2672) plus the dead Probing::measureMode (Probing.cpp:4113) already write the same terminal row with their own \r-and-spaces idiom, outside the pin entirely.
- THE OLED DEDUPE CAN SWALLOW A LEGITIMATE REPAINT. ReadingDisplay.cpp:267 drops a paint when the composed line matches `lastLine`, but `lastLine` only tracks what ReadingDisplay itself painted. If OledGui, PartLabels or a menu paints the panel in between, the next MeasureMode paint with unchanged text is dropped and the panel keeps the OTHER screen. resetLastShown() exists (line 206) but must be called by hand by every intervening painter.
- ADC POOL STARVATION, SILENT. MeasureMode holds one of ADC0-4 EXCLUSIVELY for the whole session (MeasureMode.cpp:338, mask 0x1F), while NetVoltageScan acquires and releases per tap from ADC0-3 only (NetVoltageScan.cpp:1193, mask 0x0F). A measure session on ADC0-3 permanently removes one of the scanner's four channels; with a couple of user-bridged ADCs, pickScanAdc returns -1, every tap is skipped, and the whole net-current display simply goes dark with no message anywhere.
- FLOATING LEG = PERMANENT SILENT STALE. senseNodeVoltage returns false whenever early/late differ by more than kFloatingDriftVolts 0.05 (NetVoltageScan.cpp:352); the round-robin gives up after 2 tries (line 1501). The node's voltage never refreshes, ages past kVoltageFreshMs, netScanVoltageValue returns nullptr (Highlighting.cpp:918) and the voltage row just vanishes from the OLED with no explanation of why.
- TORN CROSS-CORE READ OF netCurrentInfo[]. Written on core 1 in computePathCurrents, read on core 0 by Highlighting.cpp:1795 / netCurrent_mA (NetVoltageScan.cpp:1168-1172). The header documents float tearing as cosmetic (NetVoltageScan.h:52-58), but `valid` and `current_mA` are two separate fields with no barrier between them, so a display can read valid==true against a half-updated magnitude.
- THE INA GUARD IS A ONE-WAY STAMP. lastProbeCurrentCheckTime is set BEFORE the read (Probing.cpp:5902) and never cleared after, so MeasureMode refuses to sample the probe for 15 ms (MeasureMode.cpp:75) and refuses to update the display for 10 ms (line 414) after every switch check. Harmless at the current 500 ms ProbeSwitch interval (3% duty); it becomes a hard rate limit on measure updates the moment that interval is lowered.
- Serial.flush() AGAINST A CONNECTED-BUT-NOT-READING HOST. ReadingDisplay.cpp:181 and :203, plus Peripherals.cpp:2685. Each is an unbounded block on core 0, called 10 and 6.7 times a second respectively while a measurement is live.
- MeasureMode RETURNS BUSY IMMEDIATELY AFTER STARTING. MeasureMode.cpp:162-163: after startMeasurement it returns without ever calling updateVoltageDisplay in that pass, so the first reading always waits for the following serviceAll pass even though lastUpdateTime was forced to 0 (line 255).
- switchPosition == -1 STRANDS A MEASUREMENT. Fixed at MeasureMode.cpp:166 (the branch is `!= 0` now), but the class remains: switchPosition is a plain volatile int written by classifySwitchPosition on core 0 AND legally writable by jl_set_switch_position from MicroPython, with no validation between them.

**Quick wins:**
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.cpp:72` Raise the OLED I2C clock from 400 kHz to 1 MHz. kOledI2CClockHz is the ONLY clock in the OLED transfer path, and on the default connection_type 0 the panel is alone on Wire1 (oled.cpp:123-130) so nothing else on the board is affected; the shared-I2C0 case already hands the bus back at clkAfter. 4980 bit-times / 1e6 = 5.0 ms instead of 12.4 ms — cuts the single dominant term in every measurement display path by 60% with a one-constant change.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/sensing/MeasureMode.cpp:138` Do not tear down and rebuild the ADC route when the new node is in the SAME net as the one already being measured — the existing ephemeral route already reads it. Guard the startMeasurement call with nodeToNetIndex[] (already extern'd two functions away at MeasureMode.cpp:303). Saves TWO refreshLocalConnections + waitCore2 cycles (~16 ms typical, up to 450 ms worst) on every probe slide within a net. The displayed node label still updates; the voltage is by definition identical.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/ReadingDisplay.cpp:286` Memoize the font-fit search. findBestFitPointSize runs on every single repaint for a string whose shape ("%0.2f V", "%+.2f mA") is constant — only the digits change. Cache on (family, strlen, first char) and the search disappears from the 10 Hz path. Pure CPU, no behavior change.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:909` Show the age. nodeVoltageMs[] already carries a per-node timestamp (NetVoltageScan.h:46) and netScanVoltageValue throws it away. Dim or parenthesise the value past ~1 s instead of rendering a 4.9-second-old number identically to a 5-millisecond-old one. Removes the entire 'confident stale number' class for free — this is the north-star fix, not a latency fix.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/sensing/MeasureMode.cpp:451` Delete the unreachable oscilloscope. MeasureMode::enableOscilloscope has NO callers anywhere in the tree, so oscopeEnabled is permanently false and updateOscopeDisplay / sampleForOscope / autoRangeOscope / voltageToPixelY / drawOscopeGrid / drawOscopeWaveform / drawOscopeStatusBar (MeasureMode.cpp:451-624, ~175 lines) plus the 128-float oscopeSamples buffer (512 B of SRAM) are dead. The branch at MeasureMode.cpp:131 is also dead.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp:4084` Delete Probing::measureMode(). No callers. It is a `while (checkProbeButtonState() == 0) { ... delay(updateSpeed); }` loop with updateSpeed defaulting to 150 ms — if anything ever called it, core 0 would freeze completely (no serviceInner pump, no USB, no encoder) until the probe button was pressed. Dead code that is also a loaded gun.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Peripherals.cpp:2589` Fix the four-way scaling inconsistency inside ONE printed line of showMeasurements: ADC 0 and 1 go through readAdcVoltage (calibrated adcSpread[]/adcZero[]), ADC 2/3/7 use raw readAdc x railSpread/4095 - railSpread/2 (railSpread is a single 17.88 constant, ignoring per-channel calibration), and ADC 4 uses 5.0/4095. Four different calibrations printed side by side as if they were the same measurement. This is a correctness bug, not style.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/sensing/NetVoltageScan.cpp:1419` Let one net-scan tap per 100 ms through the user-input preempt instead of standing down entirely. The tap is ~1 ms and already yields to the encoder throughout via waitServicingEncoder (NetVoltageScan.cpp:58), so it cannot make the wheel feel worse; today continuous wheel turning holds the preempt forever and freezes exactly the numbers the user is turning the wheel to look at.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/routing/States.cpp:858` Stop blocking on waitCore2() when wiring up a measurement. The first updateVoltageDisplay is gated 100 ms out by UPDATE_INTERVAL_MS anyway, so there is no reason to spin core 0 for up to 25 ms waiting for the crossbar send. Pass applyRouting without the wait (or add a MeasureMode-owned 'route pending, don't read yet' flag consumed by updateVoltageDisplay's existing gate). Risk is reading before the CH446Q crosspoint has settled, which is exactly what the 100 ms gate already covers.
- [design-change/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.cpp:2314` Partial-region OLED update. ReadingDisplay repaints all 512 bytes to change a six-character value; the SSD1306 supports page/column addressing and the value occupies at most 3 of the 4 pages. Painting only the dirty page span drops the transfer to ~9 ms (and to ~3.7 ms combined with the 1 MHz clock above). Requires a dirty-rect layer under oled::show() — the biggest single win available, and the only one that scales to the ISENSE 20 Hz case.
- [design-change/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/sensing/MeasureMode.cpp:341` Make MeasureMode borrow the net scan's tap mechanism instead of the netlist. senseNodeVoltage closes an ADC route with fastConnectPath in ~1 ms and never touches globalState; MeasureMode's ephemeral connection rebuilds the entire netlist and blocks on core 1 to achieve the same electrical result. Holding a FastPathHandle for the session's duration gives identical hardware behaviour, removes the refresh+wait pair from both start and stop, and eliminates the double-refresh row-slide stall outright.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Peripherals.cpp:52` Match the INA poll to the chip. 16-sample averaging gives a new shunt+bus pair every 2.12 ms; polling at 50 ms discards 23 of every 24 of them. Either drop CURRENT_SENSE_POLL_INTERVAL_MS to ~20 (costs ~4 ms/s more I2C, makes the ants ~30 ms fresher) or drop averaging to 4 samples (0.276 ms/conversion) and keep 50 ms. The current pairing pays for averaging it then throws away.

**Unification notes:** DEBOUNCE / STABILITY is implemented five times over the same signal. justReadProbe holds a two-consecutive-reads confirmation plus a 500 ms duplicate window (Probing.cpp:7674-7728); MeasureMode holds stableReadingCount on top of it (MeasureMode.cpp:89-117) whose declared threshold STABLE_READINGS_REQUIRED = 5 (MeasureMode.h:134) is DEAD — the code uses `>= 1` and its own comment (line 108-115) admits the 5 only ever worked because periodUs is 0; MeasureMode then adds a third layer, SWITCH_DEBOUNCE_MS 300 (MeasureMode.h:126), on top of classifySwitchPosition's own 500 ms interval and two-consecutive-below-low rule (Probing.cpp:5836, 5865). The MeasureMode comment at line 108 names Highlighting and PartLabels as holding copies four and five of the same 'one positive cached reading IS the tap' contract. That contract should be ONE thing the probe layer publishes, not five reimplementations that each have to be re-derived when the scheduler changes.

FRESHNESS / AGE is implemented four times with four different windows and no shared type: kVoltageFreshMs 5000 (NetVoltageScan.cpp:87), kSourceMeasureFreshMs 2500 (:137), kNetAntsHoldMaxMs 5000 (Graphics.cpp:2116), and currentSenseState.lastUpdatedMs (Peripherals.cpp:254) which has a stamp but NO window — Highlighting.cpp:1741 reads filteredCurrent_mA with no age check at all and will happily paint a value from before a wavegen run. A single `Reading { float value; uint32_t stampMs; }` with one `ageMs()` accessor would collapse all four and make the 'show the age' win above a one-liner everywhere instead of per-site.

CHANGE DEADBAND + REPAINT GATING is implemented per call site, five ways, all feeding one 12.4 ms paint: MeasureMode 0.01 V / 100 ms (MeasureMode.cpp:408, 433); Highlighting ISENSE 0.05 mA / 0.1 V / 15 ms (:1759); Highlighting DAC and rail 0.05 V / 0.1 mA (:1699, :1724); Highlighting scan-fallback 0.1 mA / 100 ms (:1802-1804); ReadingDisplay's exact-string dedupe (ReadingDisplay.cpp:267). Five epsilons independently tuned, gating one shared blocking resource, so the OLED's core-0 duty cycle is set by whoever tuned their epsilon most aggressively. This belongs INSIDE ReadingDisplay as a single repaint budget (minimum interval plus per-field epsilon), which would bound the OLED cost by construction.

MOMENTARY-ROUTE-SETTLE-READ-UNROUTE exists twice with a 10x cost difference. NetVoltageScan does it with fastConnectPath + 80 us settle + 500 us dwell + fastDisconnectPath, ~1 ms, never touching globalState (NetVoltageScan.cpp:268 and :369). MeasureMode does the same electrical thing through addEphemeralConnection -> full netlist+router rebuild -> waitCore2, ~8-16 ms, twice per row change (MeasureMode.cpp:341, 377; States.cpp:856, 921). Same hardware, same purpose, two orders of engineering apart.

ADC ARBITRATION exists twice for one converter. The InfraPaths pool (infraAcquireAdc/infraReleaseAdc — MeasureMode.cpp:338 exclusive-for-the-session, NetVoltageScan.cpp:1193 per-tap) and the legacy `readingADC` atomic test-and-set (Peripherals.cpp:2797, senseNodeVoltage:276), which the ring path now short-circuits on V5 only (Peripherals.cpp:2760, NetVoltageScan.cpp:274). Two ownership models with different granularity and different masks (0x1F vs 0x0F), the second of which is a no-op on the primary board and live on OG.

THE TERMINAL LIVE ROW has three owners and no arbitration. ReadingDisplay's pinned two-row anchor with the port-1 linefeed counter (ReadingDisplay.cpp:37-56, 137-204) claims in its own header comment to be the narrow one-and-only; Peripherals::showMeasurements does its own \\r-and-spaces overwrite of the same row (Peripherals.cpp:2515, 2672); the dead Probing::measureMode does a third (Probing.cpp:4113). The linefeed counter cannot see any of these because none of them emits a linefeed — that is the documented blind spot at ReadingDisplay.cpp:30-33, and showMeasurements at 6.7 Hz is exactly the writer that exploits it.

## Feedback vocabulary emission: how the firmware says "yes/done", "no/refused", "working…", and "error" across breadboard LEDs, probe LED, logo, OLED, and terminal (there is no sound surface)

**Total: Per surface, decision -> visible: probe LED <=0.6 ms (30 us frame inside a 560 us PIO loop). Terminal ~1 ms typical (one USB frame), unbounded if a host holds DTR without draining. Breadboard LEDs 9.3 ms best (post 1 us + immediate mode-2 pickup ~0.2 ms + 0 compose + 9.0 ms wire + 0.3 ms latch) / ~13 ms typical (mode 1: +4 ms mean scheduler tick + 1.6 ms showNets) / ~19 ms worst (8 ms tick + 1.9 ms compose + 9.3 ms wire) — and ~70 ms if the post is a -2 that only lands via the 51 ms swirl pass. OLED 23.8 ms best (1057 bytes x 9 bits / 400 kHz), ~25 ms typical, up to ~70 ms when a dying panel eats three 15 ms Wire timeouts before oledConnected flips (oled.cpp:2340-2360); then the surface is OWNED for another 300-3000 ms by whichever dwell mechanism the site picked. DOMINANT TERM: the 23.8 ms OLED I2C frame — 24x the terminal, 2.6x the strip. Consequence for the vocabulary: on every single "yes", the terminal speaks first, the LEDs second, the OLED last, always in that order, always by roughly that ratio. Nothing in the tree tries to align them.**

| stage | where | mechanism | latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| Decision fork — probe commits or refuses the second node of a connect. This is the single highest-traffic yes/no site on the device. | `src/Probing.cpp:3634` | Synchronous, core 0, inside probeTick() driven by probeMode()'s `while(!s.done){ probeTick(s); jOS.serviceInner(); }` wrapper (Probing.cpp:4068-4076). connectionAllowed() is a linear scan of globalDoNotIntersects[60][2] (routing/MatrixState.cpp:421-433). | 60 iterations x 2 compares = 120 compares, ~4 cycles each at 150 MHz = 480 cycles = 3.2 us. Negligible; the fork is never the cost. |
| Accept-flash paint — fires ONE PASS BEFORE the fork resolves | `src/Probing.cpp:3579` | b.printRawRow(0b0010001, node-1, 0x000121e, ...) writes RAM pixels, then requestLedShow(2); s.flashPhase=1 arms a deadline sub-state (Probing.cpp:2938-2966) that paints frame 2 (0x0f0498) and frame 3 (0x4000e8) on later ticks. Deliberately NOT delay()-based — the M2 conversion note is in the code. | 40 ms + 40 ms + 60 ms dwells = 140 ms of animation, started before connectionAllowed() is ever consulted. |
| LED request post to core 1 | `src/Commands.cpp:66` | requestLedShow(legacy int) -> core1req::postMode(REQ_SHOW_LEDS, mode, flags, keepMask) under fixed SIO spinlock PICO_SPINLOCK_ID_OS2 (coredination/CoreMailbox.h:29-33). Mode REPLACES, flags COALESCE, a MENU flush never downgrades a pending NETS/GFX. | A handful of loads/stores inside a spinlock: ~20-40 cycles = 0.2 us, plus a cross-core spin bounded by the other core's few-instruction critical section. Call it 1 us. |
| Core-1 pickup gate | `src/main.cpp:1768` | core2stuff() peeks the slot; ledImmediate = (LED_MENU\|LED_GFX) bypasses the scheduler tick, LED_NETS waits for it. Also gated on core_sync_acquire_timeout_ms(0) (main.cpp:1725), !core1FramesHeld() (1734), and core1req::allIdle() — no pending crossbar send (1774). | schedulerUpdateTime = 8000 us (main.cpp:1499). Mode 2/3: 0-1 core-1 pass (~50-200 us). Mode 1: uniform 0-8 ms, mean 4 ms. If the post carries no mode (a swirl-only pass) the render waits on lastSwirlTime > 51 ms (main.cpp:2016) -> 0-51 ms, mean 25 ms. |
| Compose the strip picture | `src/main.cpp:1853` | showNets() then showLEDmeasurements(), showAllRowAnimations(), renderGraphicOverlays(). Only reached when rails != 2 && rails != 3; a MENU/GFX flush skips all of it and shows whatever b.print() left in the buffer. | showNets() measured 1556 us (CodeDocs/PERFORMANCE_OPTIMIZATION_SUMMARY.md:21). Full compose ~1.9 ms (the 15117-15253 delta in that same breakdown). Mode 2 compose = 0 us. |
| Breadboard + top strip transmit (the photons) | `src/LEDs.cpp:174` | ledClass::show() -> topleds.show() then bbleds.show(). Each JeoPixel instance claims its OWN PIO SM and its OWN DMA channel (lib/Jadafruit_NeoPixel/Jeopixel_RP2.cpp:35, 63), so the two strips stream concurrently. CPU returns as soon as DMA is armed. | WS2812 800 kHz = 1.25 us/bit, 24 bits/px = 30 us/px. bb 300 px x 30 us = 9.00 ms; top 145 px x 30 us = 4.35 ms; concurrent -> wall clock max(9.00, 4.35) = 9.00 ms + ~300 us reset latch = 9.3 ms to last photon. Cross-check: the historic BLOCKING measurement was 13253 us = (300+145) x 30 us sequential — same model. |
| Probe LED (the tip's own pixel) | `src/Probing.cpp:7987` | showProbeLEDs is a request int consumed by probeLEDhandler()'s switch, called from core2stuff() main.cpp:2048 (the ELSE of the scheduler-tick branch, so it runs on the fast passes). One pio_sm_put() into the merged LED+button program (Probing.cpp:714-757). | 1 px x 24 bits x 1.25 us = 30 us of frame, inside the merged program's ~560 us loop (bit loop + sample sequence + ~520 us latch gap). Worst case one full loop = 560 us. This is the FASTEST visual surface on the board. |
| OLED paint + flush — the accept path | `src/Probing.cpp:3702` | oled.clearPrintShow(bothNames, 2, ...) paints the framebuffer through the auto-fit font ladder, then oled::show() (oled.cpp:2269) -> _displayPtr->display() (oled.cpp:2314). BLOCKING on core 0, inside probeTick, so the probe tick stalls for the whole transfer. | 1024 framebuffer bytes + ~33 control bytes (WIRE_MAX=31/transaction, 'the ~33 I2C transactions that make up a frame', oled.cpp:2321) = ~1057 bytes x 9 bits (8 data + ACK) = 9513 bits / 400000 Hz (kOledI2CClockHz, oled.cpp:72) = 23.8 ms. Plus a post-write 1-byte liveness ping ~30 us (oled.cpp:2338). Font ladder is sub-ms CPU. |
| Terminal (USB CDC) | `src/Probing.cpp:3672` | Serial.print(...) + Serial.flush() into the TinyUSB CDC ring (TX 1024 B, flush threshold 512 — include/custom_tusb_config.h:165,173), pumped by tinyUSBService (CRITICAL, period 0, inner set — main.cpp:546) and by flush()'s own yield loop. | One USB full-speed frame = 1 ms; a 64-byte bulk packet carries a whole feedback line, so ~1 ms typical. Unbounded when the host holds DTR but stops draining: Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write() spins forever (the codebase's own diagnosis, src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:324). |
| Refusal branch — the same tap, denied | `src/Probing.cpp:3635` | Serial.print(" can't connect ") + node names, then node1or2 = 0, nodesToConnect[] = -1, return. No requestLedShow. No oled write. No showProbeLEDs change. s.flashPhase is left armed at 1. | Terminal: ~1 ms. Breadboard LED: never (and the 140 ms accept animation from hop 2 plays to completion). OLED: never — the panel keeps showing the node-1 name painted at Probing.cpp:3561. Probe LED: stays 0x0f0fc6 'connect bright'. |
| Undo/redo toast — the one non-blocking OLED lane in the tree | `src/remembering/Undo.cpp:2724` | OledGui::notePanelTakenByOther() -> oledHoldBegin(1800) snapshots the pre-toast framebuffer -> toastScreen.render(oled) -> priorityFlushHeldFrame() (oled.cpp:1672) bypasses the hold gate, ships the toast, then restores the shadow so ambient writes accumulate against the background. | Same 23.8 ms flush, then a 1800 ms hold during which every other show() returns early (oled.cpp:2286) and only sets oledNeedsFlushAfterHold. The catch-up flush runs from oledPeriodic() (oled.cpp:3237) on OLEDService's 20 ms period — but OLEDService is LOW and NOT inInnerSet (JumperlOS.h:568-572), so inside a menu or app the tail flush waits for the modal loop to exit. |
| Logo indicator lane (the 'working…' surface) | `src/LEDs.cpp:3814` | A precedence ladder inside logoSwirl(), evaluated once per core-1 LED-branch pass: logoRing (menu) > buttonPressAnimActive > undoActivityUntil (PALETTE_YELLOW) > filesystemActive (PALETTE_YELLOW) > measureModeActive (PALETTE_PURPLE) > default swirl. | Repaint rides the next LED frame: <=8 ms tick + 9.3 ms wire = <=17.3 ms. If nothing posts a show, it waits for the 51 ms swirl tick -> <=60 ms. Dwells: undo 500 ms (Undo.cpp:2758); filesystem = mutex-held duration + FILESYSTEM_INDICATOR_MIN_MS = 100 ms (externVars.cpp:47,168 — the comment still says '1/4 second'). |
| Blocking-toast lane — the other 12 OLED dwell sites | `src/PartsApp.cpp:451` | oled.showMultiLineSmallText(...) then a bare Arduino delay(N). Nothing in src/ overrides yield(), so jOS.serviceAll()/serviceInner() do not run for the duration — the whole service set (probe drain, menus, DisplayService animation, LED dump) is frozen. | 13 such sites, delays 300/300/800/800/1000x4/1200/1500/2000/2500/3000 ms. So a PARTS geometry refusal = 23.8 ms flush + 1200 ms of dead core 0; a PARTS success = 23.8 ms + 800 ms. |

**Bottlenecks:**
- OLED full-frame I2C: ~1057 bytes x 9 bits / 400000 Hz = 23.8 ms, blocking on core 0, across 115 direct clearPrintShow/showMultiLineSmallText call sites. This is the dominant term of every OLED feedback event and it is paid even to change two characters — there is no partial/dirty-window flush anywhere (oled.cpp:2269-2314, oled.cpp:72).
- WS2812 wire time on the 300-pixel breadboard strip: 300 x 24 x 1.25 us = 9.00 ms. Irreducible floor per LED frame. The top strip's 4.35 ms hides under it only because the two JeoPixel instances hold separate SMs and DMA channels (Jeopixel_RP2.cpp:35,63).
- The 8 ms schedulerUpdateTime gate on NETS renders (main.cpp:1499,1768). Because MENU/GFX bypass it, essentially all interactive feedback is posted as mode 2 even when it needs a nets composite — which is how the graphic-overlay path ended up on the wrong door (see stalls).
- The 51 ms lastSwirlTime idle tick (main.cpp:2016-2028) is the only self-driving repaint AND the only path that composites graphic overlays. Anything that lands there instead of on a mode-1 post pays 25 ms mean / 51 ms worst before the render even starts.
- Blocking delay() toasts: 13 sites, 300-3000 ms, with zero service pumping (PartsApp.cpp:451,491; Apps.cpp:513,534,622; Menus.cpp:4824,4943; ImagesApp.cpp:308; oled.cpp:3505). A single PARTS refusal costs 1.2 s of frozen core 0.
- VOCABULARY BOTTLENECK — there is no sound and no haptic surface at all. USBAudio is an OUTBOUND UAC2 microphone streaming the probe to the host (include/USBAudio.h:1-21); playDoom is an LED animation. Every 'no' must therefore be carried by light or by text, and the code carries most of them by text only.
- VOCABULARY BOTTLENECK — 'refused' has no LED color anywhere in the tree. Greps for a refusal color come back empty: the breadboard has accept (0x0A2A08 / the 0x000121e->0x4000e8 latch flash), warning (0x2A0004), pass (0x001400), fail (0x160000), but nothing that means 'I heard you and I am declining'. Refusals are terminal-only by construction.
- VOCABULARY BOTTLENECK — 35 feedback sites gate on oled.oledConnected / oled.isConnected(). On a board with no panel, the refusal vocabulary collapses to the terminal alone, and a user on the clickwheel+probe with no USB host has NO refusal channel at all.

**Stalls / races:**
- SILENT LED FRAME DROP (drop). ledClass::show() (LEDs.cpp:182-197) skips the transfer when isDMABusy() and leaves bbDirty/topDirty true — but NOTHING consumes those flags: the `if (bbDirty)` guards are commented out at LEDs.cpp:191 and the FOLLOW-UP note at LEDs.cpp:167-173 says so. Meanwhile core2stuff has already called core1req::complete(REQ_SHOW_LEDS, ledGen) (main.cpp:1976). So a single-shot flash posted while the strip is mid-frame — 9.0 ms out of every ~9-60 ms, i.e. 15-100% of the time — is lost with no retry, no re-post, and no counter. ledFramesShown++ (main.cpp:1960) increments anyway, so the X diagnostic over-reports shown frames.
- LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME IS 1000x TOO SMALL (dead rate limiter). #define LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME 14 (main.cpp:1536) is compared as `micros() - ledShowTime > LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME` (main.cpp:1953) — 14 MICROseconds, not the 14 ms WS2812 frame floor it obviously means. The intended pacing does nothing; the DMA-busy drop above is the only limiter, which is why drops are the failure mode instead of coalescing.
- ACCEPT-FLASH-THEN-REFUSE (double-fire / wrong meaning). s.flashPhase is armed at Probing.cpp:3588 one tick BEFORE connectionAllowed() is consulted at Probing.cpp:3634. The refusal path (3635-3643) resets node1or2 and returns without touching flashPhase, so the full 140 ms blue->violet accept animation plays out on a connection that was denied. The user sees the exact 'latched' animation for a refusal.
- DOUBLE-TAP UNDO WITH EMPTY HISTORY IS SILENT ON EVERY SURFACE (drop). The IRQ-side confirm only posts g_pendingUndo/g_pendingRedo when undoCanUndo()/undoCanRedo() is true (Probing.cpp:548-560); the main-loop drain re-guards with `if (undoCanUndo() && undoUndo())` (Probing.cpp:172,190). Both no-op paths are completely quiet: no toast, no logo flash, no serial line. The gesture is indistinguishable from a tap the hardware missed — and the double-tap path already has five documented ways to miss a tap (dblEdgeNoRelease, dblCandExpired, dblCandOppCancel, ...), so the user has no way to tell 'nothing to undo' from 'you tapped wrong'.
- GRAPHIC OVERLAYS NEVER COMPOSITE ON THEIR OWN POST (wrong door + late). renderGraphicOverlays() has exactly ONE caller: main.cpp:1883, inside the NETS branch. Every overlay mutation posts requestLedShow(-2) = LED_MENU|LED_CLEAR (GraphicOverlays.cpp:93,275,299; SelfTest.cpp:226 for the pass/fail overlay and :1354 for its clear). Mode 2 skips the composite entirely, so that post burns a full 9.3 ms strip frame showing the PRE-overlay picture; the overlay only appears on the next swirl-paced nets pass: 51 ms + 8 ms + 1.6 ms + 9.3 ms ~= 70 ms worst. GraphicOverlayState::needsRender is written 8 times and read 0 times (GraphicOverlays.h:74) — the intended trigger was never wired up.
- STRANDED clearBeforeSend (stray wipe). clearBeforeSend is a file-scope global (main.cpp:1507) set from any request carrying LED_CLEAR (main.cpp:1786) but consumed ONLY inside the NETS branch (main.cpp:1829-1832). Any -2 or -3 post therefore latches the clear and it fires on whatever unrelated nets render comes next — a board wipe attributable to no user action, arriving up to 51 ms after the thing that asked for it.
- UNGUARDED TERMINAL WRITES WEDGE THE OTHER TWO SURFACES (stall). 29 Serial.flush() calls in Probing.cpp/PartsApp.cpp/PartLabels.cpp alone, none behind jl_cdc_wait_writable() — that guard exists (Python_Proper.h:255, with a drop-on-timeout contract) but has only 7 call sites, all in the MicroPython files. Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write() spins forever when DTR is asserted and the host stops draining (Python_Proper.cpp:324). Because the Serial.print, the requestLedShow and the oled.clearPrintShow for a given event all sit on the same core-0 statement sequence, one wedged terminal takes the LED and OLED feedback for that event down with it.
- CROSS-CORE BUS FIGHT MUTES THE OLED (drop). On connection_type 2 the panel shares I2C0 with the MCP4728 that core-1 wavegen streams from, with no lock between cores. oled::show() (oled.cpp:2296-2302) refuses to transmit while wavegen.isRunning() and just sets oledNeedsFlushAfterWavegen. So during any waveform output, every OLED 'done' / 'refused' / warning is painted into RAM and never shown, until wavegen stops AND OLEDService (LOW, 20 ms, not inner-set) gets a pass — which inside a menu or app means 'not until the modal loop exits'.
- TOAST EVICTION — OLED WRITES HAVE NO PRIORITY (race). ReadingDisplay::show() (ReadingDisplay.cpp:339) is driven by four ambient services — Highlighting.cpp:997, PartLabels.cpp:185 and :283, MeasureMode.cpp:443, Peripherals.cpp:3391 — and repaints on any content change. Only the undo toast defends itself (oledHoldBegin). A PARTS geometry refusal at PartsApp.cpp:447 is protected only by the delay(1200) that follows it; a probe 'connected' at Probing.cpp:3702 is protected by nothing and can be overwritten by the next voltage reading within one service tick.
- PARTS WARNING RENDERS IN THE READING SLOT (wrong meaning). PartLabels.cpp:283 calls ReadingDisplay::show(p.name, node, hint) — three args, so the warning string lands in the `value` parameter (ReadingDisplay.h:36), not the `hint` parameter its own local variable is named for. Result: 'vcc_to_gnd!' is rendered centered in the big best-fit family font, in exactly the slot and typography that '3.30 V' occupies. A hard short is visually indistinguishable from a routine measurement.

**Quick wins:**
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/main.cpp:1536` Rename LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME to LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME_US so the next reader does not 'fix' 14 into 14000 and silently cut the strip frame rate. (Actually raising it to 14000 is the real fix but that changes behavior — do it on the bench, not tonight.)
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/LEDs.cpp:182` Make a DMA-busy drop retry instead of vanishing: have ledClass::show() report that it skipped a strip, and have core2stuff re-post the bits it took — the exact pattern already written for the frame-hold abort at main.cpp:1853-1861 (complete(gen) then post(taken)). Kills the silent loss of single-shot confirmation flashes; it only makes a frame that was already supposed to appear actually appear.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Probing.cpp:3640` Clear s.flashPhase = 0 in the connectionAllowed-refused branch so the 140 ms accept animation stops instead of playing over a denial. Not behavior-preserving by design — the current behavior IS the incoherence.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Probing.cpp:172` Give the empty-history double-tap a 'no'. The drain already has the else branch for free: when undoCanUndo() is false, print one line to Serial. Adds a refusal where there is currently silence on all four surfaces. (Adding an LED signal too is the better fix but the logo precedence ladder is shared — bench that separately.)
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp:275` Change the overlay posts from requestLedShow(-2) to requestLedShow(-1) so the overlay actually composites on the post that asked for it. Drops overlay latency from ~70 ms worst to ~19 ms, removes the wasted 9.3 ms mode-2 frame that shows the stale picture, and un-strands clearBeforeSend. Same 5 sites: GraphicOverlays.cpp:93,275,299 and SelfTest.cpp:226,1354. Costs 1.6 ms of core-1 showNets per post — verify the ImagesApp / MicroPython overlay animation loops do not now over-render.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.h:74` Delete GraphicOverlayState::needsRender — 8 writes, 0 reads. Either remove it or make the LED branch consult it; leaving a dead trigger flag next to a broken trigger path is how the -2 bug survived.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/coredination/externVars.cpp:60` Split the logo's yellow: undoIndicatorPalette and filesystemIndicatorPalette are both PALETTE_YELLOW, so 'your gesture landed' and 'the board is saving' are literally the same light. Move undo to PALETTE_CHARTREUSE or PALETTE_TURQUOISE (already in the enum, LEDs.h:386-397). Caveat: LOGO_PALETTE_COUNT is 1 on OG (LEDs.h:373-377) so every index folds to 0 there — this is a V5-only distinction.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/eyecandy/PartLabels.cpp:283` Pass the PARTS electrical warning through the hint parameter, not the value parameter: ReadingDisplay::show(p.name, node, nullptr, nullptr, hint). Right now 'vcc_to_gnd!' renders exactly like '3.30 V'. The deeper fix is a severity mark (inverted row / border) since a short is not a hint — that is a design call, not a mechanical one.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/Probing.cpp:3639` Wrap the feedback-path Serial bursts in jl_cdc_wait_writable() — the guard and its drop-on-timeout contract already exist (Python_Proper.h:255) and are used only by the MicroPython files. Behavior-preserving whenever the host is draining; it changes what happens when the host is not, which is the entire point. 29 flush sites in Probing/PartsApp/PartLabels.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/PartsApp.cpp:451` Replace the 13 blocking delay() toasts with the Undo lane's protocol: oledHoldBegin(N) + paint + priorityFlushHeldFrame(). Undo.cpp:2712-2727 is a working reference implementation of exactly this. Picture is preserved; the 300-3000 ms core-0 freeze is not. Bench per site — a few of them lean on the freeze to stop the caller advancing.

**Unification notes:** THE VOCABULARY, AS THE CODE ACTUALLY SPEAKS IT

YES/DONE — probe latch: breadboard 3-frame 0x000121e -> 0x0f0498 -> 0x4000e8 at 40/40/60 ms (Probing.cpp:3579, 2944-2956) + probe LED 0x0f0fc6 (case 11) + OLED "A - B" + serial. Connection committed: serial "\tconnected" + OLED (Probing.cpp:3672,3702). PARTS tap: 0x0A2A08 + delay(180) (PartsApp.cpp:258,287). PARTS placed: serial "PARTDB place ok=" + OLED + delay(800) + label bloom (PartsApp.cpp:479-492). Undo applied: retained OLED toast under an 1800 ms hold + serial [UNDO] in color 209 + logo PALETTE_YELLOW 500 ms (Undo.cpp:2724-2758). Self-test pass: 0x001400 overlay, row 0x001200, terminal color 84.

NO/REFUSED — probe connect denied: serial only, and the accept flash already ran (Probing.cpp:3635). Double-tap undo, empty history: nothing, anywhere (Probing.cpp:548,172). Terminal undo refused: serial only (SingleCharCommands.cpp:3014,3036). PARTS row reused: serial only (PartsApp.cpp:296). PARTS name collision / bridge table full: serial only (PartsApp.cpp:436,474). PARTS geometry bad: serial + OLED + delay(1200) (PartsApp.cpp:444-452). Six refusals, four different volumes, and only one of them reaches a second surface.

WORKING — logo PALETTE_YELLOW while the fs mutex is held +100 ms (LEDs.cpp:3824, externVars.cpp:52,168); logo PALETTE_PURPLE in measure mode (LEDs.cpp:3836); probe LED 0x170c17 or the hsv sweep for select-idle. Nothing on the OLED, nothing in the terminal — long operations simply freeze both surfaces, so "working" reads as "hung".

ERROR/WARNING — PartLabels: 5-cell column 0x2A0004 + serial "PARTWARN ... reason=" + a ReadingDisplay line that looks like a voltage (PartLabels.cpp:283). Terminal prefixes are ungoverned: "ERROR:" x89, "Failed" x75, "WARNING:" x24, "Warning:" x14, "Error " x14, "FAIL:" x11, "ok:" x9.

COLLISIONS. RED means four things: probe LED case 2 = REMOVE MODE (Probing.cpp:8003-8025), logo 0x990000 = about to reboot (RotaryEncoder.cpp:702), self-test 0x160000 = FAIL, PartLabels 0x2A0004 = short. GREEN means three: probe LED 0x003600 = MEASURE MODE, self-test 0x001200 = pass, PARTS 0x0A2A08 = accepted tap. YELLOW logo means two, identically: undo fired and flash is busy. And "warning" itself means two opposite things — Highlighting::warnNet() (Highlighting.cpp:754, one caller: Probing.cpp:1516) is an ARE-YOU-SURE arm for disconnect, rendered as a hue-shifted shimmer via warningRowFrames (Graphics.cpp:2512, 2980-3000), while PartLabels' WARN_VCC_TO_GND / GND_TO_HOT / SELF_SHORT (PartLabels.cpp:63-69) is "this is electrically wrong". The probe LED is the worst offender in principle: its colors encode MODE, not OUTCOME, so red-on-the-tip and red-on-the-board mean opposite things at the same instant.

WHAT THIS PATH REIMPLEMENTS THAT A SIBLING ALREADY DOES

1. Toast dwell, twice. The non-blocking hold/shadow/priority-flush protocol (oled.cpp:3031-3200) has exactly one user — Undo.cpp:2712-2727. The other 13 sites reimplement its dwell as clearPrintShow + delay(300..3000) with a frozen core 0. Undo is the sibling that got it right; nothing else adopted it.

2. "Say a thing on the OLED" has no API. 115 direct clearPrintShow/showMultiLineSmallText call sites, plus one semi-structured renderer (ReadingDisplay::show, ReadingDisplay.cpp:250-339) whose six callers use it for measurements, part inspection AND short warnings.
