There is no feedback(kind, text) anywhere in the tree; severity is carried only by the words, never by the layout.

3. LED clear-before-render, five ways — and LEDs.cpp:167-173 already carries the FOLLOW-UP note saying so: clearBeforeSend (main.cpp:1507), clearLEDsExceptRails(), hideNets, leds.clear()/bread::clear(), plus the -1/-2 sign encoding in requestLedShow as a fifth spelling.

4. Per-strip dirty tracking, implemented then ignored. bbDirty/topDirty (LEDs.h:253-254) are maintained by setPixelColor/clear/setBrightness but the guards in show() are commented out (LEDs.cpp:191). The sibling that actually does this job — request coalescing with a generation counter and a sticky clean bit — is core1req (CoreMailbox.h). The strip layer should defer to it instead of keeping a second, dead mechanism.

5. Blocking-vs-deadline animation, twice. probeMode's latch flash was deliberately converted from three delay()s into a deadline sub-state (flashPhase/flashNextAtUs, Probing.cpp:2684-2694, 2938-2966) so the inner set keeps running. PartsApp's accepted-tap flash is still requestLedShow(2); delay(180) (PartsApp.cpp:259-260, 288-289) — same gesture class, same 100-200 ms dwell, and the sibling has already paid the conversion cost.

6. Debounce/confirm gating, twice. The probe's double-tap machinery (Probing.cpp:232-573: confirm samples, release confirmation, per-button history, five named failure counters) and the encoder's first-detent confirm gate (RotaryEncoder.cpp:439-460: committedRaw, motionConfirmed) solve the identical problem — do not fire on a bounce — with no shared code and different outcome vocabularies (probe: an LED color mode; encoder: flashColors[4] logo pulses at 150 ms).

7. Overlay refresh, split across two mechanisms neither of which works alone. needsRender (write-only, GraphicOverlays.h:74) and requestLedShow(-2) (wrong mode). PartLabels is the sibling that does it correctly and says so in its own header (PartLabels.h:30: "every post is clear-first requestLedShow(-1)") — the overlay layer should just copy that line.

## Probe CONNECT: tap row A → tap row B → route built → LEDs show the net (+ the CLEAR twin)

**Total: CONNECT, tap B (the committing tap) → first LED photon on the new net: BEST ≈ 8.5 ms = 2.67 (ring window) + 0.1 (tick) + 2.0 (rebuild) + 0.15 (req→pickup) + 0.04 (send) + ~3 (render, gate landed at 0) + ~0.5 (near-start pixel). TYPICAL ≈ 17 ms = 2.67 + 0.25 + 2.3 + 0.18 + 0.05 + 7.0 (measured sendDone→show, which already contains the average 4 ms scheduler gate) + 4.5 (mid-board pixel at 30 µs/px). WORST (no drop, no timeout) ≈ 30 ms = 5.33 (16-window escalation) + 0.65 (max tick gap) + 4 (full board rebuild) + 0.7 + 0.24 + 10.6 (max send→show) + 9.0 (last pixel on the 300-LED chain). With one silently dropped LED frame (bb DMA busy, see stalls #5) add up to 51 ms until the swirl tick repaints → ~80 ms. If the core2busy wait at Commands.cpp:367 times out, add 200 ms. If the CDC host isn't draining, Serial.flush() in the commit path is unbounded (the doc measured 1060 ms for one big print). DOMINANT TERM at typical: the LED path — 0–8 ms scheduler gate (avg 4) + ~3 ms compose + up to 9.0 ms of WS2812 stream ≈ 11.5 of the 17 ms; the whole routing rebuild + crossbar send is only ~2.5 ms of it, and the firmware's own probe agrees (T2.2a: 'the handshake itself is already ~0.1–0.3 ms; the LED path is where the time goes'). SEPARATELY, as PERCEIVED latency and not part of tap-B→LED: the tap A → tap B floor is 140 ms of frozen input (the 40/40/60 ms latch flash), plus ~12 ms of blocking I2C per OLED write (twice per connection) whenever top_oled.enabled != 0 — it defaults to 0. CLEAR twin: tap → first LED ≈ 17 ms typical (identical arithmetic minus the latch flash and minus one OLED write), then 360 ms of fade (12 × 30 ms). Per node, CLEAR is ~150 ms ahead of CONNECT on felt latency purely because it skips the flash.**

| stage | where | mechanism | latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tip contacts row A → pad-ladder voltage lands in the ADC ring | `src/hardwarestuff/AdcRing.h:34` | No core involved. One ADC free-runs round-robin over 8 inputs into an 8 KB SRAM ring by two chained DMA channels with a hardware write-ring wrap; a 1 ms block IRQ (DMA_IRQ_1) on core 0 only counts blocks. The pad ladder is channel 5. | 1 sweep = 1/48000 Hz = 20.83 µs from contact to the sample being in RAM. Ring depth 512 sweeps × 20.83 µs = 10.67 ms of history — the hard ceiling on how long a tap can be ignored before its samples are overwritten. |
| probeTick → readProbe() → readProbeRaw() decodes the ring | `src/Probing.cpp:7361` | Polled on core 0, once per probeTick. No conversions: 8 windows (numberOfReads) × 16 sweeps (burstSamples, CONNECT mode keeps 16 because connectOrClearProbe==1) read out of ring HISTORY as ordered means via adcRingMeanWindow(5, wEnd, 16). If >2 of the first 4 windows read low it escalates to 16 windows and re-reads. | Decode window = 8 × 16 = 128 sweeps × 20.83 µs = 2.67 ms of history ending 'now'; escalated = 16 × 16 = 256 sweeps = 5.33 ms. CPU: ~128 strided halfword loads ≈ 8–15 µs (vs the ~1.2 ms of live conversions before T2.1; the doc's ProbePads measurement went 36.8 ms → 4.6–5.7 ms on the same change). |
| Acceptance gates decide this is a real new tap | `src/Probing.cpp:7491` | maxVariance across the 8 window means must be ≤ 6, AND abs(average − lastReadRaw) > 5, AND average ≥ calibration.minimum_probe_reading, AND !probeReadingIsPhantom(). In MEASURE switch position two extra gates: two consecutive bursts must agree within 6 (7501), and measureTouchLatched blocks a second accept until a burst reads back at the no-touch floor (7520). | The variance gate means the FULL 2.67 ms window must be settled — contact bounce/slew pushes acceptance out by however long the tip is still making contact. MEASURE adds ≥1 more tick (~0.25 ms) for the agree gate. Cost when it rejects: 0 (returns −1, tick calls again). |
| The probeMode tick loop — what sets the sampling cadence | `src/Probing.cpp:4068` | probeMode() is `while(!s.done){ probeTick(s); jOS.serviceInner(); }` — a synchronous modal loop that owns core 0. serviceInner() runs the inner set (CRITICAL services + AsyncPassthrough + DisplayService) with the same due-gate as serviceAll. Instrumented: probeTickGapMaxUs/AvgUs/Count, printed by X. | Measured on hardware (DEV_MERGE_HANDOFF.md row 68): pump gap avg 146 µs, max 648 µs over 201 ticks. Tick body ≈ readProbeRaw 10–15 µs + classifySwitchPosition (500 ms gated, ~1 µs) + checkSwitchPositionFast (250 ms gated, ~1 µs) + oledIsHeld + liveCrossbarService + handleEncoderCursorNavigation ≈ 50–100 µs. Tick period ≈ 200–250 µs → ~4–5 kHz re-check; adds ≤1 tick ≈ 0.25 ms of quantization. |
| Tap A latches node 1 (node1or2 0→1): terminal name, brightenNet, OLED, flash arm | `src/Probing.cpp:3516` | Same tick, all blocking on core 0 in order: Serial.print(node1Name)+flush (host-paced CDC); brightenNet(probeHighlight,5) which walks numberOfPaths and posts requestLedShow(1); oled.clearPrintShow(bothNames,…) = a full blocking I2C frame; b.printRawRow + requestLedShow(2) (LED_MENU = the ledImmediate set, so core 1 renders it on its very next pass, no 8 ms tick wait); then flashPhase=1 / flashNextAtUs=now+40 ms. | OLED: 128×32 = 512 framebuffer bytes; Adafruit_SSD1306::display() chunks them with 1 address + 1 control byte each, ≈ 525–546 bytes × 9 bit-times ÷ 400 kHz = 11.8–12.3 ms of core 0 doing nothing else — no probe reads, no serviceInner, no USB pump. ONLY when top_oled.enabled != 0 (config.h:387 defaults it to 0, and clearPrintShow returns at oled.cpp:1289 when it is). |
| Latch flash: three frames as a deadline sub-state — input frozen | `src/Probing.cpp:2938` | While flashPhase != 0 the tick returns BEFORE the loop-condition check and before readProbe(), so no probe sampling happens. Frame 1 was painted at latch; phase 1 → 40 ms → frame 2 + requestLedShow(2); phase 2 → 40 ms → frame 3 + requestLedShow(2); phase 3 → 60 ms → final show, then probingTimer and doubleSelectTimeout are re-stamped so the double-select window starts from flash END, matching the old three delay() calls. | 40 + 40 + 60 = 140 ms of frozen input per latched node. This is the floor on tap A → tap B and the largest single term the user feels. The ring only holds 10.67 ms of history and readProbeRaw reads the newest 2.67 ms, so a tap that begins AND ends inside the 140 ms is dropped outright. |
| Tap B accepted, commit gate | `src/Probing.cpp:3602` | Same hops 2–4. Extra guards: node1or2 >= 2 required (CONNECT); nodesToConnect[0] != nodesToConnect[1]; s.row[0] != s.row[1] (the same row twice is ignored until the 700 ms doubleSelectTimeout at 3865 resets row[1]); connectionAllowed() at 3634 can reject and `return` (continue). Then the names + '  connected' are printed and flushed, then xbarLatTap() stamps the latency probe. | Gate cost ~µs. Serial.print + Serial.flush is host-paced CDC — typically <1 ms, but this is the same class as the doc's measured `~` (config print) blocking core 0 for 1060 ms when the host isn't draining. |
| addBridgeToState → globalState.addConnection (RAM state) | `src/remembering/FileParsing.cpp:230` | Blocking, core 0. Validates both nodes, isConnectionAllowed, scans connections.bridges[] for a duplicate (increments the count if found), appends, markDirty(). markDirty only arms the idle auto-save — no flash write here. Then autoRefresh==true → refreshLocalConnections(1,1,0). | Linear scan over numBridges ≤ MAX_BRIDGES (128), 3 ints each → ~2–5 µs. markDirty is a flag. |
| refreshLocalConnections: wait for core 1 to be idle | `src/Commands.cpp:367` | Spin `while(core2busy)` with __dmb(), pumping TinyUSB_Device_Task() roughly every 5 ms so the CDC port doesn't drop. 200 ms timeout-and-proceed with a Serial.println WARNING (which itself blocks on CDC). core2busy is raised by core 1 around showNets/leds.show and around sendPaths. | Typically ~0 (core 1 idle between frames). Worst realistic: the length of a core-1 render in flight = 3–7 ms. Pathological: 200 ms then proceed anyway — and proceeding means the rebuild below runs WHILE core 1 renders. |
| NetManager rebuild: clearAllNTCC → infraEvaluate → loadBridgesFromState → getNodesToConnect | `src/Commands.cpp:388` | Blocking, core 0, single-threaded. clearAllNTCC memsets the whole path array and re-seeds nets[0..5] + initNets() + all 12 chipStates; infraEvaluate re-converges the system-owned bridges (probe power, lock bridges); loadBridgesFromState copies globalState.connections.bridges into newBridge[]; getNodesToConnect runs searchExistingNets per bridge and rebuilds the node→net index. core1busy = true is set AFTER clearAllNTCC, and it is only read by the swirl branch (main.cpp:2013). | clearAllNTCC measured 331 µs (PERFORMANCE_ROUND3.md) — MAX_BRIDGES(128) × sizeof(pathStruct)(~140 B) ≈ 18 KB memset, forced full by the `pathsToClear = MAX_BRIDGES; // yeah fuck that` override at NetsToChipConnections.cpp:1313. loadBridgesFromState ≤128 × 12 B ≈ 2 µs. getNodesToConnect is O(bridges × nets), unmeasured, ~100–300 µs at typical board occupancy. |
| bridgesToPaths: the actual crossbar route search | `src/routing/NetsToChipConnections.cpp:1590` | Blocking, core 0. sortPathsByNet (only when startIndex==0, which is always on this path) → mergeFakeGpioInputNets → per path findStartAndEndChips / mergeOverlappingCandidates / assignPathType → sortAllChipsLeastToMostCrowded → resolveChipCandidates → commitPaths. | Measured (PERFORMANCE_ROUND3.md, profiling on): bridgesToPaths 1215 µs total = sortPathsByNet 592 µs (49 %) + path analysis 101 µs + sortAllChipsLeastToMostCrowded 68 µs + resolveChipCandidates 61 µs + commitPaths 150 µs. Plus rebuildChangedNetColorsFromBridges 92 µs, checkChangedNetColors 27 µs, chooseShownReadings 37 µs, setGPIO 25 µs. Measured subset sum = 331+92+1215+27+37+25 = 1727 µs; with the unmeasured pieces call it ~2.0–2.5 ms typical, ~3–4 ms on a full 128-bridge board. |
| Request the crossbar send (mailbox post) + latency-probe stamp | `src/Commands.cpp:447` | core1req::post(REQ_BYPASS, 1u) — a bits-OR under a fixed SIO spinlock (PICO_SPINLOCK_ID_OS2), returns a request generation. Coalescing, never lost. Then xbarLatRequest(). Core 0 does NOT wait. Core 1 peeks REQ_BYPASS at the very top of core2stuff (main.cpp:1699), before the mutex-gated LED work, and also inside loop1's frame-hold spin (1547) — it takes it under a 1 ms core_sync try and returns immediately after. | Post: ~1 µs. Measured req→pickup (T2.3, 40 REPL ops ×2): 144–181 µs typical, max 0.3–0.7 ms. Pre-mailbox flag version: 100–200 µs typical, max 2.2 ms (a pass where core 1 was mid-frame). |
| sendPaths → sendAllPaths(clean=0) → findDifferentPaths → DMA-fed CH446Q list | `src/CH446Q.cpp:704` | On core 1, RAM-resident. dmaCollect=true, then findDifferentPaths() → updateChipStateArray() (1475): builds a fresh 12-chip × 8y × 16x bitfield from the new path list, marks changedPaths[i]=1 where a crosspoint flipped, and — critically — XORs `removed = lastChipXY & ~newChipXY` per chip row and appends sendXYrawUnchecked(chip,x,y,0) OFF-words for every crosspoint that disappeared. Then the ON-words for changedPaths[i]==1. Both go into the SAME dmaWords[] list, OFFs first. ch446qDmaKick (322) claims the wire under the send spinlock and starts two DMA channels — dmaWords→PIO0 shifter and the CS-strobe SM on PIO2. Completion is ch446qCsStrobeIsr → ch446qSendComplete → core1req::complete + xbarLatSendDone. | Measured pickup→sendDone: ~40–50 µs typical, max ~235 µs (T2.3: 'now the whole DMA send incl. strobes'). One new connection is typically 1–3 crosspoints = a handful of 32-bit words. The disconnect scan is bounded by chipHadConnections[] — worst case 12 × 8 × 16 = 1536 bit tests ≈ a few µs. |
| LED show request + the anti-stale gate | `src/main.cpp:1768` | refreshLocalConnections posts requestLedShow(1) (LED_NETS) at Commands.cpp:456; probeTick then calls brightenNet(-1) at Probing.cpp:3700 which posts requestLedShow(-1) — postMode ORs LED_CLEAR into the still-pending LED_NETS, so it is ONE coalesced clear-first nets render, not two. Core 1's LED branch requires BOTH: (a) micros()-schedulerTimer > schedulerUpdateTime (8000 µs) — LED_NETS is not in the ledImmediate set (1762), only LED_MENU/LED_GFX are; and (b) core1req::allIdle(), which covers REQ_SEND and REQ_BYPASS. **(b) is how stale frames are prevented in the LEDs-ahead-of-hardware direction: the strip physically cannot show the new net until the crossbar send has completed.** | Scheduler gate: 0–8 ms, average 4 ms (schedulerTimer is re-stamped at main.cpp:2044, at the END of the previous rendered frame, so the real period is 8 ms + render ≈ 11 ms). |
| Core 1 composes the frame: showNets + measurements + animations + overlays | `src/main.cpp:1853` | Under core_sync, core2busy=true: clearLEDsExceptRails (LED_CLEAR was set) → lightUpRail → logoSwirl → showNets() (LEDs.cpp:4295: assignNetColors then lightUpNet per net, or drawWires) → rotaryEncoderStuff mid-pass → readGPIO → readFakeGPIO → showLEDmeasurements → showAllRowAnimations → renderGraphicOverlays. Two core1FramesHeld() abort points (1836, 1907) re-post the taken request so a dropped frame is repainted. | Measured sendDone→show (T2.3): 6.7–7.4 ms typical, max 10.1–10.6 ms — this composite INCLUDES the 0–8 ms scheduler gate, so the render itself is ~3 ms. req→show max 10.1–11.5 ms. The doc's own conclusion: 'the LED path is where the time goes'. |
| leds.show() → WS2812 DMA out the strips | `src/LEDs.cpp:174` | ledClass::show(): topleds.show() only if NEITHER strip's DMA is busy; bbleds.show() if bb's DMA is not busy. Each JeoPixel::show spins `while(!canShow())` (FIFO drain + a 300 µs latch) then kicks its own PIO+DMA and returns — async, core 1 free. bb is 300 pixels on pio0, top is 145 on pio2; separate DMA channels, so they stream concurrently. LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME is 14 µs — effectively no throttle; the 8 ms scheduler tick is the real frame-rate limiter. | WS2812 @800 kHz = 24 bits × 1.25 µs = 30 µs/pixel. bb strip 300 × 30 µs = 9.0 ms + 300 µs latch ≈ 9.3 ms; top 145 × 30 µs = 4.35 ms. The connected row lights when its index is reached: index/300 × 9.0 ms — a mid-board row is +4.5 ms after the kick. Core-1 spin inside show(): up to ~312 µs (FIFO drain + latch). |
| CLEAR twin — tap commits on the SAME tick, no latch flash | `src/Probing.cpp:3570` | The flash arm at 3570 and the brightenNet+bothNames OLED at 3558 are both gated on `s.setOrClear == 1`. And the commit gate at 3602 fires on `(s.setOrClear == 0 && node1or2 >= 1)`. So CLEAR needs one tap, arms no flash, and reaches the commit in the same tick that accepted the reading. | Saves the entire 140 ms latch flash and one ~12 ms OLED write per node relative to CONNECT. CLEAR's tap→accept is identical (2.67 ms window + ≤0.25 ms tick). |
| CLEAR twin — removeBridgeFromState removes ALL bridges on the node | `src/remembering/FileParsing.cpp:272` | node2 == -1 path: iterates connections.bridges BACKWARDS, removeConnection() for every bridge containing node1, recording the OTHER node in lastRemovedNodes[] (capped at 20) and setting disconnectedNodeNewData. Then markDirty() and — only if removedCount > 0 — refreshLocalConnections(-1, 1, 0). Note updateFakeGpioAfterConnectionChange sits INSIDE that if-block here but outside it in addBridgeToState. | O(numBridges) backwards scan with an O(n) removeConnection per hit → ~5–20 µs for a node on a small net; up to ~1 ms if the node is on a 20-member net. The rebuild that follows is the same ~2.0–2.5 ms as CONNECT. |
| CLEAR twin — clear-first LED request and the teardown words | `src/remembering/FileParsing.cpp:339` | ledShowOption is **-1** here (vs **1** for connect) → LED_CLEAR\|LED_NETS, so clearLEDsExceptRails() blanks the breadboard before the repaint. That is required: showNets only paints nets that exist, so a removed net's pixels would otherwise persist. (CONNECT asks for 1 but gets the clear anyway from brightenNet(-1) one line later.) The crosspoint teardown itself is the `removed = lastChipXY & ~newChipXY` OFF-words in updateChipStateArray, appended AHEAD of the ON-words in the same DMA list — so on the wire the disconnects strobe before any reconnect. | Same send/render/stream numbers as CONNECT: req→pickup ~0.15 ms, send ~0.05 ms, sendDone→show 6.7–7.4 ms, WS2812 up to 9.0 ms. |
| CLEAR twin — the 360 ms fade animation | `src/Probing.cpp:3231` | removeFade = 10 is set at commit (3805) and fadeIndex = -3 at 3765. The tick's setOrClear==0 branch steps every 30 ms: advances fadeIndex 0..12, repaints every deleteMisses[] entry with b.printRawRow(deleteFade[offset]) and posts requestLedShow(2) per step (LED_MENU = immediate, so each step renders on core 1's next pass). Kevin's M2 note is on this constant: 12 ms/step was the pre-M2 rate that the slow passes were accidentally throttling; 30 ms was chosen to restore the old feel now that ticks are ~200 µs. | 12 steps × 30 ms = 360 ms of fade, ~12 extra immediate LED frames. The 13th step clears deleteMisses[] and stops. |

**Bottlenecks:**
- THE LED PATH, ~11.5 ms of the ~17 ms typical: the 8 ms scheduler gate at main.cpp:1768 (LED_NETS is not in the ledImmediate set at 1762 — only LED_MENU/LED_GFX are — so a nets render always waits out the tick, average 4 ms), plus ~3 ms of compose (showNets + readGPIO + readFakeGPIO + showLEDmeasurements + showAllRowAnimations + renderGraphicOverlays), plus up to 9.0 ms of WS2812 stream for the 300-pixel bb strip at 30 µs/pixel. Measured sendDone→show 6.7–7.4 ms typical / 10.1–10.6 ms max.
- THE 140 ms LATCH FLASH (Probing.cpp:2938–2967, armed at 3588) — the single biggest number the user feels, and it is a hard input blackout: probeTick returns before readProbe() for the whole 40+40+60 ms. The constants date from when a probe pass cost 8–20 ms; the tick is now ~200–250 µs, so they are ~600× the loop period they were sized against. Kevin already re-tuned this class of constant once (the clear fade, 12 ms → 30 ms per step, Probing.cpp:3226–3231).
- THE OLED WRITE, ~12 ms of blocking 400 kHz I2C on core 0, TWICE per connection (tap A bothNames at Probing.cpp:3561, tap B commit at 3702). 128×32 = 512 framebuffer bytes, chunked with 1 address + 1 control byte each ≈ 525–546 bytes × 9 bit-times ÷ 400 kHz. Core 0 does nothing else during it — no probe reads, no serviceInner, no USB pump. Only paid when top_oled.enabled != 0 (config.h:387 defaults it to 0; oled.cpp:1289 short-circuits otherwise), so the default-config board never pays this.
- THE 2.67 ms ACCEPTANCE WINDOW (readProbeRaw's 8 × 16 = 128 sweeps of history at 20.83 µs/sweep). It is a floor on tap→decision that no amount of tick speed removes, and the maxVariance ≤ 6 gate means the ENTIRE window must be settled, so contact bounce adds to it. Escalation to 16 windows doubles it to 5.33 ms.
- THE ROUTING REBUILD, ~2.0–2.5 ms, of which sortPathsByNet alone is 592 µs (49 % of bridgesToPaths' measured 1215 µs) and clearAllNTCC's forced-full 18 KB memset is 331 µs — both do whole-array work for a one-bridge delta, on a code path that already has an incremental variant (getNodesToConnectIncremental, loadBridgesFromStateIncremental, bridgesToPaths(…,startIndex)) that nothing on the probe path calls.
- UNBOUNDED SERIAL: the commit prints node names + '  connected' + Serial.flush() (Probing.cpp:3646–3680) and the tap-A latch prints + flushes (3544–3555), all before addBridgeToState. Host-paced CDC — the same class the doc measured at 1060 ms for one config print.
- BENCH NOTE: the firmware's own xbarLat probe (X to print, X! to reset; src/selfreflection/XbarLatency.h) stamps tap/req/pickup/sendDone/show — exactly the hop boundaries above. Every quick win below is measurable on hardware with it, no new instrumentation needed.

**Stalls / races:**
- STALE FRAMES — the asymmetry, and it is the answer to the question in both directions. (a) PREVENTED: the LEDs can never show a net the crossbar does not have, because core 1's LED branch is ANDed with core1req::allIdle() (main.cpp:1774), and allIdle() covers REQ_SEND *and* REQ_BYPASS (CoreMailbox.h). The probe posts REQ_BYPASS at Commands.cpp:447 BEFORE requestLedShow at 456, so the render is structurally sequenced behind the send. (b) NOT PREVENTED: core 1 can render a frame *during* the rebuild. holdCore1Frames()/releaseCore1Frames() are commented out at Commands.cpp:386 and 442 — while the sibling refreshConnections() calls them at 209 and 252. core1busy is set at 389, i.e. AFTER clearAllNTCC(388) has already memset the whole path array and re-seeded nets[0..5], and it is read by exactly one place: the swirl branch at main.cpp:2013. So a show pending when core 0 enters the rebuild is taken and showNets()/assignNetColors() walk globalState.connections.nets[] mid-rewrite → one frame of blank or wrong-colour breadboard. The probe path takes the refresh function that is missing the guard.
- THE 200 ms core2busy TIMEOUT (Commands.cpp:367–384) is timeout-and-PROCEED: if it ever fires, the rebuild runs concurrently with a core-1 render over the same arrays — the exact race the wait exists to prevent — and the WARNING Serial.println it emits itself blocks on CDC. Nothing force-clears core2busy (correctly), so this is fail-open by design.
- DROPPED TAP: the 140 ms latch flash (Probing.cpp:2938–2943) returns before readProbe() is ever called. The ring holds 10.67 ms of history and readProbeRaw reads the newest 2.67 ms, so a tap whose contact begins and ends entirely inside the flash is gone — not delayed, gone. A human tap is normally longer, but a fast tap-tap on the same pair is the failure mode.
- THE SWITCH DETECTOR POISONS THE PAD DECODER'S OWN SAMPLES. checkSwitchPositionFast() (called from the probe tick at Probing.cpp:2997) runs probeSwitchTipSense (6733–6751) every 250 ms: it drives PROBE_PIN low for 2 µs, floats it for 3 µs, reads, restores. The ADC ring is meanwhile sampling that same ladder on ch5 continuously. One poisoned sweep inside a 16-sweep window shifts that window's mean by (bad−good)/16, which blows the maxVariance ≤ 6 gate and rejects the whole read. classifySwitchPosition has touch/LED-settle/button early-outs for exactly this; checkSwitchPositionFast only guards on checkingButton and button-held. Plausible ~one rejected tick per 250 ms. PLAUSIBLE, needs a scope or a rejection counter to confirm.
- SILENTLY DROPPED LED FRAME. ledClass::show() (LEDs.cpp:182–198) skips BOTH strips when bbleds.isDMABusy() — and bb's DMA is busy for the full ~9.0 ms of a frame. The caller in core2stuff has already take()n the mailbox request and calls core1req::complete() unconditionally at main.cpp:1976. Nothing re-posts. Contrast the two core1FramesHeld() abort paths at 1843–1846 and 1909–1911, which explicitly complete-then-re-post for precisely this reason. So a composed frame vanishes and the board doesn't repaint until the next request or the 51 ms swirl tick.
- refreshLocalPending IS DEAD. Commands.cpp:353–356 sets it and returns; the re-run at 483–486 is commented out. A refresh requested while one is in progress is silently dropped, while the code reads as if it were queued.
- NO SELF-HEAL ON THE PROBE PATH. refreshConnections checks anyChipXYSuspect() and forces clean=1 (Commands.cpp:258); refreshLocalConnections has no such check. If a PIO timeout leaves a chip's lastChipXY bookkeeping suspect, the probe's incremental sends can never reconverge it — only a command-driven full refresh can.
- MODE-TOGGLE DOUBLE-FIRE. The cross-button case at Probing.cpp:3319–3325 commits the pending press AND defers the new one in the same iteration, so two mode switches land back to back. Each one goes through PROBE_ARM/PROBE_REARM, and each of those does waitCore2(), clearColorOverrides, an oled.clearPrintShow (~12 ms) and a requestLedShow — ~25 ms of blocking work for a gesture the user made in one motion.
- waitCore2() inside PROBE_ARM (Probing.cpp:2828) is another 25 ms timeout-and-proceed, paid on every arm and every mode-toggle re-arm.
- CORE1BUSY WRITE ORDERING differs between the twins: refreshConnections sets it at 211 (before infraEvaluate and clearAllNTCC), refreshLocalConnections at 389 (after clearAllNTCC). Even for the one consumer that reads it, the 18 KB memset is outside the guard on the probe's path.

**Quick wins:**
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/LEDs.cpp:193` Re-post the LED request when leds.show() drops the frame. Give ledClass::show() a bool return (or check bbleds.isDMABusy() in core2stuff before completing), and on a drop do exactly what the frame-hold abort at main.cpp:1843–1846 already does: core1req::complete(ledGen) then core1req::post(taken). It can only turn a lost frame into a repainted one — the taken bits are already in hand. Land this BEFORE the immediate-flag win below, because that one pushes the frame rate toward the 9.3 ms WS2812 floor and makes the drop more likely.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/Commands.cpp:386` Uncomment holdCore1Frames()/releaseCore1Frames() in refreshLocalConnections, and move the hold ABOVE the while(core2busy) wait rather than below it (refreshConnections has the same hold-after-wait ordering gap at 207/209). This closes the render-during-rebuild race; core 1's two abort paths already complete-and-re-post, so no frame is lost. Also move core1busy = true above clearAllNTCC to match the sibling. Risk is real: the hold was presumably commented out because mid-frame aborts showed as stutter in the T3.4 work, so this needs the X frame-abort counter (ledFrameAbortsPause) watched on a bench session before it ships.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/Probing.cpp:3561` Move the tap-A oled.clearPrintShow(bothNames,…) to AFTER the flash is armed instead of before it. The 140 ms flash is dead time anyway; 12 ms of blocking I2C inside it costs nothing, whereas 12 ms before it delays the flash's first frame and every subsequent probe read. Identical pixels on the panel, identical LED sequence — just reordered.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/main.cpp:1762` Add a one-shot urgent bit to the LED request the probe commit posts so a nets render skips the 8 ms scheduler gate the way LED_MENU/LED_GFX already do (main.cpp:1762 builds ledImmediate from LED_MENU|LED_GFX; the mailbox already has spare flag bits next to LED_CLEAR/LED_BLOCKING). Saves an average of 4 ms and a worst case of 8 ms with no change to what is rendered. Needs the bench because raising the effective frame rate toward the 9.3 ms bb-strip floor increases collisions — do the re-post win first.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/routing/NetsToChipConnections.cpp:1313` Stop forcing the full 18 KB path memset. The estimatedPaths computation two lines above (numBridges × 2 + 12) is already there and is overridden by `pathsToClear = MAX_BRIDGES; // yeah fuck that`. On a 5-bridge board that is 22 paths instead of 128 → most of the measured 331 µs back. Safer form: clear max(estimatedPaths, previous numberOfPaths) so nothing stale survives — the override exists because something read past the estimate, so restore it with the high-water mark rather than the raw estimate.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Probing.cpp:3589` Shorten the latch flash from 40/40/60 to something like 25/25/40. Same three frames, same visual grammar, 50 ms less frozen input per latched node — and the flash is a pure input blackout, so this is the cheapest large cut on the whole path. Kevin already re-tuned the sibling fade constant (12 → 30 ms/step) after M2 made ticks fast; these constants were sized when a pass cost 8–20 ms and the tick is now ~200 µs. It changes felt timing, so it is a deliberate design call, not a free win.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Probing.cpp:2997` Gate checkSwitchPositionFast() the way classifySwitchPosition already gates itself — skip when a touch is in progress (lastReadRaw above minimum_probe_reading, or node1or2 > 0, or within N ms of the last accepted reading) — so the 2 µs drive-low / 3 µs float never lands inside a window the pad decoder is about to average. Costs some mid-session switch-flip responsiveness, which is the thing it was added for, so bench it against a deliberate mid-session flip.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/Commands.cpp:353` Delete the dead refreshLocalPending flag (set at 353, never acted on because the re-run at 484 is commented out). Right now it reads as a queued request and is a dropped one — that is a trap for the next person, and removing it is a pure no-op at runtime. (Actually re-running the refresh is a separate, riskier change; do not bundle them.)
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Commands.cpp:447` Add the anyChipXYSuspect() → clean=1 check that refreshConnections has (Commands.cpp:258) to refreshLocalConnections before it posts REQ_BYPASS, so the probe path can reconverge a chip whose bookkeeping went suspect after a PIO timeout. It only ever escalates to a clean send in a state that is already broken, and REQ_BYPASS has no clean bit — this would need to post REQ_SEND|SEND_CLEAN in that case instead, which changes which core-1 branch serves it.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/routing/NetsToChipConnections.cpp:1616` Route the probe's single-bridge delta through the incremental path that already exists: loadBridgesFromStateIncremental / getNodesToConnectIncremental / bridgesToPaths(fillUnused, allowStacking, startIndex) all take a startIndex, and sortPathsByNet (592 µs, 49 % of bridgesToPaths) is already skipped when startIndex != 0. Nothing on the probe path uses any of it. Biggest single cut available in the rebuild, and the most invasive — the incremental machinery is unexercised and net merges make 'only the new bridge changed' false whenever the tap joins two existing nets.

**Unification notes:** TWO REBUILDS, ONE JOB, DIVERGED WHERE IT MATTERS. refreshLocalConnections (Commands.cpp:343) and refreshConnections (Commands.cpp:177) are the same NetManager→NetsToChipConnections→CH446Q rebuild written twice, and they differ in exactly the safety-relevant places: the core-1 frame hold (held at 209/252 vs commented out at 386/442), the core1busy write position (before vs after clearAllNTCC), infraEvaluate before vs after clearAllNTCC, anyChipXYSuspect()→clean (present vs absent), the mailbox slot (REQ_SEND vs REQ_BYPASS), and whether the caller waits for the send generation. The probe path takes the copy that is missing the guards. refreshBlind (499) is a third variant.

FOUR ANIMATION CLOCKS ON THE SAME STRIP. (1) The probe latch flash — its own flashPhase/flashNextAtUs deadline machine with a requestLedShow(2) per frame (Probing.cpp:2938–2967). (2) The clear-mode fade — its own fadeTimer, its own 30 ms step, its own deleteMisses[] ring, its own b.printRawRow painting outside showNets, its own requestLedShow(2) per step (3231–3290). (3) The menu transition engine — menuTransitionFrameDue / menuTransitionRender / menuTransitionCanShow, a properly paced frame clock with a half-drawn-buffer guard (main.cpp:1747, 1917, 1932). (4) showAllRowAnimations inside showNets (main.cpp:1880). Only (3) has a can-show gate; (1) and (2) paint into the same buffer with no such protection.

FOUR DEBOUNCE / DON'T-TAKE-IT-TWICE MECHANISMS. ProbeButton's isBlocked + blockDurationMs(200) + minimumBlockMs(20) + releaseDebounceMs(30) + suppressPressUntilRelease + the kDblConfirmSamples gate (Probing.h:206–255). The free-standing blockProbeButton/blockProbeButtonTimer globals that handleProbeButtonActions and probeMode both stomp with 400/800/1000/3000/8000 ms values. blockProbing/blockProbingTimer inside readProbe (Probing.cpp:7750). And, for the tip, s.doubleSelectTimeout/doubleSelectCountdown plus the s.row[0] != s.row[1] guard plus the 700 ms reset at 3865 plus measureTouchLatched at 7520.

FOUR CONSUMERS OF ADC CHANNEL 5, THREE ACCEPTANCE POLICIES, TWO WRITERS TO PROBE_PIN. readProbeRaw's CONNECT branch (7387) and its MEASURE one-touch latch (7520); Probing::service()'s idle highlight path at 100 Hz via justReadProbe(true) (1668–1689); ProbePads::service() on its 50 ms gate; and probeSwitchTipSense (6733) which drives the same pin the others are reading through. Each has its own idea of what counts as a touch.

EIGHT-PLUS OVERLAPPING OWNERSHIP FLAGS, OF WHICH THIS PATH USES SIX AND HONOURS TWO: core2busy, core1busy, core1FrameHoldDepth[2], the core_sync mutex, core1req::allIdle(), ledShowIdle(), ch446qSendInFlight()/dmaActive, showingProbeLEDs, checkingButton, probeActive, loadingFile. The probe commit reads core2busy (with a 200 ms fail-open), writes core1busy (which nobody on the LED path reads), never takes core_sync, and relies entirely on allIdle() for the one ordering guarantee that actually holds.

THE TYPED MAILBOX EXISTS BUT EVERY CALLER STILL SPEAKS THE OLD INT. CoreMailbox.h gave requestLedShow a proper typed request (LED_NETS/LED_MENU/LED_GFX + LED_CLEAR/LED_BLOCKING), decoded in exactly one place (Commands.cpp:66–99) — and it is still WRITTEN as magic 1 / 2 / -1 / -2 from ~a dozen sites on this path alone (Probing.cpp 2887, 2946, 2962, 3273, 3366, 3390, 3427, 3581; Commands.cpp:456; Highlighting.cpp:671, 717). The FOLLOW-UP note at Commands.cpp:48–54 asked for exactly this and the note at 55–62 says it landed — but only underneath the wrapper.

## OLED update pipeline — top SSD1306 (blocking hardware-I2C full-frame) and the breadboard part-display (soft-I2C chunked page-flip)

**Total: TOP OLED, probe tip touches a row -> the node name is lit on the panel. Best ≈ 12.9 ms: ring decode 20 us + fit/raster 0.3 ms + command phase 0.27 ms + data phase 12.285 ms + post-ping 0.05 ms. Typical 15-25 ms: add one serviceInner pass of queued work ahead of the render (DisplayService can eat 1.18-1.78 ms of it, Peripherals 10 ms cadence, etc.) plus the previous tick's tail. Worst: (a) wedged/mid-unplug bus = 17 x 3 ms Wire timeout = ~51 ms for that one frame; (b) a toast hold is active -> the write is deferred to hold expiry + OLEDService's 20 ms period, and since OLEDService is NOT inner-set that deferral is unbounded inside probe mode/menus (only probeMode's hand-rolled hold-end poll at Probing.cpp:3008 covers it); (c) show_in_terminal enabled adds 1.7-3.8 KB of synchronous CDC writes (3.4-7.6 ms healthy, unbounded on a stalled host); (d) a hot-plug reconnect costs >200 ms of blocking (Wire1.end + delay(50) + init + delay(150), oled.cpp:3399-3421). THE DOMINANT TERM IS THE DATA PHASE: 546 wire bytes x 9 bits x 2.5 us = 12.285 ms, i.e. 97% of a healthy frame. Encoder detent -> menu line: ~13.5 ms for one detent (0.9 ms to reach core 0 + 12.66 ms flush), and because core 1 releases exactly one backlogged detent per core-0 ack, a fast N-detent twist takes N x 13.5 ms to catch up (a 20-detent flick = 270 ms of the menu chasing your hand), with anything unconsumed for 250 ms dropped. PART DISPLAY (independent path, no shared bus): animation frame every 120 ms; a delta frame lands inside one 4 ms visit; a full 512-byte sweep 64 ms ambient; first bring-up ~43 ms of RAM clear inside one service call; steady-state core-0 tax 30% (modal) to 44% (ambient) while mid-frame.**

| stage | where | mechanism | latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| Trigger A — click-wheel detent reaches the menu render block | `src/Menus.cpp:1205` | Event-gated, not polled. Core 1 owns the encoder (main.cpp:1688 core2stuff -> rotaryEncoderStuff, internally throttled to 2 kHz) and emits ONE detent at a time into the `encoderDirectionState` latch, holding the rest back until the consumer acks (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1289-1312). Core 0's menu loop drains exactly one detent per pass (UP branch Menus.cpp:1101, DOWN branch Menus.cpp:1243) and each drained detent issues exactly one oled.clearPrintShow (1205 / 1226 in UP, 1415 / 1423-1426 in DOWN). The loop pumps jOS.serviceInner() (Menus.cpp:1000) and self-paces with delayMicroseconds(400) at Menus.cpp:1045. | Detent visible to core 0 within core 1's 500 us cross-core poll + the 400 us loop pace ≈ 0.9 ms. Backlog drain rate = 1 detent per core-0 pass, and a pass costs one full OLED frame (see hop 8): 1000/12.6 ms = 79 detents/s ceiling. Unconsumed events are dropped after DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US = 250 ms (RotaryEncoder.cpp:467). |
| Trigger B — probe tip lands on a row, probeTick paints the node name | `src/Probing.cpp:3834` | Polled, inside probeMode's own tick loop: `while(!s.done){ probeTick(s); jOS.serviceInner(); }` (Probing.cpp:4068-4077). probeTick calls readProbe() (Probing.cpp:3152 -> 7742), which decodes 8 bursts x 16 samples out of core 1's ADC DMA ring (Probing.cpp:7361-7385 — microseconds of memory reads, ~2.7 ms of history), then on a row match calls oled.clearPrintShow directly (3561 / 3702 / 3834). No queue, no rate limit — one full panel frame per accepted tap. | Ring decode: 2 passes x 8 windows x adcRingMeanWindow(16) ≈ 256 loads ≈ 5-20 us. Loop pace is dominated by whatever the previous tick did; probeTickGapMaxUs/AvgUs (Probing.cpp:4053-4055) are the instrumented numbers. Effective tip-sample cadence = 1/(tick time), which the OLED write pushes from sub-ms to ~13 ms. |
| Trigger C — OledGuiService renders a retained screen | `src/JumperlOS.cpp:778` | Scheduler service, NORMAL priority, periodUs() = 15000 (JumperlOS.h:600), inInnerSet() = FALSE — so it does not run inside probe mode, menus or any modal loop. Calls OledGui::tick() -> renderNow(false) (OledGui.cpp:687/623), which applies its own gate ladder: re-entrancy flag, ownsPanel_, probeActive, six modal ContextTypes, refreshInProgress/refreshLocalInProgress/core1busy/core2busy, oled.isConnected(), wavegen.isRunning(), then a millis() throttle of 15 ms foreground / 160 ms for the persistent idle screen (OledGui.cpp:672-674), then resolveBindings()+isDirty(). | Best case 15 ms scheduler period + 0 ms throttle. Idle stats page: 160 ms throttle x 12.6 ms flush = 7.9% of core 0 steady-state. Foreground screen at the 15 ms cap: 12.6/15 = 84% of core 0 — a foreground OledGui screen alone can saturate the main loop. |
| oled::clearPrintShow — auto-fit ladder + glyph raster into the GFX framebuffer | `src/oled.cpp:1287` | Straight-line CPU on the caller's core (always core 0). clearDisplay() memsets 512 B; the fit ladder walks point sizes 12 -> 5 calling setFontPointSize + getTextBounds each iteration (oled.cpp:1395-1451, up to 8 iterations); then getCenteredPosition (another getTextBounds) and getDisplay().print(text) (oled.cpp:1482-1489) which rasterizes each glyph via Adafruit_GFX drawPixel. Blocking, no yield. | memset 512 B ≈ 0.5 us. Fit ladder: ≤8 iterations x (~10 glyph metric lookups for a 10-char string) ≈ 80-150 table walks ≈ 20-50 us. Raster: 10 chars x ~120 lit pixels x ~20 cycles = 24k cycles / 150 MHz ≈ 160 us. Total CPU ≈ 0.2-0.5 ms — 2-4% of the frame cost, i.e. NOT the bottleneck. |
| oled::show() gate 1 — terminal mirror (show_in_terminal) | `src/oled.cpp:2271` | Synchronous, unpaced, and deliberately placed BEFORE the `if(!oledConnected) return` at 2278, so it runs even with no panel. dumpFrameBufferQuarterSize (oled.cpp:3525) writes ANSI positioning plus 16 rows x 64 quarter-block glyphs to Jerial -> JerialClass::writeToOutputs (Jerial.cpp:1819) -> Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write, which spins in yield() while tud_cdc_n_connected() (Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:246-254). Default is off (config.h:406 show_in_terminal = 0). | Bytes/frame = 2 border lines (~66 chars x 3 B = 198 B each) + 16 x (5 B cursor + 4 B EL + 3 B border + 64 blocks at 1-3 B + 3 B border + 2 B CRLF). All-blank ≈ 16x81 + 400 ≈ 1.7 KB; all-lit ≈ 16x209 + 400 ≈ 3.8 KB. At a healthy CDC (~500 KB/s) that is 3.4-7.6 ms ADDED to every frame; at 1 bulk packet per USB frame it is 27-60 ms; with a connected-but-not-reading host it is unbounded. |
| oled::show() gate 2 — USBSer3 framebuffer stream | `src/oled.cpp:2277` | streamFrameToSer3() (oled.cpp:3790). FNV-1a over the whole page buffer for change detection, then `if (USBSer3.availableForWrite() <= 0) return` before emitting an encoded frame. Also runs before the connected check. | Hash: 512 iterations x ~6 cycles = 3072 cycles / 150 MHz ≈ 20 us — 600x cheaper than the frame it guards. Emission when enabled: b64 = ceil(512/3)*4 = 684 B + envelope; quarter-art ≈ 1.7-3.8 KB. |
| oled::show() gates 3-6 — connected / hold / shared-I2C0-wavegen / liveness | `src/oled.cpp:2286` | Four early-outs, all cheap: `!oledConnected` (2278); `oledHoldActive()` (2286) — millis compare, sets oledNeedsFlushAfterHold and skips the transmit; `_currentDisplayWire == 0 && wavegen.isRunning()` (2299) — sets oledNeedsFlushAfterWavegen, only on connection_type 2; `checkConnection(false)` (2310) which self-caches for 1000 ms (oled.cpp:745). | Gates themselves: <1 us. checkConnection's actual ping fires at most 1/s: 1 addr + 1 data byte = 2 B x 9 bits x 2.5 us = 45 us (plus two i2c_set_baudrate calls on I2C0). Deferral cost when a gate trips: the flush waits for OLEDService's 20 ms period — and OLEDService is not inner-set, so inside probe mode / menus the wait is unbounded until the modal loop exits. |
| Adafruit_SSD1306::display() — the actual panel write | `src/oled.cpp:2314` | Blocking, monolithic, whole-framebuffer. No dirty tracking, no chunking across scheduler ticks, no DMA, no async. Command phase: ssd1306_commandList(4 bytes) + 2 x ssd1306_command1 (Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:1017-1024). Data phase: one big loop chunked at WIRE_MAX (Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:1038-1052). WIRE_MAX is **32**, preprocessor-verified against the real build flags (none of I2C_BUFFER_LENGTH / BUFFER_LENGTH / SERIAL_BUFFER_SIZE is defined in this include chain, so it falls to the `#else 32` at Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:71) — even though arduino-pico's Wire buffer is 256 B. TRANSACTION_START/END do setClock(clkDuring=400k)/setClock(clkAfter) around the whole call. | 128x32 = 512 fb bytes. 512 = 31*16 + 16 -> 16 transactions of (1 ctrl 0x40 + 31 data) + 1 of (1 + 16) = 17 transactions. On-wire bytes incl. the address byte: 16*(1+32) + 1*(1+17) = 528 + 18 = 546 B. 546 x 9 bits (8 + ACK) = 4914 bits x 2.5 us/bit @400 kHz = **12.285 ms**. Command phase: (1+5) + (1+2) + (1+2) = 12 B x 9 = 108 bits = 270 us. START/STOP/tBUF ≈ 3 us x 19 ≈ 57 us. Per-byte Wire::write software cost 512 x ~100 ns ≈ 51 us. **Frame total ≈ 12.66 ms.** For a 128x64 panel: 1024 B -> 34 transactions, 1092 wire bytes x 9 x 2.5 us = **24.57 ms**. |
| TwoWire::endTransmission -> i2c_write_blocking_until (x17 per frame) | `/Users/kevinsanto/.platformio/packages/framework-arduinopico/libraries/Wire/src/Wire.cpp:427` | Busy-wait blocking SDK call. Per-transaction timeout is 3 ms with reset_with_timeout=true (set in oled::init at src/oled.cpp:704/706 — NOTE the comment in show() at src/oled.cpp:2313 still says '15ms Wire timeout', stale). On I2C0 only, every call is additionally wrapped by the I2C0 arbiter (src/coredination/I2C0Arbiter.cpp:66) which pauses/resumes core 1's wavegen DAC stream around each transaction. | Healthy: 1.44 ms per full 33-byte transaction (33 x 9 x 2.5 us = 742.5 us... precisely 33 B x 9 bits x 2.5 us = 742.5 us; 16 of those + the 18-byte tail = 12.285 ms total). Clean unplug: the address NACKs and the SDK aborts in ~25-30 us per transaction, so a dead-panel frame is CHEAP (~0.5 ms) and the N=3 hysteresis catches it in 3 frames. Wedged bus / unplug mid-byte: the 3 ms timeout applies per transaction -> worst case 17 x 3 ms = **51 ms for one frame**, then reset_with_timeout reinits the block. |
| oled::show() post-write liveness ping + N=3 hysteresis | `src/oled.cpp:2342` | Unconditional extra I2C transaction after every frame (oledI2cPing, oled.cpp:94): setClock(400k), 1-byte write of SSD1306 control byte 0x00, endTransmission, and on I2C0 setClock back to 1 MHz. Three consecutive failures flip oledConnected=false and zero lastConnectionCheck. | 2 B x 9 bits x 2.5 us = 45 us + start/stop ≈ 50 us — 0.4% of the frame. On I2C0 add 2 x i2c_set_baudrate (~10 register writes ≈ 1 us). Detection latency for a vanished panel: 3 frames (as fast as ~40 ms in a menu) vs the 750 ms oledPeriodic poll. |
| OLEDService::service -> oled::oledPeriodic — maintenance + deferred flushes | `src/JumperlOS.cpp:752` | Scheduler service, LOW priority, periodUs() = 20000 (JumperlOS.h:571), inInnerSet() = FALSE. oledPeriodic (src/oled.cpp:3225) does, in order: post-hold flush if holdUntilMs expired AND oledNeedsFlushAfterHold; bail on refreshInProgress/refreshLocalInProgress/core1busy/enabled==0; post-wavegen flush; bail while wavegen streams on I2C0; then an adaptive connection poll gated at 750 ms connected / 2 s disconnected / 4 s backed-off. | Steady state: one 45 us ping per 750 ms = 0.006% duty. Post-hold / post-wavegen flush latency: 0-20 ms of scheduler period + 12.66 ms frame. Reconnect transition (oled.cpp:3399-3421): Wire1.end() + delay(50) + init() + clearDisplay + showJogo32h + show() + delay(150) = **>200 ms of hard blocking on core 0**, once per hot-plug. |
| DisplayService::service — breadboard part-display tick | `src/displays/DisplayService.cpp:293` | Scheduler service, NORMAL priority, periodUs() = 2000, **inInnerSet() = TRUE** (DisplayService.h:34-35) — so unlike the top OLED's services this one keeps running inside probe mode and menus, by design ('the animation surviving menus IS the point'). Per visit: pollParts (750 ms gate, skipped in modal context), YIELDED check, beacon (300 ms) or ALIVE path. | Idle/EMPTY: a board-caps check, sub-us. Beacon in ROUTED: displayI2cPing = 2 bytes bit-banged ≈ 74 us, x up to 3 addresses, every 300 ms. Init on first answer: monoOledClearRam writes 8 pages x 2 x (7+64+1 addr) = 1152 wire bytes x 37 us = **~43 ms in ONE service call** (gated out of modal contexts at DisplayService.cpp:323 for exactly this reason). |
| DisplayService animate() — startup-frame rasterizer | `src/displays/DisplayService.cpp:262` | Self-gated at DISP_ANIM_STEP_MS = 120 ms. Nearest-neighbour scale + luma threshold of a 32x21 RGB frame onto the mono framebuffer: memset(512) then h*w = 32*128 = 4096 inner iterations, each 2 integer divides + a luma sum. Skipped entirely when userOwnsContent or midFrame. | 4096 iterations x ~12-20 cycles (2 SDIV + loads) = 49k-82k cycles / 150 MHz = **0.33-0.55 ms**, every 120 ms = 0.3-0.5% duty. Runs on core 0 in one uninterrupted service call. |
| monoOledFlushChunk — delta skip, chunked page-mode write, page-flip reveal | `src/displays/DisplayDrivers.cpp:118` | The page-flip state machine. Fields: midFrame, flushCursor, backHalf, shadow, shadowValidMask (DisplayTypes.h:80-96). Delta skip (lines 132-139): memcmp the fb against the shadow half and advance flushCursor past matching spans for free. Body (157-180): ONE merged transaction of 3 x 0x80-continuation position commands + a 0x40 data run, so a chunk pays one start/addr/stop instead of four. Frame end (140-155): for pageFlip descriptors ('ssd1306_32', a 128x32 panel on a 64-row controller) it emits a single display-start-line command 0x40\|32 to atomically reveal the half just written, then toggles backHalf — tear-free at any bus speed. Non-flip 64-row panels just mark the shadow valid. On NACK it returns -1 WITHOUT toggling, so the retry re-skips every matching chunk and lands back on the flip. | Chunk = 1 addr + 7 header + 16 data = 24 bytes x 36-37 us = **~0.89 ms**. Full 512-byte sweep = 32 chunks = 28.5 ms of wire time. A delta frame is only the changed chunks. Page-flip reveal = 1 addr + 2 bytes = 3 x 37 = 111 us. |
| DisplayService burst loop — the soft-bus pacing gate | `src/displays/DisplayService.cpp:396` | Two nested gates. (a) `if (softBus) { if (now - nextChunkMs < 0) return; nextChunkMs = now + 4; }` — one visit per 4 ms regardless of the 2 ms period. (b) `do { r = flushChunk(); } while (r == 1 && micros() - burstStart < burstUs)` with burstUs = 1500 ambient / 700 under jOS.inModalContext(), and chunkBytes halved (16 -> 8) under modal load. | The budget is checked AFTER each chunk, so the loop always overshoots by one chunk. Ambient: chunk 0.89 ms -> after 1 chunk 890 us < 1500 -> second chunk -> 1.78 ms, stop. **1.78 ms per 4 ms = 44% of core 0** mid-frame, vs the 1.5 ms/37% the comment claims. Modal: chunk = 1 addr + 7 + 8 = 16 B x 37 us = 592 us -> after 1 chunk 592 < 700 -> second chunk -> 1.18 ms. **1.18 ms per 4 ms = 30% of core 0 inside probe mode / menus**, not the ~17% the halving intends. A 32-chunk full sweep = 16 visits x 4 ms = 64 ms ambient. |
| DisplayBus softWriteByte — the bit-banged wire | `src/displays/DisplayBus.cpp:88` | Blocking, push-pull raw-SIO bit bang on GP24/GP25 with delayMicroseconds(SOFT_HALF_BIT_US = 2). No IRQ, no DMA, no yield inside a byte — a chunk is an uninterruptible ~0.89 ms of core 0. Deliberately never uses Wire1 (DisplayBus.cpp:145-155: the hardware branch would panic arduino-pico via setSDA-on-a-running-bus). | 8 data bits x 2 delays x 2 us = 32 us, + ACK bit 2 x 2 us = 4 us -> **36 us/byte**, ~250 kHz effective (the file's own honest budget says ~37 us with GPIO overhead). START+STOP ≈ 10 us. displayBusUnstick = 9 clocks + STOP ≈ 40 us. |

**Bottlenecks:**
- THE frame cost: 546 wire bytes x 9 bits x 2.5 us = 12.285 ms of blocking I2C per 128x32 frame (24.57 ms for 128x64). Adafruit_SSD1306::display() has no dirty tracking — every clearPrintShow ships all 512 bytes even to change one word. src/oled.cpp:2314 -> Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:1036-1052.
- The clock is the lever, chunking is not. WIRE_MAX is 32 (verified by preprocessing the real build flags) while arduino-pico's Wire buffer is 256. Raising it to 256 would cut 17 transactions to 3 and save only 546-518 = 28 bytes = 0.63 ms (5%), because the per-byte ACK bit, not the per-transaction overhead, is 97% of the cost. Going 400 kHz -> 1 MHz cuts 12.285 ms to 4.914 ms (2.5x). src/oled.cpp:72.
- show() has NO rate limit and NO frame dedupe. Every caller path is free-running: the menu writes a frame per detent, probeMode a frame per accepted tap, MeasureMode's oscilloscope every OSCOPE_UPDATE_INTERVAL_MS = 20 ms (MeasureMode.h:112) against a 12.66 ms frame = 63% of core 0 while the scope runs, a foreground OledGui screen every 15 ms = 84% of core 0. Only ReadingDisplay dedupes, and it does so by composed TEXT (ReadingDisplay.cpp:267), not by framebuffer content.
- The terminal mirror is inside the hot path, before the connected check, and unbounded: src/oled.cpp:2271 -> dumpFrameBufferQuarterSize (3525) emits 1.7-3.8 KB of UTF-8 per frame through Jerial into Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write, which spins in yield() while connected (Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:246-254). It has no change-detection even though streamFrameToSer3 computes exactly the hash that would provide it 20 us earlier (oled.cpp:3796-3802).
- DisplayService's burst loop checks its time budget AFTER issuing a chunk (DisplayService.cpp:396-398), so it always overshoots by one 0.89 ms (ambient) / 0.59 ms (modal) chunk: 1.78 ms per 4 ms ambient and 1.18 ms per 4 ms inside probe mode/menus, vs the 1.5/0.7 ms the code intends. That is a 30-44% core-0 tax that lands squarely on the same loop the probe tick and the menu run in.
- The reconnect path is a >200 ms blocking stall on core 0: Wire.end()/Wire1.end() + delay(50) + full init() (which itself does begin(), a logo draw and a show()) + delay(150), src/oled.cpp:3392-3421.

**Stalls / races:**
- TOAST STICKS — the post-hold repaint is dropped. oledPeriodic only flushes at hold expiry when oledNeedsFlushAfterHold is set (src/oled.cpp:3235-3240), but priorityFlushHeldFrame CLEARS that flag (oled.cpp:3691) and then stashes the pre-toast snapshot back into the live framebuffer (oledHoldStashAfterPriorityFlush, oled.cpp:3178-3201). So after an undo toast with no other write during the hold window, the restored background never reaches the panel: the toast stays until something else draws. The stash's own comment ('when the hold expires, the panel transitions cleanly to background+writes with no toast residue') describes behaviour the flag gate prevents. probeMode papers over it with its own hold-end edge repaint (Probing.cpp:3008-3013); nothing covers the idle/terminal/menu case.
- DEFERRED FLUSHES CANNOT FIRE IN MODAL CONTEXTS. Both oledNeedsFlushAfterHold and oledNeedsFlushAfterWavegen are drained only by OLEDService, which is LOW priority and NOT inInnerSet (JumperlOS.h:560-578). Inside probeMode, any click menu, or an app input loop, serviceInner never runs it — so a frame skipped by show()'s hold gate (oled.cpp:2286) or its wavegen gate (oled.cpp:2299) waits for the whole modal session to end. Same for the 750 ms liveness poll: a panel unplugged during a long probe session is only noticed by show()'s own 3-strike post-write ping.
- TIP CONTACTS SHORTER THAN THE FLUSH ARE LOST. Probe BUTTON edges are safe — they are decoded in the PIO IRQ handler which runs processSample directly (Probing.cpp:840-893) — but the probe TIP is polled: readProbeRaw reads a window of core 1's ADC ring holding only ~2.7 ms of history (8 x 16 sweeps at 48 kHz, Probing.cpp:7361-7368). A 12.66 ms OLED flush inside probeTick is a sampling blackout longer than the ring, so a contact that begins and ends inside it leaves no trace. readProbe's own comment at Probing.cpp:7800-7806 already names this ('the iteration time inflates to 20-50ms whenever an OLED write or LED batch fires') and works around it for the button by calling probeButton.service() inline — the tip has no equivalent.
- MENU DETENT THROUGHPUT IS PINNED TO THE FRAME RATE. Core 1 releases one backlogged detent per core-0 ack (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1289-1312) and core 0 spends 12.66 ms per ack on I2C, so the wheel drains at 79 detents/s and anything left unconsumed for DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US = 250 ms is dropped together with the whole backlog (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1309-1313). A fast flick therefore both lags AND loses its tail. Additionally core 0 writes `encoderDirectionState = NONE` directly (Menus.cpp:1102, 1244) instead of setting encoderDirectionConsumed, racing core 1's emitter at RotaryEncoder.cpp:1290-1306 — benign today (worst case one duplicated or one skipped emission) but it bypasses the designed ack channel.
- TWO WRITERS FIGHT FOR THE PANEL OUTSIDE MODAL CONTEXTS. OledGui::notePanelTakenByOther (OledGui.cpp:593) only yields the PERSISTENT IDLE screen; a foreground screen keeps free-running at 15 ms. So a foreground GUI screen plus any direct clearPrintShow writer (PartLabels' 20 ms service, Highlighting, VoltageAdjuster's per-pass ReadingDisplay::show at Peripherals.cpp:3391) alternate full 12.66 ms frames on the same panel with no arbitration — visible flicker plus a saturated core 0.
- Ser3 STREAM BACKPRESSURE GUARD IS A NOT-ZERO CHECK, NOT A FITS CHECK. streamFrameToSer3 does `if (USBSer3.availableForWrite() <= 0) return` (oled.cpp:3802) and then writes 684 B (b64) to 3.8 KB (quarter art). With 10 bytes free it passes the guard and then blocks in the CDC write spin for the rest.
- SHARED-I2C0 GATING IS ADVISORY, NOT LOCKED. On connection_type 2 the panel shares I2C0 with the MCP4728 and both INA219s, and the only protection is `wavegen.isRunning()` polled from core 0 (oled.cpp:2299, OledGui.cpp:660, priorityFlushHeldFrame at oled.cpp:1685) plus the linker-wrap arbiter for the SDK calls (I2C0Arbiter.cpp:66). A wavegen start landing between the check and the 17 transactions is covered only by the arbiter's per-transaction pause/resume — i.e. a frame started just before a start-of-stream pauses and resumes the wave 17 times.
- THE 3 ms WIRE TIMEOUT IS TIGHTER THAN A FULL-SPEED TRANSACTION ON A SLOW BUS. A 33-byte transaction needs 742 us at 400 kHz but 2.97 ms at 100 kHz. The static placeholder _defaultDisplay (oled.cpp:58) carries the library's default clkAfter of 100 kHz; initDisplayForConnectionType replaces it on the first call, so this is currently latent — but any path that leaves the bus at 100 kHz puts every frame chunk within ~1% of the timeout, and a timeout triggers reset_with_timeout (a full I2C block reinit).
- MILLIS-WRAP INCONSISTENCY IN THE HOLD TIMER. oledHold extends with an unsigned magnitude compare `if (target > holdUntilMs)` (oled.cpp:3112) while oledHoldActive uses a wrap-safe signed difference (oled.cpp:3106-3108). Around the 49.7-day millis rollover the extend silently stops working for a few seconds; harmless in practice, but the two halves of the same timer speak different conventions.
- THE PART-DISPLAY BUS AND THE PROBE SHARE THE BREADBOARD FABRIC. DisplayService::activeDataNodes exists so core 1's net-voltage scan excludes nodes 135/136 mid-transaction (DisplayService.h:41-45), and it is a torn-read-tolerant cross-core int8 read by design — a torn read costs one corrupted bit, which the 8-strike flush-error path (DisplayService.cpp:401-425) absorbs by unsticking and retrying. Real, bounded, but it is the one place the two display paths can be made to fight by a probe tip.

**Quick wins:**
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/oled.cpp:2314` Frame-hash dedupe in show(). streamFrameToSer3 already computes an FNV-1a over the whole page buffer in ~20 us (oled.cpp:3796-3802). Hoist it: keep a lastShippedHash, and if the buffer is unchanged skip _displayPtr->display() entirely — 12.66 ms saved for 20 us spent, a 600x trade. This kills the free-running repaint cost of MeasureMode's 20 ms oscilloscope tick, a foreground OledGui screen's 15 ms tick, and every menu pass that re-renders the same line. MUST invalidate the hash on: reconnect (oledPeriodic's init path), oledConnected transitions, geometry change, and after any post-write ping failure, so a bus glitch can never leave the panel permanently stale.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/oled.cpp:72` Raise kOledI2CClockHz from 400000 to 1000000. On I2C1 (connection_type 0/1/3) the panel is the only device on the bus, so nothing else is affected, and clkDuring == clkAfter means the driver still never toggles. 546 wire bytes x 9 bits x 1 us = 4.91 ms instead of 12.285 ms — the single biggest latency cut available for one changed constant, and it also raises the menu detent ceiling from 79/s to ~200/s. Honest risk: 1 MHz is out of the SSD1306 datasheet's 400 kHz spec; real modules generally do it (the board already runs I2C0 at 1 MHz for the DAC/INAs) but this needs a bench pass across the panels you ship with, plus a re-check of the 3 ms Wire timeout headroom (a 33-byte chunk drops from 742 us to 297 us, so headroom improves).
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/oled.cpp:2271` Give the terminal mirror the change-detection the Ser3 stream already has, and an availableForWrite FITS check instead of a not-zero check. Reuse the same frame hash: identical frames produce identical ASCII art, so skipping them is invisible, and it removes 1.7-3.8 KB of synchronous CDC writes from every repeat frame. Also move the dump AFTER the `!oledConnected` early-out or keep it explicitly opt-in for the no-panel case — right now it runs even when there is no panel to mirror.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/displays/DisplayService.cpp:398` Check the burst budget BEFORE issuing the next chunk instead of after: `while (r == 1 && (micros() - burstStart) + kChunkUs < burstUs)`. Today the loop always overshoots by a full chunk, so the modal halving delivers 1.18 ms per 4 ms (30% of core 0) instead of the intended ~0.7 ms, right where probeTick and the menu loop live. Fixing it restores the documented duty and hands ~0.5 ms per 4 ms back to the probe tick inside modal contexts. It does visibly slow the part-display animation (fewer chunks per visit), so it is not literally behaviour-preserving on the panel.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/oled.cpp:3199` Make the post-hold repaint actually fire: set oledNeedsFlushAfterHold = true inside oledHoldStashAfterPriorityFlush() whenever it restores a snapshot into the live framebuffer. Right now priorityFlushHeldFrame clears the flag and then stashes, so at hold expiry oledPeriodic finds nothing to do and the toast stays on the panel. Flagged NOT behaviour-preserving on purpose: it changes what the user sees after a toast (revert to background vs. toast lingers) and the PRESERVE constraint means this needs your call on which is the intended old behaviour — probeMode's hand-rolled edge repaint at Probing.cpp:3008 suggests 'revert' is what was meant.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/JumperlOS.h:571` Make OLEDService inner-set (add `bool inInnerSet() const override { return true; }`) so the deferred post-hold and post-wavegen flushes and the 750 ms liveness ping keep running inside probe mode and menus. Its steady-state cost is one 45 us ping per 750 ms; the deferred-flush branches are flag-gated no-ops otherwise. This is also what would let probeMode DELETE its hand-rolled hold-end polling. Risk is that oledPeriodic's reconnect branch (>200 ms of delay()) would then be reachable from inside a modal loop — gate that branch on !jOS.inModalContext() the way DisplayService::pollParts already does (DisplayService.cpp:224).
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/oled.cpp:2313` Fix the stale comment: show() at oled.cpp:2313 claims 'the 15ms Wire timeout we set in oled::init()' but init() sets setTimeout(3, true) at oled.cpp:704/706. Anyone sizing a worst-case stall from the comment gets 17 x 15 ms = 255 ms instead of the real 17 x 3 ms = 51 ms.
- [design-change/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/.pio/libdeps/jumperless_v5/Adafruit SSD1306/Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:71` Do NOT bother raising WIRE_MAX. It looks like the obvious win (32 vs arduino-pico's 256-byte Wire buffer, 17 transactions instead of 3) but the arithmetic says it buys 546 -> 518 wire bytes = 0.63 ms = 5%, because the 9th ACK bit on every data byte is 97% of the cost. Recording it here so the next person doesn't spend a night on it.

**Unification notes:** DELTA FLUSH — the part-display path already implements exactly what the top OLED lacks. monoOledFlushChunk keeps a per-half shadow of what the panel holds, memcmps the framebuffer against it and skips matching spans for free (src/displays/DisplayDrivers.cpp:132-139), so a frame costs only its changed bytes. The top OLED ships all 512 bytes unconditionally through Adafruit_SSD1306::display() (src/oled.cpp:2314). The SSD1306 supports page addressing on both buses; the sibling driver proves the pattern on a 7x slower wire. Lifting it would cut a typical single-line menu repaint from 12.285 ms to the two or three pages the text actually touches.

PAGE FLIP — DisplayDrivers.cpp:141-149 already does the tear-free reveal for a 128x32 panel on a 64-row controller (write the hidden half, then one 0x40|32 display-start-line command). The top OLED is the same class of part (oled::init drives a 128x32 module at 0x3C) and gets no such treatment: it paints straight into visible RAM, which is why probe/menu repaints tear at 400 kHz.

CHUNKED, SCHEDULER-PACED FLUSH — DisplayService pushes bounded chunks under a time budget so the panel never owns core 0 for more than ~1 ms at a time (DisplayService.cpp:380-399), with an explicit modal-load reduction. The top OLED's flush is one uninterruptible 12.66 ms call with no budget, no yield and no modal awareness — inside the very loops (probeTick, getMenuSelection) that DisplayService goes out of its way to protect.

I2C PING — there are now three independent implementations of 'is the panel there': oledI2cPing (src/oled.cpp:94, hardware Wire, with a documented essay about why the 0-length probe lies), displayI2cPing (src/displays/DisplayBus.cpp:246, soft-bus), and the bare Wire1.beginTransmission/endTransmission inside displayI2cPing's hardware branch. Plus displayI2cCountGhosts (DisplayBus.cpp:257) reimplements GuideChecks' stuck-SDA heuristic verbatim by its own admission.

LIVENESS HYSTERESIS — show()'s 3-consecutive-failure counter (src/oled.cpp:2340-2365) and DisplayService's 8-consecutive-flush-error counter (DisplayService.cpp:401-425) are the same idea with different constants and different recovery (mark-disconnected vs re-beacon). DisplayService additionally unsticks the bus before retrying; show() does not.

CONNECTION-POLL CADENCE — DISP_PARTS_POLL_MS is 750 with the comment 'the oled checkConnection cadence' (DisplayService.cpp:24), i.e. the constant was copied rather than shared. oledPeriodic's adaptive 750/2000/4000 ladder (src/oled.cpp:3290-3299) and DisplayService's 300 ms beacon + 2 s init backoff + 5 s route backoff are two hand-rolled backoff state machines.

HOLD / TOAST EXPIRY — probeMode reimplements the hold-expiry repaint in its own loop (src/Probing.cpp:2999-3013) precisely because OLEDService is not inner-set and cannot run there. Making OLEDService inner-set would let that block be deleted rather than maintained in parallel with oledPeriodic's version.

MODAL-CONTEXT GATING — three separate expressions mean 'don't do expensive work under a modal UI': jOS.inModalContext() (DisplayService.cpp:224/323/388), OledGui::renderNow's six-way ContextType switch plus probeActive (src/eyecandy/OledGui.cpp:640-646), and oledPeriodic's refreshInProgress || refreshLocalInProgress || core1busy triple (src/oled.cpp:3247). OledGui's copy adds core2busy on top; oledPeriodic's does not — the same panel, two different definitions of 'core 1 is busy'.

FRAMEBUFFER CHANGE DETECTION — streamFrameToSer3's FNV-1a (src/oled.cpp:3796-3802), ReadingDisplay's composed-line strcmp (src/eyecandy/ReadingDisplay.cpp:267), OledScreen's dirty_ flag (src/eyecandy/OledGui.cpp:209+) and DisplayService's shadow memcmp are four answers to 'has anything changed'. Only one of them (the shadow) actually prevents bytes going onto a wire.

## Clickwheel CLICK / HOLD → menu dispatch → redraw (V5 RP2350: core-1 sole encoder owner → core-0 modal menu loop → core-1 LED/OLED render)

**Total: IN-MENU CLICK (descend one level) → fully latched LED frame: best ≈ 18 ms (no OLED attached — oled.cpp:1292 early-returns), typical ≈ 31 ms, worst ≈ 1.05 s or the click is dropped outright. Arithmetic (typical): 5.5 (RC release edge) + 0.25 (core-1 500 µs poll) + 0.5 (in-menu core-0 pass) + 0.7 (the extra pass the click branch costs by not rendering itself) + 0.06 (terminal echo) + 12.2 (SSD1306 I2C frame) + 0.4 (buffer paint + transition arm) + 0.8 (core-1 pickup) + 9.7 (WS2812 stream + latch) = 30.1 ms. OLED text lands at ~19 ms; the terminal line at ~7 ms; the breadboard last.

MENU-ENTRY CLICK (main screen → first menu line on the strip): add the 15 ms firstTime delayMicroseconds(15000) at Menus.cpp:1110 and swap the in-menu pass for the 278 µs serviceAll pass ⇒ ≈ 44 ms typical.

HOLD → first back-step: 500 (buttonHoldLength) + 0.5 (poll) + 0.8 (pass) + 12.2 (OLED) + 0.4 + 0.8 + 9.7 = ~524 ms. Subsequent levels every 800 ms.

DOMINANT TERM: the 12.2 ms synchronous SSD1306 I2C frame on core 0 (H8) — it is 40 % of the whole typical click-to-feedback budget and it blocks the loop that would otherwise be catching the next event. Second is the 9.4 ms WS2812 stream (H10, unavoidable at 800 kHz for 300 pixels). Third is the 5.5–8.4 ms RC release edge (H0), which is a hardware floor on every click because the click commits on RELEASE, not PRESS. Everything else in the chain sums to under 3 ms.**

| stage | where | mechanism | latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| H0 — Physical edge (hardware debounce RC on ENC_PUSH / GPIO11) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:346` | No software debounce on the button at all — deliberate. r7 net (documented in this comment block): SW3 → R8 51.1k pull-up → R22 1k series → C27 100nF to GND at the pin. The RC IS the debounce. ENC_A/ENC_B have no such RC — the push pin is the only one that got one. | PRESS edge: τ_fall = 1k × 100nF = 100 µs; t(3.3V→VIL 0.35·Vdd) = 100 µs · ln(3.3/1.155) = 105 µs. RELEASE edge: τ_rise = (51.1k+1k) × 100nF = 5.21 ms; t(0→VIH 0.65·Vdd) = 5.21 ms · ln(3.3/1.155) = 5.5 ms, and with the RP2350's default Schmitt input (VT+ ≈ 0.8·Vdd) = 5.21 ms · ln(5) = 8.4 ms. Since CLICK is committed on RELEASE (H3), every click pays 5.5–8.4 ms in hardware before a single instruction runs. |
| H1 — Core-1 owner poll entry (the only poll that does anything) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:983` | rotaryEncoderStuff() called unconditionally at the top of core2stuff() (main.cpp:1688). Four gates in order: (a) `(sio_hw->cpuid & 1) == 0` → return, so ALL core-0 call sites are no-ops; (b) !isRotaryEncoderInitialized() → return; (c) 500 µs throttle (ENCODER_POLL_INTERVAL_US, line 978); (d) encoderOverride countdown. Then rotaryEncoderStuffLocked(). Mid-pass re-polls at main.cpp:1864 (after showNets) and main.cpp:1997 (when inClickMenu==1) keep the cadence up inside a long core-1 frame. | Throttle 500 µs → 0–500 µs, mean 250 µs. Plus loop1 pass jitter: measured max core-1 kick gap (CodeDocs/SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md:792 table) = 4 ms idle, 1147 ms during a slot-file write (core 1 parked), 10 007 ms during a pre-T3.3 wavegen stream. So worst-observed press→state-machine = 4 ms idle, >1 s while flash is being written. |
| H2a — Button state machine: PRESS edge | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:590` | Polled edge detector (encoderIsPressed==1 && encoderWasPressed==0). Sets buttonHoldStart = millis(), lastEncoderClickUs = micros() (arms the rotation interlock), encoderButtonState = PRESSED. No DOUBLECLICKED branch — every press edge is a plain PRESSED (Kevin's 2026-08-22 rule). | ~0 (a handful of loads/stores, <1 µs at 150 MHz). Total press→PRESSED = 0.105 ms (RC) + 0.25 ms (poll) ≈ 0.36 ms typ. |
| H2b — Button state machine: PRESSED → HELD (the click-vs-hold discriminator) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:579` | `millis() - buttonHoldStart > buttonHoldLength` while still down, guarded against re-entry from MEDIUM_HELD/LONG_HELD. buttonHoldLength = 500 (line 342), never written anywhere else in src/ (verified by grep). This is the ONLY click/hold discriminator on this path. | HELD fires at press + 500 ms + ≤0.5 ms poll = 500.5 ms. NOTE: there is NO discrimination delay on the click side — the firmware never waits to see whether a press will become a hold. It commits to CLICK on the release edge (H3) and to HOLD at 500 ms. The 'commit window' is therefore: release before 500 ms ⇒ click, at 500 ms ⇒ hold, and the two are mutually exclusive because lastButtonEncoderState latches HELD (H3). |
| H2c — Button state machine: RELEASE edge → the click event | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:565` | Sets lastButtonEncoderState = <PRESSED or HELD> FIRST, then encoderButtonState = RELEASED, then buttonEventTimestamp = micros(). The whole consumer idiom is the pair (RELEASED && lastButtonEncoderState == PRESSED). A press that reached HELD releases as (RELEASED && last==HELD) — which no click consumer matches, so a hold can never also fire the click. That is the entire click/hold separation, and it is correct. | Release edge at finger-lift + 5.5–8.4 ms (RC), seen by the poll ≤500 µs later ⇒ 6.0–8.9 ms from lift to RELEASED. |
| H2d — The 10 ms event-hold window (drop mechanism) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:402` | BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US = 10000. While RELEASED and inside the window, lastButtonEncoderState is FROZEN (line 511-523) so a core-0 poller can still see PRESSED. Outside it, line 552-557 auto-clears to IDLE and bumps encoderClickAutoClears — the diagnostic for exactly this. Header RotaryEncoder.h:53-56 documents this as '15 ms'; the constant is 10 ms. Doc/const mismatch. | A click survives for exactly 10 ms after the release edge. Any core-0 loop pass ≥ 10 ms silently eats it. Measured core-0 idle max loop gap = 42 ms (SCHEDULER doc:792, attributed to the ProbePads block + the calibration check; T2.1 later cut ProbePads 36.8 → 4.6–5.7 ms, but the residual is unquantified). 10 ms window vs a 42 ms-class pass = dropped clicks on the main screen. |
| H3 — Core-0 pickup on the main screen (menu ENTRY) | `src/Menus.cpp:506` | main.cpp:1032 jOS.serviceAll() → priority-sorted walk (JumperlOS.cpp:236, stable bubble sort at :459). HIGH order as registered: asyncPassthrough, **menus**, slotManager, stepViewer, probing, highlighting, measureMode. Menus::service() (Menus.cpp:59) returns BLOCKING immediately if inClickMenu != 0; otherwise calls clickMenu(), which tests `RELEASED && lastButtonEncoderState == PRESSED`. | Busy-loop cadence measured at 3.6 k passes/s (SCHEDULER doc:479) ⇒ 278 µs mean, 0–278 µs. Worst observed 42 ms (see H2d). |
| H3a — Wheel-ownership veto: Highlighting gets first refusal on the click | `src/Menus.cpp:508` | clickMenu() asks Highlighting::wantsToHandleButtonPress() (Highlighting.cpp:1966: highlightedNet in 2..5 and clickAdjustEnabled()) and returns -1 WITHOUT consuming the event, so Highlighting::service() (Highlighting.cpp:124) can take it later in the SAME walk and open the rail/DAC voltage adjuster instead of the menu. This is the only place in the codebase where two wheel consumers negotiate rather than race. | Cheap (a couple of comparisons, <5 µs). Cost is a whole extra service-walk of latency only in the race case described in stalls_or_races. |
| H4 — Menu session opens | `src/Menus.cpp:515` | encoderButtonState = IDLE (ack), inClickMenu = 1, ReadingDisplay::resetLastShown(), logoRing.enabled = true, then the blocking call getMenuSelection(). From here core 0 is inside a modal loop and jOS.serviceAll() no longer runs — only jOS.serviceInner(). | <20 µs. |
| H5 — The modal menu loop (this is the in-menu poll cadence) | `src/Menus.cpp:999` | `while (Serial.available() == 0)`. Per pass: jOS.serviceInner() (JumperlOS.cpp:310 — inner set only: tinyUSB, probeButton, mpRemote, peripherals, asyncPassthrough, displayService, ledDump; ProbePads/oledGui/partLabels are NOT in it, which is why in-menu passes are far tighter than main-screen passes), rotaryEncoderButtonStuff() at :1001 (a NO-OP on core 0), delayMicroseconds(400) at :1046, probe-button read at :1053, branch dispatch, delayMicroseconds(80) at :1638, hold-back check at :1646. | 480 µs of hard-coded delay + serviceInner (~100–400 µs typical) ⇒ ~0.6–1.0 ms per pass. So in-menu click pickup = 0–1.0 ms, mean 0.5 ms — 10× better than the main screen, and comfortably inside the 10 ms window. |
| H6 — CLICK branch: state change only, NO render | `src/Menus.cpp:1054` | `(RELEASED && last==PRESSED) \|\| probePress==2`. Sets lastMenuLevel = menuLevel BEFORE menuLevel++, records previousMenuSelection[level], then two O(menuLineIndex) scans to recompute subMenuStartIndex/subMenuEndIndex, then encoderButtonState = IDLE and a bounded release-wait (`while (isEncoderButtonPhysicallyPressed())` with jOS.serviceInner() inside and a 2000 ms bail at :1099). **Nothing is drawn here.** The redraw is deferred to the DOWN branch on the NEXT loop pass, which fires because `lastMenuLevel < menuLevel` (line 1243). | Scans: ~150 iterations × 2 ≈ 5 µs. Release-wait: 0 in the normal case (RELEASED is only set once the pin already reads high). Then delayMicroseconds(80) + hold-back check + loop top serviceInner + delayMicroseconds(400) ⇒ one extra pass = +0.6–1.0 ms before anything is painted. |
| H7 — Descend render, part 1: terminal echo | `src/Menus.cpp:1389` | Serial.print(' '), '>' × depth, Serial.flush(), the menu line, Serial.flush(). `Serial` is the core's SerialUSB (framework-arduinopico cores/rp2040/SerialUSB.cpp:249; no Adafruit_TinyUSB lib in libdeps). SerialUSB::write (:143-186) loops on tud_cdc_write_available() and only gives up when `time_us_64() > last_avail_time + 1'000'000`. | Typical: ~40 chars into a non-full 256-byte CDC FIFO ≈ 20–100 µs. Worst: **up to 1 000 ms** of core-0 spin if the host stops draining port 1 (terminal scrolled/paused, or a second process on the CDC). This is the single worst term in the whole path. |
| H8 — Descend render, part 2: synchronous SSD1306 frame (DOMINANT TERM) | `src/Menus.cpp:1415` | oled.clearPrintShow() → oled.cpp:1287 (clearDisplay, autoDetectMode, FontManager point-size search with getTextBounds preflight per line) → oled.cpp:2269 show() → Adafruit_SSD1306::display() (.pio/libdeps/jumperless_v5/Adafruit SSD1306/Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:1010) — a fully BLOCKING I2C write on core 0. There is a deferral mechanism (oledHoldActive / oledNeedsFlushAfterHold, oled.cpp:1277-1281) but the menu path does not use it. | Geometry 128×32 (config.h:391-392) ⇒ 128 × ((32+7)/8) = 512 data bytes. WIRE_MAX = min(255, SERIAL_BUFFER_SIZE−1) = 63 (ArduinoCore-API/api/RingBuffer.h:34 defines SERIAL_BUFFER_SIZE 64; no BUFFER_LENGTH/I2C_BUFFER_LENGTH on arduino-pico) ⇒ 62 data bytes/transaction ⇒ ceil(512/62) = 9 transactions. Wire bytes = 512 data + 9 control (0x40) + 9 address = 530; at 9 bit-times each (8 + ACK) = 4770 bits; at kOledI2CClockHz = 400 000 (oled.cpp:72) = **11.93 ms**. Head command list = 3 transactions, 12 bytes = 108 bits = 0.27 ms. Post-write oledI2cPing ≈ 30 µs. **Total ≈ 12.2 ms of blocking core-0 time per menu step.** |
| H9 — Descend render, part 3: breadboard buffer paint + transition arm | `src/Menus.cpp:894` | renderMenuLine(): updateMenuLogoRing() (Menus.cpp:807 — sibling scan, ring hue, setMenuDepthLEDs breadcrumb, suppressed while HELD/MEDIUM/LONG so the hold sweep owns the pads) → menuTransitionBeginDraw() (MenuTransitions.cpp:160: sets mtDrawing, then **spins up to 3 ms on mtRenderBusy waiting out an in-flight core-1 render**, then restores 300 target pixels) → b.clear/b.print/printMenuReminder → menuTransitionArm() (:189: three 300-pixel loops + mtBuildAccentFrame's 300 × RgbToHsv/HsvToRaw) → requestLedShow(2) (Commands.cpp:66 → core1req::postMode, with the keep-rule that stops a MENU flush downgrading a pending NETS render). | beginDraw spin: 0 typical, ≤3 ms bounded. Pixel work: 4 × 300 loops ≈ 1200 iterations + 300 HSV round-trips ≈ 250–400 µs at 150 MHz. requestLedShow: ~2 µs (one mailbox post under the slot lock). |
