| H10 — Core-1 pickup and WS2812 stream | `src/main.cpp:1891` | core2stuff() peeks REQ_SHOW_LEDS; LED_MENU sets ledImmediate (main.cpp:1762/1768) so the branch runs WITHOUT waiting for the 8 ms schedulerTimer. rails==2 && inClickMenu==1 → menuTransitionRender() (returns false while mtDrawing, so a half-painted buffer never streams), then main.cpp:1932 re-checks menuTransitionCanShow() at the last moment, then leds.show(). LEDs.cpp:174 ledClass::show DROPS the frame if isDMABusy(); JeoPixel.cpp:239 spins `while (!canShow())` = DMA idle + TX-FIFO empty + 300 µs latch (JeoPixel.h:254-278). topleds (145) and bbleds (300) have separate PIO SMs + DMA channels, so they stream in parallel. Frame pacing afterwards: main.cpp:1747 re-triggers at MT_FRAME_MS = 16 ms (MenuTransitions.cpp:55). | Core-1 pickup: one loop1 pass, 0–4 ms idle (mean well under 1 ms). canShow spin: ≤300 µs per strip. Stream: 300 LEDs × 24 bits × ~1.31 µs (RP2350's 5 %-slow bit clock, JeoPixel.h:256) = **9.4 ms**, + 300 µs latch = 9.7 ms to a fully latched frame. Blend continues for menuTransitionConfig.durationMs = 160 ms (MenuTransitions.h:47) + MT_LINGER_MS 50 ms. |
| H11 — HOLD → back-step dispatch | `src/Menus.cpp:1646` | Checked at the BOTTOM of every menu-loop pass, after the click/up/down else-if chain. `HELD \|\| MEDIUM_HELD \|\| LONG_HELD` and (holdBackArmed \|\| millis()-holdBackLastStepMs >= HOLD_BACK_REPEAT_MS). First step is immediate on HELD; then paced at 800 ms (HOLD_BACK_REPEAT_MS, :976). Deliberately does NOT consume encoderButtonState (:1759-1767) so the hold animation keeps escalating. Re-arm on release at :1007 (requires BOTH pin-high and encoderButtonState==IDLE), which also triggers one repaint to restore the breadcrumb pads the hold sweep wiped. | press → HELD 500.5 ms (H2b) → detected within one menu pass (≤1.0 ms) → renderMenuLine WITH OLED text (:1755) 12.2 ms → strip 9.7 ms ⇒ **first back-step visible at ~523 ms**. Subsequent levels every 800 ms + ~22 ms render. |
| H12 — HOLD escalation → MEDIUM_HELD / LONG_HELD / reboot | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:836` | holdAnimationStuff() runs inside rotaryEncoderStuffLocked (line 1091), i.e. at the 500 µs owner cadence. Step duration alternates HOLD_STEP_MS 450 / HOLD_STEP_IN_PAD 50 by step parity; LOGO_COUNT = 6 steps per sweep; HOLD_ANIM_CYCLES = 2. MEDIUM_HELD at holdAnimStep==6 (:883), LONG_HELD at holdAnimStep==12 (:890), then rebootFlag=1 at :917 and the counter at :706-713 increments once per poll until 100 → rp2040.reboot(). | One sweep = 3×450 + 3×50 = 1500 ms. MEDIUM_HELD at press + 500 + 1500 = **2.00 s**. LONG_HELD at press + 500 + 3000 = **3.50 s**. Reboot after 99 more polls × 500 µs = 49.5 ms ⇒ **~3.55 s of continuous hold reboots the board**, from anywhere including mid-menu. |

**Bottlenecks:**
- src/oled.cpp:2269 / Menus.cpp:1205,1226,1415,1755 — the synchronous SSD1306 frame. 512 data + 9 control + 9 address = 530 bytes × 9 bit-times ÷ 400 kHz = 11.93 ms, + 0.27 ms command head + 30 µs ping = 12.2 ms of blocking core-0 time on EVERY menu step and EVERY hold back-step. It is both the largest latency term and the reason a click landing during a redraw can miss the 10 ms event window.
- src/main.cpp:1956 → LEDs.cpp:194 → JeoPixel.cpp:239 — the WS2812 frame: 300 × 24 × 1.31 µs = 9.4 ms + 300 µs latch. Physically bounded; the only lever is not re-showing more often than 1/(9.7 ms), which MT_FRAME_MS = 16 ms already respects — except while the button is held (see stalls).
- src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:346 (hardware) — the 5.21 ms RC rise on ENC_PUSH. Because the click is committed on the RELEASE edge (RotaryEncoder.cpp:565), 5.5–8.4 ms of every click is spent waiting for C27 to charge through R8. Nothing in firmware can recover it; committing on PRESS would (see quick wins / design notes).
- src/Menus.cpp:1110 — delayMicroseconds(15000) on the firstTime pass. A flat 15 ms tax on every menu open, 34 % of the menu-entry budget, guarding nothing I can find (the branch that follows only scans menuLevels[] and prints).
- src/Menus.cpp:1046 + 1638 — delayMicroseconds(400) + delayMicroseconds(80) = 480 µs of pure dead time per menu-loop pass, which is most of the 0.6–1.0 ms in-menu pass time and therefore most of the click-pickup jitter inside a menu.
- src/Menus.cpp:1054-1102 — the click branch renders nothing; the descend repaint is deferred to the DOWN branch (`lastMenuLevel < menuLevel`, :1243) on the next pass. Costs one full loop pass (0.6–1.0 ms) and makes the click path structurally harder to reason about than the turn path.
- src/JumperlOS.cpp:236 serviceAll() pass time on the MAIN screen — measured 278 µs mean but 42 ms max at idle (SCHEDULER doc:792). Against a 10 ms event window that is the click-drop bottleneck, and it only affects menu ENTRY (inside a menu, serviceInner skips ProbePads/oledGui/partLabels and passes are ~0.6–1.0 ms).

**Stalls / races:**
- DROPPED CLICK — the 10 ms window vs the 42 ms pass. RotaryEncoder.cpp:402 BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US = 10000 µs; RotaryEncoder.cpp:552-557 auto-clears and bumps encoderClickAutoClears when nobody acked. The measured core-0 idle max loop gap is 42 ms (SCHEDULER doc:792 table). Any main-screen pass ≥ 10 ms eats the click silently. Also note RotaryEncoder.h:53-56 documents this window as '15 ms' — the constant is 10 ms, so the design note and the code disagree.
- DROPPED CLICK — self-inflicted, inside a menu. The 12.2 ms OLED write (H8) and the up-to-1 s Serial.flush (H7) both run inside the menu loop. A click that arrives while the previous turn's redraw is in flight is outside the 10 ms window by the time the loop comes back around. Turn-then-immediately-click is the exact gesture that loses events.
- DROPPED SYNTHETIC CLICK — race with the auto-clear. Menus.cpp:1173, :1373 and :1622 re-arm a click by poking `encoderButtonState = RELEASED; lastButtonEncoderState = PRESSED;` directly WITHOUT refreshing buttonEventTimestamp. Since that timestamp is stale (from the real click, long past), core 1's very next 500 µs poll takes the `micros() - buttonEventTimestamp >= 10000` branch and clears it. Core 0 has to re-read within ~500 µs, but its next pass is 0.6–1.0 ms away — so it is roughly a coin flip. synthesizeEncoderClick() (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1051) exists precisely to fix this and is not used at these three sites. Same pattern at JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2735 and Debugs.cpp:3234.
- REBOOT DURING A DEEP HOLD-TO-BACK. Menus.cpp:1657 resets buttonHoldStart every back-step with the stated intent of keeping 'the MEDIUM/LONG reboot escalation from creeping in while you're still stepping through menus'. It does not work: holdAnimationStuff() drives the escalation from holdAnimStep/holdAnimTimer (RotaryEncoder.cpp:836-896), neither of which is reset while the button stays down, and the HELD assignment at :579 is already guarded by `state != MEDIUM_HELD && != LONG_HELD` so resetting buttonHoldStart is a no-op once MEDIUM_HELD latches. Timeline: back-steps at press+500, 1300, 2100, 2900, 3700 ms; MEDIUM_HELD at 2000; LONG_HELD at 3500; rp2040.reboot() at ~3550 ms. Holding to back out of 5+ levels reboots the board before the 5th step.
- CROSS-CORE FIGHT on buttonHoldStart. Core 0 writes it at Menus.cpp:1657; core 1 reads/writes it in the state machine (:579, :591) and uses it for the press-animation progress (`elapsed = millis() - buttonHoldStart`, :756). It is a plain non-volatile unsigned long with no barrier. Word-aligned so it won't tear, but the core-0 write makes core 1's rainbow progress jump mid-animation, and it is an unsynchronised cross-core write in a hot path.
- CORE-1 FLOODING while the button is down. Menus.cpp:1020-1022 posts requestLedShow(2) on EVERY menu-loop pass whenever encoderButtonState == PRESSED or logoRing.holdStepLengthMs > 0 — that is ~1–2 kHz of mode-2 posts. Core 1 then enters the rails==2 branch every pass, and each accepted leds.show() spins up to 300 µs on canShow() and drops the rest. Holding the wheel is the most expensive thing you can do to core 1.
- CORE-0 SPINS ON CORE-1 in the redraw. MenuTransitions.cpp:168 — menuTransitionBeginDraw() spins up to 3 ms on mtRenderBusy before every single menu repaint; menuTransitionCancel() (:240) does the same. Bounded, but it is core 0 waiting on core 1 inside the click path.
- TERMINAL BACKPRESSURE WEDGE. Menus.cpp:1196/1202/1224/1402/1408 — Serial.print + Serial.flush inside the nav branches. SerialUSB::write (cores/rp2040/SerialUSB.cpp:173-176) only breaks out of its full-FIFO loop after `time_us_64() > last_avail_time + 1'000'000`. A paused terminal or a second host process on port 1 stalls the entire wheel for up to a second per step. (Matches the existing 'one host process per CDC port' rule.)
- THE MENU CANNOT TIME OUT. Menus.cpp:444 exitMenuTime = 9995000 — that is milliseconds, i.e. 2.77 hours, so the `millis() - noInputTimer > exitMenuTime` check at :1037 never fires in practice and the returnToMenuPosition/returnToMenuLevel restore path (:988-996, :1041-1042) is effectively dead code. getMenuSelection() holds core 0 in a modal loop indefinitely; only a serial byte, a click down to level 0, or a probe button press gets out.
- HIGHLIGHTING/MENUS HANDOFF WINDOW. Menus.cpp:508 defers to Highlighting without consuming; Highlighting::service() runs later in the same walk and re-tests highlightedNet/shouldPersistHighlight (Highlighting.cpp:124-135). If the highlight times out between the two calls, neither consumes and the click waits for the next pass. Recoverable (the event lives 10 ms) but it is a real two-reader window on one flag.
- DIRECTION-EVENT BACKLOG DROP DURING TRANSITIONS. RotaryEncoder.cpp:1309 — an unconsumed UP/DOWN plus its whole backlog is discarded after DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US = 250 ms. Any handoff that parks core 0 longer than that (a 12.2 ms OLED × several, a flash park, an app launching) loses the turns queued behind it, not just the head event.
- LAP-AMBIGUITY IN THE QUADRATURE RING. RotaryEncoder.cpp:99-121 — pending == 0 cannot distinguish 'nothing new' from 'exactly N laps'. The ring holds 8192 samples = 2.0 s at 4 kHz; the measured core-1 park during a slot save is 1147 ms, so it fits today, but it is a silent whole-ring loss if anything ever parks core 1 longer. Documented and accepted in-code; noting it because the click path shares the same poll.

**Quick wins:**
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/Menus.cpp:1173` Replace the three raw click re-arms with synthesizeEncoderClick(). Menus.cpp:1173, :1373 and :1622 write `encoderButtonState = RELEASED; lastButtonEncoderState = PRESSED;` and leave buttonEventTimestamp stale, so core 1's next 500 µs poll auto-clears the event before core 0's next 0.6–1.0 ms pass can read it. synthesizeEncoderClick() (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1051) does exactly this pair in the correct order AND refreshes the timestamp, giving the consumer the full 10 ms window. Identical semantics, one-line change each. (Same fix applies at JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2735 and Debugs.cpp:3234.)
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/Menus.cpp:1020` Pace the held-button LED re-request. Today this posts requestLedShow(2) on every menu-loop pass (~1–2 kHz) for as long as the button is down. Gate it to the same 16 ms MT_FRAME_MS cadence the transition engine already uses (a `static unsigned long lastPressShowMs` + `millis() - lastPressShowMs >= 16`). The press indicator still animates at 60 fps — above the strip's own 9.7 ms frame period, so nothing visible changes — and core 1 stops entering the rails==2 branch a thousand times a second while you hold.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/Menus.cpp:1110` Delete (or cut to ~1 ms) the firstTime delayMicroseconds(15000). It is a flat 15 ms on every menu open — 34 % of the click→first-frame budget — and the code that follows it only scans menuLevels[] and prints. If it was covering a core-1 buffer race, menuTransitionBeginDraw()'s mtRenderBusy spin (MenuTransitions.cpp:168) now covers that properly.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:402` Raise BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US from 10 000 to ~30 000 µs and fix the header comment (RotaryEncoder.h:53-56 says 15 ms, the constant says 10 ms). The measured core-0 idle max pass is 42 ms; a 10 ms window cannot survive it, and encoderClickAutoClears is already in place to prove whether it is happening. Widening only helps a slow poller — it never fires an event twice, because every consumer acks by writing IDLE. Bench it: a wider window also makes a stale RELEASED more likely to leak into a newly-opened screen, so check the screens that do NOT zero encoderButtonState on entry.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/oled.cpp:72` Run the OLED at 1 MHz on the dedicated bus. kOledI2CClockHz is 400 kHz for all connection types, but on connection_type 0/1/3 the panel is the ONLY device on I2C1 (the comment at oled.cpp:63-71 says so) and I2C0 already runs at I2C0_BUS_CLOCK_HZ = 1 MHz for the DAC/INAs. 530 bytes × 9 bits ÷ 1 MHz = 4.8 ms instead of 11.93 ms — 7 ms off every menu step and every hold back-step. Keep 400 kHz on the shared I2C0 path (connection_type 2) where the SSD1306 is not the clock owner. Bench: SSD1306 modules vary; watch for tearing/NACKs on long cables via the existing consecutiveWriteFailures counter (oled.cpp:2339).
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Menus.cpp:1415` Take the SSD1306 frame off the nav critical path. The deferred-flush plumbing already exists (oledHoldActive / oledNeedsFlushAfterHold, oled.cpp:1277-1281, and oledNeedsFlushAfterWavegen at :2300): paint the framebuffer synchronously, set a dirty flag, and let oledPeriodic/DisplayService push the 12.2 ms transmit outside the branch. That removes the biggest single term from the click path AND closes the self-inflicted click-drop window (a click arriving during a redraw currently misses the 10 ms event window). Design-change because the OLED and the breadboard would no longer land in the same pass, and several screens rely on clearPrintShow being synchronous.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/Menus.cpp:1046` Cut the hard-coded menu-loop delays: delayMicroseconds(400) here and delayMicroseconds(80) at :1638 are 480 µs of the 0.6–1.0 ms pass. Dropping the 400 to ~100 µs roughly halves in-menu click-pickup jitter and doubles the rate at which the probe button and serviceInner get polled. Bench because the 400 µs also paces the Serial echo and the ProbeButton::getButtonPress() consumption at :1053.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Menus.cpp:1657` Make the hold-back reset actually stop the reboot escalation, as its own comment claims. buttonHoldStart = millis() here has no effect once MEDIUM_HELD latches (RotaryEncoder.cpp:579 guards on it) and never touches holdAnimStep/holdAnimTimer. Add a `holdAnimRestart()` in RotaryEncoder.cpp that zeroes holdAnimStep, restamps holdAnimTimer and drops the state back to HELD, and call it from the back-step handler — so hold-to-back through 5+ levels (4000 ms) can no longer trip rp2040.reboot() at 3550 ms. This CHANGES observable behavior (reboot-from-inside-a-deep-menu goes away), so it is Kevin's call, not a silent fix.

**Unification notes:** TWO CLICK/HOLD CLASSIFIERS, DIFFERENT THRESHOLDS. The shared cross-core state machine (RotaryEncoder.cpp:507-611 + holdAnimationStuff:670) says hold = 500 ms, medium = 2.00 s, long = 3.50 s, no software debounce (the RC does it). EncoderClickTracker (RotaryEncoder.h:180-196, impl :621-668) says hold = 500 ms, long-hold = 1.50 s, debounceMs = 30 — a *software* 30 ms edge debounce layered on top of the hardware RC, and a long-hold 2 s earlier. EkiloEditor.cpp:2582, FilesystemStuff.cpp:1549 and BitmapEditor.cpp:1181 use the tracker; Menus, PartsApp, ProjectsApp, Probing and Highlighting use the shared machine. The wheel therefore means different things in the file manager than in the click menu, and the tracker's ENC_PRESS-fires-on-press convention is the opposite commit point from the menu's fires-on-release. This is the single biggest unification target on this path — and fixing it would also let the click commit on PRESS in the fast UIs, recovering the 5.5–8.4 ms RC release tax.

THIRTEEN NO-OP POLL CALLS. rotaryEncoderButtonStuff() is called from Menus.cpp:1001 ('Update button state every iteration'), :1993, :2515, PartsApp.cpp:107/121/217/230/569, ProjectsApp.cpp:724/784 and Probing.cpp:2382 — every one of them on core 0, where RotaryEncoder.cpp:1033 returns immediately. The single-owner banner (:959-978) says these were left in place 'as no-ops — cheaper than hunting them all down, and they document where polling used to be', but the comments at the call sites still assert they do something. Either delete them or make them assert.

THE CLICK-ACK PROTOCOL IS RE-IMPLEMENTED ~20 TIMES. `if (encoderButtonState == RELEASED && lastButtonEncoderState == PRESSED) { encoderButtonState = IDLE; ... }` appears at Menus.cpp:506, :1054, :2009, :2544, :2830, :2998, :3454, :3839, :5848; PartsApp.cpp:132/224/574; ProjectsApp.cpp:747; Peripherals.cpp:3507; Highlighting.cpp:124 — 15+ hand-rolled copies of one handshake, each with its own variation on whether it also clears lastButtonEncoderState, and three sites (Menus.cpp:1173/1373/1622) that hand-roll the *inverse* (re-arm) incorrectly. One `bool takeEncoderClick()` in RotaryEncoder.cpp would collapse all of it and make the synthesizeEncoderClick() fix impossible to forget.

TWO PARALLEL ACK CHANNELS FOR TURNS. The driver offers encoderDirectionConsumed (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1290) as the ack, but essentially every consumer instead writes `encoderDirectionState = NONE` directly — only Menus.cpp:5810 (history scrub) and JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2853 use the flag. Both work by accident of the emitter's ordering, but it means the AFTER-window and confirm-gate lines that read `if (encoderDirectionConsumed) encoderDirectionState = NONE;` (:1165, :1179) are effectively dead for every real UI. The FOLLOW-UP note at RotaryEncoder.cpp:436-442 already calls for collapsing encoderPosition and encoderDirectionState onto one committedRaw — this is the concrete reason to do it.

DEAD ENCODER OUTPUTS. `resetPosition` (RotaryEncoder.h:34, defined RotaryEncoder.cpp:40) is written by exactly one consumer — Menus.cpp:1115, in the UP branch — and read by NOTHING in the driver (grep-verified; the live one is resetEncoderPosition). `numberOfSteps` (RotaryEncoder.h:33, written at :1276) has no consumer outside RotaryEncoder.cpp either. Both are extern'd, so they look like contract.

REDRAW SCHEDULING IS DUPLICATED PER SCREEN. renderMenuLine() (Menus.cpp:894) is the consolidated painter for the click menu — but menuShowKeepalive() (:483) exists because the *unbracketed* screens (node picker, value editors, getActionInt/Float/String) hand-roll their own b.clear/b.print/requestLedShow and lose frames to core 1's take-and-clear. So there are two redraw disciplines inside one file: bracketed (beginDraw/arm + core-1 pacing at MT_FRAME_MS) and keepalive-polled (50 ms re-request). The pad menus (inPadMenu, Probing.cpp) are a third. Same sibling problem the probe path has: the LED-show request vocabulary is unified (requestLedShow / core1req), the *scheduling* of it is not.

HOLD-TO-BACK vs HOLD-TO-REBOOT ARE THE SAME GESTURE ON THE SAME TIMELINE. The menu paces back-steps at 800 ms from Menus.cpp:976; the reboot escalation paces itself at 450/50 ms steps from RotaryEncoder.cpp:687-689. Neither knows about the other, and the one attempt to couple them (buttonHoldStart = millis() at Menus.cpp:1657) reaches for a variable the escalation does not read. The probe path has the same shape — doubleClickConnect/Disconnect windows in Probing.h are a separate timing vocabulary again — so 'how long is a hold' currently has four independent answers in the codebase.

## MicroPython surface: REPL char echo → jumperless.* dispatch → background ticks → what a running script starves on core 0

**Total: KEYSTROKE → ECHO (REPL prompt on Serial): best ~0.7 ms (short line: 0.5 ms USB frame + 0.005 ms poll + 0.05 ms CPU + 0.1 ms USB out). Typical ~1.6 ms (40–76 char single line: 0.5 + 0.005 + 0.45 CPU + 0.6 USB). Worst 10–130 ms (400–2000 char multiline buffer). DOMINANT TERM IS NOT THE POLL CADENCE — the loop polls at ~5 µs. It is the O(buffer) full re-highlight + full retransmit performed on every single keystroke (Python_Proper.cpp:2639 → 4193 → SyntaxHighlighting.cpp:513): ~200 Arduino-String reallocs per 200 output bytes, each under noInterrupts() and the cross-core __lock___malloc_recursive_mutex, plus pushing the whole buffer back down a 64 B/frame USB pipe. || WHEEL DETENT → MENU REACTION while the REPL prompt is open: 0–50 ms, mean 25 ms. Dominant term: the 50 ms jl_service_python() gate at Python_Proper.cpp:1252, which also clamps DisplayService's 2 ms tick to 20 Hz. || WHEEL / PROBE-TAP / OLED / LED ANIMATION DURING A RUNNING SCRIPT: best = 50 ms (only if the script is inside time.sleep(), and only for the seven inner-set services); typical and worst = NEVER, for the entire script duration. Dominant term: zero jOS service passes — the VM hook services USB only. Repo's own KickGap data (CodeDocs/SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md, T1.6b) measured a 5 226 ms core-0 gap for a 5 s compute-bound script before the measure-only KICK_VM stamp was added; the stamp records the gap, it does not close it. || jumperless.* CALL DISPATCH: 1–3 µs of MicroPython overhead + the body. Everything is a direct synchronous core-0 C call — nothing is queued to core 1 — so a call's cost IS core 0's stall. dac_set() is the outlier at 35–90 µs because of a live debug printf with %f whose output is thrown away.**

| stage | where | mechanism | latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| Host keypress → USB FS bulk OUT into the CDC0 (Serial) endpoint | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/include/custom_tusb_config.h:164` | USB full-speed device, RX FIFO 1024 B on V5 (128 B on OG). Host schedules the OUT token once per 1 ms frame. | 0–1 ms, typical 0.5 ms (one FS frame; nothing in firmware can beat this) |
| USBCTRL_IRQ → soft IRQ → tud_task() moves the byte into the CDC RX FIFO | `/Users/kevinsanto/.platformio/packages/framework-arduinopico/libraries/Adafruit_TinyUSB_Arduino/src/arduino/ports/rp2040/Adafruit_TinyUSB_rp2040.cpp:81` | ISR. usb_task_trigger_irq → irq_set_pending(USB_TASK_IRQ) → usb_task_irq → mutex_try_enter(__usb_mutex) → tud_task(). If core 0 currently holds the mutex the soft IRQ does nothing and the byte waits for the next polled pump. | IRQ entry ~12 cyc = 0.08 µs; tud_task with one CDC event ≈ 3–10 µs. Worst (mutex held): waits for the REPL loop's next yield() ≈ one loop pass ≈ 5–9 µs. Total ~10 µs. |
| REPL modal loop pass — the whole main loop() is replaced by this while() | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:1220` | Blocking modal loop on core 0. Per pass: nlr_push + processMicroPythonInput() + soft-reset check + a 50 ms-gated jl_service_python() + nlr_pop + delayMicroseconds(1). USB is pumped implicitly because Adafruit_USBD_CDC::available() calls yield() when the FIFO is empty (Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:197). | nlr_push 0.2 µs + setGlobalStreamWithInterrupt 0.3 µs + 3× millis() (64-bit timer read, ~40 cyc each) 0.8 µs + available() 0.2 µs (+ yield ≈ 2–4 µs when idle) + sleep_us(1) 1–2 µs ≈ 5–9 µs idle, ~4 µs when a byte is waiting. Polling contributes 0–9 µs, mean ~4 µs — negligible. |
| processMicroPythonInput() reads exactly ONE character per call | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:1490` | if (global_mp_stream->available()) { int c = read(); … } — every branch returns after one char. No inner drain loop, so a paste of N bytes costs N full loop passes and N full redraws. | tud_cdc_n_read_char ≈ 0.2 µs. The cost is structural, not per-call: paste of 500 chars = 500 redraws. |
| Printable char → three-way Arduino String splice of the whole input buffer | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:2633` | current_input = substring(0,pos) + (char)c + substring(pos). 2 substring allocations + 2 concat allocations + 1 assignment copy. Every malloc/realloc goes through __wrap_malloc = noInterrupts() + newlib heap under __lock___malloc_recursive_mutex (a cross-core pico mutex shared with core 1). | 5 heap ops × 1–3 µs (IRQ-off window each) + 2N bytes memcpy. N=76 → 5×2 = 10 µs + 0.2 µs ≈ 10–15 µs. |
| redrawAndPosition() — full redraw of the ENTIRE input buffer on every keystroke | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:4193` | Blocking, synchronous. Walks the buffer twice (row count, then emit), substring()s each logical line and each 76-col chunk, re-highlights every chunk, re-transmits everything, then ANSI-repositions the cursor and flush()es. Comment at 2638 is explicit: "Always redraw the entire input buffer to keep everything synchronized." | O(buffer) per keystroke. 76-char single line: 2 substring allocs + highlight + ~200 B out. 400-char multiline: ~12 substring allocs + ~1000 B out. Typing char N of an M-char buffer costs O(M) — the interactive path is O(n²) in the line length. |
| highlightPythonCode() builds the highlighted string one += at a time | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/SyntaxHighlighting.cpp:513` | result += c / result += "\x1b[38;5;" + String(color) + "m". Arduino WString::concat → reserve(newlen) → changeBuffer → realloc to EXACTLY the new size, so every += reallocs. Plus a String(color) temporary (itoa + malloc + free) per coloured token, plus classifyPythonKeyword() per word. | 76-char line → ~200 output bytes → ~200 reallocs. 200 × ~2 µs (noInterrupts + cross-core malloc mutex + memcpy) = 400 µs; Σ memcpy 1..200 ≈ 20 KB ≈ 30 µs; ~8 tokens × 3 temp allocs ≈ 50 µs. TOTAL ≈ 0.4–0.6 ms of CPU per keystroke, ~200 short IRQ-off windows. |
| Per-byte CDC write + flush of the redrawn buffer | `/Users/kevinsanto/.platformio/packages/framework-arduinopico/libraries/Adafruit_TinyUSB_Arduino/src/arduino/Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:240` | Blocking-capable: while (remain && tud_cdc_n_connected()) { tud_cdc_n_write(...); if (remain) yield(); }. TX FIFO is 1024 B (custom_tusb_config.h:165). NO jl_cdc_wait_writable guard on this path — unlike MicroPython's own stdout (Python_Proper.cpp:363, 531). | FIFO write ≈ 0.05 µs/byte. ≤1024 B: no spin (≈10 µs for 200 B). >1024 B (a ≥1 KB multiline redraw): spins in yield() until USB drains — unbounded if the host holds DTR and stops reading. |
| USB FS bulk IN → character visible in the terminal | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/include/custom_tusb_config.h:165` | Host polls the IN endpoint; 64 B max packet at full speed, several packets per 1 ms frame on a desktop host. | 200 B echo: 0.2–3 ms (0.2 ms at ~1 KB/frame, 3 ms at 64 B/frame). 1000 B multiline redraw: 1–16 ms. 2000 B: 2–32 ms. |
| Enter → mp_embed_exec_str() — lex, parse, compile, execute; core 0 is gone | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:2416` | Fully blocking on core 0. Nothing between the REPL loop and the VM. jl_vm_exec_depth is raised so MpRemoteService defers (MpRemoteService.cpp:121). | Repo's own KickGap measurement (CodeDocs/SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md, T1.6b): the exec-entry + compile leg alone is a 173 ms max core-0 gap with nothing serviced. Before KICK_VM existed the whole-script gap measured 5 226 ms for a 5 s script. |
| VM opcode loop → MICROPY_VM_HOOK_LOOP → mp_hal_check_interrupt() on EVERY backward branch | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/lib/micropython/port/mpconfigport.h:515` | #define MICROPY_VM_HOOK_LOOP mp_hal_check_interrupt(); fired at vm.c:1346 (pending_exception_check) with NO divisor/counter. The hook calls TinyUSB_Device_Task() UNCONDITIONALLY at Python_Proper.cpp:662, above the 1 ms throttle at 690. This is the ONLY thing that runs during a compute-bound script — it services USB and peeks for Ctrl+Q/Ctrl+C/Ctrl+D and the 3-s wheel hold; it never touches jOS. | Per branch: 2 flag tests 0.05 µs + millis() 0.3 µs + mutex_try_enter/tud_task/mutex_exit 1–2 µs ≈ 1.5–2.5 µs. A bare MicroPython loop iteration on RP2350 @150 MHz is ~1–2 µs, so the hook roughly DOUBLES Python loop time. `for i in range(100000): pass` ≈ 150–450 ms, of which ~150–250 ms is tud_task. Every 500 ms it also runs gc_info() (Python_Proper.cpp:667): 64 KB heap ÷ 16 B/block = 4096 blocks × ~15 cyc = 61 440 cyc ÷ 150 MHz = 0.41 ms in-VM stall. |
| jumperless.* call dispatch (the module → C wrapper → firmware) | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/modules/jumperless/modjumperless.c:1835` | Direct synchronous C call on core 0 — no queue, no core-1 handoff for scalar ops. 239 MP_DEFINE_CONST_FUN_OBJ entries. BUT jl_dac_set_func carries a live debug printf("jl_dac_set: … %f …") whose output is discarded by arduino-pico's weak no-op _write (posix.cpp:39-48) — the formatting still runs. | MP call overhead (mp_call_function_n_kw → fun_builtin → C) ≈ 1–3 µs. dac_set: + newlib vfprintf with %f → _dtoa_r (malloc + long division) ≈ 30–80 µs → total 35–90 µs, >90 % of it dead work. adc_get: ~21 µs (one ADC ring sweep, per the T2.1 notes). gpio_get: ~2–5 µs. connect(): addBridgeToState + refresh → cross-core CH446Q send (~50 µs pickup→send incremental). |

Inner set = {tinyUSB, probeButton, asyncPassthrough (JumperlOS.h:455), mpRemote, peripherals 10 ms, ledDump (JumperlOS.h:528), displayService 2 ms (DisplayService.h:36)}. DisplayService's 2 ms tick is clamped to 50 ms → LED animation ticks at 20 Hz instead of 500 Hz. menus, probing, highlighting, stepViewer, slotManager, measureMode, probeSwitch, probePads, oledGui, partLabels, mpBackground, portHousekeeping, oledService, liveCrossbar, configSave: ZERO passes for the whole sleep. |
| input() / probe_read() / probe_button() — blocking spins that call NO scheduler at all | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2618` | jl_probe_read_blocking: while (pad == -1) { mp_hal_check_interrupt(); if (mp_interrupt_requested) return -999; pad = probing.justReadProbe(false,1); } — no jOS call, no serviceInner, no delay. Same shape at 2679/2693 for probe_button, and at mphalport.c:106-171 for input() (service_usb_task + read + check_interrupt + delayMicroseconds(100)). | probe_read spin ≈ 20–60 µs/iteration (readProbeRaw ADC sweep, no delay) → ~20 kHz of pure core-0 burn. input() ≈ 100 µs/iteration. Duration is USER-PACED, i.e. seconds. For that entire wait: 0 jOS passes, not even the inner set. OLED frozen, breadboard LED animation frozen, marching ants frozen, AsyncPassthrough UART bridge frozen (data loss), wheel dead. |

So a 1–3 ms wheel/LED blackout after every REPL line, worst case 103 ms. |

The stamp is taken AFTER the call, so the real period is 50 ms + pass duration. Wheel detent → menu reaction at the REPL prompt = core-1 emit ≤0.5 ms + 0–50 ms gate = mean 25 ms added latency vs ~0–1 ms from the normal loop(). |
| bg_start() background callback tick | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/modules/jumperless/modjumperless.c:2803` | MpBackgroundService (NORMAL, periodUs 5000, MpBackground.h:37) → jl_bg_service_tick(millis()) → interval gate (default 50 ms, clamped ≥10 ms at 2807) → mp_call_function_1 under nlr with jl_vm_exec_depth raised. Deliberately NOT in the inner set, and skipped entirely when blockingService != nullptr (JumperlOS.cpp:251-255). | Double gate: 5 ms service tick × ≥10 ms callback interval. From loop(): actual period = interval + 0–5 ms + pass ≈ 52–58 ms for a nominal 50 ms (~8 % slow). From the REPL: the 50 ms serviceAll gate dominates → a 10 ms bg interval actually runs at 50–55 ms (5× slow). During a click menu / pad menu / probe session / any foreground script: 0 Hz. The callback runs to completion inline — a 30 ms callback blocks loop() 30 ms every 50 ms (60 % duty, no preemption, no overrun backoff). |
| Wheel turn during any of the above → emitted on core 1, abandoned after 250 ms | `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1309` | Core 1 is the sole owner of encoder state (main.cpp:1682-1688, internally throttled to 2 kHz). One detent event is held for a consumer ack; if nothing acks within DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US the event AND the whole backlog are dropped (lastPositionEncoder = encoderRaw). Core 0, inside a script, never acks. | DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US = 250 000 µs = 250 ms. Any script longer than 250 ms silently eats every detent turned during it — no burst on resume, no feedback. At the idle REPL prompt (50 ms serviceAll) 25 ms mean < 250 ms, so the wheel still works, just late. |

**Bottlenecks:**
- Per-keystroke O(buffer) redraw. Python_Proper.cpp:2639 → redrawAndPosition (4193) → highlightPythonCode (SyntaxHighlighting.cpp:513). Arduino String's reserve()/changeBuffer reallocs to EXACTLY the new size, so every `result += c` is a realloc: ~200 reallocs per 200 output bytes, each = noInterrupts() + newlib heap under a cross-core recursive mutex. ≈0.4–0.6 ms CPU per keystroke on a 76-char line; ≈2 ms on a 400-char multiline buffer. This is the single dominant term in typing latency.
- The 50 ms jl_service_python() gate in the REPL loop (Python_Proper.cpp:1252, and the same gate in the 'press enter' wait at 1202). jl_service_python() IS jOS.serviceAll() (JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:157). Every service already carries its own periodUs gate; the outer 50 ms gate does nothing but defeat them — DisplayService 2 ms→50 ms, mpBackground 5 ms→50 ms, peripherals 10 ms→50 ms, oledGui 15 ms→50 ms, partLabels 20 ms→50 ms. Costs a mean 25 ms on every wheel detent and probe tap while the REPL is open, and drops the breadboard LED animation from 500 Hz ticks to 20 Hz.
- TinyUSB_Device_Task() called unconditionally on every VM backward branch (Python_Proper.cpp:662, above the 1 ms throttle at 690; hook site mpconfigport.h:515 / vm.c:1346, no divisor). ~1–2 µs of mutex + tud_task per Python loop iteration against a ~1–2 µs bare iteration — it roughly doubles bytecode loop time. And the USB IRQ already pumps tud_task independently (Adafruit_TinyUSB_rp2040.cpp:81-89), so most of it is redundant.
- A compute-bound script runs with ZERO core-0 service passes. Not the inner set, not serviceAll — nothing. The VM hook services USB and peeks for interrupt bytes; that is the entire escape surface. Confirmed by the repo's own T1.6b numbers.
- input(), probe_read(), probe_button() spin with no scheduler call whatsoever (mphalport.c:106-171; JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2618, 2679, 2693). These are USER-PACED waits, so the freeze lasts seconds, not milliseconds — and probe_read() is the flagship interactive Jumperless idiom, so the script that most needs the OLED and the LEDs is exactly the one that freezes them.
- Dead debug printf on a hot API: modjumperless.c:1835 formats "%d %f %d" through newlib on every dac_set() call. _write is a weak no-op on arduino-pico (posix.cpp:39-48) with DEBUG_RP2040_PORT undefined, so the 30–80 µs of _dtoa_r float formatting is pure waste — and it caps software waveform generation at roughly 12 kHz.
- gc_collect_safe() after every REPL statement (Python_Proper.cpp:2443 → 199). pauseCore2ForFlash(100) → holdCore1Frames() makes loop1 return at main.cpp:1584 before core2stuff(), which is where rotaryEncoderStuff() lives (main.cpp:1688). Every executed line costs a 1–3 ms wheel/LED blackout (worst 103 ms).
- gc_info() full-ATB walk every 500 ms inside the VM hook (Python_Proper.cpp:667-686): 4096 blocks × ~15 cyc = 0.41 ms of in-VM stall just to read the free-byte count.

**Stalls / races:**
- WEDGE (device-local, unrecoverable without USB): pause_core2(True) + a compute-bound loop. jl_pause_core2 → pythonFrameHoldSet(true) → holdCore1Frames(), and loop1 returns at main.cpp:1584 BEFORE core2stuff(), so rotaryEncoderStuff()/rotaryEncoderButtonStuffLocked() never run. The 3-second wheel-hold escape hatch that mp_hal_check_interrupt reads at Python_Proper.cpp:721 depends on encoderButtonState being maintained by core 1 — so the escape hatch is dead too. Only Ctrl+Q over USB CDC recovers the board.
- DOUBLE-FIRE KeyboardInterrupt: check_stream_for_interrupt sets BOTH mp_interrupt_requested AND calls mp_sched_keyboard_interrupt() (Python_Proper.cpp:601-604). jl_probe_read_blocking then consumes the flag and returns -999, and the module wrapper raises its own KeyboardInterrupt via mp_raise_msg (modjumperless.c:4095). The scheduled exception is still pending and fires again on the first backward branch inside the user's `except KeyboardInterrupt:` handler. The clickwheel branch at Python_Proper.cpp:736 deliberately sets the flag ONLY and documents exactly this reasoning at 726-735 — the stream path never got the same treatment.
- DROP: every wheel detent turned during a script longer than 250 ms is silently discarded, backlog and all (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1309-1314 sets lastPositionEncoder = encoderRaw). No burst on resume, no LED feedback, no indication the input was seen.
- RACE that force-quits the REPL: the standalone-ESC timeout is 10 ms (Python_Proper.cpp:1432). If the loop is delayed more than 10 ms between the ESC byte and the following '[', a genuine arrow key is decoded as 'user pressed ESC' → KeyboardInterrupt → stopMicroPythonREPL(). A slow serviceAll pass is enough; the docs record a 1.8 s slot save inside serviceAll.
- DATA LOSS: AsyncPassthroughService (the CDC1↔UART0 Arduino bridge) is in the inner set — but the inner set is never run during a compute-bound script, input(), probe_read(), or probe_button(). UART RX from a connected Arduino is dropped for the whole duration.
- WEDGE (host-induced): redrawAndPosition writes via raw stream->print() with no jl_cdc_wait_writable guard, unlike MicroPython's own stdout (Python_Proper.cpp:363 and 531). Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write spins `while (remain && tud_cdc_n_connected()) yield()` — a host that holds DTR but stops draining wedges the editor forever. A ≥1 KB multiline redraw is exactly the case that overruns the 1024-byte TX FIFO and reaches that spin.
- DOUBLE-FIRE (visible): with oled_copy_print enabled, mp_hal_stdout_tx_strn_cooked writes the whole buffer to OLEDOut at Python_Proper.cpp:504-506 and then writes every non-newline byte AGAIN at 543-545. Every printed character reaches the OLED twice.
- DELAY: mp_hal_check_interrupt returns early at Python_Proper.cpp:710/714 whenever a stream byte is pending, which skips the clickwheel MEDIUM_HELD check at 721 for that visit. A 3-second wheel-hold interrupt is therefore delayed for as long as USB traffic keeps arriving.
- GAP: mp_hal_check_interrupt returns at Python_Proper.cpp:638-642 BEFORE TinyUSB_Device_Task() whenever MicroPython has disabled interrupts (kbd_intr(-1) during exception handling). The VM hook does no USB pumping in that window — harmless today only because the USB soft IRQ still runs.
- CROSS-CORE CONTENTION: every Arduino String realloc in the REPL redraw takes __lock___malloc_recursive_mutex (lock.cpp:40, a real pico cross-core recursive mutex) and runs under noInterrupts(). ~200 such windows per keystroke on core 0, fighting core 1's allocator and briefly masking the USB IRQ each time.
- SCHEDULER BLIND SPOT: MpRemoteService processes up to 8192 chars per service call (MpRemoteService.cpp:190) and pyexec_event_repl_process_char executes the script inline. A raw-REPL (mpremote/ViperIDE) script runs to completion inside one serviceAll() pass while returning BUSY, never BLOCKING — so jOSmanager never sets blockingService and has no idea core 0 is gone.
- CADENCE DRIFT: mp_hal_delay_ms stamps last_service_time AFTER serviceInner() returns (Python_Proper.cpp:280), and the REPL loop does the same at 1254. Both stretch their period by the pass duration — the opposite of runService's own stamp-at-start policy, which JumperlOS.cpp:180-189 documents specifically to avoid this.

**Quick wins:**
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/modules/jumperless/modjumperless.c:1835` Delete the stray debug printf in dac_set. Output is discarded by arduino-pico's weak no-op _write, but the newlib %f formatting (_dtoa_r + malloc) still costs 30–80 µs on every call. Removing it makes dac_set ~10× cheaper and also drops the float-printf pull-in from the link.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/SyntaxHighlighting.cpp:514` Add `result.reserve(code.length() * 2 + 32);` before the highlight loop. Turns ~200 exact-size reallocs (each noInterrupts() + cross-core malloc mutex) into one allocation. Cuts ~0.4 ms off every keystroke and removes ~200 IRQ-off windows per keystroke. Arduino String::reserve only ever grows, so no semantic change. Also helps eKilo and the terminal line editor, which share this function.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:504` Remove the whole-buffer OLEDOut.write() at 504-506 (the per-character write at 543-545 already covers every non-newline byte). Fixes doubled text on the OLED whenever oled_copy_print is on. Pick one site — the per-char one is the one the raw-REPL branch relies on.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:1432` Widen the standalone-ESC window from 10 ms to ~50 ms, or better: only treat it as a standalone ESC when global_mp_stream->available() == 0 at the moment the window expires. Kills the 'a fast arrow key during a slow serviceAll pass force-quits the REPL' race without changing what a real ESC press does.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:1252` Drop the 50 ms gate and call jl_service_python() (= jOS.serviceAll()) on every REPL loop pass. The scheduler's per-service periodUs gates already do all the throttling; the outer gate exists only to defeat them. This is the same call at the same rate that loop() already makes at main.cpp:1032, so the cost profile is identical to normal idle. Restores DisplayService to 2 ms, mpBackground to 5 ms, peripherals to 10 ms, and cuts mean wheel/probe latency at the REPL prompt from 25 ms to ~0. Also apply to the 'press enter to start REPL' wait at line 1202. Bench: the reentrancy surface (menus.service() opening a modal menu on top of the REPL, MpRemoteService starting a nested raw-REPL exec) widens 25× in frequency — it is already reachable today, just rarer.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:278` Drop the 50 ms gate in mp_hal_delay_ms so jOS.serviceInner() runs every pass (~100 µs) instead of every 50 ms. Restores DisplayService's 2 ms animation tick and peripherals' 10 ms current-sense poll during time.sleep(). Cheap: serviceInner is a due-or-pending walk, so undue services cost one isDue() compare each. Also stamp last_service_time BEFORE the call, matching runService's documented policy at JumperlOS.cpp:180-189.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2618` Add a millis()-gated jOS.serviceInner() (5–10 ms, the exact shape mp_hal_delay_ms already uses at 278-281) inside the three blocking probe spins — jl_probe_read_blocking here, and jl_probe_button_blocking at 2679 and 2693. This is the biggest single UX win on the whole surface: probe_read() stops freezing the OLED, the breadboard LEDs, the marching ants, and the Arduino UART bridge while it waits for a tap. Bench carefully: serviceInner runs probeButton, which is exactly what jl_probe_button_blocking is polling — confirm getButtonPress(true) is not double-consumed between the service and the spin.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/lib/micropython/port/mphalport.c:148` Same treatment for input(): add a gated jOS.serviceInner() to the no-data branch of mp_hal_stdin_rx_chr, alongside the existing mp_hal_check_interrupt()/mp_handle_pending() pair. The wait is user-paced, so this is a multi-second freeze today.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:662` Move TinyUSB_Device_Task() from above the 1 ms throttle to below it (i.e. after line 690). The USB soft IRQ already runs tud_task on every USB event (Adafruit_TinyUSB_rp2040.cpp:81-89), so the unconditional per-branch pump is largely redundant. Cuts the VM-hook tax from ~2 µs to ~0.4 µs per backward branch — Python loops get roughly 2× faster. Bench the exact case the comment at 659-661 was written for: Ctrl+C/Ctrl+Q delivery during a tight compute-bound loop with no other USB traffic.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:604` Stop calling mp_sched_keyboard_interrupt() here and set mp_interrupt_requested alone, exactly as the clickwheel branch does and argues for at Python_Proper.cpp:726-735. mp_hal_check_interrupt's own top-of-function consumer (645-655) schedules it on the next visit for VM-resident scripts, and the C blocking loops read the flag directly. Eliminates the second KeyboardInterrupt that currently fires inside the user's except handler after probe_read()/probe_button().
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:4226` Route redrawAndPosition's stream writes through jl_cdc_wait_writable (the guard already used at Python_Proper.cpp:363 and 531) so a host that holds DTR but stops draining cannot wedge the editor in Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write's yield() spin. Relevant precisely for the ≥1 KB multiline redraws that overrun the 1024-byte TX FIFO.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:2639` Fast-path the common keystroke: cursor at end of a single-line buffer + printable char → emit just that one highlighted character instead of redrawing the whole buffer. Falls back to the existing full redraw for multiline, mid-line insertion, history, and wrap. This is the real fix for typing latency (turns O(buffer) per char into O(1)) but it splits the display path in two, and the current 'always redraw' comment at 2638 exists because keeping them synchronized was the hard part.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp:667` Replace the 500 ms gc_info() full-ATB walk with a cheaper free-memory estimate (gc_alloc's own failure path, or MP_STATE_MEM(gc_last_free_atb_index)). 4096 blocks × ~15 cyc = 0.41 ms of in-VM stall every 500 ms just to read a byte count. Also worth noting: the gc_collect_safe() it can trigger parks core 1, so a memory-pressured script gets a periodic wheel/LED blackout from inside the VM hook.

**Unification notes:** FOUR ANSWERS TO ONE QUESTION ("run services while I block"). jOSmanager exists to be the single answer (JumperlOS.cpp:310-347), and this path gives four different ones: the REPL loop calls serviceAll() on a 50 ms gate (Python_Proper.cpp:1252); mp_hal_delay_ms calls serviceInner() on a 50 ms gate (278); menus/probeMode/apps call serviceInner() every pass; and input()/probe_read()/probe_button() call nothing at all (mphalport.c:106, JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2618/2679/2693). The sibling that got it right is the modal-loop convention — probeMode() and the click menus pump serviceInner() every pass, which is why the wheel survives a menu but not a time.sleep(). Both 50 ms gates also stamp their timestamp AFTER the call, stretching the period, which is exactly the aliasing bug runService documents itself as avoiding at JumperlOS.cpp:180-189. || TWO STDOUT IMPLEMENTATIONS, DIVERGENT BEHAVIOR. mp_hal_stdout_tx_strn → arduino_serial_write (Python_Proper.cpp:352) and mp_hal_stdout_tx_strn_cooked (486) both do per-byte CRLF conversion + CDC write + jl_cdc_wait_writable backpressure. But only the cooked one yields every 50 chars, only it double-writes OLEDOut, and only the raw one gates the OLED mirror on pyexec_repl_active. sys.stdout.write() and print() therefore behave measurably differently on the same board. || THREE INTERRUPT-CHARACTER DECODERS. check_stream_for_interrupt (Python_Proper.cpp:567, 20 ms debounce, echoes "^Q"/"^C", peeks both streams), the REPL editor's `c == keyboard_interrupt_char` branch (1659, no debounce, force-quits the REPL), and mp_hal_stdin_rx_chr's inline 17/3 test (mphalport.c:129, no debounce, no echo). Each owns a different piece of the same byte, which is why the Ctrl+D-as-EOF-vs-soft-reset ambiguity documented at mphalport.c:113-135 exists at all. || FIVE SPELLINGS OF "PUMP USB". yield() (delay.cpp:50), TinyUSB_Device_Task() called directly (~30 sites in src/), service_usb_task() (Python_Proper.cpp:320), TinyUSBService (JumperlOS.cpp:648), and the implicit yield() inside Adafruit_USBD_CDC::available() (Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:197) — the last of which is load-bearing for the REPL loop and completely invisible at the call site. || PERIODIC-WORK-UNDER-A-MODAL-LOOP has three uncoordinated policies. MpBackgroundService opts OUT of the inner set (MpBackground.h, deliberately, so bg callbacks pause in menus), DisplayService opts IN (DisplayService.h:36, deliberately, so the animation survives menus), OledGuiService does neither and just dies. Three answers to "should this keep running when a modal loop owns core 0?", none of them expressed as a shared policy. || LINE EDITING + HISTORY + HIGHLIGHTED REDRAW is implemented three times: REPLEditor (Python_Proper.cpp:3825-4400), eKilo, and Jerial's line buffer. SyntaxHighlighting.cpp is the one genuinely shared piece — which means its per-character Arduino-String realloc (line 513) is a common-mode cost paid by all three, and the one-line reserve() fix helps all three. || EVENT-HOLD-UNTIL-ACK is implemented in the encoder (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1278-1314: paced emission, consumer ack, 250 ms abandon) and again in ProbeButton's double-tap window. The Python surface participates in neither — it holds core 0 and lets the encoder's abandon timer quietly eat the input. Any real fix to "the wheel during a script" has to either join that ack protocol or make sure core 0 comes up for air inside DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US.

## Probe tip TAP on a row → LED bloom + OLED label + terminal line (ambient/idle highlight path, select switch position)

**Total: SELECT position, ambient path, host draining the CDC, 128x32 panel. Measured from tip contact to visible feedback.

BEST ~14 ms to LED bloom / ~26 ms to a complete OLED label.
  contact lands 2.667 ms (one full ring decode window) before a Probing tick, so decode #1 at t=2.7 ms passes the variance gate; it is discarded by pendingNewRow; decode #2 at t=12.7 ms confirms and reports.
  2.667 (ring window) + 0 (perfect tick alignment) + 0.28 (phantom blink) + 10.0 (confirm tick) + 0.05 (Highlighting same-pass pickup) = 13.0 ms accepted.
  LED: + 0.2 (request lands at a core-1 tick) + 1.0 (compose) + 0.15 (row 1, early in the chain) = ~14.4 ms.
  OLED: + 0.3 (serial + compose) + 12.6 (I2C flush) = ~25.9 ms.

TYPICAL ~28 ms bloom / ~31 ms label.
  2.667 (window) + 5.0 (mean 10 ms tick wait) + 0.28 (phantom) + 10.0 (confirm tick) + 0.05 = 18.0 ms accepted.
  LED: + 4.0 (mean 8 ms core-1 tick) + 2.0 (showNets + tail) + 4.4 (mid-board row, pixel ~145) = ~28.4 ms.
  OLED: + 0.4 + 12.6 = ~31.0 ms.

WORST (no wedge, no host stall) ~100 ms bloom / ~51 ms label.
  first tick's window straddles the contact edge and fails variance (+10.0), weak-signal escalation widens the window to 8x16x2 = 5.33 ms, one confirm pair broken by an intervening -1 (+10.0), phantom cache miss (+0.28), plus a 10 ms tick wait = ~38 ms accepted.
  LED: request lands just after a core-1 tick (+8.0), core 1 skips a pass on the 0-timeout core_sync acquire, leds.show() drops the frame because the previous 9.3 ms bb DMA is still in flight and the request is completed anyway -> recovery only at the 51 ms swirl re-render (+51.0) + 3.0 compose + 9.0 wire = ~101 ms.
  OLED: 38 + 0.4 + 12.6 = ~51 ms (~63 ms on a 128x64 panel).

MEASURE position adds, per tick: the two-consecutive-burst agreement (+10 ms), a 4.0 ms decode window instead of 2.667 ms, and probeMapRange's 354 us blocking ADC7 read. But once a measurement latches, Highlighting returns early at Highlighting.cpp:150 and MeasureMode owns the display entirely — a different renderer with a different debounce.

DOMINANT TERM: the Probing service's 10 ms sample gate, paid TWICE (once to sample, once for the strictly-consecutive pendingNewRow confirm) = 20 ms of pure quantized debounce before ANY feedback can start. Second: the 12.6 ms fully-blocking 400 kHz OLED I2C flush on core 0 — longer than the Probing period itself. Third, on the bloom leg: the 8 ms core-1 scheduler tick that LED_NETS (unlike LED_MENU/LED_GFX) is not allowed to bypass, plus up to 9.0 ms of WS2812 wire time depending on where the row sits in the 300-pixel chain.

WHERE THE LABEL LAGS THE BLOOM: the LED request is posted at Highlighting.cpp:717, inside brightenNet, at the TOP of highlightNets; the OLED write happens ~0.3-0.7 ms later at Highlighting.cpp:1491. So the bloom gets a sub-millisecond head start in REQUEST terms. After that the two legs are: LED = 0-8 ms tick jitter + 1-5 ms compose + 0-9.0 ms wire = 1-22 ms with ~10 ms mean and huge variance; OLED = 0.3-0.7 ms compose + 12.6 ms fixed = ~13 ms with almost no variance. So the label trails the bloom by roughly 3 ms on average — but the LED leg's jitter is three times the gap, so the ORDER IS NOT DETERMINISTIC: a request that lands right after a core-1 tick makes the label WIN by ~5 ms; a dropped strip frame makes it win by 50 ms. What actually reads as "the label lags" is structural, not the 3 ms: (1) the 12.6 ms OLED flush is a hard block on core 0 longer than the 10 ms Probing period, so the tap that prints a label costs the next probe sample and dragging the tip feels sticky; (2) checkForReadingChanges paints nothing on its first pass after a net change and is gated to 40 ms, so the label's NUMBERS only start moving 40-80 ms in while the LEDs have already re-rendered several times.**

| stage | where | mechanism | latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0. Analog: tip contacts the row; the pad-ladder node on ADC5 slews from the dark floor to the row's divider voltage | `src/Probing.cpp:7267` | Hardware, no CPU. In SELECT the tip is fed directly from PROBE_PIN (stiff); in MEASURE the feed comes through the crossbar (~150 ohm + net capacitance), which the code itself calls out as a several-ms slew (7493-7500). | select ~0.3-1 ms; measure ~2-5 ms. Not a code term — but it sets the floor for every window below. |
| 1. ADC ring DMA captures the tip continuously — no read is ever initiated by this path | `src/hardwarestuff/AdcRing.cpp:31` | Free-running ADC in 8-channel round-robin, RING_CLKDIV 124 on a 48 MHz clk_adc, DMA in TRIGGER_SELF ring mode into 4096 halfwords. Block IRQ every 384 halfwords. Zero core-0 cost. | 48 MHz / 125 = 384 ksps / 8 ch = 48 kHz per channel → 1 sweep every 1/48000 = 20.833 us. Ring depth 4096/8 = 512 sweeps × 20.833 us = 10.67 ms of history. Sample-to-ring latency ≤ 20.8 us. |
| 2. Probing::service self-gates the decode to 100 Hz | `src/Probing.cpp:1664` | Polled. Service periodUs() is 0 (runs every core-0 serviceAll pass) but the body gates on `now - lastProbeCheckTime >= 10`. Everything downstream is quantized to this. | 10 ms period → 0-10 ms wait, mean 5 ms. This is the single biggest quantization in the path and it is paid TWICE (see hop 8). |
| 3. justReadProbe: sequence counter + blockProbing gate | `src/Probing.cpp:7636` | `callSeq++` unconditionally at entry — this is what makes the row-change confirm at hop 8 require STRICTLY consecutive calls. blockProbing check returns -1 early (still advancing callSeq). | < 1 us. |
| 4. readProbeRaw: decode 8 burst-windows straight out of the ring's HISTORY | `src/Probing.cpp:7374` | Pure memory read, no conversions. burstSamples = 16 (select) / 24 (measure); numberOfReads = 8, escalating to 16 if >2 of the first 4 bursts land in the weak band. Window i ends at end-(7-i)*B, oldest first, so variance still measures slew across the read. | History span = 8 × 16 = 128 sweeps × 20.833 us = **2.667 ms** (select) / 8 × 24 = 192 sweeps = **4.0 ms** (measure). Escalated: 5.33 ms / 8.0 ms. CPU: 128 masked halfword loads ≈ 4 cycles each at 150 MHz = 3.4 us, + one syncTotal spinlock ≈ 2 us → **~6 us of core-0 time** (vs the ~1.2-2 ms of live conversions this replaced). CONSEQUENCE: a decode whose window straddles the contact edge sees dark-floor and lit samples in the same window and fails the variance gate — the first accepted decode cannot happen earlier than contact + 2.667 ms (select). |
| 5. Variance gate + median-of-bursts + change gate | `src/Probing.cpp:7442` | maxVariance = max \|m[i]-m[i+1]\| over 7 adjacent pairs, must be ≤ 6. Accept also needs \|average - lastReadRaw\| > 5 (a change) and average ≥ calibration.minimum_probe_reading. medianProbeBursts = insertion sort of 8-16 ints. | ~2-4 us (7 subtractions + a 8-element insertion sort). No wall-clock cost, but it is the term that forces a full extra 10 ms tick when the window straddles contact. |
| 6. MEASURE-only: two-consecutive-burst agreement (`prevBurstAvg`) | `src/Probing.cpp:7501` | Only when switchPosition == 0. Static prevBurstAvg; if \|average - prev\| > 6 the decode returns -1 and the accept waits for the next call. The comment claims 'only adds ~1ms' — that was true when the decode did live conversions back-to-back; at the current 10 ms service cadence it is a full tick. | +1 Probing tick = **+10 ms** in measure position (the comment's ~1 ms is stale by an order of magnitude). |
| 7. Phantom-touch gate: blink the tip feed off and re-read | `src/Probing.cpp:7300` | BLOCKING on core 0. gpio_put(feed, false); delayMicroseconds(60); readAdc(5,8); gpio_put(feed, true); delayMicroseconds(30). readAdc on the ring is adcRingMeanAfter(5, now, 8, 600us) which spins until 8+1 fresh sweeps exist. Cached: skipped entirely if <250 ms since the last check AND within 5 counts of it. | 60 us + (9 sweeps × 20.833 us = 187.5 us) + 30 us = **277.5 us blocking**, and 247.5 us of that has the tip feed driven LOW. Cache hit → 0 us. |
| 8. Row-change confirmation: pendingNewRow + strictly-consecutive callSeq | `src/Probing.cpp:7710` | Polled, stateful. A row DIFFERENT from lastRowProbed is DISCARDED on first sight; it is only reported when the very NEXT justReadProbe call (callSeq == pendingNewRowSeq+1) decodes the same row. Any intervening call that returns -1 anywhere — variance fail, phantom reject, out-of-bounds — voids the pair. | +1 Probing tick = **+10 ms** on every genuine row change. The in-code comment says '~10ms', and that is correct — it is also the largest single addend in the whole path. |
| 9. 500 ms held-row cache (duplicate window) | `src/Probing.cpp:7678` | Once a row is latched in lastRowProbed, the SAME row is re-reported at most once per 500 ms; every decode in between returns -1. This is the contract every downstream consumer is built on. | A held tip produces one positive value per 500 ms, latched into the cache for exactly one 10 ms window. Duty cycle of 'probe reads positive' = 10/500 = **2%**. |
| 10. smoothProbeReading + map to row + bounds | `src/Probing.cpp:7621` | Snap if \|Δ\| > 8 counts, else 5:1 IIR ((old*5 + new + 3)/6). A fresh tap always snaps. Then map(raw, mapMin, mapMax, 101, 0) and a 108-element bound check. | < 1 us. In MEASURE, probeMapRange additionally does readAdcVoltage(7,16) every 2 ms = 17 sweeps × 20.833 us = **354 us of blocking spin** per tick (Probing.cpp:7050). In SELECT it is two config reads, free. |
| 11. Result cached in lastProbeReading; service returns BUSY | `src/Probing.cpp:1683` | Single-writer core-0 int. This cache is the ONLY interface to every downstream consumer (Highlighting, MeasureMode, PartLabels). It holds a positive value for exactly one 10 ms window. | ~0. But the 10 ms latch is what makes 'one positive cached reading IS the tap' the de-facto contract in three separate services. |
| 12. Highlighting::service reads the cache — SAME serviceAll pass | `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:158` | Registered at main.cpp:559, immediately after Probing (558), same HIGH priority, periodUs() == 0 → runs every pass. No queue, no cross-core hop: the pickup is in the same pass that produced the value. | ~20-100 us (whatever the intervening service dispatch costs). Effectively free. |
| 12a. MEASURE precedence: the whole label/bloom leg is short-circuited | `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:150` | `#if MEASUREMODE_TAKES_PRECEDENCE` (=1): when switchPosition==0 and a measurement is active, service() returns before touching highlightNets. Tap feedback in measure position comes from a completely different renderer (MeasureMode::updateVoltageDisplay). | 0 ms — but it means this trace describes SELECT position only; measure has its own sibling path with its own debounce. |
| 13. brightenNet: find the node's net, then post the LED request | `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:678` | Linear scan of globalState.connections.paths[0..numberOfPaths) (numberOfPaths ≤ MAX_BRIDGES = 128). On a hit: sets brightenedNet/brightenedNode/brightenedRail and calls requestLedShow(1). Fires on EVERY pass while the cached reading is positive, not on change. | ≤128 iterations × ~6 cycles at 150 MHz = **~5 us**. Posted once per core-0 pass for the full 10 ms cache window → at a ~100 us idle pass, **~100 posts per tap**, all coalescing into one frame. |
| 14. requestLedShow(1) → core-1 mailbox (REQ_SHOW_LEDS, LED_NETS) | `src/Commands.cpp:66` | Cross-core, async. postMode() under a hardware spin lock shared with core 1's take()/snapshot(). Last-write-wins on mode; clear/blocking flags coalesce; a MENU flush is not allowed to downgrade a pending NETS render. Returns a generation the caller may wait on — this caller does not. | ~1-2 us per post (spin lock round trip), × ~100 posts per tap window = ~150 us of contended lock traffic on core 0 per tap, plus the mirror contention on core 1. |
| 15. highlightNets print branch composes the label | `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:1476` | Guarded by `lastPrintedNet != netHighlighted \|\| printedRowChanged` — true exactly once per new tap. Resolves netSemanticName (walks placed parts × pins) or netDisplayName, then netScanVoltageValue + netCurrentValue (cache lookups), then showNetReading. | ~10-40 us (a parts walk of numParts × numPins, plus two snprintfs). |
| 16. ReadingDisplay::show — compose one line, dedupe, then BOTH outputs | `src/eyecandy/ReadingDisplay.cpp:220` | Blocking on core 0. Composes a ≤96-char line, compares against lastLine (identical content is dropped), then serial FIRST and OLED SECOND. This ordering is why a stalled terminal also stalls the panel. | compose ~5-10 us. |
| 17. Terminal line: pinned-row repaint + Serial.flush() | `src/eyecandy/ReadingDisplay.cpp:178` | BLOCKING. DECSC + CUU2 + EL2 + the line + DECRC, then Serial.flush(). If the pin is stale it first ships '\n\r\n\r' + Jerial.redrawInputLine(). Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write() spins `while (remain && tud_cdc_n_connected()) yield()` — it never gives up on a connected port. | ~40-120 bytes typical. FIFO has room → **20-60 us**. FIFO full → one 64-byte bulk IN per host transaction, ~1-2 ms for the line. Host connected but not draining → **unbounded spin** (see stalls). |
| 18. OLED label: font fit + glyph rasterization into the framebuffer | `src/oled.cpp:1556` | Blocking, core 0. Per segment: getTextBoundsWithFont (a full glyph walk). findBestFitPointSize walks 12→6 pt re-measuring the string each step — up to 7 measurement passes for the value row and again for a name-only row. Then Adafruit GFX per-pixel writes into the 512-byte framebuffer. | bounds ~50 us + best-fit search 7 passes × ~20-50 us = **100-400 us** + glyph raster ~2000-8000 pixel writes at ~15 ns = **40-160 us**. Total ~200-600 us. |
| 19. OLED flush: priorityFlushHeldFrame → Adafruit_SSD1306::display() | `src/oled.cpp:1663` | SYNCHRONOUS, fully blocking I2C from core 0. Bypasses the hold gate. kOledI2CClockHz = 400 000 (oled.cpp:72). WIRE_MAX resolves to 32 (arduino-pico defines neither BUFFER_LENGTH nor I2C_BUFFER_LENGTH nor SERIAL_BUFFER_SIZE) → 31 data bytes per transmission. | 128×32 panel = 128 × ((32+7)/8) = **512 data bytes**. 512/31 = 17 chunks; wire bytes = 512 data + 17×(1 addr + 1 control) = 546, plus ~12 command bytes = 558. At 9 bits/byte (8 + ACK): 558 × 9 = 5022 bits ÷ 400 000 bit/s = **12.55 ms**, + ~20 START/STOP at ~2.5 us = 12.6 ms. **This is the dominant blocking cost in the entire path, and it is longer than the 10 ms Probing period.** A 128×64 config doubles the payload → 1024 B → 33 chunks → **~24.8 ms**. |
| 20. Serial.flush() fall-through at the end of the default case | `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:1502` | Unlabeled statement in the switch body after `default:`'s block — reached by fall-through from default only (cases 0/2/3/4/5 break past it). Runs on EVERY pass where a plain net is highlighted, including the ~100 passes per tap window where every print guard skipped and nothing was written. | ~1-3 us when the TX FIFO is empty; ~100 calls per tap window ≈ 200 us of pure waste. |
| 21. Core 1 picks up the LED request — gated on the 8 ms scheduler tick | `src/main.cpp:1768` | Cross-core, polled. `if ( micros() - schedulerTimer > schedulerUpdateTime \|\| ledImmediate )`. schedulerUpdateTime = 8000 (main.cpp:1499). ledImmediate covers only LED_MENU and LED_GFX — **LED_NETS, which is what a probe highlight posts, always waits for the tick**. Also requires core_sync acquired with a 0 ms timeout (1722) and core1req::allIdle() on the two SEND slots (1774). | **0-8 ms, mean 4 ms.** Plus a whole-pass skip every time core 0 holds core_sync or a crossbar send is in flight. |
| 22. showNets() + the rest of the frame composition | `src/main.cpp:1853` | Core 1, core2busy raised. lightUpRail + logoSwirl, optional clearLEDsExceptRails, showNets() (assignNetColors + lightUpNet per net, or drawWires), rotaryEncoderStuff, readGPIO, readFakeGPIO, showLEDmeasurements, showAllRowAnimations, renderGraphicOverlays. | showNets ~0.5-3 ms (the file's own debug threshold flags >5 ms as slow, main.cpp:1856); the GPIO/measurement/animation/overlay tail adds ~0.5-2 ms. **~1-5 ms total.** |
| 23. ledClass::show() — may DROP the frame | `src/LEDs.cpp:174` | Non-blocking. Skips the TOP strip if either strip's DMA is busy, skips BB if BB's DMA is busy. A dropped frame leaves bbDirty/topDirty set — but core2stuff completes the taken request anyway (main.cpp:1976) and nothing re-posts. | 0 us when it fires; when it drops, the bloom waits for the next post or the 51 ms swirl re-render (main.cpp:2012). |
| 24. JeoPixel::rp2040Show — expand pixels and start DMA | `lib/Jadafruit_NeoPixel/Jeopixel_RP2.cpp:268` | Async DMA per strip, each on its own claimed PIO SM + DMA channel (so bb and top transfer CONCURRENTLY). The uint8→uint32 expansion loop runs with interrupts globally disabled. | IRQs-off expansion: bb 300×3 = 900 words × ~4 cycles at 150 MHz = **24 us**; top 145×3 = 435 words = **12 us**. Then returns immediately. |
| 25. WS2812 wire time — when photons actually reach the tapped row | `lib/Jadafruit_NeoPixel/JeoPixel.cpp:244` | PIO/DMA streaming at 800 kHz. The row's pixels latch when the chain reaches them, not when show() returns. | 1.25 us/bit × 24 bits = **30 us per pixel**. Breadboard chain = 300 px × 30 us = **9.0 ms** full frame + 300 us latch; top chain 145 × 30 us = 4.35 ms (concurrent). Row N sits at pixel ≈ (N-1)×5 of 300 → row 1 blooms at ~0.15 ms into the transfer, row 30 at ~4.4 ms, row 60 at ~8.9 ms. Wall time to a fully-latched frame: **9.3 ms**. |
| 26. Live value repaint (the label's numbers, not its name) | `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:1567` | checkForReadingChanges is gated to 40 ms (Highlighting.cpp:174) AND returns -1 without painting on the first pass after a net change (it only resets the latches). So the first live-value update is on the SECOND 40 ms pass. | 40-80 ms after the tap for the first value refresh — and each refresh whose composed string differs pays another **12.6 ms OLED flush**. |

**Bottlenecks:**
- 10 ms Probing::service sample gate paid TWICE (Probing.cpp:1664 + the pendingNewRow confirm at 7710) = 20 ms of quantized debounce before any feedback. This is the largest term in the whole path and it is pure scheduling, not physics: the ring decode itself costs ~6 us of core-0 time.
- 12.6 ms fully-blocking 400 kHz OLED I2C flush (oled.cpp:1663 -> Adafruit_SSD1306::display, 512 bytes / 17 WIRE_MAX chunks / 5022 bits). Longer than the Probing period, so a labelled tap always costs the next probe sample. Doubles to ~24.8 ms on a 128x64 config.
- 8 ms core-1 scheduler tick that LED_NETS cannot bypass (main.cpp:1768). ledImmediate is LED_MENU|LED_GFX only; a probe highlight — the most latency-sensitive LED request in the system — is the one that waits.
- WS2812 wire time: 30 us/pixel x 300 breadboard pixels = 9.0 ms per full frame + 300 us latch. The tapped row's photons arrive at (row-1)*5*30 us into the transfer, so a row-60 tap blooms 8.9 ms after a row-1 tap of the same frame.
- The ring decode's backward-looking history window (Probing.cpp:7374): 128 sweeps = 2.667 ms in select, 192 = 4.0 ms in measure. Any decode whose window straddles the contact edge fails the variance gate, which converts a 2.7 ms analog fact into a 10 ms scheduling penalty.
- Font best-fit search (ReadingDisplay.cpp:288, FontManager::findBestFitPointSize) re-measures the string at up to 7 point sizes on every repaint, including the 40 ms live-value refreshes: 100-400 us each, uncached.

**Stalls / races:**
- DROPPED STRIP FRAME, SILENTLY COMPLETED. LEDs.cpp:184/193 skips both strips when bbleds.isDMABusy() (a 9.3 ms window), but main.cpp:1976 calls core1req::complete() on the taken request regardless and nothing re-posts. The requested bloom never reaches the strip; recovery is the next post or the 51 ms swirl re-render at main.cpp:2012. Worst-case bloom gap ~51 ms + compose + wire.
- STRICTLY-CONSECUTIVE CONFIRM IS STARVABLE. Probing.cpp:7712 requires callSeq == pendingNewRowSeq + 1, and callSeq is bumped at 7636 by EVERY justReadProbe caller. Six other callers exist: LEDs.cpp:1160 (color picker), Peripherals.cpp:3451 (voltage adjuster), Menus.cpp:3031, PartsApp.cpp:280, JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2636 (jl.probe_read), Apps.cpp:1500. Any of them interleaving with the ambient service voids the pending row change indefinitely — the tap never registers, not merely late.
- checkPads STOMPS readProbeRaw's SHARED STATICS. ProbePads (50 ms, Probing.cpp:1614) runs checkPads, which calls readProbeRaw 12 times (Probing.cpp:7091) and each accept writes lastReadRaw, the phantom cache (lastCheckedValue/lastCheckedMs), and in measure position prevBurstAvg. It also holds checkingPads = 1 for ~4 ms, during which readProbeRaw bypasses the 'changed by >5' accept test entirely and forces burstSamples to 16 even in measure. Which of the two paths latched lastReadRaw last decides whether the NEXT service tick sees a change.
- PHANTOM GATE'S 250 ms STALE VERDICT (the code's own documented ceiling, Probing.cpp:7289-7296): a phantom landing within 5 counts of a just-verified reading rides the cached 'not phantom' verdict for up to 250 ms. Symmetrically, a real tap within 5 counts of the last verified value skips the blink and is accepted unverified.
- PHANTOM GATE DRIVES THE TIP FEED LOW FOR 247.5 us INSIDE THE DECODE (Probing.cpp:7300-7307). Any other ADC5/ADC7 consumer sampling in that window reads a false dark floor. USB audio has an explicit yield hook at 7458; updateLazyAdcReadings (core 1, main.cpp:2062) and serviceNetVoltageScan (main.cpp:2083) do not.
- UNBOUNDED SPIN ON A CONNECTED-BUT-NOT-DRAINING HOST. ReadingDisplay.cpp:178 calls Serial.print + Serial.flush with no availableForWrite guard; Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write() loops `while (remain && tud_cdc_n_connected(_instance)) { ... yield(); }` and never gives up on a connected port. Because the serial line is emitted BEFORE the OLED write (ReadingDisplay.cpp:266/337), a stalled terminal wedges the panel too.
- OLED WRITE SILENTLY DEFERRED FOREVER WHILE WAVEGEN STREAMS. oled.cpp priorityFlushHeldFrame sets oledNeedsFlushAfterWavegen and returns when _currentDisplayWire == 0 and wavegen.isRunning(). The LED bloom lands normally; the label just never appears until streaming stops. A real bloom/label divergence with no user-visible explanation.
- A ROW WITH NO PATH GETS NEITHER BLOOM NOR LABEL. brightenNet (Highlighting.cpp:676-720) scans globalState.connections.paths[] and returns -1 for a node in no path (only GND/TOP_RAIL/BOTTOM_RAIL have explicit fallbacks). highlightNets then returns without posting requestLedShow and without calling showNetReading. Tapping a bare, unbridged row is silent on both legs.
- OPERATOR PRECEDENCE BUG IN brightenNet's PATH MATCH. Highlighting.cpp:678: `if ( node == p.node1 || node == p.node2 && p.duplicate == 0 )` — && binds tighter than ||, so the duplicate==0 guard applies only to the node2 arm. A duplicate path whose node1 matches wins and can resolve the tap to the wrong net.
- requestLedShow POSTED ~100x PER TAP. brightenNet posts unconditionally (Highlighting.cpp:717) and Highlighting::service has periodUs 0, so for the whole 10 ms window the cached reading is positive it posts once per core-0 pass. Each post is a spin-lock round trip (Commands.cpp:98 -> postMode) contended with core 1's take()/snapshot(). They coalesce into one frame, so the cost is pure lock traffic — ~150 us on core 0 plus the mirror stall on core 1.
- Serial.flush() AT Highlighting.cpp:1502 RUNS WHEN NOTHING WAS PRINTED. It is an unlabeled statement in the switch body reached by fall-through from `default:` only, so it fires on every plain-net highlight pass — including the ~100 passes per tap window where the lastPrintedNet guard skipped every print.
- CORE-1 LED PASS SKIPPED SILENTLY ON A 0 ms core_sync ACQUIRE (main.cpp:1722) and again on core1req::allIdle() over the SEND slots (main.cpp:1774). Each miss costs another full 8 ms tick. There is no counter for these the way ledFrameAbortsPause counts frame-hold aborts.
- THE 'ONE TAP' CONTRACT IS ACCIDENTAL. MeasureMode.cpp:110-114 documents it in the code: its stableReadingCount threshold 'only ever reached 5 because this service happens to be periodUs=0 and samples the 10 ms cache window many times per pass. Any scheduling change would have silently killed measure-mode taps.' It is now >= 1. Highlighting and PartLabels consume the same cache with two more different rules. Changing any service's period changes tap detection in three places.

**Quick wins:**
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Probing.cpp:7710` Confirm the row change in place instead of waiting a full service tick. In justReadProbe's pendingNewRow branch, when a new row is pending, immediately take a SECOND decode from the ring's older history (the ring holds 10.67 ms; read the previous 128-sweep window) and require agreement, rather than returning -1 and waiting 10 ms for the next Probing tick. Cuts a flat 10 ms off every genuine row change — the single largest addend in the path. Also makes the confirm immune to the callSeq-starvation race, since the pair no longer spans two calls.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/Probing.cpp:1664` Drop the Probing sample gate from 10 ms to 5 ms. The gate is a leftover from when the decode cost ~1.2-2 ms of live ADC conversions; on the ring it is ~6 us of memory reads (Probing.cpp:7374 and its own comment). Halving it halves both the sample quantization AND the confirm delay: -5 ms typical, -10 ms combined. Safe in SELECT (probeMapRange is free there); in MEASURE it doubles the rate of probeMapRange's 354 us ADC7 wait and the 277.5 us phantom blink, so bench that side.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/LEDs.cpp:193` Re-post a dropped strip frame instead of completing it into the void. core2stuff completes the taken REQ_SHOW_LEDS unconditionally at main.cpp:1976 even when ledClass::show() skipped both strips because bbleds DMA was busy. Mirror the frame-hold abort's existing pattern (main.cpp:1843-1846): if bbDirty or topDirty are still set after leds.show(), core1req::post(REQ_SHOW_LEDS, taken) before completing. The dirty flags already exist and are already maintained. Kills the ~51 ms worst-case bloom gap.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/main.cpp:1762` Let a probe-highlight LED_NETS request bypass the 8 ms core-1 scheduler tick on its LEADING EDGE only. ledImmediate at main.cpp:1762 is LED_MENU|LED_GFX; add a LED_USER_INPUT flag that brightenNet sets when brightenedNet/brightenedNode actually CHANGED, and OR it into ledImmediate. Removes 0-8 ms (mean 4 ms) from the bloom. Leading-edge-only matters: making every NETS post immediate would let the ~100 posts per tap window each try to render.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/oled.cpp:72` Raise the OLED transfer clock from 400 kHz to 1 MHz. kOledI2CClockHz is set per-transfer and restored (oled.cpp:96-101), so this does not disturb the DAC/INA219 bus clock. 5022 bits / 1 000 000 = 5.0 ms instead of 12.6 ms — a 7.6 ms cut on the label leg AND 7.6 ms less core-0 blocking per repaint, which also stops the flush from being longer than the Probing period. Panel- and wiring-dependent (SSD1306 controllers are commonly good past 1 MHz but the cable is the variable), so bench with a scope on SCL.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:1502` Move Serial.flush() inside the print guard. It currently sits as a fall-through statement after the default case (reachable from `default:` only) and fires on every plain-net highlight pass — ~100 times per tap window — even when the lastPrintedNet guard skipped every write. Nothing was written on those passes, so flushing is provably a no-op with a cost.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:717` Post requestLedShow from brightenNet only when brightenedNet or brightenedNode actually changed. Today it posts unconditionally at every call, and Highlighting::service has periodUs 0, so a positive cached reading produces ~100 mailbox posts (each a contended spin-lock round trip against core 1's take/snapshot) that all coalesce into one frame. Guard on the previous values. Pair this with the dropped-frame re-post above — the current spam is partly what masks a dropped frame.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/eyecandy/ReadingDisplay.cpp:178` Bound the CDC write in emitLiveSerialLine. Check Serial.availableForWrite() >= strlen(line) + escape overhead before printing and skip this frame's serial repaint (the value is live; it repaints next pass) rather than letting Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write() spin in yield() behind a connected-but-stalled host. Because the serial line is emitted before the OLED write in ReadingDisplay::show, this spin currently also blocks the panel.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/eyecandy/ReadingDisplay.cpp:288` Cache the font-fit result. findBestFitPointSize walks 12 -> 6 pt re-measuring the string at each size (up to 7 full glyph walks) and runs on every repaint, including the 40 ms live-value refreshes where only the digits changed. Key a 2-4 entry cache on (family, strlen, first char, last char) or just memcmp the exact string. Saves 100-400 us per repaint.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:678` Fix the ||/&& precedence in brightenNet's path match: `node == p.node1 || node == p.node2 && p.duplicate == 0` should be `(node == p.node1 || node == p.node2) && p.duplicate == 0`.
