Not a latency win — a correctness defect on the flagship path, where a duplicate path matching on node1 resolves the tap to the wrong net. Wants a hardware check precisely because it changes which net a node resolves to.
- [design-change/behavior-preserving] `src/oled.cpp:1663` Stop repainting the whole panel for a value change. checkForReadingChanges' second-pass repaint (40-80 ms after the tap) pays a full 12.6 ms flush for what is usually a few changed digits. SSD1306 supports a windowed PAGEADDR/COLUMNADDR write; flushing only the dirty value row (8-16 of 32 rows) cuts that repaint to 3-6 ms. Bigger change than the others — it needs a dirty-rect notion in oled.cpp that does not exist yet.

**Unification notes:** NOTE ON THE PROMPT'S PREMISE: there is no "stable-count debounce >= 25" anywhere in the current row-decode. `git log -S stableCount -- src/Probing.cpp` is empty and no such constant exists. What actually debounces the row decode is four separate mechanisms: smoothProbeReading's 5:1 IIR with an 8-count snap threshold (Probing.cpp:7619-7625), the maxVariance <= 6 gate across 8-16 burst windows (7442-7448, 7491), the MEASURE-only two-consecutive-burst agreement on prevBurstAvg (7501-7507), and the strictly-consecutive pendingNewRow confirm (7710-7717). The 500 ms held-row cache (7678) is real and is exactly where the prompt says it is. MeasureMode.cpp:110-114 documents the closest thing to a "stable count": a threshold of 5 that was reduced to >= 1 once someone noticed it "only ever reached 5 because this service happens to be periodUs=0".

FIVE INDEPENDENT TAP/DEBOUNCE IMPLEMENTATIONS OVER ONE CACHED INT. Every one of them reads probing.getLastProbeReading() (Probing.h:392), a value that is positive for exactly one 10 ms window per 500 ms, and each invents its own idea of what constitutes a tap:
  - justReadProbe: 500 ms duplicate window + strictly-consecutive pendingNewRow confirm (Probing.cpp:7674-7728) — the authoritative one.
  - MeasureMode::service: stableReadingCount, threshold >= 1, decremented on gaps (MeasureMode.cpp:88-117).
  - PartLabels::listenForInspectTap: lastInspectNode + LBL_LIFT_MS 700 + LBL_SWITCH_DEBOUNCE_MS 300 (PartLabels.cpp:146-200) — its comment explicitly says a wait-for-N-stable-reads scheme "can never reach its threshold" on this cache, which is a bug report about its two siblings.
  - Highlighting::service: no debounce at all — one positive reading is a tap (Highlighting.cpp:161).
  - checkPads: samePadCount + lastPadTouchedTime (Probing.cpp:7124-7130), computed and then never read.
  Plus ProbeButton::processSample's release-debounce state machine (Probing.cpp:296+), the button-side sibling of the same problem.
The natural unification is a single ProbeTap event source that owns the debounce contract and emits {node, edge} once, with the four consumers subscribing instead of each re-deriving it from a shared int.

TWO INDEPENDENT "READING -> PANEL + TERMINAL" RENDERERS, FIGHTING FOR THE SAME PINNED ROWS. Highlighting::highlightNets/checkForReadingChanges and MeasureMode::updateVoltageDisplay both funnel into ReadingDisplay::show, and MeasureMode.cpp:176-186 documents the collision in prose: "Highlighting is registered immediately before this service at the same priority, so in the SAME pass it pins a live reading and we wipe it — clearLiveSerialLine() keys off the shared pinReserved flag and has no idea who owns the row." The fix that landed was to make the teardown edge-triggered; the underlying issue (no ownership token on the pinned rows) is still open.

THREE INDEPENDENT REDRAW SCHEDULERS ON CORE 1, all in core2stuff: the 8 ms schedulerUpdateTime tick (main.cpp:1768), the 51 ms lastSwirlTime swirl (main.cpp:2012), and menuTransitionFrameDue() (main.cpp:1747). Only the last can bypass the first. requestLedShow's packed mode/flag vocabulary (Commands.cpp:66-98) is the sole common interface, and its own FOLLOW-UP note asks for exactly this consolidation — CoreMailbox got it half done (the packed int is now a typed slot) but the three cadences were never merged.

THREE OVERLAPPING "CORE 1 MUST NOT RENDER NOW" FLAGS: core1FramesHeld()/holdCore1Frames(), core2busy, and the core_sync mutex — checked in different combinations at main.cpp:1544, 1584, 1619, 1636, 1722, 1735, 1836, 1907, and waited on differently by waitCore2() (Commands.cpp:131). The LED path in this trace touches all three, and the 0 ms acquire at 1722 is the one that silently costs an 8 ms tick with no counter.

FOUR OVERLAPPING CLEAR MECHANISMS for the strip, flagged in LEDs.cpp's own FOLLOW-UP at line 167-173: clearBeforeSend, clearLEDsExceptRails, hideNets, leds.clear, bread::clear — plus the per-strip bbDirty/topDirty flags that are written on every setPixelColor and then ignored (the `if (bbDirty)` guards are commented out at LEDs.cpp:191). Re-enabling those flags is what quick-win #3 depends on, and would also let an idle frame cost 0 ms instead of 9.3 ms of wire time.

TWO PLACES DECIDE "which net is this node in": brightenNet's linear paths[] scan (Highlighting.cpp:676-720, with the precedence bug) and findNodeInNet / validNetForNode (PartLabels.cpp:133-140, NetManager). They can disagree, and PartLabels already carries a guard comment about findNodeInNet's gpio/adc fallbacks returning NODE values rather than net numbers.

## Clickwheel TURN → visible response (main menu getMenuSelection + PartsApp::partsPicker)

**Total: Terminology: core 1 = loop1 = the sole encoder owner (the code's comments say "Core 2"; it gates on sio_hw->cpuid & 1).

BEST (mid-spin detent, gate not armed, nothing contending) — MAIN MENU:
  2.0 (mean autopush word) + 0.25 (poll) + 0.001 (decode) + 0 (no confirm gate mid-spin) + 0.27 (core-0 poll) + 0.1 (Serial) = terminal at ≈2.6 ms.
  OLED at 2.6 + 12.8 = ≈15.4 ms. Breadboard at 15.4 + 0.5 (paint) + 0.2 (core-1 pickup) + 0.03–9.0 (strip position) = ≈16–25 ms.

TYPICAL (first detent of a turn, or ANY detent below 6.7 detents/s — i.e. normal deliberate use) — MAIN MENU:
  add STEP_CONFIRM_US = 35 ms → terminal ≈37.6 ms, OLED ≈50.4 ms, breadboard ≈51–60 ms.
  DOMINANT TERMS: the 35 ms confirm gate (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1175) plus the 12.8 ms blocking SSD1306 frame on core 0 (Menus.cpp:1205). Together they are 48 of the ~50 ms; every other hop in the chain sums to under 2 ms.

APP (PartsApp/ProjectsApp picker), typical first detent: breadboard ≈38–47 ms, OLED ≈51 ms, terminal ≈51 ms — same 35+12.8 dominance, but the breadboard leads instead of trailing because the LED request is posted before the OLED block.

WORST REALISTIC (first detent + full 4 ms autopush word + slow OLED + dropped LED frame):
  4.0 + 0.5 + 35 + 0.53 + 0.1 + 12.8 + 0.8 + 16 (MT_FRAME_MS retry) + 9.0 (strip) ≈ 79 ms.

QUEUED-SPIN behaviour (this is what "lagging behind fast spins" actually is): each detent costs one full redraw, so the sustained drain rate is 1 detent per (12.8 OLED + 0.8 paint + 0.5 loop) ≈ 14.1 ms = 71 detents/s, against the driver's own hard-flick figure of ~80 detents/s (RotaryEncoder.cpp:77). A 20-detent flick delivered in 250 ms therefore keeps scrolling for 20 × 14.1 = 282 ms after the hand stops. The app pickers are the same 14.1 ms.

PATHOLOGICAL: a turn taken while core 1 sits in the flash-park wait (main.cpp:1544, up to ~1.1 s for a slot save) is not dropped by the ring (2.05 s of depth) but IS collapsed on resume: the confirm gate re-arms (+35 ms) and then the >4-detents-per-poll clamp (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1249) commits ONE step and swallows the rest. A full second of spinning becomes a single menu item.**

| stage | where | mechanism | latency |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Detent → quadrature edges (hardware) | `src/hardwarestuff/encoder_sampler.pio:16` | Free-running 1-instruction PIO SM (`in pins, 2`) on GPIO12/13 (PIN_AB, RotaryEncoder.cpp:31). NOT an ISR, no CPU in the data path at all. One physical detent = 8 raw quadrature counts (rotaryDivider, RotaryEncoder.cpp:382; RotaryEncoder.h:346). | 0 — pure hardware. Edge rate for the code's own 'hard flick' figure (RotaryEncoder.cpp:77): 80 detents/s × 8 counts/detent = 640 counts/s, i.e. 1.56 ms between counts at the fastest human spin. |
| 2. PIO sample → SRAM ring (first moment the CPU can see it) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:199` | encoder_sampler_program_init at ENC_SAMPLE_HZ = 4000 (:77). sm_config_set_in_shift autopushes at 32 bits = 16 two-bit AB samples per FIFO word. A TRIGGER_SELF DMA channel (:211) self-rearms forever into a 512-word write-ring — no IRQ, no CPU. encSamplerDrain (:108) derives `pending` from the live dma_hw write_addr, so only words STRICTLY behind it are readable: the newest 1–16 samples are structurally invisible. | 1/4000 Hz = 250 µs per sample × 16 samples per autopushed word = 4.000 ms per word. A given edge is therefore 0–4.0 ms old before any poll can see it; mean 2.0 ms. Ring depth = 512 words × 16 samples ÷ 4000 Hz = 2.05 s before an overrun loses counts. |
| 3. Core-1 poll entry (the SOLE writer of all encoder state) | `src/main.cpp:1688` | POLLED, never an ISR. rotaryEncoderStuff() is the first statement of core2stuff(); it is also called at main.cpp:1864 (mid-pass, after showNets), :1997 (menu tail) and :2035 (idle else-branch). RotaryEncoder.cpp:996 hard-returns when (sio_hw->cpuid & 1) == 0 — so EVERY Core-0 call site (Menus.cpp:1001, PartsApp.cpp:107/:121, ProjectsApp.cpp:723, waitForButtonRest) is a NO-OP that only reads a register and returns. TERMINOLOGY: this file's banner calls the owner 'Core 2' (RotaryEncoder.h:47); the code means cpuid 1 = loop1. Throughout this trace, core 1 = loop1 = the encoder owner, core 0 = loop()/menus/apps. | Self-throttled to ENCODER_POLL_INTERVAL_US = 500 µs (RotaryEncoder.cpp:978) = 2 kHz ceiling. Effective cadence = max(500 µs, loop1 period); an idle loop1 pass measures ~30–150 µs of work, so the 500 µs throttle governs. Poll-entry latency 0–500 µs, mean 250 µs. |
| 4. Ring drain + quadrature LUT decode | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:111` | Blocking inline loop on core 1; walks every 2-bit sample through s_quadLUT (:94) into s_cpuQuadCount. Bounded only by ring occupancy — there is no per-poll cap. | At a 2 kHz poll against a 4 kHz sample rate, ≤1 word arrives per 8 polls: 16 LUT steps × ~6 cycles ÷ 150 MHz = 0.64 µs. Full-ring worst case (only reachable after a >2 s core-1 stall): 512 words × 16 = 8192 steps × 6 cyc ÷ 150 MHz = 328 µs in one poll. |
| 5. RP2350-E9 pad-unlatch blip (inline hard spin inside the poll) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1071` | Blocking busy_wait_us_32 inside the poll body, self-gated to 200 Hz. Drops and reasserts the pad input-enable on GPIO12, then GPIO13, one at a time. | 4 × busy_wait_us_32(2) = 8 µs, once per 5000 µs = 0.16 % duty. Adds 8 µs to one poll in every ten (5000 µs ÷ 500 µs). |
| 6. Click/rotation interlock — AFTER window (rotation deafness) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1160` | Blocking gate: the poll RETURNS EARLY, pinning encoderPosition to heldEncoderPos, sliding lastDetentRaw under the shaft, zeroing numberOfSteps and suppressing all direction events. Re-anchored to now() on every poll while the button is physically down (:1154), so it also covers the release jiggle of a long hold. | CLICK_SUPPRESS_AFTER_US = 100 000 µs = 100 ms of total rotation deafness after every press edge, and for 100 ms past the release of any hold. Not a delay — the motion inside the window is discarded, not queued. |
| 7. Click/rotation interlock — first-detent CONFIRM gate (the largest fixed adder) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1175` | Blocking gate: raw counts keep accumulating but encoderPosition and encoderDirectionState stay frozen and the poll returns, so nothing is drawn. Armed at :1118 the moment rawCount changes after a stretch of raw stillness. On expiry the arm-time offset is restored (:1194) and the whole window's travel flushes through the detent block in one commit. | STEP_CONFIRM_US = 35 000 µs = 35 ms added to the first detent out of rest. ENCODER_REARM_IDLE_US = 150 000 µs = 150 ms, so at turn rates below 1 ÷ 0.150 s = 6.7 detents/s the gate re-arms for EVERY detent: +35 ms on every step of a slow deliberate turn. The code states this outcome itself at :1190. |
| 8. Detent hysteresis + backlash / recoil / glitch-storm guards | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1205` | Pure arithmetic on the already-drained count; non-blocking. Thresholds, not delays. | ~1 µs of compute. Forward step threshold = rotaryDivider = 8 counts; a reversal needs 8 + 8/2 = 12 counts (:1219). Recoil guard (:1228) disbelieves reversals outright for RECOIL_GUARD_US = 200 ms after any run of commits closer than RECOIL_ARM_GAP_US = 100 ms apart — so a deliberate direction change during a fast spin waits up to 200 ms. Glitch clamp (:1249): >4 detents inside one 500 µs poll collapses to ±1 and rebases the baseline. |
| 9. Paced event emission (one detent per consumer ack) | `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1290` | Lock-free cross-core handshake over two volatiles. Emits exactly one UP/DOWN and holds the rest back; the next only goes out when encoderDirectionState is NONE again. Backlog depth = encoderRaw − lastPositionEncoder, drained one per poll. | Driver-side ceiling = 1 event per 500 µs poll = 2000 detents/s (never the limit). The real ceiling is the consumer's redraw period — hops 12–14 for the menu, hop 16 for the app. Abandon path: DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US = 250 000 µs; an emitted event left unconsumed for 250 ms drops itself AND the entire backlog (:1313). |
| 10. MENU consume — getMenuSelection poll (core 0) | `src/Menus.cpp:1103` | Core-0 modal loop `while (Serial.available() == 0)` (:999). Polled, non-blocking read of encoderDirectionState; acked by writing NONE (:1105 for UP, :1244 for DOWN). Per-pass body = jOS.serviceInner() (:1000) + delayMicroseconds(400) (:1046) + one ProbeButton::getButtonPress() (:1053) + delayMicroseconds(80) (:1638). | serviceInner walks 14 registered services and runs the 7 inner-set ones that are due (tinyUSB every pass, mpRemote, asyncPassthrough, probeButton, peripherals @10 ms, displayService @2 ms, ledDump @10 ms) ≈ 10–40 µs idle. 400 µs + 80 µs of hard delay + ~10 µs probe read = ~490–530 µs per idle pass. Detection latency 0–530 µs, mean 265 µs. |
| 11. MENU redraw 1/3 — terminal (fastest surface) | `src/Menus.cpp:1184` | Blocking. 4–12 Serial.print calls + 2 Serial.flush. Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write (Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:246) spins `while (remain && tud_cdc_n_connected())` calling yield() → tud_task(); flush() is only tud_cdc_n_write_flush(), which is non-blocking. | ~60 bytes memcpy into the 256-byte CDC TX FIFO ≈ 5 µs, plus two String::replace() heap ops ≈ 20–60 µs = ~0.1 ms of CPU. Host-visible on the next USB SOF frame: ≤1 ms. UNBOUNDED if the host holds the port open but stops draining. |
| 12. MENU redraw 2/3 — OLED I2C (DOMINANT TERM) | `src/Menus.cpp:1205` | BLOCKING I2C on core 0, no DMA, no async path, no content dedupe. clearPrintShow (oled.cpp:1287) runs a font auto-fit ladder, then show() (:1496) → Adafruit_SSD1306::display() (:2314), then a post-write liveness ping. | 128×32 panel (config.h:390-391) → count = 128 × ((32+7)/8) = 512 framebuffer bytes. WIRE_MAX = 32 (Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:71 fallback — arduino-pico defines none of BUFFER_LENGTH / I2C_BUFFER_LENGTH / SERIAL_BUFFER_SIZE), so 31 data bytes per transaction → ceil(512/31) = 17 transactions. Bytes on the wire = 512 data + 17 control(0x40) + 17 address = 546. 546 × 9 bit-times (8 data + 1 ACK) = 4914 bit-times ÷ 400 000 Hz (kOledI2CClockHz, oled.cpp:72) = 12.29 ms. Plus PAGEADDR/COLUMNADDR command list ≈ 3 txns × 5 B × 22.5 µs = 0.34 ms, post-write ping (:2342) ≈ 30 µs, font ladder (up to 7 setFontPointSize+getTextBounds passes, :1395-1456) ≈ 0.1–0.5 ms → ≈12.8 ms typical, entirely blocking core 0. Dead/NACKing panel: Wire.setTimeout(3, true) (oled.cpp:702/704) × ~20 txns = ~60 ms per frame, and 3 consecutive failures (~180 ms) before oledConnected flips (:2350). |
| 13. MENU redraw 3/3 — breadboard buffer paint (core 0, inside the no-show bracket) | `src/Menus.cpp:1231` | Blocking, core 0. renderMenuLine (:894): updateMenuLogoRing → menuTransitionBeginDraw (MenuTransitions.cpp:160) → b.clear / b.print / printMenuReminder → menuTransitionArm (:189) → requestLedShow(2). mtDrawing is TRUE for the whole bracket and core 1 refuses to show anything while it is (MenuTransitions.cpp:274-279), bounded by MT_DRAW_TIMEOUT_MS = 300 ms. | beginDraw spins on mtRenderBusy bounded at 3 ms (:168), normally ~0; then 300 leds.setPixelColor to restore targetFrame ≈ 300 × ~12 cyc ÷ 150 MHz = 24 µs. b.clear + b.print glyph raster ≈ 100–300 µs. arm(): 300 lerps + 300 getPixelColor + mtBuildAccentFrame (RgbToHsv + HsvToRaw per LIT pixel; a 7-char row ≈ 60–150 lit pixels × ~250 cyc ÷ 150 MHz) ≈ 100–250 µs. Total ≈ 0.3–0.8 ms. |
| 14. Core-1 LED render + WS2812 DMA (breadboard becomes visible) | `src/main.cpp:1917` | requestLedShow(2) sets the LED_MENU bit → ledImmediate (main.cpp:1762), so the branch runs on the NEXT core2stuff pass with no 8 ms scheduler wait. menuTransitionRender (default type MENU_TRANSITION_GLOW, MenuTransitions.h:46 — the new frame lands whole, only a flourish overlays) → leds.show() (:1956). ledClass::show (LEDs.cpp:174) DROPS the frame outright if the strip DMA is busy (:184/:193); otherwise JeoPixel::show spins `while (!canShow())` then fills the DMA buffer with interrupts disabled and returns at transfer START. | request → next loop1 pass = 30–150 µs. lightUpRail + logoSwirl still run for rails==2 (main.cpp:1789-1794) = 50–150 µs. menuTransitionRender over 300 px = 30–60 µs. canShow spin = DMA idle + TX FIFO empty + 300 µs latch (JeoPixel.h:254-278) = 0–400 µs. DMA buffer fill = (900 + 435) words × ~4 cyc ÷ 150 MHz = 36 µs. WS2812 stream: 300 px × 24 bits × 1.25 µs = 9.00 ms + 300 µs latch = 9.30 ms; the pixel at strip index k lights at k × 24 × 1.25 µs = k × 30 µs. A dropped frame is retried only by menuTransitionFrameDue at MT_FRAME_MS = 16 ms (MenuTransitions.cpp:55) inside the MT_LINGER_MS = 50 ms linger → +16 ms. |
| 15. APP consume — PartsApp::partsPicker poll (core 0) | `src/PartsApp.cpp:136` | Same handshake, different loop shape: `while (true)` at :99 with the DRAW at the top (:100) and the CONSUME at the bottom (:136/:140), so every accepted detent pays one extra trip through the delay before it is drawn. rotaryEncoderButtonStuff() at :107 is a core-0 no-op. Self-keepalive requestLedShow(2) every 250 ms (:111) because core 1's end-of-frame CAS can swallow a mid-frame request. | delayMicroseconds(1000) (:145) + serviceInner 10–40 µs ≈ 1.04 ms per idle pass — 2× the menu's poll. Detection 0–1.04 ms, mean 0.52 ms, plus ~1.04 ms more before the draw at the top of the next pass runs. ProjectsApp::runPicker (ProjectsApp.cpp:752/:759) is byte-for-byte the same shape and the same 1.04 ms. |
| 16. APP redraw — surface ordering INVERTED vs the menu | `src/PartsApp.cpp:47` | partsDrawItem: b.clear/b.print then requestLedShow(2) FIRST (:62), THEN the blocking OLED write (:68), THEN Serial + flush (:71-76). No menuTransitionBeginDraw/Arm bracket at all, so core 1's rails==2 branch can stream a half-painted buffer while inClickMenu == 1. | LED request lands ~0.2 ms into the redraw → breadboard visible 0.3–9.3 ms later (plus 16 ms if the frame is dropped). oled.showMultiLineSmallText ≈ 12.8 ms (identical 512-byte frame arithmetic to hop 12). Terminal LAST, at ~13 ms. Net per detent: breadboard ≈ 3.5–12 ms, OLED ≈ 15.5 ms, terminal ≈ 15.7 ms — the exact opposite ordering to the main menu, where the terminal leads and the breadboard trails the OLED by the full 12.8 ms. |

**Bottlenecks:**
- OLED (12.8 ms, per detent, blocking core 0) — src/Menus.cpp:1205 → oled.cpp:2314. 512 framebuffer bytes at WIRE_MAX=32 → 17 I2C transactions → 546 wire bytes × 9 bit-times ÷ 400 kHz = 12.29 ms, plus command list and font ladder. There is no DMA path, no async path, and no skip-if-unchanged on this route. This single call is ~90 % of every redraw and it sets the 71 detents/s ceiling on fast spins.
- Confirm gate (35 ms, per detent below 6.7 detents/s) — src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1175. STEP_CONFIRM_US holds the first detent out of a 150 ms rest completely invisible. Because ENCODER_REARM_IDLE_US is 150 ms, deliberate turning re-arms it every step, so the fixed 35 ms is paid on essentially every detent a careful user makes.
- WS2812 strip frame (9.30 ms) + 16 ms drop-retry — src/main.cpp:1956 → LEDs.cpp:174. 300 px × 24 bits × 1.25 µs = 9.00 ms + 300 µs latch. ledClass::show DROPS rather than queues while the DMA is busy, and recovery is only the 16 ms MT_FRAME_MS retry inside a 50 ms linger (menus) or the 250 ms keepalive (apps).
- PIO autopush granularity (0–4.0 ms, mean 2.0 ms) — src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:77 + encoder_sampler.pio:16. 4 kHz sampling × 16 samples per 32-bit autopush = 4.000 ms per readable word, and the drain deliberately reads only whole words behind the DMA write pointer. Irreducible without changing the autopush threshold or the sample rate.
- Core-0 poll floor — 490–530 µs in the menu (delayMicroseconds(400) at Menus.cpp:1046 + 80 at :1638) and 1.04 ms in the apps (delayMicroseconds(1000) at PartsApp.cpp:145 / ProjectsApp.cpp:759). Small next to the OLED, but it is pure dead time and the app value is double the menu's for no stated reason.
- Core-1 poll throttle 500 µs (RotaryEncoder.cpp:978) — fine in isolation, but it is the drain rate for the paced backlog, so it compounds with any core-0 stall rather than absorbing it.

**Stalls / races:**
- WEDGE + DROP — core-0 CDC write can block unboundedly. Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write (Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:246) spins `while (remain && tud_cdc_n_connected())` calling yield()→tud_task(). A host that holds the port open but stops draining (a paused terminal, a scrolled-back screen session, a blocked jumperless client on port 1) freezes the menu redraw at Menus.cpp:1184-1227 — and once that exceeds 250 ms, DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US throws away the emitted event and the whole backlog. One fault produces both a wedge and silent detent loss.
- DROP — DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US (250 ms) discards the entire backlog, not just the stale event (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1309-1313). Any consumer that takes >250 ms on one item loses everything queued behind it. Reachable via: a NACKing OLED (~60 ms/frame × 4 frames before oledConnected flips), a stalled CDC write, doMenuAction entering a file/flash operation, or an app doing blocking work between polls. The check is unconditional — it cannot tell 'no UI is listening' (its stated purpose) from 'the UI is busy'.
- DROP — a turn during a core-1 flash park is collapsed to one detent. loop1's `while (core1FramesHeld())` (main.cpp:1544) stops the encoder poll entirely for up to ~1.1 s (the slot save, per the ring-sizing comment at RotaryEncoder.cpp:82). The 2.05 s ring preserves the raw counts, but on resume the confirm gate arms (+35 ms) and then the >4-detents-per-poll glitch clamp (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1249-1252) commits ±1 and rebases. The clamp exists for dying-encoder noise bursts; it cannot distinguish those from a legitimate spin that the firmware simply refused to watch.
- STALL + STALE SURFACE — wavegen. wavegen.service() contains a blocking I2C streaming loop on core 1 (main.cpp:1602-1608's own comment) and runs BEFORE core2stuff(), so the encoder poll cadence stretches while the function generator streams. Simultaneously, when the OLED is on connection_type 2 (shared I2C0), oled::show() bails out entirely (oled.cpp:2297-2302, setting oledNeedsFlushAfterWavegen) — the redraw gets cheaper but the panel goes stale until oledPeriodic flushes it after streaming stops. On the default connection_type 0 (Wire1) the OLED still writes, so only the poll degrades.
- DEAD CODE / LATENT DOUBLE-USE — the encoderDirectionConsumed ack channel is not used. RotaryEncoder.cpp:1278-1289 documents it as the handshake, but ~30 consumers ack by writing `encoderDirectionState = NONE` directly and never set it true; only Menus.cpp:5810 and JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2853 do. It therefore latches false after the first event, which makes the stale-event guards at :1165 and :1179 (clear a pending direction inside the AFTER window / confirm freeze) permanently dead. The only thing covering a direction event that a click was supposed to cancel is the press-edge clear at :1142.
- OWNERSHIP FIGHT — encoderDirectionState is one unowned global with no consumer token. StepViewer (StepViewer.cpp:283), Highlighting (Highlighting.cpp:359/:529), Menus, Probing (Probing.cpp:5555/:5672/:7780), Graphics (Graphics.cpp:4103/:4777), the apps and the MicroPython API all read and clear it. The registration order at main.cpp:549 exists solely to give StepViewer first refusal over Highlighting — an acknowledged priority hack. Whoever polls first eats the detent; there is no way for a screen to say 'that turn was mine'.
- BASELINE-EATER — any rotaryDivider change discards up to a full detent. RotaryEncoder.cpp:1102-1110 reseeds lastDetentRaw = rawCount and zeroes lastStepDir on ANY change, throwing away up to rotaryDivider−1 = 7 accumulated raw counts (7/8 of a detent). getMenuSelection re-writes the value on every pass (Menus.cpp:1036) and five other screens save/restore it by hand (ProjectsApp.cpp:701/:768, Menus.cpp:2632, :3389, :5705). If an inner-set service ever wrote a different value, the menu's 2 kHz per-pass write would fight it and rotation would go permanently dead.
- FRAME DROP WITH NO RETRY PATH — ledClass::show (LEDs.cpp:174) skips the strip when the DMA is busy and leaves bbDirty/topDirty set, but the `if (bbDirty)` retry it comments about is itself commented out (:191/:197). Recovery depends entirely on someone re-requesting: MT_LINGER_MS = 50 ms in menus, the 250 ms self-keepalive in the apps. A detent whose show lands inside the previous 9.3 ms frame is invisible until the next retry.
- TEAR — unbracketed app repaints. PartsApp::partsDrawItem (PartsApp.cpp:56-62) and ProjectsApp::drawPickerItem paint b.clear/b.print with no menuTransitionBeginDraw/Arm bracket while inClickMenu == 1, so core 1's rails==2 branch can stream a half-painted buffer. The menus solved exactly this with the mtDrawing gate (MenuTransitions.cpp:274-279); the apps never adopted it.
- CROSS-CORE LED-BUFFER FIGHT during turn-while-holding. holdAnimationStuff() runs INSIDE the core-1 encoder poll (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1091) and writes setLogoOverride + requestLedShow(2) at up to 2 kHz, while core 0's renderMenuLine concurrently writes setLogoRing and setMenuDepthLEDs. Menus.cpp:596-601 documents the same collision class from the other side (an unconditional clearColorOverrides erasing the hold sweep's pad writes, caught with a debugger watchpoint).
- NARROW EATER — the DOWN branch fires once without an encoder event. Menus.cpp:1243's condition `encoderDirectionState == DOWN || (lastMenuLevel < menuLevel)` is true on the pass right after a click descends a level (lastMenuLevel was stamped at :1056 before menuLevel++ at :1070, and is only caught up at :1632), and the branch unconditionally clears encoderDirectionState at :1244. Honest qualification: the 100 ms AFTER window (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1160) and the press-edge backlog drop (:1147) already suppress rotation across the click, so the window where this eats a REAL detent is narrow — a turn landing >100 ms after release but before the descend pass runs.
- SAMPLE THEFT — getEncoderRawCount() races the drain. RotaryEncoder.cpp:321-329 warns it is only safe while core 1's poll is suspended via encoderOverride, because both cores would advance s_encRdIdx. Any core-0 caller that forgets the override does not merely delay counts, it consumes ring words core 1 will never decode — the counts vanish from s_cpuQuadCount permanently.

**Quick wins:**
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/Menus.cpp:1231` Move renderMenuLine() above the Serial/OLED block in getMenuSelection's UP and DOWN branches. Today the breadboard LED request (Menus.cpp:1231) is posted AFTER the ~12.8 ms blocking SSD1306 write at :1205/:1226, so the most prominent surface is the LAST to update. renderMenuLine reads only menuLines[]/menuColors[] and shares no state with the Serial/OLED block, so hoisting it changes nothing about what is eventually displayed — it just posts the LED request ~12.8 ms earlier and puts the breadboard first, matching what the app pickers already do (PartsApp.cpp:62 before :68).
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Menus.cpp:1205` Coalesce the OLED frame when another detent is already queued. Guard the clearPrintShow calls with a check that no further direction event is pending (encoderDirectionState == NONE), skipping the 12.8 ms panel write for intermediate items during a spin and letting the final item paint it. A 10-detent flick goes from 10 × 12.8 ms = 128 ms of blocking I2C to one 12.8 ms frame, lifting the sustained rate from 71 to several hundred detents/s. The landed item is identical; only mid-flick frames a user cannot read are dropped. Needs a bench pass because it changes what the panel does during a spin, and Kevin may want the blur.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/PartsApp.cpp:68` Same coalesce in the app pickers: skip oled.showMultiLineSmallText when another detent is already pending. Identical arithmetic (12.8 ms per skipped frame) and the same 14.1 ms → sub-2 ms per-item cost. ProjectsApp.cpp:719 needs the matching change.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1176` Commit the confirm gate early once the accumulated travel clears a full detent while the button is still up. The gate's stated job (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1112-1147) is discarding press-jiggle, which is ±1–3 raw counts — not 8. isEncoderButtonPhysicallyPressed() is already read every poll at :1134, so adding `|| (labs(rawCount - lastRawForIdle) >= rotaryDivider && !pressedNow)` to the expiry test at :1176 keeps the anti-jiggle behaviour for small motions and removes the fixed 35 ms from every real detent — roughly 70 % of typical turn latency. Behaviour changes for the deliberately-slow-turn case, and Kevin tuned STEP_CONFIRM_US on hardware, so bench it.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1313` Do not drop the backlog on abandon while a UI is demonstrably listening. DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US exists for 'no UI is listening' (the driver says so at :1287), but it also fires when a listening UI is merely slow — one dead-OLED redraw sequence throws the whole turn away. Gate the backlog clear at :1313 on inClickMenu == 0 && inPadMenu == 0, leaving the event expiry itself intact. Changes what happens after a long stall, so bench it against the main screen where the original behaviour matters.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/Menus.cpp:1046` Move the per-pass hard delays below the event dispatch. getMenuSelection pays delayMicroseconds(400) at :1046 BEFORE the click/UP/DOWN dispatch at :1054+, and the pickers pay delayMicroseconds(1000) before their next check. Relocating the delay to the end of the loop body is behaviour-identical (same duty cycle, same service cadence) and shaves up to 400 µs from the menu and 1.0 ms from every app picker on the pass that carries the event. Small, but free. Mirror in PartsApp.cpp:145 and ProjectsApp.cpp:759.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/Menus.cpp:1036` Make the per-pass rotaryDivider write conditional (write-if-changed) rather than hoisting it. NOT a latency fix and NOT a hoist: the unconditional store is harmless while the value already equals 8, and it is the self-repair for an editor that leaked 3 or 12 — the exact hazard ProjectsApp.cpp:696-701 documents. Hoisting it out of the loop would remove that repair. Wrapping it as `if (rotaryDivider != 8) rotaryDivider = 8;` keeps the repair, keeps the reseed-on-change semantics at RotaryEncoder.cpp:1102, and stops a 2 kHz cross-core store on a variable the other core reads every poll. Coherence, not speed.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `src/oled.cpp:72` Raise the OLED I2C clock on the dedicated bus. kOledI2CClockHz is 400 kHz (the SSD1306 datasheet rating), but on the default connection_type 0/1/3 the panel is alone on Wire1 and every shipped SSD1306 module runs 1 MHz. 4914 bit-times ÷ 1 000 000 Hz = 4.91 ms instead of 12.29 ms — a straight 7.4 ms off every detent. Must stay 400 kHz on connection_type 2 where the panel shares I2C0 with the DAC and INA219s (the clkDuring/clkAfter dance at oled.cpp:155-160 already distinguishes them). Out of datasheet spec, so it depends on the specific panel and wiring: bench it.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/PartsApp.cpp:56` Give the app pickers the menu's draw bracket. Wrapping partsDrawItem's b.clear/b.print in menuTransitionBeginDraw()/menuTransitionArm() closes the tear window where core 1 streams a half-painted buffer while inClickMenu == 1, and costs ~0.3–0.8 ms of core-0 time that the 12.8 ms OLED write dwarfs. It also gets the pickers the GLOW transition and the MT_LINGER_MS re-show that already rescues dropped menu frames, so the 250 ms keepalive at PartsApp.cpp:111 stops being the only recovery path. Same change in ProjectsApp::drawPickerItem.

**Unification notes:** FIVE HAND-ROLLED PICKER LOOPS. getMenuSelection (Menus.cpp:999), PartsApp::partsPicker (PartsApp.cpp:99), ProjectsApp::runPicker (ProjectsApp.cpp:715), the File Manager (remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp:1594) and the node picker (Menus.cpp:5809) each independently re-implement the identical body: reset encoderButtonState/lastButtonEncoderState/encoderDirectionState on entry → loop { serviceInner; rotaryEncoderButtonStuff (dead); LED-show keepalive; HELD → back; Serial.available → exit; RELEASED && lastButtonEncoderState == PRESSED → select; UP/DOWN → move; delayMicroseconds(400 or 1000) }. They disagree on the poll period (490 µs vs 1.04 ms), on the keepalive cadence (50 ms MT_LINGER vs a hand-rolled 250 ms), on whether the LED request precedes or follows the blocking OLED write, and on whether the repaint is bracketed. One `wheelPicker(items, onDraw)` would collapse all five and make every one of the fixes above a single edit.

DEAD POLL CALLS ADVERTISING A CONTRACT THAT NO LONGER EXISTS. rotaryEncoderStuff() and rotaryEncoderButtonStuff() both hard-return on core 0 (RotaryEncoder.cpp:996 and :1033), yet every picker still calls one of them every iteration (Menus.cpp:1001, PartsApp.cpp:107 and :121, ProjectsApp.cpp:723 and :783). The driver's own comment at :993-995 admits they were left in as documentation rather than removed. A reader of any picker loop reasonably concludes the loop polls the wheel; it does not — core 1's 500 µs poll and the 10 ms BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US hold are the entire reason clicks and turns are seen at all. Deleting them (or renaming the call to something honest) removes a real source of misunderstanding about where latency comes from.

TWO PARALLEL BUTTON MODELS. The shared cross-core state machine (encoderButtonState / lastButtonEncoderState with the 10 ms hold window) and the per-UI EncoderClickTracker (RotaryEncoder.h:180, a debounced classifier on the raw pin) coexist. RotaryEncoder.h:147-152 states outright that the shared machine is ambiguous because it gets re-armed by various consumers — yet the menu and both app pickers still use the shared machine, and the click/hold semantics differ between them (the pickers fire on RELEASED, EncoderClickTracker users can fire on ENC_PRESS). This is the button-side twin of the encoderDirectionState ownership problem below.

TWO DEBOUNCE/ANTI-JIGGLE IMPLEMENTATIONS OF THE SAME IDEA. The driver's confirm gate + AFTER window (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1112-1196, 35 ms / 100 ms) and EncoderClickTracker's debounceMs = 30 (RotaryEncoder.h:183) both exist to keep a press from being read as motion or a bounce as an edge, at different layers with independently tuned constants. Note that the ENC_PUSH net is already hardware-debounced (RotaryEncoder.cpp:346-352: R8 pullup + R22 series + C27 100 nF, ~100 µs fall / ~5 ms rise) — so a third, physical debounce is in the stack too, and the two software ones were tuned without reference to it.

THREE SEPARATE EVENT/BACKLOG POLICIES. The turn path has the paced emitter with a 250 ms abandon (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1290-1314). The probe path has its own double-tap/press queue (Probing.h's doubleClickConnect/Disconnect). The LED path has a mailbox with generations and completion (core1req, Commands.cpp:57-130). Three answers to 'one producer, one consumer, don't lose or replay the event', none sharing code — and the turn path is the only one of the three whose overflow policy is 'throw the whole backlog away'.

FIVE REDRAW SCHEDULERS. The menus use the transition engine's MT_FRAME_MS = 16 ms pacer plus a 50 ms linger plus menuShowKeepalive at 50 ms (Menus.cpp:483-489). The apps use a hand-rolled 250 ms re-request (PartsApp.cpp:111, ProjectsApp.cpp:726). Core 1 has its own 8 ms schedulerUpdateTime and a 51 ms swirl timer (main.cpp:1499/:2012). ledClass::show has a 14 µs gate (main.cpp:1536, effectively nothing). All five exist because leds.show() silently drops frames when the DMA is busy — one honest 'request coalescing with guaranteed eventual delivery' layer at the requestLedShow boundary would retire every one of them, including the commented-out bbDirty retry at LEDs.cpp:191.

ROTARY SENSITIVITY AS AN UNOWNED GLOBAL. rotaryDivider is written by at least eight sites (Menus.cpp:1036, :1817, :2474, :2874, :3390, ProjectsApp.cpp:702, and the save/restore pairs at Menus.cpp:2632/:2853 and :5705). ProjectsApp.cpp:696-701 documents the convention ('every picker-shaped consumer owns this global for its duration and puts it back') in a comment because there is no mechanism enforcing it — and RotaryEncoder.cpp:1102-1110 makes every uncoordinated change cost up to 7/8 of a detent of real travel. A scoped guard object (or the divider living in the picker abstraction above) makes the convention structural instead of aspirational.

## Terminal single-char command → dispatch → routing → LEDs (V5 / RP2350, line-buffered default)

**Total: Enter-key to lit LEDs, line-buffered mode, one bridge. BEST ≈ 17.3 ms = 0.25 (USB frame) + 0.28 (pass pickup) + 0.25 (echo/line) + 0.05 (dispatch+parse) + 1.2 (refreshLocalConnections) + 0.15 (req→pickup) + 0.05 (CH446Q DMA) + 0 (LED tick already due) + 5.6 (render to show-start) + 9.45 (WS2812 wire, 300 px × 24 bits × 1.25 us × 1.05). TYPICAL ≈ 23.2 ms = 0.5 + 0.14 + 0.25 + 0.05 + 1.5 + 0.15 + 0.05 + 4.0 (mean half of the 8 ms core-1 tick) + 7.1 (measured send→show) + 9.45. WORST without a flash write ≈ 231 ms = 200 (refreshLocalConnections' core2busy spin, Commands.cpp:375) + 1.5 + 8.0 (full tick) + 11.5 (measured req→show max) + 9.45. WORST overall ≈ 1.9 s when a slot auto-save (measured 1.59 s service, 1820 ms core-0 gap) or a config save (~700 ms core-1 pause) lands in the same window. DOMINANT TERM on the happy path is the LED pipeline — 4 ms tick + 7.1 ms render + 9.45 ms wire ≈ 20.5 of the 23.2 ms; everything from the USB byte through the crossbar send is under 2.5 ms combined. DOMINANT TERM in the tail is flash writes executed on core 0.**

**Bottlenecks:**
- WS2812 wire time, 9.45 ms — 300 breadboard pixels × 24 bits × 1.25 us = 9.00 ms, ×1.05 for the RP2350's slow bit clock (JeoPixel.h:257). Hardware floor, unfixable without splitting the strip or dropping to a partial update. It is 41% of the typical end-to-end and is NOT counted by the xbarLatShow() stamp, which fires at DMA start (main.cpp:1963).
- The 8 ms core-1 scheduler tick in front of every net repaint. main.cpp:1768 gates the LED branch on micros()-schedulerTimer > schedulerUpdateTime (8000, main.cpp:1499), and ledImmediate (main.cpp:1762) covers only LED_MENU and LED_GFX. A routing change posts LED_NETS, so it pays a mean 4 ms / max 8 ms of pure wait — 17% of the typical total for nothing.
- showNets() + the surrounding render, measured send→show 5.6–8.5 ms typical / 10.2–10.4 ms max. The largest software term anywhere on the path; the crossbar send it follows is ~50 us.
- Flash writes executed on core 0 with FlashPark holding core 1. SlotManager::service() max 1.59 s / 37 overruns and a 1820 ms core-0 kick gap (SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md); ConfigSaveService's own comment records ~700 ms of core-1 pause per config.txt write (configManager.cpp:89). Neither is on the 'f' path itself, but both land in the busy loop between commands.
- refreshLocalConnections' 200 ms core2busy spin (Commands.cpp:367-384). Nothing on core 0 runs during it except an ad-hoc TinyUSB_Device_Task() gated by `(micros()-start) % 5000 < 10`.
- renderCurrentLine() re-renders and flushes the entire line on every keystroke (Jerial.cpp:717-767). O(n²) bytes per line; a 100-char paste emits ~15 KB through a 1024-byte TX FIFO, and Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write blocks on yield() until the host drains it.
- ProbePads at 4.6–5.7 ms per poll every 50 ms (post-T2.1) plus Probing at up to 5.6 ms every 10 ms — ~11–15% of core 0 and a multi-ms hole in the byte-poll cadence even when nothing is touched.
- serviceUSBSer3() runs INSIDE Jerial::service() (Jerial.cpp:1103), ahead of the port-1 line assembler. A CSI sequence on port 4 blocks it 40 ms (Ser3Backchannel.cpp:1086,1091); a ':' verb blocks it up to 2000 ms (usbSer3_readLine, :1018).
- Service-period convoy. Periods 2/5/10/20/50/100 ms all divide 100 ms, so they re-align periodically. Worst co-due sum with no flash write: LedDump 52 + OLED frame 23 (1024 B + ~65 B of control/address at 400 kHz × 9 bits = 24.5 ms, ~33 I2C transactions per oled.cpp:2318) + ProbePads 5.7 + Probing 5.6 + ProbeSwitch 5.2 + Peripherals ~0.1 + rest ~1 ≈ 93 ms in one pass. That is the worst-case delay before the next byte is even read.

**Stalls / races:**
- WEDGE: Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write() spins `while (remain && tud_cdc_n_connected()) { ...; yield(); }` (Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:240-257). A host that opens the CDC port and stops reading blocks core 0 forever in the middle of a command. Jerial::flush() was explicitly defanged for exactly this (Jerial.cpp:1001-1028) but write() was not, and TermControl::renderCurrentLine() calls the RAW stream->flush() once per keystroke (Jerial.cpp:766).
- DROP/CORRUPT (char mode only, terminal_line_buffering == 0 via the 'B' command or an app's SI 0x0F at Jerial.cpp:216): helpQuestionApplies('f') is true (main.cpp:790), so a bare typed 'f' arms the 100 ms wait; when it expires with an empty stream, cmd_loadNodeFile runs with currentCommandLine == "f" (length 1), so savePreformattedNodeFile takes the non-preformatted branch and calls specialFunctionsString.read(Jerial) — which is NON-BLOCKING (SafeString.cpp:4781) and reads nothing. globalState.clearAllConnections() (FileParsing.cpp:920) has already run, connCount == 0 so no refresh follows, and the node list the user types next falls through to the main menu as single-char commands — '-' is 'remove connections'.
- PROCEED-ANYWAY RACE: refreshLocalConnections' 200 ms core2busy timeout breaks out and continues (Commands.cpp:375-378), then rebuilds nets/paths while core 1 may be mid-render. CH446Q.cpp:97-99 argues the DMA words are a snapshot so the wire order is safe, but the LED frame core 1 is currently drawing uses the pre-rebuild net tables — one frame of stale colours.
- SILENTLY DROPPED ARGUMENT: refreshLocalConnections(ledShowOption, fillUnused, clean) never uses `clean`. It posts core1req::post(REQ_BYPASS, 1u) (Commands.cpp:447), and every REQ_BYPASS take calls sendPaths(0, ...) (main.cpp:1551, main.cpp:1707) — clean == 0 always. 'f' asks for clean=1 (FileParsing.cpp:1087) and never gets the RESETPIN pulse + full re-send that refreshConnections would do (CH446Q.cpp:732-743).
- DOUBLE-FIRE: every command returning CMD_SHOW_MENU triggers clearHighlighting() twice — once at the top of loop() (main.cpp:924) and once inside printMenu() (SingleCharCommands.cpp:192) — and clearHighlighting defaults to updateLEDs = 1 (Highlighting.h:113), so each issues requestLedShow(1). postMode is last-write-wins so they coalesce into one render, but the intent is duplicated and a caller reading the returned generation gets the wrong one.
- INPUT EATEN: printMenu() drains the input stream whenever Jerial.available() > 20 (SingleCharCommands.cpp:175-183), reading through Jerial.read() → term_control->read(). A multi-line paste whose first command returns CMD_SHOW_MENU loses its tail. main.cpp:1366-1370 documents removing the OTHER drain for this exact reason but left this one.
- ONE-LAP DEFERRAL: loop() has two executeCommand() call sites — the CommandBuffer/UART one (main.cpp:1178) and the terminal one (main.cpp:1355). They consume different buffers so the same bytes cannot double-fire, but the CommandBuffer branch ends in `goto dontshowmenu` (main.cpp:1206) before the completed terminal line is even looked at, so a typed command waits one extra busy-loop lap whenever a UART command is pending.
- CROSS-SURFACE FLAG LEAK: cmd_loadNodeFile sets the global probeActive = 1 for the duration of the command (SingleCharCommands.cpp:1294). Core 1 reads it in logoSwirl(swirlCount, spread, probeActive) (main.cpp:1792) and in the defcon gate (main.cpp:1817), and ConfigSaveService refuses implicit saves while it is set (configManager.cpp:100). A terminal command impersonates a probe session on the other two surfaces. It also overwrites the global `input` with ' ' (:1309).
- THREE LED-SHOW ABORT PATHS that re-post: main.cpp:1843-1846 and :1909-1912 complete-then-re-post a taken REQ_SHOW_LEDS when a core-1 frame hold appears mid-render. Correct, but it means a routing repaint can be deferred by an arbitrary number of 8 ms ticks while any flash write holds the frame — and the user sees the old netlist on the board while the crossbar has already changed.
- LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME is a dead knob: main.cpp:1536 defines it as 14 and main.cpp:1953 compares it against micros(), i.e. a 14 microsecond floor while the name and its placement read like milliseconds. Two shows 14 us apart are impossible at this call site, so the gate never fires; the real pacing is the 8 ms tick plus ledClass::show()'s isDMABusy() drop (LEDs.cpp:183,191).

**Quick wins:**
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/main.cpp:1122` Move Jerial.service() from the END of the busy-loop body to the TOP, ahead of jOS.serviceAll(). Today a byte that arrives just after the reader runs waits an entire serviceAll() (278 us mean, ~5.7 ms when ProbePads is due, up to ~93 ms on a convoy pass) before it is even copied out of the CDC FIFO. Reading first costs nothing and removes one full service pass of latency from every keystroke and every command.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/Commands.cpp:381` Replace refreshLocalConnections' USB pump heuristic `if ((micros() - core2_wait_start) % 5000 < 10) TinyUSB_Device_Task();` with an explicit next-pump deadline (`if ((long)(micros() - nextPumpUs) >= 0) { nextPumpUs += 5000; TinyUSB_Device_Task(); }`). The modulo only fires if a spin iteration happens to land inside a 10 us window; a compiler or clock change silently turns the pump off for the entire 200 ms wait, which is the documented USB-disconnect failure mode.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `src/main.cpp:1953` Delete the LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME gate (or convert it to an honest millisecond throttle). As written it is `micros() - ledShowTime > 14`, which can never be false at this call site, so removing it changes nothing; leaving it invites someone to 'fix' it to 14 ms and cap the strip at 71 Hz. The real pacing is the 8 ms tick plus ledClass::show()'s isDMABusy() frame drop.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/main.cpp:1762` Let LED_NETS skip the 8 ms scheduler tick when the mailbox is idle: add core1req::LED_NETS to the ledImmediate mask. A routing change is exactly as interactive as a menu flush, and today it pays a mean 4 ms / max 8 ms of dead wait before rendering. Cuts ~17% off the typical end-to-end. Bench it: the same tick also paces the idle logo swirl branch's re-entry, so a heavy routing burst would raise core-1 frame rate and could starve the swirl or the encoder poll.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Jerial.cpp:1103` Move singleCharCommands.serviceUSBSer3() out of Jerial::service() into its own registered service (NORMAL, ~5 ms period). Today a port-4 CSI sequence blocks the port-1 line assembler for 40 ms and a ':' verb for up to 2000 ms, because the port-4 drain runs first inside the function whose job is reading port 1. Bench it: serviceUSBSer3 interacts with Jerial's current/pending response-target machinery, so the ordering relative to term_control->service() may matter.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/SingleCharCommands.cpp:175` Gate printMenu()'s '>20 bytes' input drain on !Jerial.hasCompletedLine() (and on the relay stream being empty), so a queued pasted command is not eaten. The drain exists to swallow a mash of keypresses, not a legitimately buffered command; main.cpp:1366-1370 removed the sibling drain in loop() for precisely this reason but left this one.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/SingleCharCommands.cpp:1294` Stop cmd_loadNodeFile writing the probe surface's globals. probeActive drives core 1's logoSwirl variant and the defcon gate, and suppresses ConfigSaveService's implicit saves; `input = ' '` clobbers loop()'s dispatch variable. A terminal routing command should not look like a probe session to the other two surfaces. Bench it: readInNodesArduino/probeActive may be load-bearing for the Arduino-relay path that shares this handler.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Jerial.cpp:717` Make renderCurrentLine() append-only when the edit was a plain append at end-of-line and the highlighter's colouring of the existing prefix is unchanged — echo one character instead of redrawing the whole line. This is the O(n²)→O(n) fix for pastes and for the CDC backpressure they cause. Bench it: the syntax highlighter recolours earlier text when a token completes (typing '-' in '1-30' turns the node numbers into a connection), so a naive append breaks the highlight; needs a 'prefix colouring changed?' check or a dirty-from-column hint out of the highlighter.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Commands.cpp:447` Decide what refreshLocalConnections' `clean` argument means. It is accepted, documented at every call site, and then dropped: REQ_BYPASS always issues sendPaths(clean=0). Either honour it (post REQ_SEND|SEND_CLEAN when clean==1, which pulses RESETPIN and re-sends every crosspoint — tens of ms and a visible board blink) or delete the parameter so callers stop believing they asked for something. Not shippable as a silent behaviour change.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `src/Jerial.cpp:1819` Give Adafruit_USBD_CDC writes a bound. Wrap the Jerial output path so a write that cannot place its bytes within N milliseconds drops the remainder (or sets a 'terminal stalled' flag) instead of spinning in yield() forever while a host holds the port open without reading. Jerial::flush() already made this decision for flush; write() is the remaining unbounded path and it is reached from renderCurrentLine, printMenu and every command's output. This is a policy change (dropping terminal output is user-visible), so it needs a design call on what to drop and how to say so.

**Unification notes:** FIVE independent 'wait for the rest of the command' implementations, all reachable from this one path. (1) The help/'?' look-ahead: 100 ms, armed as a loop-level state machine that keeps servicing (main.cpp:799, 1307-1315). (2) getCommandArgs(): 50 ms default / 30 ms for '<', a `delay(1)` spin with NOTHING serviced (SingleCharCommands.cpp:1785-1814) — the sibling that '<', '@', 'J', 'Y' use. (3) savePreformattedNodeFile's char-mode branch: no wait at all, SafeString::read(Jerial) takes whatever happens to be buffered (FileParsing.cpp:898). (4) readPastedBlock(): 500 ms quiet / 30 s idle, reading RAW Serial and bypassing Jerial entirely (SingleCharCommands.cpp:1841). (5) usbSer3_readLine(): 2000 ms idle with its own inline echo and backspace handling (Ser3Backchannel.cpp:1018). Five answers, five timeouts, three different opinions on whether services keep running.

TWO command dispatch sites in loop(), with duplicated inMainMenu bracketing, duplicated TinyUSB_Device_Task pre-pumps and duplicated CommandResult switches: main.cpp:1178 (CommandBuffer / UART-relayed) and main.cpp:1355 (terminal). The relayed one wins arbitration by running first and jumping to dontshowmenu.

THREE line-completion owners feeding one hasCompletedLine(): TermControl's single completed_line/line_ready slot, TermControl's separate command_queue[] with per-entry response targets (Jerial.cpp:327-343), and CommandBuffer (main.cpp:1126,1135). Jerial::hasCompletedLine() sees two of the three; the third is polled separately in two places.

TWO line editors on two ports: TermControl (Jerial.cpp — ANSI state machine, history, syntax highlight, cursor motion) and usbSer3_readLine's inline echo/backspace loop (Ser3Backchannel.cpp:1030-1040). The port-4 one is invoked from inside the port-1 one.

'Commit a routing change and repaint' is reimplemented per surface. The terminal path here does clearAllConnections → addBridgeToState* → refreshLocalConnections(-1,1,1) → REQ_BYPASS + requestLedShow(-1). The probe path (Probing.cpp) does its own addBridgeToState/removeBridgeFromState → refresh → the same requestLedShow(-1), and sets the same probeActive that this terminal command now also sets. The wheel path reaches the same requestLedShow(-1) through clearHighlighting() (Highlighting.h:113). Three surfaces, one unnamed operation, three copies of the ordering rules — and the LED-clear semantics ride on the sign of an int passed to requestLedShow (Commands.cpp:66-99), which the file's own comment already flags as the thing to retire.

Redraw scheduling is split across three mechanisms that do not know about each other: core 1's 8 ms schedulerUpdateTime tick (main.cpp:1768), the ledImmediate bypass for menus/graphics (main.cpp:1762), and ledClass::show()'s isDMABusy() frame drop (LEDs.cpp:183,191). A request can therefore be delayed by the tick, then dropped by the DMA guard, then re-posted by the frame-hold abort (main.cpp:1843) — three independent deferrals for one repaint.

clearHighlighting() is the de-facto 'reset the interaction' call and is invoked from loop()'s head (main.cpp:924), printMenu() (SingleCharCommands.cpp:192), the highlight timeout (Highlighting.cpp:863) and probe exit — each with its own opinion about whether it should also request an LED show.

## Make one connection (node 1 to node 5) via three surfaces: terminal `f 1-5`, probe two-tap, click-menu Connect > Add

**Total: Terminal `f 1-5` (line mode): keystroke -> crossbar = 1.3 (USB+pass) + 0.29 (line) + 0.01 (dispatch) + 0.13 (name substitution) + 1.5 (undo snapshot of the whole board) + 0.05 (add bridge) + 2.9 (route) + 0.23 (mailbox+send) = ~6.4 ms typical; keystroke -> LEDs adds 4 ms mean scheduler wait + 7 ms show = ~17 ms typical, ~21 ms with the full 8 ms tick. Worst case is 200 ms (core2busy spin) + 200 ms (SEND wedge) on top. Durable at 750-800 ms after the last keystroke. DOMINANT TERM: bridgesToPaths at 1215 us (51% of the rebuild, of which sortPathsByNet is 592 us), with the clear-all undo snapshot (est 1-3 ms) a close second and unique to this path.

Probe two-tap: tap-1 contact -> "ready for tap 2" = 2.7 (ADC ring history) + 0.15 (tick) + 12.7 (blocking OLED) + 140 (latch flash dwell) = ~156 ms. Tap-2 contact -> crossbar = 2.7 + 0.15 + 2.9 (route) + 0.23 = ~6 ms. Tap-2 -> LEDs = 6 + 12.7 (the post-commit OLED frame sits AHEAD of the LED frame on core 0) + 4 (scheduler) + 7 (show) = ~30 ms typical, ~34 ms worst. Durable 750 ms after probeMode EXIT. DOMINANT TERM: the 140 ms latch flash, then the two 12.7 ms blocking SSD1306 frames - the actual routing and crossbar work is only ~3 ms of the 156 ms.

Menu Connect > Add: identical to the probe once probeMode starts, plus ~102 ms of confirm/transition machine time to reach it and ~13 ms of trailing OLED that erases the confirmation. DOMINANT TERM: same 140 ms flash, plus 35 ms per detent confirm.

Cross-path: the three surfaces agree to within ~1 ms on the part that matters electrically (route + crossbar, ~3.2 ms). Everything the user actually perceives as "how fast is it" - 140 ms flash, 12.7 ms OLED frames, 8 ms LED scheduler tick, 35 ms detent confirm - lives entirely outside that.**

**Bottlenecks:**
- bridgesToPaths in the shared rebuild: 1215 us of a 2365 us profiled total (51%), sortPathsByNet alone 592 us (CodeDocs/PERFORMANCE_ROUND3.md:12-13). Every surface pays it once per connection.
- The 140 ms latch-flash dwell after the FIRST probe tap (Probing.cpp:3588 + the sub-state at Probing.cpp:2938-2963, 40+40+60 ms). It is the single largest term in a two-tap connect and it freezes input. Terminal `f` never pays it.
- Blocking SSD1306 I2C on core 0: 512 B framebuffer at 400 kHz in ~17 chunks = (512+51)x9/400000 = 12.7 ms per frame. Probe pays 2 per connection (Probing.cpp:3560, :3702); menu pays 3 (+ Menus.cpp:4659). Terminal pays 0.
- The 8 ms LED scheduler tick on core 1 (schedulerUpdateTime, main.cpp:1499; gate at main.cpp:1768). LED_NETS is NOT in the immediate class that LED_MENU/LED_GFX are, so every netlist change waits 0-8 ms before rendering even starts.
- The full-board undo snapshot on the terminal path only: undoRecordClearAll -> undoForceSnapshot -> globalState.toYAML into an Arduino String (Undo.cpp:2194 -> :315). Est 1-3 ms, scales with board size, and it fires on every single `f` including `f 1-5` on an empty board.
- STEP_CONFIRM_US = 35000 us per detent (RotaryEncoder.cpp:444) plus MT_FRAME_MS = 16 ms transitions: ~102 ms of machine time to walk two menu levels before the connect gesture can even begin.
- The 200 ms core2busy spin at the head of every refreshLocalConnections (Commands.cpp:366-386). Not usually hit, but when core 1 is wedged EVERY connect on EVERY surface pays it and then proceeds anyway.

**Stalls / races:**
- DATA LOSS, easily reachable: `f` clears the netlist BEFORE it parses. FileParsing.cpp:920 calls globalState.clearAllConnections() unconditionally; the refresh at FileParsing.cpp:1087 is gated on connCount > 0. Type `f` alone and press Enter (line mode, the default): preformattedData.length() is 1, not > 1, so FileParsing.cpp:889 falls to specialFunctionsString.read(Jerial), and SafeString::read(Stream&) is NON-blocking (it drains only what is already buffered - verified in SafeString.cpp:4489-4508). The buffer is empty, the parse yields nothing, connCount stays 0 -> the board is wiped in RAM, dirty, and NEVER refreshed. The crossbar still holds the old connections, the LEDs still show them, and 750 ms later the auto-save writes the empty board over the slot file. The command's own help text says it 'Prompts for file selection' (SingleCharCommands.cpp:424) - it does not prompt.
- DROPPED WORK: a coalesced local refresh is silently discarded. Commands.cpp:353-356 sets refreshLocalPending and returns when a refresh is already in flight, but the re-run at Commands.cpp:483-486 is COMMENTED OUT - the flag is cleared and nothing happens. fastRefresh has the same dead tail (Commands.cpp:703-705). Its sibling refreshConnections DOES re-run (Commands.cpp:319-322). The comment right above the dead code says it is 'critical for MicroPython scripts that make multiple connections quickly'. A dropped refresh leaves the crossbar behind the netlist until some unrelated refresh catches up.
- CROSS-CORE / CROSS-PATH RACE: refreshInProgress (Commands.cpp:173) and refreshLocalInProgress (Commands.cpp:339) are independent guards that do not check each other. Menu Show>Voltage goes through refreshConnections (Menus.cpp:4234) while probe and `f` go through refreshLocalConnections; both write globalState.connections.paths and CH446Q lastChipXY, and neither guard excludes the other.
- UNREACHABLE SAVE: probeMode's own 3-second idle save (Probing.cpp:3190-3200) calls SlotManager::service(), but externVars.cpp:261 vetoes systemIdleForFlush() while probeActive is set (Probing.cpp:2823). The auto-save branch at States.cpp:4797 can never run during a probe session, s.numberOfLocalChanges never resets, and an arbitrarily long session holds every change in RAM only.
- SILENT NO-OP DURABILITY: both 'save it now' calls on the terminal path are inert. SingleCharCommands.cpp:1315-1318 says 'Flush now so it's on flash before they move on' and States.cpp:730-737 calls itself a 'big-event flush', but USE_FILE_CACHE defaults to 0 (FileCache.h:46) and is not overridden in platformio.ini, so FileCache.cpp:1582's pass-through reduces both to fatFsForceSync() (a no-op in delta-journal mode per main.cpp:268-277) plus a possible EEPROM commit. The slot YAML still waits for the 750 ms idle gate.
- STALE HIGHLIGHT ACROSS A NETLIST REPLACEMENT: Highlighting::service only brightens when probeReading > 0 (Highlighting.cpp:159) and never clears on lift, so brightenedNet survives after the probe is lifted. `f` returns CMD_DONT_SHOW_MENU (main.cpp:1358), which skips BOTH clearHighlighting() sites (main.cpp:924 and SingleCharCommands.cpp:192). brightenedNet is then an index into the net table `f` just replaced, so the wrong net stays lit.
- DOUBLE LED POST ON THE PROBE PATH: refreshLocalConnections posts requestLedShow(1) (Commands.cpp:456) and then Probing.cpp:3700 brightenNet(-1) posts requestLedShow(-1) microseconds later. They coalesce in the mailbox (Commands.cpp:91-96) so the end frame is right, but if core 1 takes the request between the two posts the first frame renders WITHOUT the clear and the previous frame's pixels survive one 8 ms tick.
- LOST CLEAN REBUILD: refreshLocalConnections accepts a `clean` argument (Commands.cpp:343) and never reads it - Commands.cpp:447 always posts REQ_BYPASS, which is clean=0. `f` asks for clean=1 (FileParsing.cpp:1087) and does not get the CH446Q RESET pulse + chip-ordered re-index (CH446Q.cpp:731-742). Functionally the incremental path does open removed crosspoints (CH446Q.cpp updateChipStateArray), so this is a lost belt-and-braces reset rather than a stuck crosspoint - but `x`, which is no more destructive than `f`, goes through refreshConnections and DOES get it.

Needs a per-action opt-out flag - the wipe is deliberate for app launches.

**Unification notes:** FIVE rebuild entry points in Commands.cpp - refresh() :155, refreshConnections() :177, refreshLocalConnections() :343, refreshBlind() :499, fastRefresh() :554 - that differ in ways the callers cannot see: who waits for core 1 (refreshConnections waits up to 1000 ms on the SEND generation plus a 25 ms waitCore2; refreshLocal posts BYPASS and returns), whether `clean` is honoured (only refreshConnections), whether a coalesced re-run actually happens (only refreshConnections), and whether assignTermColor runs. The same logical action reaches different ones by surface: `f` and probe -> refreshLocalConnections; menu Show>Voltage -> refreshConnections (Menus.cpp:4234); `x` and slot loads -> refreshConnections. That is why "connect 1 to 5" has three different hardware-convergence guarantees.

TWO netlist-mutation front doors that each reimplement parsing: savePreformattedNodeFile (FileParsing.cpp:861, SafeString stoken) for `f`, and readStringFromSerial (FileParsing.cpp:1865, indexOf/substring) for `+` and `-`. Both end at addBridgeToState; only one clears first; only one strips the `{}` wrapper; only one handles the single-node remove form. The probe and menu paths bypass both and call addBridgeToState directly (Probing.cpp:3685, Menus.cpp:4089+).

THREE unrelated debounce/dedupe schemes for "the same input twice", with no shared vocabulary: the probe's row[1]/lastProbedRows plus a 700 ms reset (Probing.cpp:3865-3872) and doubleSelectCountdown at 200/400/700 ms; the encoder's STEP_CONFIRM_US 35 ms + ENCODER_REARM_IDLE_US 150 ms + RECOIL_GUARD_US 200 ms (RotaryEncoder.cpp:444-487); and the terminal's helpArmed 100 ms look-ahead (main.cpp:799). A fourth exists in ProbeButton for double-tap.

TWO "is the system idle enough to touch flash" gates, one of which is dead because the other vetoes it: systemIdleForFlush() (externVars.cpp:254, 750 ms + context stack + probeActive) and probeMode's own idleTime / idleSaveTime = 3000 (Probing.cpp:1845-1846, called at :3190).

TWO dirty concepts: globalState.dirty (States.cpp:568, the one that drives auto-save) and the per-slot validation flags markSlotAsModified()/slotIsValidated() (FileParsing.cpp:2953-2969). Every live call site of markSlotAsModified is commented out (FileParsing.cpp:1611, :1812) while its readers at :2247 and :2410 still consult the stale flag.

THREE LED-request idioms stacked for the same "the board changed" event: refreshLocalConnections' internal requestLedShow(ledShowOption) (Commands.cpp:456), brightenNet(-1)'s own requestLedShow(-1) (Highlighting.cpp:671), and the menu's post-action b.clear() + requestLedShow(-1) (Menus.cpp:4658 and :618-619). Terminal posts one, probe posts two, menu posts four - all for one connection. The mailbox coalesces them, which is why nobody has noticed.

TWO "save it now" APIs that both no-op under the current build config: fileCacheFlushNowAll (SingleCharCommands.cpp:1318, States.cpp:737) and SlotManager::saveActiveSlot. Only the second actually writes the slot, and only the idle-gated auto-save calls it. Any future caller reading the comments at SingleCharCommands.cpp:1315-1317 will believe the wrong thing.

## Clickwheel menu: open → one detent of navigation → close (Menus.cpp initMenu / clickMenu / getMenuSelection, core-1 LED gate, exit restoration)

**Total: MENU OPEN (finger lifts → first menu line lit on the breadboard): ~42.5 ms typical with the OLED enabled = 5–6.3 ms button RC rise (tau 5.21 ms) + 0.25 ms core-1 button poll + ~0.5 ms core-0 serviceAll pass + 15.0 ms hard delayMicroseconds(15000) at Menus.cpp:1110 + ~0.02 ms terminal + 12.2 ms OLED I2C frame + ~0.4 ms renderMenuLine + ~0.1 ms core-1 pickup + 9.30 ms WS2812. With top_oled.enabled = 0 (shipped default): ~30.3 ms. Dominant term: the 15 ms firstTime delay, then the 12.2 ms OLED, then the 9.3 ms strip.

ONE DETENT of navigation (mid-turn, event → both panels updated): best ~10.0 ms (poll phases aligned, OLED disabled: 0.25 encoder + 0.25 core-0 + 0.1 branch + 0.02 terminal + 0.25 render + 0.1 pickup + 9.30 strip). Typical ~22.6 ms (add the 12.2 ms OLED frame, which runs BEFORE the LED repaint). Worst ~42 ms (both polls land late, renderMenuLine hits the 3 ms mtRenderBusy spin, and the strip frame is dropped by the DMA-busy gate and only recovered on the next 16 ms transition tick). Dominant term: the OLED I2C frame; second: the WS2812 frame, which is then re-shipped ~13 times per detent by the 160 ms GLOW + 50 ms linger at 16 ms pacing (58% strip duty).

FIRST detent after the shaft has rested >=150 ms: +35 ms (STEP_CONFIRM_US) → ~57.6 ms.
FIRST detent after the click that OPENED the menu: the wheel is deliberately deaf for CLICK_SUPPRESS_AFTER_US = 100 ms measured from the last 'pressed' sample, with the 35 ms confirm gate overlapping it → 100–135 ms before the step registers, then the ~22.6 ms pipeline. Worst case ~158 ms from wrist to lit LED. This is by design (the click/rotation interlock) and is the single largest 'the menu feels slow' number on this path.

MENU CLOSE via an action: ~10.3–22 ms for the breadboard to show wires again (LED_NETS rides the 8 ms scheduler tick, then showNets + 9.30 ms strip), plus a 512 B–2.0 kB terminal reprint that blocks in yield() above 1024 B. The OLED is never repainted — it keeps the last menu line.
MENU CLOSE via hold-to-back past the top: up to ~60 ms of stale menu text on the breadboard (posted show swallowed by the inClickMenu gate, recovered only by the 51 ms swirl tick) + a 12.2 ms showJogo32h, and the terminal is never restored at all.**

The 182-line menuTree (src/remembering/menuTree.h, max depth 5) is FatFS-read and String-parsed into menuLines[]/menuLevels[]/actions[]/numberOfChoices[] here. | Boot-time only: 0 us on every menu open. **Bottlenecks:**
- OLED I2C frame, ~12.2 ms per detent, BLOCKING core 0 — and it runs BEFORE the breadboard repaint. 512 B framebuffer (config.h:391) chunked by WIRE_MAX into 9 transactions at 400 kHz (oled.cpp:72): 530 wire bytes x 9 bit-times / 400 kHz = 11.93 ms, + 0.27 ms of page/column commands + 0.05 ms post-write ping. It is the largest single term in a navigation detent and it delays the primary surface (the breadboard) by its full duration for no reason. Menus.cpp:1205 / :1415.
- WS2812 breadboard frame, 9.30 ms (300 px x 30 us + 300 us latch), multiplied by ~13 shows per detent. The default GLOW transition is 160 ms (MenuTransitions.h:50) plus MT_LINGER_MS = 50 ms (MenuTransitions.cpp:46), re-triggered every MT_FRAME_MS = 16 ms from main.cpp:1747 → (160+50)/16 = 13 renders + 13 show attempts. Strip duty 9.30/16 = 58%; a fast scroll restarts the clock every detent, so most intermediate frames are dropped by the DMA-busy gate at LEDs.cpp:184/193. Both strips are pushed every time because the bbDirty/topDirty skip is commented out (LEDs.cpp:167-172).
- Menu-open's hard delayMicroseconds(15000) at Menus.cpp:1110 — 15 ms of nothing, once per open, executed on the firstTime pass before the first line is ever drawn. It is the single biggest term in the open path and predates the click/rotation interlock that now owns press-bounce rejection.
- The click/rotation interlock deliberately deafens the wheel for the first 100–135 ms of every menu session: CLICK_SUPPRESS_AFTER_US = 100000 (RotaryEncoder.cpp:443, applied at :1160) plus STEP_CONFIRM_US = 35000 (:444, applied at :1176). Correct behavior, but it is why the menu 'doesn't respond' right after you click it in.
- Menu-exit terminal reprint: 7 B of clear-screen plus printMenu() at 512 B (showExtraMenu = 0), 1066 B (level 1), 1668 B (level 2) or 1990 B (level 3) — each of ~55 printMenuLine calls also emits an ~11 B SGR escape from cycleTerminalColor (Graphics.cpp:5762). Above the 1024 B CDC TX FIFO (custom_tusb_config.h:165), Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write() spins in yield() until the host drains (Adafruit_USBD_CDC.cpp:246-255): ~16 extra 64 B packets at level 3.
- Core-0 loop delay floor: 480 us of pure delayMicroseconds per idle menu pass (400 at Menus.cpp:1046 + 80 at :1638), plus 100 us on every event pass (:1183 / :1388). This sets event-detection granularity at ~250 us average and, more importantly, gives the 10 ms BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US window only ~20 polls to catch a click.
- Encoder push RC network: ~5–6.3 ms rise before the release edge is even readable (R8 51.1k + R22 1k, C27 100 nF, tau = 5.21 ms; RotaryEncoder.cpp:348-351). Hardware, unfixable in firmware, but it is a real 5 ms floor under menu-open latency because the menu opens on RELEASE.

**Stalls / races:**
- Menus.cpp:565-567 (-2 exit) posts the restore show BEFORE clearing the ownership flag: b.clear(); requestLedShow(-1); inClickMenu = 0. The core-1 paint gate at main.cpp:1811 requires inClickMenu == 0, so a request taken inside that window is consumed and completed with needsLedShow = false and nothing drawn — and the LED_CLEAR bit has already latched clearBeforeSend = 1 (main.cpp:1783), which then leaks into whatever show comes next. The menuSessionRan path (:608-619) has the same three statements in the CORRECT order, so the two exits disagree. Window is only two core-0 statements wide and LED_NETS is tick-scheduled (8 ms), so it is rare — but when it hits, the menu text sits on the breadboard until the 51 ms swirl tick (main.cpp:2010) repaints it.
- Menus.cpp:544-548 (-3 probe-button exit) returns WITHOUT clearing inClickMenu, without b.clear()/requestLedShow(), without menuTransitionCancel() and without the terminal restore. If it ever fires, Menus::service() (Menus.cpp:62-65) returns BLOCKING forever and main.cpp:1082-1084 (if (menus.inClickMenu != 0) goto loadfile) triggers a FULL SLOT RELOAD on every busy-loop pass — an unbounded wedge, not a glitch. Today it is unreachable: getMenuSelection() only returns -1 when Serial.available() != 0, which immediately fails the outer while's own Serial.available() == 0 test at :541. Latent, but it is one refactor away from live.
- logoLedAccess (Graphics.cpp:4387) is a plain volatile bool test-and-set used as a cross-core lock, and every loser just returns with NO retry. Core 1 takes it in logoSwirl on every menu flush (LEDs.cpp:3768-3771); core 0 takes it in setLogoOverride (LEDs.cpp:1448), setLogoRing (LEDs.cpp:3768) and clearColorOverrides (LEDs.cpp:1575). So a detent's breadcrumb-pad update (setMenuDepthLEDs, Menus.cpp:775, six setLogoOverride calls) or its ring update can be SILENTLY DROPPED whenever core 1 is mid-logoSwirl — the pads just show the previous level's colors for one detent. The same flag makes the menu-exit clearColorOverrides at :610 a no-op on contention, stranding the depth pads lit after the menu closes — precisely the symptom the exitMenuModeForAction comment at :461-467 was written to fix.
- Menus.cpp:1020-1022 fires requestLedShow(2) on EVERY pass while the button is physically down or a hold-step is animating — ~2000 requests/second against a strip that can latch 107 frames/second. Core 1 coalesces the pending bit, but each accepted request still runs lightUpRail + logoSwirl + menuTransitionRender + a leds.show() attempt that the DMA-busy gate mostly drops, and it holds ledShowIdle() false so the swirl branch (main.cpp:2010) is starved for the whole hold.
- RotaryEncoder.cpp:571 calls requestLedShow(1) from core 1 on every button release, including releases inside a menu. Core 1 takes it, gets rails == 1, and the inClickMenu == 0 gate at main.cpp:1811 kills the paint — but lightUpRail(-1,-1,1) and logoSwirl (main.cpp:1789-1793) have already run. Every click inside a menu pays for a nets frame that is thrown away.
- Dropped click: BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US = 10000 us (RotaryEncoder.cpp:402). Any core-0 stretch longer than 10 ms between polls lets core 1 auto-clear the RELEASED event (:552-558) and the click is silently eaten; the codebase already counts these in encoderClickAutoClears. A single OLED frame from ANY core-0 writer is 12.2 ms — longer than the window. So a wheel click that lands while the OLED is pushing a frame does not open the menu.
- Frame-tear window between menuTransitionRender() clearing mtRenderBusy and JeoPixel's pixels[] → dma_buffer copy (Jeopixel_RP2.cpp:275-277). menuTransitionBeginDraw (MenuTransitions.cpp:167) waits only on mtRenderBusy, and menuTransitionCanShow (main.cpp:1938) is checked before leds.show(), not during the copy. ~10–20 us per frame in which a core-0 repaint can be half-captured into the DMA buffer.
- leds.show() DROPS a frame outright when either DMA is busy (LEDs.cpp:184/193) with no retry of its own. The only recovery is MT_LINGER_MS = 50 ms of extra show attempts plus the 16 ms re-trigger — which exists solely to paper over this. Any painter that does not use the beginDraw/arm bracket falls back to menuShowKeepalive (Menus.cpp:483-490), which retries at only 50 ms.
- UP/DOWN surface incoherence. Turning UP onto an action row (Menus.cpp:1217-1227) prints the label to the terminal AND updates the OLED. Turning DOWN onto the same row (:1428-1432) prints " \n" and does NOT touch the OLED — so the breadboard shows the new item, the OLED shows the PREVIOUS item, and the terminal shows a blank line. Font-preview rows compound it: textSize 2 on UP (:1212) vs textSize 1 on DOWN (:1423). The same row looks different depending on which way you turned the wheel.
- Terminal incoherence within a single scroll: label rows rewrite in place with \r + 46 spaces + \r (:1186), action rows use Serial.println() (:1223) and scroll. Scrolling past a mixed list leaves a ragged trail of action-row lines interleaved with one rewritten label line.
- Menu exit via an action never repaints the OLED (Menus.cpp:607-628) — the last menu line stays on the panel until some unrelated writer (ReadingDisplay, OledGui, an app) happens to repaint. Only the -2 path calls showJogo32h (:571). Two exits, two different final OLED states.
- exitMenuTime = 9995000 (Menus.cpp:444) is 9,995 SECONDS (2 h 46 m), so the no-input menu timeout never fires. The whole returnToMenuPosition / returnToMenuLevel / returningFromTimeout restore machinery (:1040-1044, :1084-1092, :1116-1120) is dead code in practice, yet returningFromTimeout still suppresses the menuPosition += 1 at :1118 and has to be cleared at :1120.
- Any single byte on USB CDC closes the menu: the loop condition is while (Serial.available() == 0) at Menus.cpp:998. A host tool that writes one keepalive byte to port 1 yanks the wheel menu out from under the user mid-navigation.
