Pure statement reordering: the breadboard — the primary surface — gets its show request ~12.2 ms earlier, and the OLED still lands before the next input poll. Cuts perceived detent latency from ~22.6 ms to ~10.4 ms with no behavior change.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp:565` Fix the -2 exit ordering to mirror the menuSessionRan block at :608-619 EXACTLY: inClickMenu = 0; logoRing.enabled = false; clearColorOverrides(false,true,false); menuTransitionCancel(); b.clear(); requestLedShow(-1). menuTransitionCancel MUST come before b.clear() or a linger frame can restomp the cleared buffer. Removes the swallowed-restore race and the leaked clearBeforeSend latch.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp:544` Give the -3 probe-button exit the same teardown as the other two paths (inClickMenu = 0, menuTransitionCancel, b.clear, requestLedShow(-1)) before returning. Today it leaves inClickMenu = 1, which would pin Menus::service() at BLOCKING and fire main.cpp:1084's goto loadfile — a full slot reload — on every busy-loop pass. Currently unreachable, so this is pure insurance against a future refactor making it live.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp:1428` Replace the DOWN branch's action-row body (Serial.println(" ") and no OLED write) with a copy of the UP branch's body at :1220-1226 — label to the terminal, same clearPrintShow. Also make the font-preview textSize match (1 here vs 2 at :1212). Restores UP/DOWN parity so the three surfaces agree regardless of turn direction.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp:1020` Gate the pressed-state flush behind ledShowIdle() plus a ~16 ms timer, the same shape as menuShowKeepalive at :483. Today this fires on every ~500 us pass while the button is down — a ~2 kHz request flood at a strip that latches 107 fps. The center-V press animation is paced by holdAnimationStuff(), not by this call, so the visual is unchanged; core 1 stops burning lightUpRail + logoSwirl + a doomed show attempt 2000 times a second and the swirl branch stops starving.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp:1110` Delete (or cut to ~1-2 ms) the hard delayMicroseconds(15000) on the firstTime pass. Nothing downstream reads state that the delay establishes — it predates the click/rotation interlock, which now owns press-bounce rejection via CLICK_SUPPRESS_AFTER_US. Removes 15 ms from every single menu open (42.5 ms → 27.5 ms with the OLED on).
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp:1046` Drop delayMicroseconds(400) to ~100 us (and consider deleting the 80 us at :1638). Halves the average event-detect latency from 250 us to ~60 us and, more importantly, gives the 10 ms BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US click window ~5x more polls to hit — directly attacking the dropped-click class that encoderClickAutoClears counts. Bench for TinyUSB pump churn and MpRemote reentrancy at the higher serviceInner rate.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/main.cpp:1789` Skip lightUpRail(-1,-1,1) when rails == 2 && inClickMenu == 1. The rails and header do not change during a menu, yet this ~100-pixel float-math pass runs on every one of the ~13 flushes per detent. Not strictly behavior-preserving: a rail voltage changed by a menu action would repaint one frame (16 ms) later than it does now.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/platformio.ini:49` Add -DI2C_BUFFER_LENGTH=256 to build_flags so Adafruit_SSD1306's WIRE_MAX (Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:62-71) becomes 255 instead of 63/32. The 512 B framebuffer then ships in 3 transactions instead of 9 (or 17), saving 12-32 control+address bytes = 0.27-0.7 ms per OLED frame. Small but free. Bench that no other Wire consumer on either bus assumes the 32 B default.
- [safe-tonight/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp:619` Repaint the OLED on the action-exit path the way the -2 path does at :571, or hand the panel back to ReadingDisplay explicitly. Today an action exit leaves the last menu line on the panel indefinitely if no live reading is configured — the two exit paths leave the OLED in two different states.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/LEDs.cpp:1448` Make logoLedAccess a real lock (pico spin_lock or a bounded retry) instead of drop-on-contention. Every menu detent's breadcrumb-pad and ring write can currently be silently lost to a concurrent core-1 logoSwirl, and the menu-exit clearColorOverrides can no-op and strand the pads lit. It is load-bearing against core-1 renders, so bench for added core-1 jitter.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/MenuTransitions.h:50` Cut menuTransitionConfig.durationMs from 160 to ~80. Halves the shows-per-detent from ~13 to ~8 and the strip duty from 58% to ~36%, so a fast scroll's detents actually land instead of each one restarting a 210 ms blend that the next detent interrupts. Purely a visual-length change.
- [design-change/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.cpp:2311` Partial-window OLED update for menu navigation: menu text occupies a subset of the panel's 4 pages, so bounding ssd1306_commandList(PAGEADDR/COLUMNADDR) to the dirty pages and shipping only those bytes cuts the frame proportionally (one 128 B page = ~3.0 ms instead of 12.2 ms). Requires reaching past Adafruit_GFX's display() into a project-owned partial-flush, hence design-change.

**Unification notes:** The modal input loop is hand-rolled at least nine times in Menus.cpp alone, and each copy re-derives its own constants. getMenuSelection (:998), selectSubmenuOption (:1810), yesNoMenu (:2471), selectNodeAction (:2592), getActionFloat (:2862), getActionInt (:3367), getActionString (:3636), LEDbrightnessMenu (:5065) and runHistoryScrubMenu (:5783) all spell out the same shape — while(...) { jOS.serviceInner(); rotaryEncoderButtonStuff(); read encoderDirectionState; b.clear/b.print; oled.clearPrintShow; requestLedShow(2); delayMicroseconds(...) } — with different delays, different OLED-mirroring policies and different flush cadences. Peripherals.cpp:3405, PartsApp.cpp:618 and ProjectsApp.cpp:704 are three more copies outside this file. The terminal erase idiom alone appears at 20 sites in Menus.cpp at six different widths (48 at :1186/:1391, 32, 30, 28, 26, 24) — the same "rewrite the line in place" gesture, each caller guessing its own field width.

The atomic-repaint bracket exists in exactly ONE of them. renderMenuLine (Menus.cpp:894) is the only painter that wraps its writes in menuTransitionBeginDraw/menuTransitionArm, which is what actually guarantees core 1 never streams a half-drawn buffer. Every other modal screen paints bare, which is why menuShowKeepalive (Menus.cpp:483-490) had to be invented — it is a second, weaker implementation of the same guarantee, retrying at 50 ms instead of gating properly. Any of those screens is one unification away from getting the real bracket for free.

Debounce and click classification are implemented three times over one hardware-debounced pin: rotaryEncoderButtonStuffLocked's PRESSED/RELEASED/HELD machine with BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US (RotaryEncoder.cpp:507), EncoderClickTracker with its own lastEdgeMs/debounceMs (RotaryEncoder.cpp:625), and getMenuSelection's own 2-second "wait for physical release" spin at Menus.cpp:1088-1094 plus its own HOLD_BACK_REPEAT_MS pacing at :984. Probing.cpp adds a fourth for the probe tip's double-tap. The RotaryEncoder.cpp:437-443 FOLLOW-UP comment already names the fix: collapse encoderPosition (numeric editors) and encoderDirectionState/position (menus) onto one committedRaw with the AFTER window and confirm gate as policies over it.

"Who owns the LEDs right now" is six independent latches: inClickMenu (Menus.h:38), inPadMenu, g_historyScrubActive (Menus.h:99), hideNets, ledGraphicsOwned() (Commands.h:23) and logoLedAccess (Graphics.h:82). Core 1's paint gate at main.cpp:1809-1813 has to spell out four of them in a single boolean, and Graphics.cpp:2088 (the net-currents marching-ants animation) re-checks two of them independently. Every new modal screen has to remember to raise and lower the right subset — which is exactly how the -2 and -3 exits ended up wrong.

Menu-exit restoration is written three times inside this one file with three different results: the menuSessionRan block (:607-628 — LEDs and terminal, no OLED, correct ordering), the -2 block (:553-573 — LEDs and OLED, no terminal, inverted ordering), and exitMenuModeForAction (:468-473 — logo and pads only). Probing.cpp:3964 and PartsApp.cpp:710 add a fourth and fifth variant. One restoreAfterMenu() that takes a "what did I own" mask would collapse all five and make the ordering bug impossible to reintroduce.

Redraw scheduling has three independent pacers for the same 300 pixels: menuTransitionFrameDue's 16 ms tick (main.cpp:1747), menuShowKeepalive's 50 ms cadence (Menus.cpp:483) and the 51 ms logo-swirl timer (main.cpp:2010) — plus 40-odd bare requestLedShow(2) calls in Menus.cpp that pace nothing at all. The LEDs.cpp:167-172 FOLLOW-UP comment already flags the sibling problem on the output side: bbDirty/topDirty are written on every setPixelColor and then ignored, and clearBeforeSend / clearLEDsExceptRails / hideNets / leds.clear / bread::clear are five overlapping ways to say "blank the strip first."

## Entering and leaving apps: clickwheel menu → runApp() → app modal loop → exit → idle board (Apps.cpp / PartsApp / eyecandy apps)

**Total: ENTRY (wheel click on the Apps row → first app frame): typical ≈ 10.6 ms to a fully latched LED frame = 0.25 (mean encoder poll) + 0.28 (mean menu-loop detect) + 0.56 (the extra pass the DOWN-branch wrap costs) + 0.05 (transition drain) + 0.03 (getActionCategory) + 0.10 (runApp name scan + println) + 0.15 (pushContext mutex, uncontended) + 0.05 (partsDrawItem paint) + 0.15 (core-1 pickup) + 9.00 (WS2812 bb DMA, 300 px × 24 bit × 1.25 µs). **Dominant term: the 9.0 ms WS2812 frame — it is the floor; everything else in the entry chain sums to ~1.6 ms.** Click → first OLED item ≈ 13.5 ms typical (the 12.0 ms I2C flush replaces the DMA as the tail, and core 0 is unresponsive for all of it). Worst case ≈ 19 ms (500 µs poll + 2 full loop passes + 3 ms transition drain + 5 ms contended core_sync mutex).

EXIT (last app action → idle board + terminal main menu): typical ≈ 25 ms = 13 (showJogo32h: 1–3 ms uncached FatFS read of images/bubbleJumpThin.bin + 12 ms blocking I2C) + 0.2 (popContext) + 7 (refreshConnections: 2 ms waitCore2 + 2.0 ms rebuild + 1.3 ms CH446Q DMA send + 2 ms LED wait) + 4 (printMenu ~1.3 KB out USB CDC with a blocking flush), with the second LED frame's 9 ms DMA overlapped. **Dominant terms: the uncached jogo file read + 12 ms OLED flush, and the CDC terminal repaint — the routing rebuild is only 2 ms of it.** Worst case > 1 s if core 1 is captured (25 ms waitCore2 timeout + the 1000 ms REQ_SEND wait at Commands.cpp:280).

PLACEMENT EXIT (accepted probe tap → idle board) ≈ 840 ms = 180 (tap confirmation flash, PartsApp.cpp:289) + 7 (refreshConnections inside partsCommitPlacement) + 12 (OLED \"placed\" toast) + **800 (bare delay(800) at PartsApp.cpp:491)** + 25 (the exit chain above). **Dominant term: the hard-coded 800 ms toast delay — 95 % of the exit, during which nothing is serviced and any wheel backlog is discarded by the 250 ms DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US timer.**

TEMP-SLOT APP EXIT (Calib DACs / Switch Calib / Probe Calib / Full Test) adds exitTemporarySlot's slot-YAML reread + applyStateToHardware + its OWN refreshConnections(-1,0,1) ahead of doMenuAction's second one ⇒ ≈ 40–70 ms, two full routing rebuilds back to back.**

**Bottlenecks:**
- The 12.0 ms blocking OLED I2C flush is the single largest core-0 term on every hop of this path, and it appears at least four times per app session (entry frame, every wheel detent, every refusal/confirm toast, exit showJogo32h). Arithmetic: kOledI2CClockHz = 400 000 (oled.cpp:72); a 128×32 panel is 512 framebuffer bytes; 512 × 9 bits (8 data + ACK) ÷ 400 000 = 11.52 ms, plus ~16 chunk control bytes and the 7-byte page/column prelude ≈ 0.5 ms ⇒ ~12.0 ms. On a 128×64 panel it is 1024 × 9 ÷ 400 000 = 23.0 ms ⇒ ~24 ms. Adafruit_SSD1306::display() is fully synchronous (oled.cpp:2314) — no DMA, no dirty-window, no serviceInner during it.
- The 9.0 ms WS2812 frame is the irreducible floor on every visible LED change: bb strip = 300 px × 24 bits × 1.25 µs = 9.00 ms (top strip 145 × 30 µs = 4.35 ms in parallel on its own PIO). This matches the codebase's own measured 13.25 ms for the pre-DMA blocking both-strip show (PERFORMANCE_OPTIMIZATION_SUMMARY.md:22; 445 × 30 µs = 13.35 ms). Nothing in the app path can beat 9 ms to a fully repainted board.
- showJogo32h() re-reads /images/bubbleJumpThin.bin from FatFS on EVERY call (oled.cpp:3012 → loadBitmapFromFile, oled.cpp:448) with no cache — safeFileExists + safeFileOpen under fs_mutex + a 1024-byte read = 1–3 ms of flash traffic added to the 12 ms flush, on every app exit, every probe-mode exit and every menu cancel.
- PartsApp.cpp:491 delay(800) after a successful placement. It is 95 % of the tap→idle latency, it is unserviced (no jOS.serviceInner), and it exceeds DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US (250 ms) so any queued encoder motion is thrown away with it. PartsApp.cpp:451 delay(1200) on a geometry refusal is the same shape.
- The exit path renders the same final picture three or four times: PartsApp's requestLedShow(-1) (PartsApp.cpp:707), refreshConnections' requestLedShow(-1) + waitCore2 (Commands.cpp:300-301), clickMenu's b.clear + requestLedShow(-1) (Menus.cpp:618-619), and printMenu's clearHighlighting() → requestLedShow(1) (Highlighting.h:113). Each un-coalesced one costs core 1 a showNets (1.56 ms) plus a 9 ms DMA frame.
- ContextManager::pushContext/popContext take core_sync_acquire() = mutex_enter_blocking with NO timeout (externVars.cpp:97, called from JumperlOS.cpp:945 and :983). Core 1 holds that same mutex for its entire LED render block (main.cpp:1722–2000: showNets + readGPIO + readFakeGPIO + showLEDmeasurements + showAllRowAnimations + renderGraphicOverlays + show). So entering and leaving every app can stall core 0 for a full core-1 frame, ~2–5 ms each, twice per app.
- printMenu() on every app exit: ~14 colored menu lines ≈ 1–1.5 KB out USB CDC preceded by \x1b[2J\x1b[H, with a blocking Serial.flush() (Menus.cpp:625-627). At realistic FS bulk-CDC drain this is 2–10 ms and is entirely gratuitous for a wheel-driven app the user never looked at the terminal for.

**Stalls / races:**
- DROPPED CLICK (confirmed, per-panel severity). A RELEASED event auto-expires 10 ms after buttonEventTimestamp (BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US = 10 000 µs, RotaryEncoder.cpp:402; the auto-clear at :552-556 sets lastButtonEncoderState = RELEASED, so the click cannot be reconstructed and bumps the encoderClickAutoClears diagnostic). Every picker redraw blocks core 0 for the full OLED flush with no serviceInner. Window arithmetic: a click is lost when the release lands at t within the flush and t + 10 ms < flush length. 128×32: flush 12 ms ⇒ t < 2 ms ⇒ ~17 % of mid-redraw clicks drop. 128×64: flush 24 ms ⇒ t < 14 ms ⇒ ~58 % — the MAJORITY of mid-redraw clicks drop on 64-tall panels. Affects PartsApp.cpp:57, ProjectsApp.cpp:647 and every app that calls clearPrintShow/showMultiLineSmallText inside its input loop.
- PROBE PRESS SURVIVES THE APP AND DROPS YOU INTO PROBE MODE. ProbeButton::buttonPress is a sticky latch with no age-out (written at Probing.cpp:498/506, cleared only by getButtonPress consume at :610 or clearButtonState). Probing::service() is HIGH priority and NOT in the inner set, so handleProbeButtonActions() (Probing.cpp:1471) never runs during an app. popContext's flush switch (JumperlOS.cpp:1022-1031) covers PYTHON_REPL / MAIN_MENU / CLICKWHEEL_MENU / DEBUG_MENU — APP_GENERIC, the type runApp actually pushes (Apps.cpp:187), is absent. The comment right above it (JumperlOS.cpp:1019) claims "getButtonPress() also rejects stale latches by age"; the code does not implement any such age check. So a press during e.g. calibrateDacs' delay(1000) blocks, or PartsApp's delay(800) toast, fires probeMode() on the first serviceAll pass after the app exits.
- DOUBLE-TAP UNDO FIRES INSIDE APPS. ProbeButton IS in the inner set (CRITICAL), so its PIO-IRQ double-tap machine keeps arming (Probing.cpp:484-562) and its deferred drain (Probing.cpp:166-195) runs undoUndo() + undoToast() from inside jOS.serviceInner() while the app owns core 0. In a temp-slot app (netSlot = 8, cleared state) the undo ring is the EMPTY slot-8 ring, so a genuine user undo gesture is silently swallowed with no toast and no feedback; in PartsApp it repaints the board under the picker mid-placement.
- TEMPORARY SLOT HAS NO NESTING DEPTH. enterTemporarySlot returns false when temporarySlotActive is already set (States.cpp:3951) — silently, return value ignored at every call site (Apps.cpp:422, :728, :1338, :1981; SelfTest.cpp:1171) — but exitTemporarySlot (States.cpp:3985) unconditionally exits. A nested app's leaveApp() therefore restores the OUTER app's original slot mid-session; the outer leaveApp() then no-ops, and the outer app's remaining hardware writes and any markDirty() land in the USER'S real slot. Reachable via MicroPython run_app() (JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2598) from a script launched inside another app, and via projectRunFromTemplate re-entry.
- UNBALANCED CONTEXT POP. runApp pushes APP_GENERIC without checking the return (Apps.cpp:192) but pops unconditionally (:200). pushContext REJECTS a duplicate type (JumperlOS.cpp:934-941) after printing a WARNING and dumping the whole stack to Serial mid-launch — so a nested runApp pops the PARENT's context instead, running the parent's onExit and losing its cleanup.
- RE-ENTRANT CLICK MENU ON APP EXIT (double-fire). Apps that exit on a raw button-down never wait for release: bounceStartup (Apps.cpp:1724, :1728), customApp (:1385, :1475, :1496, :1524, :1540, :1550, :1565), xlsxGui (:1604), scanBoard on encoderButtonState == PRESSED without clearing it (:1784). Core 1's state machine then produces RELEASED with lastButtonEncoderState == PRESSED on the physical release — exactly clickMenu's entry pattern (Menus.cpp:506) — so the click menu re-opens the moment the user lifts their finger. calibrateDacs (:2720-2723) is the one that gets it right by forcing IDLE; switchCalibWaitClick (:391-405) is the correct pattern.
- SERIAL BYTE LEAKS INTO THE COMMAND HANDLER. The same raw-exit apps break on Serial.available() > 0 without consuming the byte (scanBoard Apps.cpp:1748, bounceStartup :1724, xlsxGui :1604, calibrateDacs :2717). After runApp returns, the main busy loop's `Jerial.available() == 0` condition fails (main.cpp:999) and the byte executes as a single-char command. PartsApp (:128-131) and ProjectsApp (:742-746) deliberately consume it and document why.
- clearColorOverrides() IS A SILENT NO-OP UNDER A RACE. LEDs.cpp:1575 returns early if logoLedAccess is true; that flag is a plain volatile bool taken with a non-atomic test-and-set by logoSwirl() on CORE 1 (LEDs.cpp:3768-3771) while exitMenuModeForAction() calls clearColorOverrides on CORE 0 (Menus.cpp:471). When it loses, the menu depth pads stay lit for the whole app session. There is no retry — only clickMenu's cleanup (Menus.cpp:610) gets a second try after the app has already run.
- TRANSITION DEAD TIME: A FLASH OF NETS MID-LAUNCH. exitMenuModeForAction() zeroes inClickMenu at Menus.cpp:4574; partsAppLauncher raises it again at PartsApp.cpp:618. In that window (~0.2–5 ms, dominated by the blocking pushContext mutex) core 1's 51 ms swirl tick (main.cpp:2012) can fire a full idle render — showNets() into the just-cleared buffer plus a 9 ms DMA — so ~1–10 % of launches flash one frame of nets before the app's first frame. The same window on exit lets the pre-refresh net picture land before refreshConnections rebuilds it.
- CORE 1 PARKED = WHEEL DEAD. bounceStartup takes holdCore1Frames() for its whole animation (Apps.cpp:1718). Because rotaryEncoderStuff/rotaryEncoderButtonStuff are core-1-only (RotaryEncoder.cpp:996, :1033), the entire button and detent state machine freezes — which is why that app has to poll the raw GPIO — and on release the resumed state machine emits the stale click described above. refreshConnections takes the same hold for ~2 ms on every rebuild (Commands.cpp:209).
- UNSERVICED SPIN. eKiloApp's entry gate is `while (Serial.available() == 0) delayMicroseconds(100);` with no jOS.serviceInner() (FilesystemStuff.cpp:2793-2795): no USB pump, no probe-button drain, no AsyncPassthrough bridge, and no way out from the wheel. It waits indefinitely on a keypress the user may never send.
- STALE OLED AFTER EXIT. runSnakeGame leaves its instruction text on the panel forever (GraphicOverlays.cpp:769-776 — no showJogo32h, no oled.clear); nothing else in the exit chain touches the OLED. Symmetrically, when StepViewer is armed, PartsApp's oled.showJogo32h() early-returns at oled.cpp:2991 without painting, so the panel keeps the last picker frame until OledGuiService's next tick — which the one-pass BLOCKING latch (Menus.cpp:70) delays by an extra serviceAll pass.

**Quick wins:**
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlOS.cpp:1022` Add ContextType::APP_GENERIC to popContext's probe-button flush switch. One case label; it makes the code do what the comment two lines above it already claims ("discard any probe-button press latched while we were inside an interactive child context"). Kills the "exit an app, land in probe mode" surprise for every apps[] row.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Apps.cpp:185` Check pushContext's return value in runApp and only pop when the push succeeded: `bool pushed = !appPushesOwnContext && ContextManager::getInstance().pushContext(ctx);` then `if (pushed) popContext();`. Today a rejected duplicate push still pops, tearing down the parent's context.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Apps.cpp:1724` Make the raw-button exits wait for release before returning, using the pattern that already exists in this file (switchCalibWaitClick's `delay(50); encoderButtonState = IDLE;` at Apps.cpp:403-405, or getMenuSelection's serviced `while (isEncoderButtonPhysicallyPressed())` with a 2 s bail at Menus.cpp:1097). Apply at bounceStartup (:1724, :1728), customApp's eight exits, xlsxGui (:1604) and scanBoard (:1784). Stops the click menu re-opening the instant the user lifts their finger.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp:769` Add `oled.showJogo32h();` (or at minimum oled.clear()) at snake_exit so the game's instruction text does not sit on the panel indefinitely after the app returns. Every other picker-shaped app already ends this way (PartsApp.cpp:710, ImagesApp.cpp:430).
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp:2793` Put jOS.serviceInner() inside eKiloApp's entry spin (it currently only delayMicroseconds(100)), so the USB pump, probe button and AsyncPassthrough bridge stay alive while the app waits for Enter. Matches every other modal loop in the codebase.
- [safe-tonight/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/LEDs.cpp:696` Delete the dead code. colorPicker() returns unconditionally on its first line, so ~700 lines below it — including the only two writers of the global `hideNets` (LEDs.cpp:780 sets, :1396 clears) — can never execute; and oled::resetMultiLineSmallText() has an empty body, so the ~8 call sites that "reset the scroll position" do nothing. Removing both stops future readers reasoning about state that cannot change.
- [needs-bench/behavior-preserving] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.cpp:3012` Cache the idle-logo bitmap instead of re-reading /images/bubbleJumpThin.bin from FatFS on every showJogo32h(). customBitmapBuffer is already a 1 KB static — load once, keep a loaded-path + mtime stamp, and skip the safeFileExists/open/read. Saves 1–3 ms on every app exit, menu cancel and probe-mode exit. NEEDS BENCH: customBitmapBuffer/customBitmapWidth/Height are exposed to MicroPython and users can replace the file at runtime, so the cache needs an explicit invalidation hook on write.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:402` Raise BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US above the worst blocking OLED flush (12 ms on 128×32, 24 ms on 128×64 — so ~40 ms with margin), or have the auto-clear path preserve lastButtonEncoderState = PRESSED so a late poller can still see the edge. Closes the 17 %/58 % dropped-click window without touching any redraw code. NEEDS BENCH: this lengthens the window in which a consumed-then-restored click can be double-read by two pollers; verify against the encoderClickAutoClears / dblEdgeSuppressed diagnostics that already exist for exactly this question (task #27).
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp:618` Cut the redundant exit renders. clickMenu's cleanup b.clear() + requestLedShow(-1) (Menus.cpp:618-619) repaints exactly what refreshConnections(-1,0) at Menus.cpp:4578 just rendered; printMenu's clearHighlighting() posts a third. Gate the cleanup show on whether the action arm already refreshed (the APPSACTION/CALIBRATIONACTION/HISTORYACTION arms all call refreshConnections before returning 10). Saves one showNets (1.56 ms) plus one 9 ms DMA frame per app exit. NEEDS BENCH: the b.clear + NEGATIVE show is load-bearing for the paths that DON'T refresh (the comment at Menus.cpp:613-617 says so); the gate must be per-arm, not blanket.
- [needs-bench/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp:491` Replace PartsApp's bare delay(800) placement toast (and the delay(1200) refusal toast at :451) with a serviced wait that pumps jOS.serviceInner() and exits early on any input — the pattern already written twice in this file (partsPicker's hold-drain at :118-123). Turns 800 ms of dead time into 800 ms of live time and stops the encoder backlog being discarded by the 250 ms abandon timer. NEEDS BENCH: servicing during the toast lets ProbeButton's double-tap drain fire an undo mid-toast, which today it cannot.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/routing/States.cpp:3943` Give the temporary slot a nesting depth (or make leaveApp() a scoped RAII guard that only exits the slot it actually entered). Today enterTemporarySlot silently refuses to nest while exitTemporarySlot always exits, so a nested app's leaveApp() drops the OUTER app's slot and the outer app's remaining hardware writes land in the user's real slot. DESIGN-CHANGE: it alters the ownership contract in States.h:841-844 and every one of the five call sites currently ignores the return value.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Apps.cpp:1748` Consume the exiting serial byte in scanBoard / bounceStartup / xlsxGui / calibrateDacs so it does not fall through to the single-char command handler, matching the convention PartsApp.cpp:128-131 and ProjectsApp.cpp:742-746 spell out. DESIGN-CHANGE, NOT a bug fix: the current behavior means "press n to leave scanBoard and immediately get the net list," which may be a deliberate workflow. The picker convention is an argument for changing it, not proof it is wrong — ask before touching.
- [design-change/CHANGES-BEHAVIOR] `/Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp:57` Bracket the app pickers' repaints with menuTransitionBeginDraw()/menuTransitionArm(), or route them through Menus.cpp's renderMenuLine(). PartsApp's partsDrawItem and ProjectsApp's drawPickerItem paint b.clear + b.print + requestLedShow(2) unbracketed, so core 1's menu branch (main.cpp:1917/1932, which checks menuTransitionCanShow) has nothing to hold off on and can stream a half-painted buffer. DESIGN-CHANGE: it opts the pickers into the transition engine's blend frames, which is a visible look change, not just a tear fix.

**Unification notes:** WHAT THIS PATH REIMPLEMENTS, AND WHO ELSE IMPLEMENTS IT:

1. THE PICKER LOOP EXISTS FOUR TIMES. PartsApp::partsPicker (PartsApp.cpp:82) says so in its own header comment — it is "runPicker's ownership discipline (ProjectsApp.cpp:683) rendering from rodata const char* instead of String". The four copies are Menus.cpp's getMenuSelection/selectSubmenuOption, ProjectsApp.cpp:684 runPicker, PartsApp.cpp:82 partsPicker, and ImagesApp.cpp:338. Each independently re-derives: save/restore rotaryDivider, raise/lower inClickMenu, zero the three encoder globals on entry, a HELD-cancels off-ramp, a serial-byte-cancels off-ramp, an LED-show keepalive, and a b.clear + requestLedShow(-1) exit. Only three of the four restore rotaryDivider (ImagesApp does not); only two consume the cancelling serial byte; only two wait out the hold. The single missing abstraction is a `Picker` type owning (inClickMenu, rotaryDivider, encoder state, LED buffer, OLED, terminal cursor) as one RAII scope.

2. THREE DIFFERENT ENCODER IDIOMS ON ONE WHEEL. Menus/ProjectsApp/PartsApp consume encoderDirectionState UP/DOWN and get the paced backlog queue for free (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1290-1308). ImagesApp.cpp:344-371 instead diffs the raw `encoderPosition` with its own ENCODER_THRESHOLD = 8 and SCROLL_DEBOUNCE = 10 ms — reimplementing rotaryDivider (also 8) and the detent hysteresis by hand, and bypassing the backlog so fast spins collapse. runSnakeGame (GraphicOverlays.cpp:693, :721) is a third variant: raw position delta with a ±2 threshold and a 250 ms ENCODER_TURN_COOLDOWN_MS. The divider/hysteresis logic already lives in one place; two of the three consumers duplicate it badly.

3. THE LED-SHOW KEEPALIVE IS COPY-PASTED THREE TIMES. Menus.cpp:483 menuShowKeepalive (50 ms cadence, gated on ledShowIdle()), PartsApp.cpp:111 (250 ms, ungated), ProjectsApp.cpp:728 (250 ms, ungated) — the two app copies even cite the Menus one in their comments. All three exist to paper over the same thing: core 1's take-and-complete can swallow a request posted mid-frame. That belongs inside requestLedShow/CoreMailbox, not in three UI loops with three different cadences.

4. THE MENU-EXIT TEARDOWN IS WRITTEN FOUR TIMES. Menus.cpp:468 exitMenuModeForAction, Menus.cpp:546-548 (probe-button bail), Menus.cpp:565-571 (cancel bail), Menus.cpp:607-628 (session cleanup) all re-list inClickMenu = 0 / logoRing.enabled = false / clearColorOverrides / menuTransitionCancel / b.clear / requestLedShow(-1) — the comment at :461-467 is a scar from exactly this having drifted apart before. The app exit chain then runs three of these back to back (app's own, refreshConnections', clickMenu's) for one visible result.

5. HOLD-DRAIN AND BUTTON-REST ARE BOTH REINVENTED. "Wait out the hold so the release can't echo into the next level" appears at PartsApp.cpp:118-123, PartsApp.cpp:227-232, Menus.cpp:1097-1102 and, with different semantics again, ProjectsApp.cpp:779 waitForButtonRest (150 ms of continuous rest, 3 globals reset). EncoderClickTracker (RotaryEncoder.cpp:621-660) is the proper abstraction for this and none of these four use it.

6. THE "OLED SHOWS A TWO-LINE ITEM" PAINT IS THREE FUNCTIONS. PartsApp.cpp:64-69, ProjectsApp.cpp:656-663, StepViewer.cpp's viewerBuildScreen/showStep — the first two do resetMultiLineSmallText() + showMultiLineSmallText() (and resetMultiLineSmallText is an EMPTY function, oled.cpp:2816, so both are calling a no-op believing it resets scroll state), the third builds a retained OledScreen and lets OledGuiService render it on the right core. Only the StepViewer path is deferred; the two app paths block core 0 for 12 ms inline and are the ones that drop clicks.

BRIGHTNESS, EXPLICITLY (the task names it): NO app on this path reads or writes LEDbrightness / LEDbrightnessRail / LEDbrightnessSpecial — a full grep of Apps.cpp, PartsApp.cpp, ProjectsApp.cpp, the eyecandy apps and the snakes finds only Highlighting.cpp:1873/:1911 READING them. So brightness is neither saved nor restored around apps, and nothing needs it to be. The one real brightness-loss bug is on the SIBLING menu path, not the app path: Menus.cpp's brightness selector has `case 3:` (LEDbrightness / display.led_brightness, :2164-2179) FALLING THROUGH into `case 4:` (LEDbrightnessSpecial / display.special_net_brightness, :2181) — the `break` is missing after case 3's b.print block. Setting LED brightness therefore silently rewrites special-net brightness to `highlightedOption * 5 + 5` as well, and both are persisted through jumperlessConfig, so the corruption survives reboot. Worth folding into the same pass since it is the only place in this whole area where a display setting is permanently lost.

SLOT STATE, EXPLICITLY: the only save/restore that exists anywhere on this path is SlotManager::enterTemporarySlot/exitTemporarySlot (States.cpp:3943/:3985) and it is used by exactly five apps (Apps.cpp:422, :728, :1338, :1981; SelfTest.cpp:1171). It saves the slot NUMBER and PATH, conditionally flushes a dirty state to flash first, and on exit re-reads the original YAML and re-asserts power. It does NOT save or restore: the LED buffer, LED brightness, the OLED framebuffer, logoRing/colorOverride state, highlight state, overlay state, rotaryDivider, terminal line-buffering mode, or the probe-button latch. Every one of those is either torn down blind (exitMenuModeForAction), cleared as a side effect of something else (highlight state, via printMenu → clearHighlighting), or left stale (the OLED after Snake; the depth pads when clearColorOverrides loses the logoLedAccess race).


# Unification proposals

## InputEvent: one take/ack primitive with one expiry policy  (days)

**Problem:** Four incompatible answers to "one producer, one consumer, don't lose or replay the event", none sharing code. (a) TURN: paced emitter with an ack flag and a 250 ms abandon-the-whole-backlog rule (src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1290-1314, DIR_EVENT_ABANDON_US at :430). The designed ack channel `encoderDirectionConsumed` is used by exactly 2 consumers (src/Menus.cpp:5810, src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2853) while 44 sites ack by writing `encoderDirectionState = NONE` directly — which makes the driver's own stale-event guards at src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1165 and :1179 permanently dead code (the flag latches false after the first event). (b) CLICK: a 10 ms auto-expire window (BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US, src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:402; auto-clear at :549-557 which bumps `encoderClickAutoClears`), acked by writing IDLE — hand-rolled at 24 sites (src/Menus.cpp:506, :1054, :2009, :2544, :2830, :2998, :3454, :3839, :5848; src/PartsApp.cpp:132, :224, :574; src/ProjectsApp.cpp:747; src/Probing.cpp:5160, :5568, :5684; src/Peripherals.cpp:3507; src/Apps.cpp:875; src/oled.cpp:4653; src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp:393; src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:124; src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp:1685). (c) PROBE BUTTON: a sticky latch with NO age-out at all (src/Probing.cpp:601-613) — and src/JumperlOS.cpp:1019 documents an age check that does not exist, which is why a press latched inside an app survives into probe mode (APP_GENERIC is absent from the flush switch at src/JumperlOS.cpp:1022-1031). (d) PROBE TIP: a cached int plus a file-static `callSeq` strictly-consecutive gate (src/Probing.cpp:7635, :7710-7717) that any of 7 external justReadProbe callers can void indefinitely (src/LEDs.cpp:1160, src/Peripherals.cpp:3451, src/Menus.cpp:3031, src/PartsApp.cpp:280, src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2629/:2636, src/Apps.cpp:1500). Three of the four also lack a re-arm API: 5 sites poke the click state directly without refreshing the timestamp (src/Menus.cpp:1173-1174, :1373-1374, :1622-1623; src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2733-2734; src/selfreflection/Debugs.cpp:3234-3235) even though synthesizeEncoderClick() (src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1051-1055) exists precisely to do it correctly and has exactly one caller (src/Probing.cpp:4041).

**Proposal:** One templated slot type owned by the producing driver, with a single documented lifecycle, replacing all four ad-hoc protocols:

  template<class T> struct InputEvent {
      void post(T v);            // producer; stamps micros(), sets pending
      bool take(T& out);         // consumer; one-shot, clears pending, returns false if empty/expired
      bool peek(T& out) const;   // non-consuming
      uint32_t ageUs() const;
      void drop();               // explicit discard (context exit / mode change)
      static constexpr uint32_t kExpireUs; // ONE policy, per event kind, named in one place
  };

Instantiated four times next to `noteUserInput()` (src/coredination/externVars.h:71-74) and exposed as four free functions with identical shape: `bool takeEncoderClick()`, `bool takeEncoderTurn(int& dir)`, `bool takeProbeButton(int& btn)`, `bool takeProbeTap(int& node)`. Two rules the current code violates and this fixes structurally: (1) the expiry policy drops ONE event, never the backlog — the turn path's `lastPositionEncoder = encoderRaw` at src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1313 is the only one of the four that throws away everything queued behind a slow consumer, and it cannot tell "no UI is listening" (its stated purpose) from "the UI is busy in a 12 ms OLED write"; (2) posting is the only way to arm an event, so `synthesizeEncoderClick`-style re-arms become `post()` and cannot forget the timestamp.

Corollary the primitive makes cheap: `noteUserInput()` moves inside `post()`, which closes two current gaps — the encoder CLICK path never calls it (only the direction commits at src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1298/:1305 do), and the probe TIP never calls it (only button transitions, src/Probing.cpp:398). Today a user clicking through menus or dragging the probe tip looks idle to `systemIdleForFlush()` (src/coredination/externVars.h:76-80).

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/ProjectsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Apps.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlOS.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/coredination/externVars.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/selfreflection/Debugs.cpp

**V6 relevance:** This is the load-bearing one. A V6 input device (second wheel, D-pad, capacitive strip, a probe with a third button) currently has to invent its own hand-off protocol AND teach 24+ consumer sites to speak it, because there is no shared shape for 'an input event happened'. With InputEvent, new hardware posts into a new slot and every consumer that already speaks take()/drop() can be pointed at it. It also makes the ownership question askable: today `encoderDirectionState` is one unowned global that StepViewer, Highlighting, Menus, Probing, Graphics, the apps and MicroPython all read and clear, with the service registration order at src/main.cpp:549 existing solely to give StepViewer first refusal over Highlighting — an acknowledged priority hack that a second input source would make unmanageable.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Add `bool takeEncoderClick()` to src/RotaryEncoder.cpp doing exactly what the majority idiom does (test `encoderButtonState == RELEASED && lastButtonEncoderState == PRESSED`, set `encoderButtonState = IDLE`, return true) and convert ONLY the exact-match call sites — src/ProjectsApp.cpp:747, src/PartsApp.cpp:132, src/Menus.cpp:506, :2544, src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:124, src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp:393, src/Apps.cpp:875, src/oled.cpp:4653. Do NOT flatten the variants: sites that OR in a probe press (src/Menus.cpp:1054, :2009, :2830, :2998, :3454, :3839) or that also clear `lastButtonEncoderState` are load-bearing differences and belong in the full pass. Zero behavior change, and it makes the eventual expiry-policy edit a single-file change.

## ButtonGesture: one debounce + hold classifier for every binary input  (days)

**Problem:** Five software classifiers with five independently-tuned threshold sets sit over three physical buttons and one hardware RC, and no two agree on what a hold is. (1) The shared cross-core machine: hold = 500 ms (`buttonHoldLength`, src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:341), MEDIUM_HELD/LONG_HELD driven separately by holdAnimationStuff's 450 ms/50 ms step pacing (src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:687-689), no software debounce at all — deliberately, because ENC_PUSH is hardware-debounced (R8 51.1k + R22 1k + C27 100 nF, ~100 us fall / ~5 ms rise, documented at src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:346-352). (2) EncoderClickTracker: hold 500, longHold 1500, debounceMs 30 (src/RotaryEncoder.h:181-183, impl src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:636-667) — a third, software debounce layered on the same already-RC-debounced pin, and a long-hold 2 s earlier than the shared machine's. (3) ProbeButton: blockDurationMs 200, minimumBlockMs 20, releaseDebounceMs 30, connectHoldThresholdMs 800, removeHoldThresholdMs 1000 (src/Probing.h:207-217) plus `suppressPressUntilRelease` and `releaseConfirmed` (src/Probing.h:236-255). (4) getMenuSelection's own HOLD_BACK_REPEAT_MS = 800 (src/Menus.cpp:976) and its own 2-second wait-for-physical-release spin (src/Menus.cpp:1094-1100). (5) ProjectsApp::waitForButtonRest, 150 ms of continuous rest (src/ProjectsApp.cpp:772-800). Consequence: the wheel means different things in the file manager (which classifies on the physical pin via EncoderClickTracker, src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp:1549-1550, :1618) than in the click menu (shared machine, fires on RELEASE) — the tracker's ENC_PRESS convention is the opposite commit point. The turn side has the same disease: ImagesApp re-derives detent hysteresis by hand with ENCODER_THRESHOLD 8 / SCROLL_DEBOUNCE 10 ms (src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp:318-319, :352) and the snake with ±2 counts / 250 ms cooldown (src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp:626, :723) — both re-implementing `rotaryDivider` (also 8, src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:382) against the raw `encoderPosition`, bypassing the paced backlog entirely so fast spins collapse.

**Proposal:** One classifier struct, parameterised, instantiated per physical input rather than per UI:

  struct ButtonGesture {
      uint16_t debounceMs, holdMs, longHoldMs, repeatMs;   // 0 = feature off
      GestureEvent poll(bool physicallyDown, uint32_t nowMs);
      // emits: TAP (release before holdMs) | HOLD | HOLD_REPEAT | LONG_HOLD | HOLD_RELEASE
  };

The thresholds move out of five files and into ONE table, alongside the board descriptor (see proposal 6), so 'how long is a hold on this board' has one answer. Three structural rules the current code cannot express: (a) `debounceMs = 0` is a legal, documented value meaning 'this line is hardware-debounced' — the encoder push should use it, which retires the redundant third debounce at src/RotaryEncoder.h:183; (b) HOLD_REPEAT is a first-class event, so the menu's hold-to-back pacing (src/Menus.cpp:976) stops being a hand-rolled timer that has to fight holdAnimationStuff's reboot escalation on the same timeline; (c) the classifier is pure — it takes a bool and a clock — so it is instantiable for the probe buttons, the encoder push, and any V6 button without touching the driver that samples the pin.

EncoderClickTracker (src/RotaryEncoder.h:180-196) is 80% of this already and is the right seed: it is per-instance, has no shared static state, and its header comment already argues the case ('the shared encoderButtonState machine is cross-core and gets re-armed by various consumers, which makes did the user click or hold ambiguous for any one UI', src/RotaryEncoder.h:148-152). Generalise it to take the pin state as an argument instead of calling isEncoderButtonPhysicallyPressed() internally, and ProbeButton's release-debounce state machine becomes a configuration of it rather than a second implementation.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/ProjectsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp

**V6 relevance:** New input hardware means new buttons, and every button in this codebase today costs a bespoke state machine plus a bespoke threshold set. A V6 with (say) four nav buttons would add four more. With one classifier, a new button costs one struct instance and four numbers in the board table. It also unblocks the felt-latency work without redesigning anything: committing the click on PRESS in fast UIs recovers the 5.5-8.4 ms RC release tax (src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:346-352) — and that becomes a per-instance policy flag instead of a fork of the whole machine.

**Tonight-safe slice:** none — every merge of two threshold sets changes felt behavior on at least one surface. The honest prep step is documentation-only: put the five threshold sets in one comment block in src/RotaryEncoder.h next to the EncoderClickTracker banner, with their file:line origins, so the eventual merge is an argument about numbers rather than an archaeology exercise.

## Canonical probe-button vocabulary: do the revision swap once, in the driver  (hours)

**Problem:** `ProbeButton::getButtonPress()` / `getButtonState()` return a RAW, revision-dependent code: src/Probing.cpp:1388-1392 maps an all-high sample to 2 on probe_revision >= 4 and to 1 on <= 3, and the reverse for all-low. src/Probing.h:99-114 states the fixed user-facing convention (0 = none, 1 = CONNECT/front, 2 = REMOVE/rear), says callers reach it by applying a compensating swap when probe_revision > 3, and warns 'copy one of them, don't invent a third rule'. Only 3 of roughly 18 live consumers apply that swap: src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2661-2665, :2708-2712, and src/PartsApp.cpp:30-33 (`partsProbeButton`). Every click-menu site speaks raw: src/Menus.cpp:1054 and :1663 (getMenuSelection's select/back), :2009, :2663, :2999, :3445, :3454, :3819, :3839, :5843, :5849, plus src/Peripherals.cpp:3492 and :3507 (the voltage adjuster), src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp:656, :756 and src/eyecandy/BitmapEditor.cpp:1215. Because the default is probe_revision = 5 (src/config.h:61), raw 2 currently happens to be CONNECT and the menus behave correctly — but on a probe_revision <= 3 probe the SAME `probePress == 2` test is REMOVE, so select and back swap in every click menu, in the node picker, and in the value editors. The bug is invisible on the default board and 100% reproducible on an old probe, which is exactly the failure mode a new probe revision reintroduces.

**Proposal:** Move the swap into the driver and make the raw form the exception rather than the default. Concretely: (1) add `int probeButtonPress(bool consume = true)` and `int probeButtonState()` free functions in Probing.h that return the USER-FACING code, applying the revision swap in one place; (2) rename the existing methods `getButtonPressRaw()` / `getButtonStateRaw()` — the two decoders that legitimately need the pre-swap value are the sample decoder at src/Probing.cpp:1388 and the PIO IRQ handler's `processSample` (src/Probing.h:126-134), and nothing else; (3) convert all ~15 raw consumers to the free functions. Symbol names carry the contract so the compiler catches the next new call site instead of a comment asking people not to invent a third rule.

The same argument generalises: the probe-revision swap is the only place in the input layer where a hardware variant leaks a coordinate system into consumers, and it is a preview of what a V6 probe does at scale. The right shape is that a driver publishes events in the fixed logical vocabulary and never exports codes that need per-caller correction.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Peripherals.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/BitmapEditor.cpp

**V6 relevance:** A V6 probe is the single most likely piece of new input hardware, and probe_revision is already at 5 (src/config.h:61). Every new revision that reorders or adds a button today costs a grep across ~18 call sites with no compiler help, and a bug class that only shows up on non-default hardware. One swap site plus a name that says 'raw' turns the next revision into a one-line change in src/Probing.cpp:1388-1392.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Extract `int probeButtonPress(bool consume = true)` containing exactly the swap body that already appears three times, and convert those three sites (src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2661-2665, :2708-2712, src/PartsApp.cpp:30-33) to call it. Pure refactor, identical behavior on every revision. Leave the raw-speaking Menus/Peripherals sites alone tonight — flipping those is the rev<=3 polarity FIX and belongs in the main proposal, not in a behavior-preserving slice.

## wheelPicker(): one modal picker loop instead of nine  (weeks)

**Problem:** The 'show a list, turn to move, click to select, hold to cancel' loop is hand-written at least nine times with the same body and different constants: src/Menus.cpp:938 getMenuSelection, :1810 selectSubmenuOption, :2471 yesNoMenu, :2592 selectNodeAction, :2862 getActionFloat, :3367 getActionInt, :3636 getActionString; src/PartsApp.cpp:82 partsPicker; src/ProjectsApp.cpp:685 runPicker; src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp:1549-1620 (the file manager); src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp:338. partsPicker's own header comment concedes it is 'runPicker's ownership discipline (ProjectsApp.cpp:683) rendering from rodata const char* instead of String'. They disagree on: the poll floor (delayMicroseconds(400) at src/Menus.cpp:1046 + 80 at :1638 vs delayMicroseconds(1000) at src/PartsApp.cpp:145 and src/ProjectsApp.cpp:762 — double, for no stated reason); the LED keepalive (menuShowKeepalive's 50 ms gated on ledShowIdle(), src/Menus.cpp:483, vs a hand-rolled ungated 250 ms at src/PartsApp.cpp:111 and src/ProjectsApp.cpp:727, which even cite the Menus one in their comments); whether rotaryDivider is restored (src/ProjectsApp.cpp:702/:768 and src/PartsApp.cpp:620/:705 do; ImagesApp does not); whether the cancelling serial byte is consumed (src/PartsApp.cpp:129-131 and src/ProjectsApp.cpp:745-746 do and document why; getMenuSelection's `while (Serial.available() == 0)` at src/Menus.cpp:999 does not, so one keepalive byte on port 1 yanks the menu out from under the user); whether the hold is drained before returning (src/PartsApp.cpp:118-123 and src/Menus.cpp:1094-1100 do, src/ProjectsApp.cpp:747 does not); and whether the repaint is bracketed against core 1's half-buffer read (only renderMenuLine, src/Menus.cpp:894, uses menuTransitionBeginDraw/Arm — the apps paint bare, which is exactly why menuShowKeepalive had to be invented as a weaker second guarantee). Nine copies also means the LED-request ordering differs: in the menu the blocking OLED write runs before the LED request, in the app pickers after, so the same gesture makes the breadboard lead on one screen and trail on the other.

**Proposal:** One `wheelPicker(const PickerSpec&)` owning the whole modal contract, with the per-screen difference expressed as a draw callback and a small options struct:

  struct PickerSpec {
      int count, startIdx;
      void (*drawItem)(int idx, void* ctx);   // the ONLY thing that varies today
      void* ctx;
      uint8_t divider;                        // scoped, restored on exit (see proposal 5)
      bool consumeCancelByte;                 // one honest flag, not nine opinions
      bool wrap;
  };
  int wheelPicker(const PickerSpec&);         // >=0 selected, -1 cancelled, -2 serial

The loop body itself is already identical across the copies — compare src/PartsApp.cpp:99-146 with src/ProjectsApp.cpp:718-765 line for line. Folding them gives one place to: bracket every repaint with menuTransitionBeginDraw/Arm (currently one screen out of nine), post the LED request before the blocking OLED write everywhere, use one keepalive cadence, and pump serviceInner at one cadence. Every latency and event-loss fix on the wheel path then becomes a single edit instead of a nine-site sweep — which is the actual reason the nine copies have drifted.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/ProjectsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp

**V6 relevance:** The picker is where new input hardware has to land to be useful. Adding a D-pad, a second wheel, or touch-to-scroll today means teaching nine loops about it — which in practice means it gets added to two of them and the rest silently do not support the new hardware. With one wheelPicker the new source is wired in once and every list screen on the device gains it simultaneously. It is also the natural consumer of proposals 1, 2 and 5: the picker is where take()/gesture/scope all get used together.

**Tonight-safe slice:** none — the nine loops' differences are load-bearing (the divider values, the cancel-byte policy, the hold drain, the OLED/LED ordering), so converting even one is a behavior change on that screen. The safe prep is a side-by-side diff note listing which of the nine differences are deliberate and which are drift, so the conversion is a series of intentional decisions rather than a merge.

## InputScope: RAII ownership for the unowned input globals  (hours)

**Problem:** The input layer's per-screen state lives in bare globals whose ownership convention exists only in prose. `rotaryDivider` has 34 live write sites across 6 files: src/Menus.cpp:1036, :1817, :2474, :2668, :2853, :2874, :3390, :3446, :3456, :3465, :3538, :3663, :3711, :3763, :3780, :3822, :3850, :3934, :5803, :5884; src/Peripherals.cpp:3427, :3498, :3515, :3530, :3674; src/Probing.cpp:2920, :3967, :5549, :5661; src/PartsApp.cpp:620, :705; src/ProjectsApp.cpp:702, :768; src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp:342. src/ProjectsApp.cpp:696-701 documents the convention ('every picker-shaped consumer owns this global for its duration and puts it back') in a comment precisely because nothing enforces it, and src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1102-1110 makes every uncoordinated change cost real travel: any divider change reseeds `lastDetentRaw = rawCount` and zeroes `lastStepDir`, discarding up to rotaryDivider-1 = 7 accumulated raw counts, i.e. 7/8 of a detent. getMenuSelection then re-writes the value on every loop pass at ~2 kHz (src/Menus.cpp:1036), so if any inner service ever set a different value the two would fight and rotation would go permanently dead. The same shape repeats for `inClickMenu` (raised/lowered by hand in each picker: src/ProjectsApp.cpp:707/:766, src/PartsApp.cpp:618/:704), `encoderOverride` (src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1015-1021 — a countdown in units of throttled polls, poked as a bare int from MicroPython and Debugs), and the probe's `blockProbeButton`/`blockProbeButtonTimer` pair, which handleProbeButtonActions and probeMode stomp with 300/400/800/1000/3000/8000 ms values (e.g. src/Probing.cpp:605-606, :1489-1490, :1495-1496).

**Proposal:** Replace the save/restore convention with a scope object that cannot be forgotten:

  struct InputScope {
      explicit InputScope(uint8_t divider, bool ownsMenuLeds = true);
      ~InputScope();   // restores divider, lowers inClickMenu, drops pending events
      void drainPending();  // explicit re-arm point on entry
  };

One instance at the top of each modal screen replaces the four-to-six lines every picker currently opens and closes with (compare src/ProjectsApp.cpp:700-708 against :764-768, and src/PartsApp.cpp:618-620 against :704-706). Two properties the current code cannot have: (a) the restore runs on every exit path including early returns and the ones that currently forget — src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp never restores the divider at all, and the -3 probe-button bail at src/Menus.cpp:544-548 returns without clearing inClickMenu; (b) because the scope is the only writer, the divider stops being written ~2000 times a second by src/Menus.cpp:1036, which removes the standing hazard that a future inner-set service could fight it.

A second, cheap property: the destructor is the natural home for 'drop any event that was posted for me and never consumed' (proposal 1's `drop()`), which is what the popContext flush switch at src/JumperlOS.cpp:1018-1031 is trying to do by hand and gets wrong for APP_GENERIC.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/ProjectsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Peripherals.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlOS.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp

**V6 relevance:** Sensitivity, capture, and pending-event ownership are per-input-source concepts that a V6 multiplies. With one global int per concept, two simultaneous input sources cannot each have their own sensitivity, and adding a source means auditing 34 write sites for interference. A scope object makes the state per-screen-per-source by construction, which is the precondition for ever having two live input sources at once.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Add `struct RotaryDividerScope { int prev; explicit RotaryDividerScope(int v) : prev(rotaryDivider) { rotaryDivider = v; } ~RotaryDividerScope() { rotaryDivider = prev; } };` to src/RotaryEncoder.h and convert only the sites that ALREADY do exactly save-then-restore with a local `lastDivider`: src/ProjectsApp.cpp:702/:768, src/PartsApp.cpp:620/:705, src/Menus.cpp:2874/:2853, :3390/:3538, :3663/:3934, :5803/:5884, src/Peripherals.cpp:3427/:3674. Behavior-identical (same value in, same value out, same order), and it makes the ones that DON'T restore visible by their absence.

## board::InputSources + one inputPost() injection seam  (days)

**Problem:** src/boards/board.h:12-17 promises that adding a board 'e.g. the future V6' is mechanical and that 'no shared-core file needs to change', and the header's opening rule is that 'the shared core must NEVER branch on board #defines'. The input layer is the part that never joined. `BoardCaps.hasRotaryEncoder` and `hasProbePads` (src/boards/board.h:84-85) are read at exactly three sites in the whole tree — src/main.cpp:556, src/main.cpp:654, src/eyecandy/PartLabels.cpp:427 — while src/RotaryEncoder.cpp still carries 4 raw `OG_JUMPERLESS` branches (including the whole-function early return at :990-996 and the ENC_C7_BUILD gate at :69-73) and src/Probing.cpp carries 8. Everything the board contract enumerates for GPIO and analog (`GpioEntry`, `AnalogChannel`, src/boards/board.h:63-77 — 'ENUMERATED, never counted... an LLM/MicroPython tool reads these tables to discover exactly what the board can do') has no input equivalent: there is no table saying which input sources exist, what gestures they emit, or what they are called. Separately, the ONLY input-injection path in the tree writes the consumer-facing globals directly and gets it wrong: `jl_clickwheel_up(int clicks)` / `jl_clickwheel_down(int clicks)` (src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2719-2729) ignore the `clicks` argument entirely and set `encoderDirectionState` without touching `encoderDirectionConsumed` or the pacer, and `jl_clickwheel_press()` (:2733-2736) writes `lastButtonEncoderState = PRESSED` BEFORE `encoderButtonState = RELEASED` — the inverse of the order src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1044-1050 documents as load-bearing for the lock-free handoff — and never stamps `buttonEventTimestamp`, so core 1's next 500 us poll can overwrite lastButtonEncoderState at src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:519 and eat the click. test/hil/test_encoder_ui.py:74-86 says the quiet part out loud: it 'writes encoderDirectionState/encoderDirectionConsumed over OpenOCD', i.e. injects at the OUTPUT of the decode, and its own comment concedes this 'is not encoder coverage'.

**Proposal:** Two additions that make input a first-class part of the board contract:

(1) An enumerated table beside GpioEntry/AnalogChannel:

    enum class InputKind : uint8_t { Rotary, Button, TouchPad, ProbeTip, Terminal };
    struct InputSource {
        InputKind kind;
        uint8_t   id;
        const char* label;      // 'clickwheel', 'probe.connect', 'probe.tip'
        uint16_t debounceMs, holdMs, longHoldMs, repeatMs;   // proposal 2's numbers
        uint8_t   countsPerDetent;                            // rotaryDivider's home
    };
    // BoardTopology gains: const InputSource* inputs; size_t inputCount;

  `hasRotaryEncoder` stops being a bool nobody reads and becomes 'is there a Rotary entry in the table'. The gesture thresholds and the detent divider stop being globals and become board data. The USBSer3 capability JSON that already publishes GPIO and analog tables gains input discovery for free, which is what an agent or the desktop app needs to know what gestures this board can even produce.

(2) ONE entry point that every producer calls — the real driver, the MicroPython injectors, and a HIL harness alike:

    void inputPost(const InputSource& src, InputEventKind ev, int32_t value);

  It posts into proposal 1's slot and calls noteUserInput(). The V5 encoder driver calls it from src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1290-1308 instead of assigning the globals; jl_clickwheel_up/down/press become three-line wrappers that honour `clicks` and cannot get the write order wrong; the HIL rig pokes ONE function through OpenOCD and thereby exercises the pacing, the ack protocol and the expiry policy that it currently bypasses entirely. That single seam is what turns test_encoder_ui.py from 'not encoder coverage' into real coverage of everything downstream of the quadrature decode.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/boards/board.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/boards/board.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/boards/v5/board_v5.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/boards/og/board_og.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/main.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/test/hil/test_encoder_ui.py

**V6 relevance:** This IS the V6 story. Today 'plug in new input hardware' means writing a driver AND hand-editing 24 consumer sites AND adding board #defines to files whose own header forbids them AND having no way to test any of it. With the table plus inputPost(), a V6 input device is: add rows to src/boards/v6/board_v6.cpp, write a driver that calls inputPost(), done — which is exactly the promise src/boards/board.h:12-17 already makes for crossbar topology and peripherals. The seam is also what lets V5 and V6 input coexist in one binary rather than forking the UI.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Add the `InputKind` / `InputSource` types to src/boards/board.h and populate `inputs`/`inputCount` for V5 and OG from the values already in the tree (encoder: countsPerDetent 8 from src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:382, holdMs 500 from :341; probe buttons: 800/1000 from src/Probing.h:216-217; debounce 30 from :215). Nothing reads the table yet — pure addition, zero behavior change, and test/test_boards/ already unit-tests descriptors on the host so it comes with coverage. Do NOT touch jl_clickwheel_press tonight: fixing its write order is a bug fix, not a behavior-preserving change.

## One command source, one dispatch site, one arg-wait policy for terminal input  (days)

**Problem:** The terminal surface has the same three diseases as the wheel and probe, in its own dialect. (a) THREE line-completion owners feed one predicate: TermControl's single `completed_line`/`line_ready` slot (src/Jerial.cpp:642-643, :320-322, :346-352), TermControl's separate `command_queue[]` with per-entry response targets (src/Jerial.cpp:327-343, :610-618, :402), and CommandBuffer, which is polled separately in two places (src/main.cpp:1126 inside the busy loop and src/main.cpp:1135 outside it). `Jerial::hasCompletedLine()` (src/Jerial.cpp:1115-1117) sees two of the three. (b) TWO dispatch sites with duplicated `inMainMenu` bracketing, duplicated TinyUSB pre-pumps and duplicated CommandResult switches: src/main.cpp:1178 (relayed/UART) and src/main.cpp:1355 (terminal). The relayed one wins arbitration by running first and jumping to `dontshowmenu` at src/main.cpp:1206, so a typed command waits one extra busy-loop lap whenever a UART command is pending. (c) FIVE 'wait for the rest of the command' policies with five timeouts and three opinions about whether services keep running: the help look-ahead, 100 ms, armed as a loop-level state machine that keeps servicing (HELP_WAIT_MS at src/main.cpp:799, armed at :1310, drained at :1218-1225); getCommandArgs, 50 ms of `delay(1)` with NOTHING serviced (src/SingleCharCommands.cpp:1785-1814); savePreformattedNodeFile's char-mode branch, which does not wait at all (src/FileParsing.cpp:889-898, where SafeString::read(Jerial) is non-blocking); readPastedBlock, 500 ms quiet / 30 s idle reading RAW Serial and bypassing Jerial entirely (src/SingleCharCommands.cpp:1841); and usbSer3_readLine, 2000 ms idle with its own inline echo and backspace handling (src/Ser3Backchannel.cpp:1018). (d) One of them actively eats input: `printMenu()` drains the whole stream whenever `Jerial.available() > 20` (src/SingleCharCommands.cpp:174-183), so a multi-line paste whose first command returns CMD_SHOW_MENU loses its tail — src/main.cpp:1366-1370 documents removing the OTHER drain for exactly this reason and left this one.

**Proposal:** Give the terminal the same three mechanisms the other two surfaces get, so 'a command arrived' is one event with one owner:

(1) ONE command queue. Fold `completed_line`, `command_queue[]` and CommandBuffer into a single `CommandEvent { const char* line; Source src; Stream* respondTo; }` ring — proposal 1's InputEvent with a payload. `hasCompletedLine()` then genuinely means 'is there a command', instead of seeing two of three producers.

(2) ONE dispatch site. Collapse src/main.cpp:1178 and :1355 into a single `dispatchCommand(const CommandEvent&)` that does the inMainMenu bracket, the USB pre-pump and the CommandResult switch once. Source-specific behavior (respond-to-UART, `goto dontshowmenu`) becomes a field on the event, not a duplicated code path, and the arbitration between UART and typed input becomes an explicit queue order rather than an accident of which `if` runs first.

(3) ONE arg-wait policy: `bool awaitArgs(String& out, uint32_t timeoutMs)` that always pumps `jOS.serviceInner()` while it waits — the property getCommandArgs currently lacks (src/SingleCharCommands.cpp:1811 spins on `delay(1)`, so the wheel, probe and LEDs are dead for its 30-50 ms) and the help look-ahead already has. Line mode short-circuits it exactly as getCommandArgs does today at src/SingleCharCommands.cpp:1794-1797, so the common path stays free. Then delete printMenu's stream drain (src/SingleCharCommands.cpp:174-183): once the queue owns the bytes, no consumer needs to defensively eat them.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/main.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/SingleCharCommands.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/SingleCharCommands.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Jerial.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Jerial.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/FileParsing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Ser3Backchannel.cpp

**V6 relevance:** The terminal is the surface a V6 will grow MORE of, not fewer: a second CDC port, a BLE console, a companion-app channel, an agent driving the board. Each new source today means a fourth line-completion owner, a third dispatch site and a sixth arg-wait policy. With one queue carrying a Source tag and a respondTo stream, a new command source is one producer calling post() — and it inherits the arbitration, the response routing and the service-pumping wait for free. It is also the only surface where the unified event already half-exists (CommandBuffer's `shouldRespondToUART`), so the shape is proven.

**Tonight-safe slice:** none — every one of the three merges changes arbitration order or timing on at least one source. The closest safe step is additive and diagnostic: have all three producers bump a shared counter so `hasCompletedLine()`'s blind spot (CommandBuffer, src/main.cpp:1126/:1135) becomes measurable before it is fixed.

## Make the single-owner poll contract honest: retire the no-op calls and dead outputs  (hours)

**Problem:** Under single-core ownership (src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:959-978) both encoder poll functions hard-return on core 0 — `rotaryEncoderStuff()` at src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:996 and `rotaryEncoderButtonStuff()` at :1033 — yet 18 core-0 call sites still call them every loop iteration, several with comments asserting they do something. `rotaryEncoderButtonStuff()`: src/Menus.cpp:1001 ('Update button state every iteration'), :1993, :2515 ('Update button state'); src/PartsApp.cpp:107, :121, :217, :230, :569; src/ProjectsApp.cpp:724, :784; src/Probing.cpp:2382. `rotaryEncoderStuff()`: src/Menus.cpp:3439, :3702, :5834; src/Peripherals.cpp:3448; src/Graphics.cpp:4102; src/Probing.cpp:5669; src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp:720. The driver's own banner admits they were left in 'as no-ops - cheaper than hunting them all down, and they document where polling used to be' (src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:975-977). The cost is not cycles, it is comprehension: a reader of any picker loop reasonably concludes the loop polls the wheel, and therefore mis-attributes every dropped click and every latency number. The real mechanism is core 1's 500 us poll (ENCODER_POLL_INTERVAL_US, src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:979) plus the 10 ms BUTTON_EVENT_MIN_DURATION_US hold. Three extern'd outputs are dead the same way and therefore read as contract: `resetPosition` (declared src/RotaryEncoder.h:34, defined src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:40, written by exactly one consumer at src/Menus.cpp:1115, read by nothing — the live one is `resetEncoderPosition`); `numberOfSteps` (src/RotaryEncoder.h:33, written src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:1276, no consumer outside the driver); and on the probe side `samePadCount` (src/Probing.cpp:7072, written :7125 and :7127, read only inside commented-out debug at :7234).

**Proposal:** Replace the silent no-op with an explicit contract, in one edit per direction. (1) Rename the core-0-facing entry points to say what they are: `encoderServiceYield()` already exists for the legitimate core-1 use (src/RotaryEncoder.h:108-113) and documents the yield contract properly; every core-0 call site either deletes the call or, where the author wanted 'let the input layer breathe', calls the honestly-named yield. (2) Under a debug flag, make the core-0 entry count or assert instead of returning silently, so a future refactor that reintroduces core-0 polling is caught rather than absorbed. (3) Delete the three dead externs so the header stops advertising outputs nobody produces or consumes.

This is small, but it is the precondition for every other proposal here being reviewable: proposals 1, 2, 4 and 6 all require a reader to know where input actually enters the system, and right now 18 call sites lie about it.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/ProjectsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Peripherals.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Graphics.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp

**V6 relevance:** A V6 bring-up starts by reading these loops to find out where input enters, and the loops currently point at the wrong place. Worse, the single-owner rule (core 1 owns all encoder state) is enforced only by a silent `if (cpuid & 1) return` — a V6 with a second input source polled from core 0 would hit that return and see nothing, with no diagnostic. Making the contract explicit and asserted is what stops that from being a multi-day bring-up mystery.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Delete the 11 core-0 `rotaryEncoderButtonStuff()` calls (src/Menus.cpp:1001, :1993, :2515; src/PartsApp.cpp:107, :121, :217, :230, :569; src/ProjectsApp.cpp:724, :784; src/Probing.cpp:2382) and the 7 core-0 `rotaryEncoderStuff()` calls (src/Menus.cpp:3439, :3702, :5834; src/Peripherals.cpp:3448; src/Graphics.cpp:4102; src/Probing.cpp:5669; src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp:720), plus the three dead externs. All eighteen are in core-0 modal loops and already return at src/RotaryEncoder.cpp:996/:1033, so this is provably behavior-preserving. CRITICAL: do NOT touch the core-1 call sites at src/main.cpp:1688, :1864, :1997, :2035 — those are the real owner poll and the encoderServiceYield contract.

## P6 (keystone) — one focus stack, one authority: make ContextManager the single owner-of-record  (weeks)

**Problem:** There is no single answer to "who owns this surface right now" — there are three unrelated mechanisms, none of which knows about the others.

(a) THE CONTEXT STACK, which already claims to be the answer. externVars.cpp:249-253 states it in prose: "the JumperlOS ContextManager already tracks what UI mode we're in... Each of those pushes its own context on entry, so we don't need per-mode flags." The very next comment (externVars.cpp:255-258) admits the claim is false: "PROBING is the notable exception - probe mode does NOT push a context today, so we also check probeActive directly below."

(b) THE BLOCKING LATCH. jOSmanager::blockingService is a single bare pointer (JumperlOS.cpp:62), set when a service returns BLOCKING and cleared only when that same service runs again and returns something else (JumperlOS.cpp:273-283). While it holds, serviceAll skips every non-inner-set service (JumperlOS.cpp:251-254). It carries no owner identity beyond the pointer and no timeout.

(c) innerDepth, incremented/decremented around ONE serviceInner dispatch (JumperlOS.cpp:318, 340). jOS.inModalContext() = innerDepth > 0 || blockingService != nullptr (JumperlOS.h:328) therefore means "am I being dispatched from the inner set at this instant", NOT "a modal loop owns core 0" — JumperlOS.cpp:314-316 says so directly ("getBlockingService() is nullptr in that context (the loop never RETURNED BLOCKING, it just... blocks)"). Any code asking the question from outside a service dispatch gets the wrong answer. It has exactly three callers, all DisplayService (DisplayService.cpp:224, 323, 388); StepViewer.cpp:256 asks the fourth way, getBlockingService() != nullptr.

The stack itself is not hygienic. pushContext REJECTS a duplicate type (JumperlOS.cpp:932-941) after printing a WARNING and dumping printStack() to Serial mid-launch. runApp pushes APP_GENERIC without checking the return (Apps.cpp:187-192) and pops unconditionally (Apps.cpp:199-201), so a nested app pops the PARENT's context and runs the parent's onExit. Both push and pop take core_sync_acquire() = mutex_enter_blocking with NO timeout (externVars.cpp:92-96) — the same mutex core 1 holds across its entire LED render block (acquired main.cpp:1722, released at the end of core2stuff), so entering and leaving every app can stall core 0 for a full core-1 frame.

The cross-core primitives are the same story one level down: core1busy and core2busy are declared in TWO headers (coredination/externVars.h:7-8 and Probing.h:607-608), defined once (externVars.cpp:18-19), raised and lowered at a dozen points inside core2stuff, and read by almost nobody (core1busy: main.cpp:2013's swirl arm, oled.cpp:3252, NetVoltageScan.cpp:606/1402, States.cpp:4798). core1FrameHoldDepth[2] (externVars.h:26, externVars.cpp:24-40) is a proper per-core depth counter; core_sync is a real mutex; core1req::allIdle() is a real generation handshake. Five synchronisation primitives with overlapping scope and no stated hierarchy. src/ headers declare 99 `extern volatile` globals in total.

**Proposal:** Make the context stack the ONE owner-of-record, and give it the fields the ad-hoc globals are standing in for.

1. Extend ContextEntry (JumperlOS.h:69-78) with an ownership mask + saved input state: `uint8_t ownsSurfaces` (STRIP | PANEL | WHEEL | PROBE | ROUTING), `int savedRotaryDivider`, `uint8_t savedLedOwner`. pushContext saves and sets; popContext restores — so the four hand-written menu teardowns (P1) and the twenty hand-written rotaryDivider save/restore pairs (P3) become structural instead of conventional.

2. Make PROBING actually push a context, as externVars.cpp:255-258 says it should, and delete probeActive as an *idle/ownership* input (it stays as a probe-LED animation input only). This is the single change that makes the stack's own claim true.

3.
