Replace the three modal predicates with two named, latched states on jOSmanager: `uiOwnsLoop()` (raised on modal-loop entry / BLOCKING, lowered on exit — not the transient innerDepth) and `renderBusy()` (the cross-core composite). Define each ONCE and have DisplayService, OledGui, oledPeriodic and StepViewer call it instead of re-deriving four different expressions.

4. Push/pop must not take an unbounded mutex on the path core 1 holds for a full LED frame: use core_sync_acquire_timeout_ms(2) with a fail-loud path, matching what core 1 itself already does at main.cpp:1722 (0 ms try-and-skip).

5. Make pushContext's duplicate rejection a returned error with no Serial output, and make runApp honour it (Apps.cpp:192) so an unbalanced pop is impossible.

Nothing about timing, debounce, or animation changes — this is purely relocating "who owns what" from 99 globals into the one stack that already exists to hold it.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlOS.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlOS.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/coredination/externVars.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/coredination/externVars.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Apps.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/main.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/displays/DisplayService.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/OledGui.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/StepViewer.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.cpp

**V6 relevance:** This is the seam CodeDocs/OG_BACKPORT.md:104-105 already demands: "No shared-core file should need to change. If it does, that's a missing seam in the contract — add the seam, don't sprinkle #ifdefs." The routing/LED-geometry layer got that treatment (board.h:139-142 boardRowToChipY / boardY0Node; Graphics.cpp:1056, 1077, 3750, 3812 branch on caps.ledsPerRow), but the UI-ownership layer did not: it is 99 `extern volatile` file-scope globals that each assume exactly one instance of exactly one surface that is always present. board.h:81-97 already enumerates hasProbePads / hasRotaryEncoder / hasOled / hasBreadboardText / breadboardDisplays / ledCount, and main.cpp:556 and main.cpp:654 already register services conditionally on two of them. A context entry carrying an ownership mask over *named surfaces* is what lets a V6 module be absent, present twice, or hot-swapped without every consumer re-deriving presence from a global.

**Tonight-safe slice:** none — the honest slice here is diagnostic only: add a counter next to the duplicate-rejection return at JumperlOS.cpp:938 and next to the unconditional pop at Apps.cpp:200, printed in the existing `X` service table, so a nested-app unbalanced pop stops being theoretical. Everything structural in this proposal changes when a flag flips and belongs in the body.

## P1 Strip render ownership — put the owner and the clear-flag INSIDE the LED request  (days)

**Problem:** Whether core 1 is allowed to paint the 445 pixels is decided by a hand-spelled boolean over eight independent globals at main.cpp:1811-1813 — `rails != 2 && rails != 3 && (inClickMenu == 0 || SlotManager::isPreviewMode() || g_historyScrubActive) && inPadMenu == 0 && hideNets == 0` — plus `loadingFile` at main.cpp:1772, plus `ledGraphicsOwned()` at main.cpp:1773 and 1782. Nine more flags orbit it, and two of them are request state stranded in globals:

- clearBeforeSend (main.cpp:1507) is latched from ANY request carrying LED_CLEAR (main.cpp:1786) but consumed ONLY inside the NETS branch (main.cpp:1829-1832). Every clear-carrying menu/graphics post — and there are 115 mode-2 call sites in src/ — latches a clear that survives as a global and fires on some later, unrelated nets frame.
- s_ledGfxOwned (Commands.cpp:64) is written as a side effect of the NEXT requestLedShow's mode (Commands.cpp:83), never by the graphics owner exiting. Its own comment at Commands.cpp:77-80 admits this is "just where that ownership flag turns off". While it holds, ledShowIdle() is false (Commands.cpp:103-105), which kills the transition pacer (main.cpp:1747), the swirl arm (main.cpp:2013) and menuShowKeepalive (Menus.cpp:485).
- logoLedAccess (Graphics.h:82, Graphics.cpp:4387) is a plain `if (flag) return; flag = true;` used as a cross-core lock: core 1 takes it in logoSwirl (LEDs.cpp:3768-3771) and SPINS on it in lightUpHeader (LEDs.cpp:3907-3911); core 0 takes it in getLogoOverride (LEDs.cpp:1433-1438), setLogoOverride (LEDs.cpp:1448, 1467) and clearColorOverrides (LEDs.cpp:1575-1578). Losers return silently, so clearColorOverrides is a no-op under contention — which is exactly the "depth pads stay lit after the menu closes" scar the comment at Menus.cpp:596-601 describes from the other side ("caught live via a debugger watchpoint on GPIOcolorOverride0").
- The logo region has its own uncoordinated precedence ladder, hard-coded inside logoSwirl: filesystemActive/filesystemActiveUntil (externVars.cpp:44-46) at LEDs.cpp:3824, then measureModeActive (externVars.cpp:55, written MeasureMode.cpp:80 and :192) at LEDs.cpp:3836, then probeActive, all under logoLedAccess. undoActivityUntil (externVars.cpp:59) and logoColorOverride/Top/Bottom + logoOverriden (LEDs.h:97-100, 149) are four more writers into the same pixels with no arbitration.
- bbDirty/topDirty are written on every setPixelColor and read by nothing — the `if (bbDirty)` guards are commented out at LEDs.cpp:182 and :191, and the file's own FOLLOW-UP note at LEDs.cpp:166-173 says so and names the five overlapping clear paths (clearBeforeSend / clearLEDsExceptRails / hideNets / leds.clear / bread::clear).

Ownership TEARDOWN is written four times in Menus.cpp with two different orderings and one outright leak: exitMenuModeForAction (Menus.cpp:468-473); the -3 probe-button bail (Menus.cpp:544-548) which returns with inClickMenu still 1 from :516, no b.clear, no requestLedShow, no menuTransitionCancel — and main.cpp:1082-1084 does `if (menus.inClickMenu != 0) goto loadfile;` on every busy-loop pass; the -2 cancel (Menus.cpp:565-569) which posts `b.clear(); requestLedShow(-1); inClickMenu = 0;` i.e. posts the restore WHILE it still owns the gate at main.cpp:1811, so a take landing in that two-statement window is completed with nothing drawn and leaves clearBeforeSend latched; and the session cleanup (Menus.cpp:607-619) which does inClickMenu = 0 FIRST. Two exits from one function, opposite orderings.

**Proposal:** Move ownership into the request that already exists. core1req::REQ_SHOW_LEDS is a typed slot with mode, flags and a generation (Commands.cpp:63-101) — the FOLLOW-UP note at Commands.cpp:48-54 asked for exactly this and it landed only half way.

1. Add an `owner` field to the request: NONE / NETS / MENU / PADMENU / GFX / PREVIEW / SCRUB, written by requestLedShow from one `stripOwner()` accessor backed by P6's context entry. s_ledGfxOwned and swirled become request state; clearBeforeSend becomes a per-request flag consumed by whichever branch runs, not only by NETS.
2. Replace the boolean at main.cpp:1811-1813 with one named predicate `stripPaintAllowed(rails)` defined once in Commands.cpp, with the current expression as its body. Every future modal screen then raises an owner rather than remembering to poke the right subset of six latches.
3. Replace logoLedAccess with a real primitive (the SIO spinlock the mailbox already uses, or core_sync) and give losers a bounded retry instead of a silent return. Turn the logo precedence ladder at LEDs.cpp:3820-3845 into one `logoOwner` enum with the same order, so filesystemActive / measureModeActive / undoActivityUntil / logoColorOverride stop being four independent latches read in a fixed if-chain.
4. Give the strip a scope object — `LedScope(owner)` whose destructor performs the ONE canonical teardown (wipe buffer, post clear+nets, drop the owner). The four Menus teardowns and the copies in Probing.cpp:3955-3995, PartsApp.cpp:704-707 and ProjectsApp.cpp:767 collapse to one, the ordering inversion becomes impossible to reintroduce, and the -3 bail cannot leak inClickMenu.
5. Either re-enable the bbDirty/topDirty skip in ledClass::show or delete the flags and mark* helpers, as LEDs.cpp:166-173 asks.

No timing constant, animation, transition or pacing value changes.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/main.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Commands.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Commands.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/coredination/CoreMailbox.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/LEDs.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/LEDs.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Graphics.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Graphics.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/ProjectsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/sensing/MeasureMode.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/coredination/externVars.cpp

**V6 relevance:** Strip GEOMETRY is already a board capability — board.h:96-97 (ledsPerRow, ledCount) and Graphics.cpp:1056/1077/3750/3812 branch on it — but strip OWNERSHIP is eight file-scope globals with no strip identity in them at all. A V6 with a second strand, a different logo region, or no breadboard text (caps.hasBreadboardText, board.h:87) cannot express "the menu owns strand 0 while the probe owns strand 1": requestLedShow's vocabulary has no place to put the answer. Putting the owner in the request is the prerequisite for a per-strand owner.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Two mechanical, semantics-identical moves. (1) Extract the boolean at main.cpp:1811-1813 verbatim into `bool stripPaintAllowed(int rails)` in Commands.cpp and call it at that one site — same expression, one definition, and every future reader sees the gate in one place. (2) Add a diagnostic counter next to main.cpp:1786 that increments when LED_CLEAR is taken by a request whose rails end up 2 or 3 (i.e. the clear can never be consumed this frame), surfaced in the existing ledTakeLog / `X` table (Commands.h:28-29). That proves the clearBeforeSend leak on hardware without changing a single behavior.

## P2 Panel ownership — one arbiter for the top OLED, and one definition of "someone else is busy"  (days)

**Problem:** The 128x32 panel has 143 direct `clearPrintShow` / `showMultiLineSmallText` call sites in src/ and no ownership concept whatsoever. Presence is re-derived at 42 separate `oled.oledConnected` / `oled.isConnected()` tests. board.h:85 declares `caps.hasOled` and board.cpp:114 advertises it in the capability JSON — and it gates NOTHING in the firmware; a grep for caps.hasOled finds only the JSON emitter.

"Is someone else busy?" has three incompatible answers for this one panel:
- DisplayService asks jOS.inModalContext() (DisplayService.cpp:224, 323, 388), which is innerDepth || blockingService (JumperlOS.h:328) — see P6 for why that is transient.
- OledGui::renderNow re-derives it as probeActive (OledGui.cpp:640) + a six-way ContextType switch over FILE_MANAGER/EKILO_EDITOR/HELP_DOCS/DEBUG_MENU/PROBING/CLICKWHEEL_MENU (OledGui.cpp:643-648) + `refreshInProgress || refreshLocalInProgress || core1busy || core2busy` (OledGui.cpp:653) + wavegen.isRunning() (OledGui.cpp:659).
- oledPeriodic re-derives it AGAIN with a different set: `refreshInProgress || refreshLocalInProgress || core1busy` (oled.cpp:3248-3252) — no core2busy, no context switch.
Same panel, three definitions, and the one that is closest to correct (OledGui's) is the one that only yields the *idle* screen: notePanelTakenByOther (called from oled.cpp:1296, 1527, 1780) is documented at OledGui.cpp:634-637 as guarding the persistent idle screen only, so a foreground screen keeps free-running against any direct writer.

Deferred flushes have no owner and cannot fire where they are needed. oledNeedsFlushAfterHold (oled.cpp:3068) is set by show() at oled.cpp:2287, cleared by oledPeriodic at :3240 — and ALSO cleared by priorityFlushHeldFrame at :1693. oledNeedsFlushAfterWavegen (oled.cpp:1280) is set at :1686 and :2300, cleared at :3262. Both are drained only by OLEDService, which is LOW priority (JumperlOS.h:566) and therefore NOT in the inner set (the default is `getPriority() == CRITICAL`, JumperlOS.h:172). Inside probe mode, any click menu, or an app input loop, serviceInner never runs it, so a deferred frame waits for the whole modal session to end. That is why probeMode carries a hand-rolled hold-end repaint in its own loop.

The live-reading row on the panel and on the terminal has the same shape of problem one layer up: ReadingDisplay's `pinReserved` is a single flag with no owner id, and MeasureMode.cpp:176-186 documents the resulting collision in prose ("Highlighting is registered immediately before this service at the same priority, so in the SAME pass it pins a live reading and we wipe it").

**Proposal:** One `PanelOwner` arbiter living next to OLEDService.

1. `panelAcquire(Owner, Priority)` / `panelRelease()` with Owner ∈ { IDLE_GUI, GUI_FOREGROUND, MENU, PROBE, READING, TOAST }. `oled::show()` becomes the single choke point that consults it: a non-owner's write is marked deferred rather than executed, instead of every one of the 143 writers re-deriving a gate it mostly does not have. Priority order reproduces today's de-facto order exactly (toast > probe > menu > reading > foreground GUI > idle GUI), so behavior is preserved.
2. Collapse the three modal predicates onto P6's single `uiOwnsLoop()` / `renderBusy()`. DisplayService, OledGui and oledPeriodic then agree by construction.
3. Fold oledNeedsFlushAfterHold and oledNeedsFlushAfterWavegen into one `deferredFlushReason` owned by the arbiter, and make OLEDService inner-set so it can actually drain inside modal loops — which lets probeMode's hand-rolled hold-end repaint be deleted rather than maintained in parallel.
4. Give ReadingDisplay's `pinReserved` an owner id from the same enum, so clearLiveSerialLine can refuse to erase another owner's rows (the exact fix MeasureMode.cpp:176-186 says is still open).
5. Gate the whole subsystem's *construction* on caps.hasOled + jumperlessConfig.top_oled.enabled at one place, keeping the current OG behavior (main.cpp:576 notes the OLED is deliberately preserved on OG because a user can wire a panel to GP18/19 — so the runtime `enabled` check stays; only the scattered re-derivation goes).

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/OledGui.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/ReadingDisplay.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/PartLabels.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/sensing/MeasureMode.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlOS.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlOS.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/displays/DisplayService.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp

**V6 relevance:** The OLED is the surface most likely to be optional, relocated or duplicated on a modular V6, and it is the one surface with a declared capability (board.h:85 caps.hasOled) that gates zero code. Meanwhile the sibling display stack already has the right shape: DisplayService owns exactly one DisplayInstance (DisplayService.h:40, :57), publishes its claimed bus nodes for other subsystems to avoid (DisplayService.h:42-46, DisplayService.cpp:445-452), and gates itself on caps.breadboardDisplays (DisplayService.cpp:298). The top OLED needs the same instance-with-an-owner treatment before a second panel or a panel-less V6 is expressible.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Extract the three divergent "someone else is busy" expressions verbatim into three named functions in one header — `uiModalNow()` (JumperlOS.h:328's current body), `oledGuiDeferNow()` (OledGui.cpp:640-659's current composite) and `oledPeriodicDeferNow()` (oled.cpp:3248-3252's current composite) — and call them at their existing sites. Zero behavior change; the divergence (core2busy present in one and absent in the other, context switch present in one and absent in two) becomes a three-line diff in one file instead of a cross-file archaeology exercise.

## P3 Input focus ownership — the wheel and the probe have no consumer token  (weeks)

**Problem:** THE WHEEL. encoderDirectionState is one unowned global that consumers claim by writing NONE into it: 44 such sites across 12 files (ProjectsApp, JumperlessMicroPythonAPI, Menus, RotaryEncoder, PartsApp, Graphics, Probing, FilesystemStuff, SelfTest, Python_Proper, StepViewer, Highlighting). The driver's DESIGNED ack channel, encoderDirectionConsumed (RotaryEncoder.h:92, defined RotaryEncoder.cpp:398), is set true by exactly two consumers — Menus.cpp:5810 and JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2853 — so the two stale-event guards that read it, RotaryEncoder.cpp:1165 and :1179, are dead for every real UI. Priority between consumers is SERVICE REGISTRATION ORDER, stated as such in main.cpp:539: StepViewer is "registered BEFORE the probe stack so it sees turns ahead of Highlighting".

The click handshake is hand-rolled ~18 times and the copies are NOT uniform: Menus.cpp:506, 1054 (`|| probePress == 2`), 2009 (`|| getButtonPress() == 2`), 2544, 2830, 2998, 3454, 3839, 5848; PartsApp.cpp:132, 224, 574; ProjectsApp.cpp:747; Peripherals.cpp:3507 (`|| probeButton.getButtonState() == 2`); Highlighting.cpp:124; Apps.cpp:875; Probing.cpp:5160; oled.cpp:4653; ImagesApp.cpp:393. They disagree on whether they also clear lastButtonEncoderState. The INVERSE (re-arm a synthetic click) is hand-rolled wrong at five sites — Menus.cpp:1173, 1373, 1622, JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2735, Debugs.cpp:3234 — each poking `encoderButtonState = RELEASED` without refreshing buttonEventTimestamp, so core 1's next 500 us poll takes the auto-clear branch at RotaryEncoder.cpp:548-556 and the click is a coin flip. synthesizeEncoderClick() exists at RotaryEncoder.cpp:1051-1055 precisely to do this correctly, and its own comment (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1041-1043) explains why the timestamp matters; none of the five use it.

rotaryDivider (RotaryEncoder.cpp:382) is a global sensitivity knob written at 20+ sites across 9 files — Menus.cpp:1036, 1817, 2474, 2874, 3390, 3663, 5803 plus twelve restore sites; PartsApp.cpp:620/705; ProjectsApp.cpp:702/768; Peripherals.cpp:3427 plus four restores; Probing.cpp:2920, 3967, 5549, 5661; Highlighting.cpp:342; GraphicOverlays.cpp:770 — with the "borrow it and put it back" convention enforced only by comments.

encoderOverride (RotaryEncoder.h:36, RotaryEncoder.cpp:384) is the suspend token that RotaryEncoder.cpp:320-329 warns getEncoderRawCount() requires — but it is a COUNTDOWN decremented every core-1 poll (RotaryEncoder.cpp:1020-1021), set to 10 by three MicroPython entry points (JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2721, 2727, 2733) and to 100000 by the debug capture (Debugs.cpp:2321, 2752, 2809), and zeroed by two unrelated sites (Debugs.cpp:3084, Python_Proper.cpp:5025). A shared countdown, not an owner: two overlapping borrowers cannot be distinguished.

THE PROBE. ProbeButton::buttonPress is a sticky latch with no age-out — getButtonPress (Probing.cpp:601-612) reads it, sets a 300 ms block, and consumes, with NO timestamp test anywhere. JumperlOS.cpp:1019 asserts the opposite in a comment ("getButtonPress() also rejects stale latches by age"), and popContext's compensating flush switch (JumperlOS.cpp:1022-1028) covers only PYTHON_REPL, MAIN_MENU, CLICKWHEEL_MENU and DEBUG_MENU — omitting APP_GENERIC, which is the type runApp actually pushes (Apps.cpp:187-192), plus PROBING, FILE_MANAGER, EKILO_EDITOR and HELP_DOCS.

probeActive (main.cpp:722) is the probe-session token, and a TERMINAL command owns it: cmd_loadNodeFile sets it to 1 at SingleCharCommands.cpp:1294 and back to 0 at :1310. For that duration a typed `f` command makes core 1's logoSwirl (main.cpp:1792, 1799) and defcon gate (main.cpp:1817) believe a probe session is live, makes OledGui yield the panel (OledGui.cpp:640), stops the net-voltage scan (NetVoltageScan.cpp:610, 1454), and vetoes implicit config saves (configManager.cpp:100).

switchPosition (Probing.h:552) has four unrelated writer families with no validation between them: the classifier (Probing.cpp:5840, 6028, 6062, 6080, 6104, 6131, 6836), MicroPython (JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2801), an app (Apps.cpp:857) and MpRemoteService.cpp:387.

**Proposal:** One input router with a consumer token, layered over today's emitter without touching a single timing constant.

1. `takeEncoderTurn(FocusId)`, `takeEncoderClick(FocusId)`, `takeProbeTap(FocusId)` — each returns an event only to the CURRENT focus owner, where the focus owner is a value on P6's context stack. That replaces "whoever polls first eats the detent" and retires the registration-order priority hack at main.cpp:539. encoderDirectionConsumed becomes the one real ack, which resurrects the two dead guards at RotaryEncoder.cpp:1165/1179.
2. rotaryDivider becomes a field of the focus entry, set on push and restored on pop (P6 item 1). The twenty hand-written save/restore pairs delete themselves, and the reseed-on-change cost at RotaryEncoder.cpp:1102-1110 is paid once per focus change instead of per uncoordinated write.
3. encoderOverride becomes an `EncoderSuspend` RAII object with a depth, not a shared countdown — same suspend semantics, but two overlapping borrowers no longer clobber each other's deadline.
4. Probe side: give ProbeButton's latch a timestamp and make getButtonPress honour it, so JumperlOS.cpp:1019's comment becomes true and the popContext switch (JumperlOS.cpp:1022-1028) can be deleted rather than extended context-by-context.
5. probeActive splits into two things that were conflated: `probeSessionOwner` (a focus entry, per P6 item 2) and `probeLedAnimationHint` (what LEDs.cpp reads). cmd_loadNodeFile (SingleCharCommands.cpp:1294) stops impersonating a probe session on four unrelated subsystems.
6. switchPosition gets one writer (the classifier) plus an explicit `overrideSwitchPosition(source)` for MicroPython/apps, so a script and the classifier cannot silently fight.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/RotaryEncoder.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Menus.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/PartsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/ProjectsApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Peripherals.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/StepViewer.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/ImagesApp.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Apps.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/SingleCharCommands.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/selfreflection/Debugs.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/snakes/MpRemoteService.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FilesystemStuff.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/JumperlOS.cpp

**V6 relevance:** caps.hasRotaryEncoder and caps.hasProbePads (board.h:83-84) already gate SERVICE REGISTRATION — main.cpp:556 skips the whole probe stack on OG, main.cpp:654 skips encoder init — but every CONSUMER reads the raw globals directly, so the capability seam stops at the door. An event that carries a source id and is delivered to a focus owner is what lets a V6 have two wheels, a wheel plus a touch strip, or a probe module that is absent at boot, without 44 consumer sites each assuming the one global they read is the one and only input device. This is the "missing seam" case CodeDocs/OG_BACKPORT.md:104-105 describes.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Introduce `bool takeEncoderClick()` in RotaryEncoder.cpp implementing today's exact idiom (`RELEASED && lastButtonEncoderState == PRESSED` -> set IDLE, return true) and convert only the TEXT-EXACT-MATCH call sites — Menus.cpp:506, 2544, PartsApp.cpp:132, ProjectsApp.cpp:747, Highlighting.cpp:124, Apps.cpp:875, Probing.cpp:5160, oled.cpp:4653, ImagesApp.cpp:393. The sites with extra `|| probePress == 2` clauses (Menus.cpp:1054, 2009, 2830, 3454, 3839, 5848, Peripherals.cpp:3507, PartsApp.cpp:224/574) need a per-site audit and stay untouched. Separately, swap the five broken re-arm sites (Menus.cpp:1173, 1373, 1622, JumperlessMicroPythonAPI.cpp:2735, Debugs.cpp:3234) to synthesizeEncoderClick() — stated honestly, that is a behavior change: it turns a coin flip into the documented behavior the helper was written for.

## P4 Routing rebuild ownership — two guards that ignore each other, five entry points, one fabric  (days)

**Problem:** refreshInProgress (Commands.cpp:173) and refreshLocalInProgress (Commands.cpp:339) are independent re-entrancy guards over the SAME arrays — globalState.connections.nets/paths and CH446Q lastChipXY. refreshConnections checks only its own at Commands.cpp:188; refreshLocalConnections checks only its own at Commands.cpp:353. Neither excludes the other, and the surfaces reach different ones: the terminal `f` and the probe go through refreshLocalConnections, the menu's Show>Voltage and slot loads go through refreshConnections. The codebase already carries the scar — States.cpp:4640-4649: "CRITICAL FIX: Check BOTH refreshInProgress AND refreshLocalInProgress! ... Missing this caused the 1-second freeze bug." And the fix was applied at ONE call site: systemIdleForFlush (externVars.cpp:267) still checks only refreshInProgress, with States.cpp:4798 patching the composite locally (`systemIdleForFlush() && !refreshLocalInProgress && !core1busy`).

The two functions are the same NetManager -> router -> CH446Q rebuild written twice, and they diverge in exactly the safety-relevant places:
- Core-1 frame hold: refreshConnections calls holdCore1Frames()/releaseCore1Frames() (Commands.cpp:208, 250). refreshLocalConnections has BOTH commented out (Commands.cpp:386, 442). So core 1 can render a frame out of a half-rewritten net table on the probe and terminal paths.
- core1busy ordering: set at Commands.cpp:210, BEFORE clearAllNTCC at :218, in the full path; set at Commands.cpp:389, AFTER clearAllNTCC at :388, in the local path — the array wipe happens outside the guard on the path the probe takes.
- Self-heal: `if (anyChipXYSuspect()) clean = 1;` exists only in refreshConnections (Commands.cpp:255-257).
- The `clean` parameter is DEAD in refreshLocalConnections: declared at Commands.cpp:343, never read in the body (verified by scanning lines 343-489), because the function always posts REQ_BYPASS at Commands.cpp:447. Callers that ask for clean=1 silently get clean=0.
- Coalescing: refreshConnections genuinely re-runs a queued rebuild (Commands.cpp:314-317). refreshLocalConnections' re-run is COMMENTED OUT (Commands.cpp:483-486) and fastRefresh's likewise (Commands.cpp:703-705) — the pending flag is cleared and the queued rebuild is dropped, immediately under a comment that calls it "critical for MicroPython scripts that make multiple connections quickly" (Commands.cpp:481-482).
- The 200 ms core2busy spin at the head of refreshLocalConnections (Commands.cpp:366-384) is timeout-and-PROCEED (:375-378): if it fires, the rebuild runs concurrently with a core-1 render over the same arrays — the exact race the wait exists to prevent.

Five entry points share these globals with five different contracts: refresh() (Commands.cpp:155), refreshConnections() (:177), refreshLocalConnections() (:343), refreshBlind() (:499), fastRefresh() (:554).

**Proposal:** One rebuild owner and one body.

1. Replace the two booleans with a single `RouteRebuild` guard holding `{ Owner owner; RebuildKind kind; bool queued; RebuildOpts queuedOpts; }`. "A rebuild is in progress" becomes one question with one answer that every caller — including systemIdleForFlush, InfraPaths.cpp:658/670, oled.cpp:3252, OledGui.cpp:653, NetVoltageScan.cpp:606/1402 and States.cpp:4798 — asks the same way.
2. Merge refreshConnections and refreshLocalConnections into one `rebuild(RebuildOpts)` where every divergence above is a named field: `holdFrames`, `honourClean`, `selfHealSuspectChips`, `waitForSendGeneration`, `mailboxSlot`, `assignTermColours`. Today's two behaviours ship as two presets, so nothing changes at runtime — but the safety-relevant difference becomes a two-struct diff instead of two 130-line functions that drifted.
3. Make the core1busy + frame-hold bracket span clearAllNTCC on BOTH paths (it already does on one). Honour `clean` or delete the parameter; do not keep a lie in the signature.
4. Make the queued re-run real on the local path (it is already real on the full path), so a coalesced rebuild is not silently dropped.
5. Keep the 200 ms fail-open behaviour but attribute it: on timeout, record which owner was blocking, so the WARNING at Commands.cpp:376 names a cause instead of a symptom.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Commands.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Commands.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/coredination/externVars.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/routing/States.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/routing/InfraPaths.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/sensing/NetVoltageScan.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/oled.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/eyecandy/OledGui.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FileParsing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp

But every V6 rebuild still funnels through these five entry points, and the ordering rules that keep core 1 off a half-rewritten net table are copied, not shared. A different chip count or Y0 rule changes what happens *inside* the rebuild; five copies of the bracket is five places to get the new topology's concurrency wrong. One rebuild owner is what makes the topology seam actually safe to exercise.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Add `refreshLocalInProgress` to systemIdleForFlush's veto list at externVars.cpp:267. Verified equivalence: the function has exactly two live callers — States.cpp:4798, which already ANDs `!refreshLocalInProgress` at the call site (so the composite there is unchanged), and FileCache.cpp:1408, which is compile-gated off because USE_FILE_CACHE defaults to 0 (FileCache.h:45-46, not overridden in platformio.ini). Strictly a narrowing of a flash-write gate. Pair it with replacing the dead `int clean` parameter name in refreshLocalConnections (Commands.cpp:343) with a commented-out name pointing at Commands.cpp:447, so the signature stops advertising a contract it does not honour.

## P5 Dirty / idle / flash ownership — two dirty concepts, one phantom, and an idle gate a terminal command can lie to  (hours)

**Problem:** THREE "this changed" flags, only one of which is alive.
- globalState.dirty via markDirty() (States.cpp:568-577, ~20 call sites) is the live one, consumed by the auto-save at States.cpp:4798.
- The slot-validation bitmask is READ but never SET: slotIsValidated() (FileParsing.cpp:2953-2956) is consulted on load paths at FileParsing.cpp:2247 and :2410, but every call to its writer markSlotAsModified() (FileParsing.cpp:2967-2973) is commented out — FileParsing.cpp:1611 and :1812. A slot is therefore validated-or-not according to a flag nothing ever invalidates.
- netsUpdated is a pure phantom in a public header: declared extern at FileParsing.h:19, defined `= true` at FileParsing.cpp:29, written `false` once at FileParsing.cpp:2243, and read by NOTHING — a grep across src/, include/ and lib/ returns exactly those three lines.

THE IDLE GATE HAS ONE OWNER AND EIGHT INPUTS, ONE OF WHICH IS SPOOFABLE. systemIdleForFlush (externVars.cpp:254-273) checks the context stack, then probeActive, inClickMenu, refreshInProgress, loadingFile, core1busy, usbMountedByHost and a quiet window. Its own header comment (externVars.cpp:249-253) argues the context stack should make the per-mode flags unnecessary, and then adds three of them anyway because PROBING does not push a context (:255-258, see P6).
Two consequences fall straight out:
- probeMode's own 3-second idle save can never fire, because systemIdleForFlush vetoes on probeActive (externVars.cpp:264) and probeMode is what sets probeActive (Probing.cpp:2823). An arbitrarily long probe session holds every change in RAM only.
- A TERMINAL command can spoof the gate: cmd_loadNodeFile sets probeActive = 1 (SingleCharCommands.cpp:1294) for the duration of an `f`, which also blocks implicit config saves (configManager.cpp:99-100). Neither of these is a probe session (flag ownership catalogued in P3).
The filesystem-activity indicators are a fourth, unrelated notion of "busy with flash": filesystemActive / filesystemActiveUntil (externVars.cpp:44-46) feed only the logo colour (LEDs.cpp:3824), and never the idle gate.

**Proposal:** One dirty concept, one idle authority.

1. Delete netsUpdated outright (FileParsing.h:19, FileParsing.cpp:29 and :2243). It is unreferenced; leaving it in a public header advertises a contract that does not exist.
2. Resolve the slot-validation flag in one direction: either restore the two commented-out markSlotAsModified writers (FileParsing.cpp:1611, :1812) or delete the readers at FileParsing.cpp:2247 and :2410 along with the API. A flag that is read but never set is worse than either.
3. Make systemIdleForFlush ask P6's stack ONE question — `contextIdleForFlush()` — instead of re-deriving the same answer from three per-mode globals. Once PROBING pushes a context (P6 item 2), the probeActive term goes away, probeMode's own 3 s idle save becomes reachable, and a terminal `f` stops impersonating a probe session.
4. Fold refreshLocalInProgress into the gate (see P4's tonight slice) so "mid-reroute" means the same thing on all five rebuild entry points.
5. Have the gate return a REASON, not a bool. Today "why didn't my board save?" is an eight-term boolean nobody can inspect; one `IdleVeto` enum surfaced in the existing `X` diagnostic makes it a one-line answer.

**Affected:** /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/coredination/externVars.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/coredination/externVars.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FileParsing.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FileParsing.h, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/routing/States.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/configManager.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/remembering/FileCache.cpp, /Users/kevinsanto/Documents/GitHub/JumperlOS/src/Probing.cpp

**V6 relevance:** Nothing here is board-specific today, which is precisely the risk: a modular V6 adds hot-plug events (a module appearing, a display attaching, a bus being claimed) that all want to say "not now" to a flash write, and the current answer is to add a ninth `extern volatile` term to a boolean in externVars.cpp that no caller can inspect. DisplayService already generates exactly this class of event — it routes pins, claims nodes and self-heals (DisplayService.cpp:224, 298, 323) — and it participates in the idle gate not at all. A gate that returns a named veto is the version of this that survives more surfaces.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Delete netsUpdated (FileParsing.h:19, FileParsing.cpp:29, FileParsing.cpp:2243) — provably unreferenced across src/, include/ and lib/, so removal cannot change behavior. Alongside it, add a one-line comment at externVars.cpp:264 recording that this veto is what makes probeMode's own 3 s idle save at Probing.cpp:3190 unreachable, and a comment at FileParsing.cpp:2247/2410 recording that slotIsValidated has no live writer. Documentation of a live contradiction is worth more than a flag deleted in the wrong direction.

## Surface: one present-contract for every output (the paradigm)  (days)

**Problem:** Three output surfaces, three unrelated present models — and the newest one already solved it.

BREADBOARD PART DISPLAY (got it right): DisplayTypes.h:80-99 holds fb + shadow + shadowValidMask + flushCursor + chunkBytes + midFrame + dirty; DisplayOps (DisplayTypes.h:31-38) is one vtable per family; monoOledFlushChunk (DisplayDrivers.cpp:117-175) memcmps each chunk against the shadow and skips matches for free, merges position+data into one transaction (:160-170), and does a tear-free atomic reveal via the pageFlip quirk (DisplayTypes.h:53-57, DisplayDrivers.cpp:141-149).

TOP OLED (no shadow at all on the ship path): oled.cpp:2314 calls _displayPtr->display(), and Adafruit_SSD1306::display() (Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:1036-1051) ships WIDTH*((HEIGHT+7)/8) bytes unconditionally with no dirty tracking. Arithmetic at 128x32: fb = 128*((32+7)/8) = 512 B; WIRE_MAX = 32 (Adafruit_SSD1306.cpp:71) means 1 control + 31 data per transaction = ceil(512/31) = 17 transactions; wire bytes = 512 data + 17 control + 17 address = 546; 546 x 9 bit-times / 400 kHz (oled.cpp:72) = 12.285 ms of BLOCKING core-0 time to change one character. 128x64 doubles it to 24.6 ms. This is the largest single term in every one of the twelve path traces.

LED STRIP (dirty flags built, then ignored): LEDs.cpp:167-173's own FOLLOW-UP says so; the `if (bbDirty)` guards are commented out at LEDs.cpp:182 and :191; markBBDirty/markTopDirty/markAllDirty (LEDs.cpp:242-252, LEDs.h:240-242) have no consumer. Both strips are pushed unconditionally, so an idle frame costs the full 9.30 ms (300 px x 24 bits x 1.25 us = 9.00 ms + 300 us latch) instead of 0.

TWO THINGS ALREADY EXIST AND ARE WASTED. oled.cpp:3089 declares oledPanelShadow — its own comment: "Mirrors the bytes that were last shipped to the physical OLED via I2C" — but it is only maintained inside a toast hold. And oled.cpp:3790-3801 (streamFrameToSer3) computes a full-frame FNV-1a and skips identical frames — for the USBSer3 stream only, and it runs at oled.cpp:2277, thirty-seven lines BEFORE the panel transmit that has no such check.

**Proposal:** THE PARADIGM, in one sentence: every output is a Surface with {shadow-diff dirty marking, a present(budgetUs) that reports whether the bytes actually landed, one priority bit, and a core1req mailbox generation as the only cross-core handshake}. P2-P6 are migration steps onto it.

Concretely: lift DisplayInstance's render block (DisplayTypes.h:80-99) and DisplayOps (DisplayTypes.h:31-38) out of src/displays/ into a surface-neutral OutputSurface + SurfaceOps { PresentResult present(uint32_t budgetUs); } returning {DONE, MORE, ERR}. Three implementations: LedStripSurface, TopOledSurface, PartDisplaySurface. Nothing above the boundary changes — clearPrintShow, b.print and requestLedShow keep their exact vocabulary; only what happens under them changes.

CROSS-CORE HANDOFF, stated as policy: mailbox generations (CoreMailbox.h:100-116) are the only handshake. The bare-bool test-and-set locks around output state migrate onto it — logoLedAccess (declared Graphics.h:82, defined Graphics.cpp:4387) is taken non-atomically from core 0 at Graphics.cpp:4599-4600 (dumpLEDs) and from core 1 at LEDs.cpp:3768-3771 (logoSwirl), plus LEDs.cpp:1433-1438/:1448-1467 (setLogoOverride) and :1575-1578 (clearColorOverrides), and every loser returns with no retry — so a detent's breadcrumb-pad update is silently dropped whenever core 1 is mid-swirl.

Why the OLED is the first migration: getBuffer() is public (Adafruit_SSD1306.h:166) and oled.cpp already uses it at :3162, :3187 and :3220; ssd1306_command() is public (Adafruit_SSD1306.h:164). So a shadow + 0x21/0x22 column/page-window delta flush is implementable entirely inside oled.cpp with zero library changes, in exactly the shape DisplayDrivers.cpp:160-170 already proves. One changed page costs 128 + 5 control + 5 address = 138 B x 9 / 400 kHz = 3.1 ms instead of 12.285 ms; a column-windowed delta on a 6-character change is ~36 B = ~0.9 ms.

**Affected:** src/oled.cpp, src/oled.h, src/displays/DisplayTypes.h, src/displays/DisplayDrivers.cpp, src/displays/DisplayService.cpp, src/LEDs.cpp, src/LEDs.h, src/Graphics.cpp

**V6 relevance:** This IS the V6 story, and DisplayService already wrote the thesis: DisplayTypes.h:8-17 — "A display IS a part: its wiring is part bridges, its driver is a const descriptor in rodata (many part names -> one descriptor), and its pixels are pushed by DisplayService in bounded chunks." A V6 peripheral plugs in as a descriptor + one present(). Today that door only exists for breadboard-attached panels; the onboard OLED and the strips are hand-written one-offs, so nothing V6 adds can reuse their pacing, liveness hysteresis, or tear-avoidance. Note the two liveness state machines that would converge: oled.cpp:2340-2365's 3-consecutive-failure counter and DisplayService.cpp:401-425's 8-consecutive-flush-error counter are the same idea, different constants, and only the latter unsticks the bus before retrying.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Promote oledPanelShadow (oled.cpp:3089, sized by oledHoldComputeBufferSize at :3098) to always-valid, and gate the transmit at oled.cpp:2314 on memcmp(getBuffer(), oledPanelShadow, n) != 0. A 512-byte memcmp is ~128 words x ~3 cyc = ~2.6 us at 150 MHz against 12.285 ms of I2C. Behavior-preserving by definition: an identical frame is invisible. THREE RULES OR IT BREAKS: (a) update the shadow only after a SUCCESSFUL transmit — never on the hold early-return (oled.cpp:2286-2288) or the wavegen early-return (:2298-2301), both of which return true without transmitting; (b) invalidate the shadow on init/reconnect/size change, because GDDRAM is unknown after a reinit — mirror DisplayTypes.h:84-86's shadowValidMask-cleared-on-reinit; (c) honest scope: this kills the no-op-repaint class (ReadingDisplay re-shows, OledGui's 15 ms free-run), not the live ones — MeasureMode's oscilloscope changes every frame, so the 12.3 -> 3.1 ms headline belongs to the per-page delta, which is the days work.

## Delivery, not fire-and-forget: complete() only when the bytes landed  (days)

**Problem:** core1req already has the right primitive — request generation, done generation, coalescing bits, sticky flags (CoreMailbox.h:100-116). Core 1 then throws the guarantee away: main.cpp:1977 calls complete() unconditionally, and ledClass::show() (LEDs.cpp:174) returns void.

What show() actually does: LEDs.cpp:184 and :193 skip BOTH strips when isDMABusy(), leave bbDirty/topDirty set, and nothing reads those flags. The comment excusing it at LEDs.cpp:179-180 — "The double-buffering in the driver will queue the next frame automatically" — is factually wrong: JeoPixel::isDMABusy() (JeoPixel.h:314-317) is a bare dma_channel_is_busy(); there is no queue. The frame is gone. ledShowIdle() (Commands.cpp:101-102) then reports delivered, and ledFramesShown++ (main.cpp:1960) over-counts.

WHEN IT FIRES, with arithmetic: schedulerTimer is stamped at the END of the block (main.cpp:2044), so the next NETS attempt is at show_start + 8.00 ms + tail + R, where R is render time. bb DMA is busy until show_start + 9.30 ms. Drop iff R + tail < 1.30 ms. R backed out of the tree's own hardware probe (send->show 7.1 ms typical, SCHEDULER_AND_HARDWARE_OFFLOAD.md:719-720 and :748-749, minus the 4.0 ms mean tick wait) is 2.5-3.1 ms. So a BUSY board is safe and a SPARSE board drops — the safety margin is inverted, which is why the failure reads as random.

EVERY KEEPALIVE IN THE TREE IS A WORKAROUND FOR THIS, and each says so: MenuTransitions.cpp:41-46 ("leds.show() DROPS frames while the previous DMA transfer is still streaming (a full strip takes ~9ms)... The linger guarantees several more show attempts") -> MT_LINGER_MS = 50 at :47; menuShowKeepalive at Menus.cpp:483-489, 50 ms, called from :2661, :2980, :3441, :3704; PartsApp.cpp:110-114 at 250 ms and ProjectsApp.cpp:726-730 at 250 ms, both citing menuShowKeepalive by name in their comments. Plus the 51 ms swirl (main.cpp:2012) as recovery of last resort.

**Proposal:** Make ledClass::show() return bool — true only when every dirty strip actually shipped. Core 1 completes the generation only on true; on false it completes and re-posts the taken bits. The two frame-hold abort paths (main.cpp:1843-1846 and :1908-1912) already do exactly this and are the proof the pattern works; factor them and the new drop path into one abortAndRepost(taken, ledGen) helper.

THIS ALSO FIXES A LIVE BUG IN THOSE TWO ABORT PATHS: they re-post with core1req::post() — a plain bitwise OR — instead of postMode() with the MENU keep-rule requestLedShow() enforces (Commands.cpp:97-98). So a NETS posted during the abort window becomes NETS|MENU, and the rails ladder at main.cpp:1782-1785 checks MENU before NETS: the next take renders a menu-buffer flush and the full netlist render is consumed and lost. That is precisely the failure the keep-rule was written to prevent, reintroduced by the abort path.

Once delivery is guaranteed, delete MT_LINGER_MS (MenuTransitions.cpp:47), menuShowKeepalive (Menus.cpp:483-489) and its four call sites, and the two 250 ms app keepalives. Their comments become false the moment this lands, which is the tell that they were never features.

**Affected:** src/LEDs.cpp, src/LEDs.h, src/main.cpp, src/Commands.cpp, src/eyecandy/MenuTransitions.cpp, src/Menus.cpp, src/PartsApp.cpp, src/ProjectsApp.cpp

**V6 relevance:** Delivery reporting is the contract a modular peripheral needs and cannot fake. A V6 output on a slower or contended bus WILL sometimes fail to ship a frame; if the framework's answer is 'complete it anyway and let each UI invent a keepalive', every new peripheral drags in a sixth and seventh hand-rolled retry loop with its own cadence. DisplayService already returns the right thing — DisplayTypes.h:34-36 defines flushChunk as '>0 while mid-frame, 0 when the frame completed, <0 on a bus error' — so this makes the strip speak the language the display framework already speaks.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Change ledClass::show() (LEDs.cpp:174) to return bool, and at main.cpp:1977 replace the unconditional complete() with complete-then-conditionally-repost using postMode() and the same keep-rule as Commands.cpp:97-98. Touch nothing else — leave every keepalive in place. Behavior-preserving by construction: the only observable change is that a frame which was silently lost now arrives one tick later, and the keepalives that used to cover it simply stop having anything to cover. Define the partial case explicitly: return true only if every dirty strip shipped; re-showing an already-landed strip on the retry is harmless. Note the re-posted MENU retries on every pass for the <=9.3 ms DMA window — bounded and intended.

## Split priority from compositing: the ledImmediate ladder is inverted and doubles as the layer switch  (days)

**Problem:** Exactly one place in the tree expresses output priority, and it is backwards. main.cpp:1762 defines ledImmediate = LED_MENU | LED_GFX; the 8 ms schedulerUpdateTime gate (main.cpp:1499, applied at :1768) is therefore paid by LED_NETS and skipped by MENU/GFX. LED_NETS is what a probe highlight posts (Highlighting.cpp:717, requestLedShow(1)), what a routing change posts (Commands.cpp:456 and :300), what a terminal `f` posts. LED_MENU is the menu text flush and the button-hold animation. The most latency-sensitive request in the system waits a mean 4.0 ms / max 8.0 ms; the least waits zero. That 4 ms mean sits in the probe-tap, terminal-connect, and probe-connect budgets in every trace.

WORSE, PRIORITY AND COMPOSITING ARE THE SAME SWITCH. renderGraphicOverlays() has exactly one call site — main.cpp:1883, inside the NETS branch (grep-verified: the only other hits are the definition at GraphicOverlays.cpp:319 and the declaration at GraphicOverlays.h:155). Every overlay mutation posts requestLedShow(-2) = LED_MENU|LED_CLEAR (GraphicOverlays.cpp:93, :275, :299), which takes the MENU branch and skips the composite entirely. So an overlay's own post burns a full 9.30 ms strip frame showing the PRE-overlay picture, and the overlay appears only on the next NETS pass — up to 51 ms later via the swirl tick (main.cpp:2012). The intended trigger was never wired: GraphicOverlayState::needsRender (GraphicOverlays.h:74) is written at eight sites (GraphicOverlays.cpp:105, 136, 176, 201, 218, 257, 279, 303) and read at zero.

**Proposal:** Split the two axes that `rails` (main.cpp:1782-1785) conflates. (a) WHAT to compose: a layer set — nets, menu buffer, overlays, staged graphics — each with its own dirty bit, so marking the overlay layer dirty composites the overlay whatever else renders. (b) WHEN: one priority bit per request (INTERACTIVE = next pass, AMBIENT = tick-paced), independent of what it composes. A probe tap's NETS is INTERACTIVE; the 51 ms swirl is AMBIENT; a menu flush is INTERACTIVE as it is today. That is one bit added to the LED_* enum in CoreMailbox.h:66-75, which already has room.

CAVEAT THE IMMEDIATE-NETS CHANGE NEEDS, or it saturates core 1: the 8 ms tick is accidentally load-bearing as a coalescer. Highlighting has no periodUs override (Highlighting.h:29 declares priority only, so the base default of 0 at JumperlOS.h:161 applies) and posts requestLedShow(1) once per core-0 pass for the whole window a cached reading stays positive. Make NETS immediate and those become back-to-back full renders (2.5-3.1 ms each) colliding with the 9.30 ms DMA for the duration of every tap. So the interactive flag must ship with either edge-triggered posting in Highlighting (post on net CHANGE — it already tracks lastPrintedNet/brightenedNet to do this) or the surface-level nextFrameDue gate from P6. It is not a free 4 ms.

**Affected:** src/main.cpp, src/coredination/CoreMailbox.h, src/Commands.cpp, src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp, src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.h, src/eyecandy/Highlighting.cpp

**V6 relevance:** V6 multiplies output requests without multiplying wire bandwidth. A framework whose only priority expression is a hardcoded two-mode bitmask in one if-statement on core 1 cannot answer 'this new peripheral's request is interactive and that one is ambient' — every addition re-litigates main.cpp:1762. One priority bit in the request, read by the surface, is what lets a V6 module declare its own latency class without editing the core-1 loop. Splitting layers from priority is also what lets a V6 peripheral own a compositing layer without needing its own branch in the rails ladder.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Change GraphicOverlays.cpp:93, :275 and :299 from requestLedShow(-2) to requestLedShow(-1) — three lines. That is the one move that makes clear + composite land in the SAME frame instead of one arriving up to 51 ms after the other, and it is the idiom PartLabels already documents as correct in its own header (PartLabels.h:30: 'every post is clear-first requestLedShow(-1)'). Inside a menu it degrades to exactly today's behavior (the NETS branch is gated off by inClickMenu at main.cpp:1810-1813, so the clear leaks precisely as it does now — no worse). Outside a menu it is a strict fix. Do NOT do the immediate-NETS half tonight; it needs the Highlighting edge-trigger as a companion.

## clearBeforeSend: the clear must live in the request that asked for it  (days)

**Problem:** LEDs.cpp:167-173's own FOLLOW-UP names the target: 'consolidate the overlapping clear mechanisms (clearBeforeSend / clearLEDsExceptRails / hideNets / leds.clear / bread::clear) into one path.' The concrete failure is one global.

clearBeforeSend is a file-scope int (main.cpp:1507). It is latched to 1 from ANY taken request carrying LED_CLEAR (main.cpp:1786) but consumed ONLY inside the NETS branch (main.cpp:1829-1832). Every requestLedShow(-2) = LED_MENU|LED_CLEAR therefore latches a clear it can never consume — including all three GraphicOverlays posts (GraphicOverlays.cpp:93, :275, :299) and, per the menu trace, the -2 exit path in Menus.cpp. The latch then fires on whatever unrelated NETS render comes next, up to 51 ms later at the swirl tick (main.cpp:2012): a board wipe attributable to no user action, arriving well after the thing that requested it.

The deeper shape: the clear is a property of the frame being composed, but it is stored outside the frame, so which frame it lands on depends on branch scheduling rather than on who asked.

**Proposal:** Consume the clear in every branch that renders, not just NETS — i.e. derive it from `taken` (main.cpp:1786 already has the bit) and apply it inside the take block's local scope so it cannot survive the pass. Then fold the other four spellings into one Surface::clear(region) that the request carries: clearBeforeSend, clearLEDsExceptRails, hideNets, leds.clear/bread::clear, and the negative-sign encoding in requestLedShow (Commands.cpp:67-69) are five ways to say one thing, and the sign encoding is the one Commands.cpp:48-54's own FOLLOW-UP already asked to retire.

SEQUENCING MATTERS: this proposal is downstream of P3. The -2 posts are the population that latches an unconsumable clear; once GraphicOverlays posts -1 (P3's slice), the biggest source of the leak is gone and the remaining -2 sites can be audited individually rather than in the dark. Do P3 first, then enumerate what still posts LED_CLEAR without LED_NETS.

**Affected:** src/main.cpp, src/LEDs.cpp, src/LEDs.h, src/Commands.cpp, src/Menus.cpp, src/eyecandy/GraphicOverlays.cpp

**V6 relevance:** 'Blank the surface before composing' is the single most common operation a new output needs, and it is the one this codebase has five incompatible answers for. A V6 peripheral inheriting the current model inherits a global that only one of three render branches can consume. Putting the clear in the request — where CoreMailbox.h:73 already puts the bit — means a new surface gets correct clear-then-compose semantics for free from the mailbox, with no branch of its own in core2stuff.

**Tonight-safe slice:** none. Making clearBeforeSend a local is NOT behavior-preserving: the leaked clear is currently load-bearing for the -2 overlay posts. showNets() alone does not wipe non-net pixels (that distinction is exactly why -1 and 1 both exist), so evaporating the clear leaves overlay ghosts indefinitely, while applying it in the bare rails==2 branch at main.cpp:1922-1925 — which does no buffer repaint at all — ships a mostly-blank board until the 51 ms swirl. Land P3's -2 -> -1 change first; then this becomes a bounded audit of whatever LED_CLEAR posts remain without LED_NETS.

## Terminal is the third surface: one guarded write path, not 413 bare flushes  (hours)

**Problem:** The terminal is an output surface with no present-contract at all, and it is the top wedge in every one of the twelve traces.

JerialClass::writeToOutputs (Jerial.cpp:1794 for the byte form, :1819 for the buffer form) hands bytes straight to output_streams[i]->write() with no room check whatsoever. Adafruit_USBD_CDC::write() spins `while (remain && tud_cdc_n_connected())` calling yield() — a host that holds DTR and stops draining stalls core 0 unboundedly, taking the LED and OLED feedback for that same event down with it, because the Serial.print, the requestLedShow and the oled.clearPrintShow for one user action sit on the same core-0 statement sequence.

THE GUARD ALREADY EXISTS AND REFUSES TO PROTECT THE MAIN STREAM. jl_cdc_wait_writable(stream, need, timeoutMs) (Python_Proper.cpp:329-350, declared Python_Proper.h:260) has an explicit drop-on-timeout contract — 'the caller must DROP its output instead of handing it to a CDC write() that would spin forever.' It has six call sites, all in the MicroPython files: Python_Proper.cpp:363, :531, :1280 and MpRemoteService.cpp:276, :467, :486. Its own exclusion at Python_Proper.cpp:331-333 says it skips non-CDC streams because 'Other streams (Jerial, OLEDOut, ...) may not override availableForWrite() (Print's default returns 0), so gating them here would wrongly drop all their output.' That premise is false for Jerial: JerialClass::availableForWrite() exists at Jerial.cpp:1039-1067 and correctly returns the min across the CDC endpoints.

And the asymmetry is already half-acknowledged: JerialClass::flush() was deliberately defanged for exactly this hazard, with the reasoning written out at Jerial.cpp:1002-1010 — but write() never got the same treatment, and 413 bare Serial.flush() calls in src/ bypass Jerial entirely.

**Proposal:** Put the guard inside writeToOutputs: check jl_cdc_wait_writable(stream, size) before handing bytes down, and on timeout drop and bump a per-stream counter surfaced in the `i!` dump. Correct the stale exclusion at Python_Proper.cpp:331-333 to include Jerial, since it has the accessor.

That gives the terminal the same three properties the other two surfaces are getting under P1/P2: a bounded present, an explicit drop policy instead of an unbounded stall, and a counter so a quietly-dropping host is distinguishable from a healthy one. It is the same shape as the strip's DMA-busy decision and the display framework's flush-error decision — the difference today is only that the terminal has no decision at all.

USE A SHORT TIMEOUT PLUS A STICKY DROP MODE, not the 100 ms default: at 100 ms per write call, a per-line print loop multiplies into multi-second stalls on a wedged host, which is a different bad outcome rather than a fix. Wait briefly, then latch drop-mode for that stream until availableForWrite() reports room again.

HONEST TRADE TO FLAG: dropping terminal output on a wedged host is an observable behavior change. But the current behavior is freezing the board, so this is a considered trade, not a regression — and it is the trade Jerial::flush() already made at Jerial.cpp:1002-1010.

**Affected:** src/Jerial.cpp, src/Jerial.h, src/snakes/Python_Proper.cpp, src/snakes/Python_Proper.h

**V6 relevance:** V6 adds output consumers, and the terminal is the surface every one of them will reach for first, because it needs no hardware. If the write path can block forever, every new peripheral's diagnostic print is a latent whole-board wedge, and each subsystem re-derives its own ad-hoc availableForWrite check (the Ser3 stream already did — oled.cpp:3801 uses `availableForWrite() <= 0`, a not-zero check rather than a fits check, which passes with 10 bytes free and then blocks for the remaining 674-3800). One guarded write path retires that whole class.

**Tonight-safe slice:** Add the guard to the buffer form only — JerialClass::writeToOutputs(const uint8_t*, size_t) at Jerial.cpp:1819 — with a short timeout, a sticky per-stream drop flag, and a drop counter. One function. It converts the tree's worst stall class into a bounded drop on exactly the path that carries the large writes (the 1-2 KB printMenu repaints, the per-keystroke REPL redraws) while leaving the single-byte path untouched, so echo latency is unaffected.

## One frame clock from the hardware, replacing five hand-rolled cadences  (weeks)

**Problem:** Five independent pacers run on core 1 for one 300-pixel strip, and not one of them is derived from the strip's actual 9.30 ms frame floor (300 px x 24 bits x 1.25 us + 300 us latch):

- schedulerUpdateTime = 8000 us (main.cpp:1499, gate at :1768) — SHORTER than the frame it paces.
- MT_FRAME_MS = 16 (MenuTransitions.cpp:55).
- the 51 ms swirl (main.cpp:2012).
- menuShowKeepalive's 50 ms default (Menus.cpp:484).
- LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME = 14 (main.cpp:1536), compared against micros() at main.cpp:1953 — a 14 MICROsecond floor where both the name and the placement read as the ~14 ms WS2812 frame period. Two shows 14 us apart are impossible at that call site, so the one rate limiter written for this job never fires. It is off by 1000x.

PLUS A PHASE BUG: schedulerTimer is stamped at the END of the block (main.cpp:2044), including on the do-nothing `else { rotaryEncoderStuff(); }` branch at main.cpp:2033-2037. So the real NETS period is 8000 + R (10.5-11.0 ms with R = 2.5-3.1 ms), not 8000 us, and a pass that did no LED work at all still pushes the next render out a full 8 ms.

The probe LED is a sixth cadence and is anti-correlated by construction: probing.probeLEDhandler() sits in the ELSE of the tick gate (main.cpp:2046-2049), so the probe LED gets exactly zero service on any pass where the main strip renders, and vice versa.

**Proposal:** One nextFrameDueUs() on the Surface, derived from what the driver already knows — JeoPixel::canShow() arms the latch endTime (JeoPixel.cpp:290) and isDMABusy() reads dma_channel_is_busy() (JeoPixel.h:314-317), so the true 'when may I present again' answer exists in the driver and is simply never asked. Every caller asks the surface instead of guessing a constant. That retires all five timers, plus the phase bug (stamp at start, which is the policy runService already documents for itself at JumperlOS.cpp:180-189), plus the probe LED's anti-correlation, which becomes a scheduling choice ('which strand is due next') rather than an accident of which branch of an if-else it landed in.

ORDERING IS LOAD-BEARING: this lands AFTER P2. Today a frame the LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME gate rejects is dropped with no re-post, because main.cpp:1953-1958 sits inside a block whose `taken` is completed unconditionally at :1977. Making the gate real before delivery is guaranteed turns a dead knob into a live frame-dropper.

**Affected:** src/main.cpp, src/LEDs.cpp, src/LEDs.h, lib/Jadafruit_NeoPixel/JeoPixel.h, src/eyecandy/MenuTransitions.cpp, src/Menus.cpp, src/Probing.cpp

**V6 relevance:** Cadence is the thing every new peripheral re-invents — the traces counted five on the LED strip alone, and DisplayService promptly added a sixth (DISP_PARTS_POLL_MS = 750 at DisplayService.cpp:24, whose comment admits it was copied from 'the oled checkConnection cadence' rather than shared). A V6 peripheral with a different bus speed and a different frame cost cannot be paced by a constant tuned for 300 WS2812 pixels. Asking the surface is the only formulation that scales, and it is what lets a V6 module with a 40 ms panel and one with a 1 ms panel coexist without either starving the other.

**Tonight-safe slice:** DELETE the dead LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME gate — the #define at main.cpp:1536 and the wrapping `if (micros() - ledShowTime > LED_SHOW_MIN_TIME)` at main.cpp:1953 (keeping the ledShowTime stamp for the diagnostics). Provably behavior-preserving: the condition is a 14 us floor at a call site whose minimum spacing is a full core-1 pass, so it is always true and removing it changes nothing at runtime. Do NOT 'fix' it to 14000 — that would newly impose a 71 Hz cap below the strip's own 102 Hz (9.75 ms) floor and throttle NETS bursts. Deleting it removes the trap and makes the real nextFrameDueUs() the only place pacing can live.
